# FluentCMS backend: technical specification (draft for review)

Version 1, 1 Nov 2026. Status: **approved**. The eighteen questions in section 10.1 were decided on 1 Nov 2026 and the text reflects those decisions. GitHub issues for milestones M0 and M1 were created from section 9 the same day; later milestones get their issues when they are planned.

This document takes the approved [feature specification](specification/README.md) and keeps only what runs on the server: the NuGet packages a host adds, the reference host, the server halves of the first-party plugins, and the developer tooling that talks to them. The Blazor admin, the admin halves of plugins, frontend starter kits and the documentation site are out of scope here and get their own documents later. Once this document is approved, GitHub issues are created from section 9.

How to read it: sections 1 to 3 set scope and architecture; sections 4 to 6 describe each backend area with its design, key types, storage and endpoints; section 7 maps every specification row to a backend area and a milestone; section 8 sequences the work; section 9 is the proposed issue list; section 10 records the decisions taken on the open questions and the suggestions that shaped the design. Everything marked *proposed* is still open to change during review.

## 1. Scope

### 1.1 What counts as backend

| In scope | Out of scope (separate documents) |
| --- | --- |
| `FluentCMS.Sdk` public contract | Blazor WebAssembly admin shell and generated views |
| Core engine: schema, storage, content service, versioning, publishing, localization, sites, identity, authorization, activity log, events, hooks, job runner, settings, plugin host | Admin halves of plugins (Razor Class Libraries, field editors, pages, widgets) |
| Delivery API and Management API (REST), error model, OpenAPI document, caching, rate limiting, CORS | Admin distributions (`fluentcms-admin` template, static build) |
| Authorization server (OpenIddict), including the server-rendered login, consent, two-factor, invitation and reset pages it needs | Preview iframe and `postMessage` protocol (the backend ships only preview keys and draft reads) |
| First-party plugins, server side: media, webhooks, GraphQL, MCP, AI assist, multi-site, search | Frontend starter kits (Next.js, Astro, Blazor) |
| Infrastructure: configuration, health, observability, multi-instance safety, export and import, startup diagnostics, reference host image | Documentation site, `llms.txt`, agent skills and repository instructions |
| Tooling that targets the backend: CLI, `fluentcms` and `fluentcms-plugin` templates (server half), test host, source generator, analyzers | The full C# and TypeScript client packages and their publishing (M6 builds only what generated code needs; the rest is tracked with the frontend work) |

The generated C# and TypeScript types are produced from the backend's schema snapshot (4.2). The CLI command that produces them is in scope, with minimal `FluentCMS.Client` and `@fluentcms/client` packages for the generated code to compile against (10.3, P9). The rest of the client packages, and publishing them, are tracked with the frontend work.

**Clients as built (B-93, [#3016](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3016)).** `FluentCMS.Client` and `@fluentcms/client` hold the Delivery reads (list, by id, by a unique field, a singleton), the content group of the Management API (create, update, delete, publish, unpublish), the typed filters, sort, paging, `populate`, locale and status, and a problem with its `FCMS` code. The users, roles, keys and settings endpoints are not in them. CI packs both and publishes neither (6.1).

### 1.2 Decisions this document is bound by

From [scope and locked decisions](specification/00-scope-tiers-locked-decisions.md) and the [decisions log](specification/12-decisions-log.md). Numbers are decision numbers in the log.

| # | Decision | Effect on the backend |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 1, 2 | Human-first, headless-first | The content API is the only read path; no rendering in core. |
| 3 | NuGet packages plus a reference host image | `AddFluentCms()` on any ASP.NET Core host; one Docker image for standalone use. |
| 6, 22 | Two API surfaces with separate prefixes | `/api/delivery/...` public and cacheable; `/api/management/...` authenticated and complete. |
| 7, 30 | OpenIddict in the API host on ASP.NET Core Identity | The host is the single token issuer; external identity providers federate through it. |
| 8, 19, 60 | EF Core, hybrid storage mapping, PostgreSQL 15+ first, SQLite 3.45+, SQL Server 2019+ | Relational columns for scalars and relations, JSON for blocks and rich text. |
| 11, 13, 14, 46, 47, 48 | Compile-time plugins on a public SDK, dogfooded core split, table prefix | Core is schema, storage, REST, identity, versioning, job runner, event bus; the rest are plugins. |
| 17, 18, 20 | GUID v7 ids, ProseMirror JSON, nesting depth 3 | Fixed in the SDK types. |
| 21, 23, 25 | Bracket filter grammar, preview key plus `draft` scope, populate depth 1 | Fixed in the query model. |
| 24, 31, 32, 33 | HotChocolate, additive RBAC, C# row rules, separate member pool | Fixed in the GraphQL plugin and the identity design. |
| 38, 39, 40, 41 | Snapshot versions, draft and published rows, own job runner, warn on unpublished references | Fixed in the publishing design. |
| 42, 43, 44, 45 | Site id in core, shared row plus locale rows, requested-locale filtering, BCP 47 | Fixed in the storage and localization design. |
| 50, 51, 52, 53 | Per-type MCP tools plus `query`, Streamable HTTP, pgvector with fallback, usage logging | Fixed in the MCP and AI assist plugins. |
| 54, 55, 56 | One migration set per provider, auto-migrate only on SQLite by default, ASP.NET Core output caching | Fixed in the data provider packages and caching. |
| 58, 59 | Source generator for C# in the host, monorepo, `dotnet tool` CLI | Fixed in the repository layout and tooling. |
| 61 | `FluentCMS.*` package family, new IDs start at 1.0.0 | Package names in section 2.4. |

### 1.3 Platform targets

| Target | Version | Note |
| --- | --- | --- |
| .NET | 10 LTS (`global.json` pins SDK 10.0.100, roll forward latest feature) | C# 14, file-scoped namespaces and primary constructors per `.editorconfig`. |
| EF Core | 10 | Property-bag entity types, JSON columns, compiled model where useful. |
| PostgreSQL | 15+ | Primary. `jsonb`, `FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED`, full-text search, pgvector optional. |
| SQLite | 3.45+ | Development, tests, small sites. JSON functions, FTS5, polling job runner. |
| SQL Server | 2019+ | `nvarchar(max)` JSON with `ISJSON` checks and `OPENJSON` on every version, 2025 included ([ADR 0001](../decisions/0001-storage-spike-outcome.md#sql-server-json)); `READ_COMMITTED_SNAPSHOT` on, turned on by the system migrations ([ADR 0005](../decisions/0005-sql-server-read-committed-snapshot.md)); `READPAST` job runner. |
| Containers | Linux and Windows, multi-arch image | Reference host. |

## 2. Architecture

### 2.1 Layering

```text
ASP.NET Core host (your app, or FluentCMS.Host)
|
+-- HTTP surfaces
|   Delivery API, Management API, OAuth/OIDC endpoints and hosted pages,
|   GraphQL (plugin), /mcp (plugin), /media (plugin), /health
|   -> authenticate, resolve site and locale, parse the query, map errors, cache
|
+-- In-process services: the only place rules are enforced
|   IContentService<T>, IMediaService, IAuthorizationEvaluator,
|   IActivityLog, IEventBus, IJobScheduler
|   -> permissions, row rules, field filtering, validation, hooks, versions
|
+-- Schema and storage
|   SchemaModel (built from C# types) -> EF Core model (property bags) -> migrations
|
+-- EF Core provider: FluentCMS.Data.PostgreSql, .Sqlite or .SqlServer
```

Rules that follow from the layering:

- HTTP, GraphQL and MCP layers carry identity and intent only. They never touch the `DbContext` and never evaluate permissions themselves. An architecture test enforces this (3.4).
- The in-process service is the public API for host code and, later, for the rendering plugin. Whatever REST can do, `IContentService<T>` can do, with the same access checks. There is no "override access" flag; a `System` principal exists for jobs, seeding and the CLI.
- Every operation runs inside an `OperationContext` (2.2). Versions, the activity log, hooks, webhooks and MCP origin tagging all read from it, so it exists from the first commit.

### 2.2 Operation context

One ambient, immutable record flows through every call:

```csharp
public sealed record OperationContext(
    Principal Principal,      // admin user, member, API key, service account, or System
    Origin Origin,            // Admin, Api, Mcp, Job, Cli, Seed
    SiteId Site,
    string Locale,            // resolved request locale, BCP 47
    string? ClientId,         // OAuth client id or API key id
    string CorrelationId,
    DateTimeOffset Timestamp);
```

The entry point sets the origin: Delivery and Management endpoints set `Api`, requests from the admin's OAuth client map to `Admin`, the MCP plugin sets `Mcp`, the job runner sets `Job`. Origin, principal and client id are persisted on every version and activity row. As built, the request bridge also sets `IsDelivery` on a request of the Delivery API, core's routes and a plugin's under `MapDelivery` alike, from the surface of the endpoint ([#3109](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3109)). It says which API was called and grants nothing; a service whose answer there differs reads it, as the media plugin's resolver does (5.1). It is not persisted.

### 2.3 One enforcement point

`IAuthorizationEvaluator` answers three questions, and nothing else reads permission data:

1. May this principal perform operation *X* on type *T*, optionally on this entry, in this site and locale?
2. Which fields of *T* may it read or write? Hidden fields are omitted from responses, not blanked.
3. Which row-rule predicate must be appended to list queries for *T*?

`IContentService` asks on every operation. The schema introspection endpoint and the MCP tool list ask to filter what they expose. The admin reads the same answers through the Management API.

Every answer is about the principal of the current `OperationContext`, so a check without one fails closed. The content service throws (4.4), and so do the permission checks of the Management services (users, roles, keys, sites, jobs, plugins, the activity log, schema introspection) and `IPermissionChecker`: an `InvalidOperationException` with `FCMS1408` that names `SystemOperation.Begin`. Throwing rather than denying makes code that forgot its operation fail where it runs, not as a 403. `SystemOperation.Begin`, from `Origin.Job`, `Origin.Cli` or `Origin.Seed`, is the one way to run as the system, which every check allows. The job runner and the seed runner open one for every job and seeder. Other host code that calls a Management service, such as a hosted service or a test fixture, opens its own.

### 2.4 Package map

Proposed NuGet packages, all `FluentCMS.*`, first release 1.0.0. A host adds two packages (`FluentCMS.Core` and one data provider) for a working API. Heavy optional dependencies stay out of core.

| Package | Contents | Depends on |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `FluentCMS.Sdk` | Interfaces, attributes, base classes, DTOs, events, permission names. No implementation. Semver-guaranteed, public API tracked by analyzers. | `Microsoft.Extensions.*` abstractions only |
| `FluentCMS.Plugins.Abstractions` | The plugin contracts that need EF Core or ASP.NET Core ([ADR 0003](../decisions/0003-plugin-contribution-contracts.md)): `IPluginModelContributor`, `PluginDbContext<TPlugin>`, `AddModel`, `AddMigrations`, `MapGroup`, `MapManagement` and `MapDelivery`. Semver-guaranteed like the SDK. | `FluentCMS.Sdk`, EF Core Relational, the ASP.NET Core framework |
| `FluentCMS.Core` | The engine and the HTTP surfaces: schema, storage model, content service, query model, versioning, publishing, localization, sites, identity and authorization server, RBAC, activity log, events, hooks, job runner, settings, plugin host, Delivery and Management APIs, OpenAPI, caching, health, diagnostics, export and import, rich text renderers. | `FluentCMS.Sdk`, `FluentCMS.Plugins.Abstractions`, EF Core, ASP.NET Core Identity, OpenIddict, the Google, Microsoft account, GitHub and Apple authentication handlers (Q10) |
| `FluentCMS.Data.PostgreSql` | Npgsql wiring, provider-specific SQL (JSON paths, `SKIP LOCKED`, full-text search), the PostgreSQL system migration set. | `FluentCMS.Core`, Npgsql.EntityFrameworkCore.PostgreSQL |
| `FluentCMS.Data.Sqlite` | Same for SQLite (polling runner, JSON1, FTS5, auto-migrate on by default). | `FluentCMS.Core`, Microsoft.EntityFrameworkCore.Sqlite |
| `FluentCMS.Data.SqlServer` | Same for SQL Server (`READPAST`, `OPENJSON` over `nvarchar(max)`, the system migration that turns `READ_COMMITTED_SNAPSHOT` on). | `FluentCMS.Core`, Microsoft.EntityFrameworkCore.SqlServer |
| `FluentCMS.Plugins.Media` | Assets, folders, local storage provider, transforms, signed URLs, media Delivery API, the `media` field type. | `FluentCMS.Sdk`, `FluentCMS.Plugins.Abstractions`, SkiaSharp |
| `FluentCMS.Plugins.Media.S3`, `.AzureBlob` | Storage providers. | `FluentCMS.Plugins.Media`, AWSSDK.S3 or Azure.Storage.Blobs |
| `FluentCMS.Plugins.Webhooks` | Subscriptions, HMAC signing, delivery with retries, delivery log. | `FluentCMS.Sdk`, `FluentCMS.Plugins.Abstractions` |
| `FluentCMS.Plugins.GraphQL` | Schema generated from the content schema, queries and mutations, limits, `IGraphQLSchemaContributor`. | `FluentCMS.Sdk`, `FluentCMS.Plugins.Abstractions`, HotChocolate |
| `FluentCMS.Plugins.Mcp` | Streamable HTTP MCP server, tools, resources, prompts, client management, agent instructions. | `FluentCMS.Sdk`, `FluentCMS.Plugins.Abstractions`, ModelContextProtocol |
| `FluentCMS.Plugins.AiAssist` | Provider-agnostic AI actions over OpenAI-compatible endpoints, a provider slot for any other client, and the usage log. | `FluentCMS.Sdk`, `FluentCMS.Plugins.Abstractions`, Microsoft.Extensions.AI, Microsoft.Extensions.AI.OpenAI |
| `FluentCMS.Plugins.MultiSite` | Hostname resolution, per-site hosts, cross-site operations. | `FluentCMS.Sdk`, `FluentCMS.Plugins.Abstractions` |
| `FluentCMS.Plugins.Search` | Full-text `q=` parameter on PostgreSQL FTS and SQLite FTS5, a match on a part of the text on SQL Server (10.4, H6); an external index fills the same slot. | `FluentCMS.Sdk`, `FluentCMS.Plugins.Abstractions` |
| `FluentCMS.Caching.Redis` (Should) | Distributed cache, output cache and distributed lock on one connection string. | `FluentCMS.Sdk` |
| `FluentCMS.Testing` | `FluentCmsTestHost`, fixtures for users, keys and content; SQLite by default. | `FluentCMS.Core`, `FluentCMS.Data.Sqlite` |
| `FluentCMS.Cli` | The `fluentcms` dotnet tool. Its project commands run inside the host (6.1). | `FluentCMS.Core` design-time services for `migrations add`, System.CommandLine |
| `FluentCMS.Generators` | Roslyn incremental source generator: typed filter and sort builders for the content types, components and blocks of the host (6.4). The package carries the generator under `analyzers/dotnet/cs` and no library. | `Microsoft.CodeAnalysis.CSharp` 5.0.0, the Roslyn of SDK 10.0.100, private to the generator |
| `FluentCMS.Analyzers` | Roslyn analyzers for schema attributes. | Roslyn |
| `FluentCMS.Templates` | `dotnet new` templates (`fluentcms`, `fluentcms-plugin`). | none |
| `FluentCMS.Client` | Minimal typed HTTP client that generated C# compiles against: Delivery reads, content management, filters, paging, problems (6.1). | no FluentCMS package |

Not a package: `FluentCMS.Host`, the reference host application and Docker image. Not a NuGet package: `@fluentcms/client` in `clients/typescript`, the same set as `FluentCMS.Client` for TypeScript.

**Client packages as built (B-93).** `FluentCMS.Client` references no FluentCMS package and no package at all, which an architecture test holds for its project and its assembly. `@fluentcms/client` is ES modules over `fetch` with no runtime dependency; `typescript` 6.0.3, pinned in its `package.json`, is its only development dependency. Both are packed by the `pack` job of CI, `dotnet pack` and `npm pack`, into the same artifact, and CI publishes neither. Since B-108 the `Publish` workflow (3.2) pushes `FluentCMS.Client` to nuget.org with the other packages (10.4, H1), and `@fluentcms/client` to npm when the repository variable `NPM_SCOPE_OWNED` says the scope is the project's, and to the GitHub release until then (H12).

A host's `Program.cs`:

```csharp
builder.Services.AddFluentCms(cms =>
{
    cms.UsePostgreSql(builder.Configuration.GetConnectionString("cms"));
    cms.AddContentTypesFromAssembly(typeof(Program).Assembly);
    cms.AddPlugin<MediaPlugin>(media => media.UseS3(builder.Configuration.GetSection("S3")));
    cms.AddPlugin<WebhooksPlugin>();
    cms.AddPlugin<McpPlugin>();
});

var app = builder.Build();
app.MapFluentCms(); // delivery, management, oauth, health, plugin endpoints
app.Run();
```

A split into separate `FluentCMS.Identity` and `FluentCMS.Api` packages was considered and rejected (Q1).

Monorepo (decision 59). The admin, starters and docs-site folders are reserved and not covered here.

|   +-- planning/                 this document and specification/

|                                          `generate csharp` writes for it
|   +-- FluentCMS.Plugins.*.Tests/
+-- templates/

|   +-- fluentcms-plugin/                  plugin template, server half (admin half later)

### 3.1 Core internal structure

`FluentCMS.Core` is organised by feature area, one folder per area, each with its own registration method called from `AddFluentCms()`:

```text
src/FluentCMS.Core/
+-- DependencyInjection/   AddFluentCms, FluentCmsBuilder, MapFluentCms, options validation
+-- Schema/                attribute reader, fluent builder, SchemaModel, field type registry,
|                          schema validation, introspection DTOs, snapshot and hash
+-- Storage/               CmsDbContext, model builder from SchemaModel, conventions, JSON
|                          mapping, system entities, migration runner, SQL dialect abstraction
+-- Content/               IContentService, ContentDocument, query model, filter compiler,
|                          projection, population, validation pipeline, hook pipeline, bulk
+-- Publishing/            state machine, versions, publish, unpublish, discard, restore,
|                          scheduling, trash and purge, reference integrity checks
+-- Localization/          locale registry per site, fallback resolution, request locale
+-- Sites/                 Site entity, default site, site resolution by header or parameter
+-- Identity/              Identity stores, OpenIddict server, hosted pages (Razor Pages),
|                          users, invitations, 2FA, SSO federation, API keys, members
+-- Authorization/         permission catalogue, role permissions, evaluator, field filter,
|                          row rule registry, public access
+-- ActivityLog/           writer, query, retention job
+-- Api/
|   +-- Delivery/          per-type read endpoints, status and locale handling, cache headers
|   +-- Management/        content, schema, users, roles, keys, sites, locales, settings,
|   |                      jobs, activity, plugins, transfer, system, me
|   +-- Common/            problem details, filter and sort parsing, pagination, ETag,
|   |                      rate limiting policies, CORS, API key authentication handler
|   +-- OpenApi/           document transformers: per-type schemas, block unions, security
+-- Events/                in-process event bus, event types, outbox for reliable fan-out
+-- Jobs/                  job store, runner, scheduler, retries, dead letter, leases
+-- Plugins/               plugin host, manifest validation, registration order, per-site
|                          enablement, provider slots
+-- Settings/              typed settings model, per-site persistence, change events
+-- Transfer/              export package writer, import with upsert and dry run
+-- Caching/               output cache tags and eviction, database-backed IDistributedLock
+-- Diagnostics/           health checks, startup report, OpenTelemetry instrumentation
+-- RichText/              ProseMirror document model, HTML and Markdown renderers,
                           link and media reference extraction
```

### 3.2 Build and quality conventions

- `Directory.Build.props`: `net10.0`, nullable enabled, warnings as errors, .NET analyzers at `latest-recommended`, deterministic builds, SourceLink, package icon and license expression.
- `Directory.Packages.props`: central package versions. A new dependency is named in the issue before it is added, so it is visible in review.
- `FluentCMS.Sdk` and `FluentCMS.Plugins.Abstractions` use `Microsoft.CodeAnalysis.PublicApiAnalyzers` (`PublicAPI.Shipped.txt`, `PublicAPI.Unshipped.txt`) and `EnablePackageValidation` against the last released version, so the semver promise in decision 14 is enforced by the build. `FluentCMS.Client`, which generated C# compiles against, has the analyzer and its two files too, with no package validation; `RS0026` is off there, since each call has an overload that names the type and one that takes it from the class, with the same optional parameters ([#3310](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3310)). Until 1.0.0 every entry stays in `PublicAPI.Unshipped.txt` and no baseline version is set, so a preview may break the contract (10.4, H1; `docs/releasing.md`, "Through the previews").
- Tests: xUnit v3 with Shouldly (Q13). Tests use SQLite by default. The data tests also run on PostgreSQL 15 and SQL Server 2019 and 2025 through Testcontainers, in CI's data matrix and locally with `FLUENTCMS_TEST_PROVIDER` (Q19, [#2711](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/2711)). The data tests hold the plugins' cleanup of a deleted site too, so it runs on each provider. The fast checks, those of Feature CI and the Windows job of a pull request into, or a push to, `main`, run the SDK, core unit and architecture tests, the tool's tests (`FluentCMS.Cli.Tests`) and the host's commands with export and import on SQLite (`FluentCMS.Core.IntegrationTests`, namespace `Commands`), so that a crash of the tool or of transfer shows before full CI ([#3074](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3074)).
- Public types in `FluentCMS.Sdk` carry XML docs because they ship in the package. Implementation code does not.

**Versions, metadata and publishing as built (B-108).** One version for every package, the image and `@fluentcms/client`: `Directory.Build.props` holds `VersionPrefix` 1.0.0 (decision 61), and a build that is not a release gets a pre-release suffix that no release has, `ci.{run id}.{attempt}` on GitHub Actions and `dev` elsewhere (`fresh.{time}` from a local run of `eng/fresh-machine.ps1`). A release passes the version of its tag with `-p:Version`, the image takes it as the build argument `VERSION`, `npm version` sets it on the client, and `FluentCMS.Templates` stamps its own version into what it makes (6.2). The `Templates` job, the `Fresh machine` jobs and the `Pack` job read the version of the run from MSBuild instead of naming 1.0.0. Every package has a description, the `README.md` beside its project, which `Directory.Build.props` packs, its tags with `fluentcms-plugin` on every package of `src/plugins` (decision 15), the icon, the licence expression, the repository with its commit, and SourceLink in its PDB, in the `.snupkg` or, for the analyzers and the generator, which ship no symbols package, embedded. The `Pack` job runs `eng/check-packages.cs`, a file-based app, which fails on a package that misses a part and on a `@fluentcms/client` without its version, description, licence or README, and checks that a copy of `FluentCMS.Sdk` without each part, SourceLink included, fails it; SourceLink must point at the origin of the clone that packed. `.github/workflows/publish.yml`, the `Publish` workflow, starts from a tag `v*`: the job `check` refuses a tag that is not a version of the prefix, a pre-release `ci`, `dev` or `fresh` in any case, a version without its notes in `docs/releases/{version}.md` or with a GitHub release already, a commit that is not on `main`, a commit without a successful run of `CI` and a successful `Fresh machine` job on Linux and on Windows that tested its own packages (from CI, or from its own workflow without a feed: a run with a feed is named `Fresh machine of the published {version}` and its jobs `Published packages (…)`), read through the Actions API, and a `release` environment that does not exist, has no required reviewer or deploys to anything but the tags `v*`, so that no job names a missing environment, which GitHub would make without a reviewer; `pack` packs every package at the version and checks it; `image` builds the reference host for `linux/amd64` and `linux/arm64` with `docker buildx` and no emulation; `list` prints the packages, the client and where it goes, the image tags and the release; `publish`, in the `release` environment with its required reviewer, stops when the environment holds no `NUGET_USER` and otherwise pushes the image to `ghcr.io/fluentcms/fluentcms` with the version and `preview` (`latest` for a release that is not a preview) first, since a package on nuget.org cannot be deleted, then the packages with their symbols to nuget.org, signed in with trusted publishing (`NuGet/login`, a key of one hour), and the client to npm with the dist-tag `next` for a preview when `NPM_SCOPE_OWNED` is true; `release` makes the GitHub release, a pre-release for a preview, with its notes and the client's `.tgz` while it is not on npm. Each job has its own `permissions`, the workflow none; no credential is a repository secret. A manual run, with a tag to rehearse or none, is a dry run: the first four jobs, with the refusals of the commit as warnings. A pull request that changes the workflow, `Directory.Build.props`, a project file under `src/`, the templates' project, the Dockerfile or the client's `package.json` runs the same dry run without the checks of the commit, with no environment and no secret. `eng/fresh-machine.ps1` takes a feed at a URL with `-Feed` and `-Version`, such as nuget.org, and `-Client`, and the `Fresh machine` and `Compose` workflows take a feed and an image when run by hand, for the checks after a release (`docs/releasing.md`). New actions: `actions/download-artifact` 8.0.2 and `NuGet/login` 1.2.0, pinned by SHA; Docker, `gh` and npm run from the runner.

### 3.3 Naming conventions

| Thing | Convention | Example |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Content type name | lower kebab-case, from the attribute or the class name | `article`, `site-settings` |
| Field name in the API | camelCase of the C# property | `publishedOn` |
| Table | `{prefix}{type}`, prefix default `cms_`; system tables `cms_*`; plugin tables `cms_{plugin}_*` | `cms_article`, `cms_media_asset` |
| Content index and check | `uq_`, `ux_`, `ix_`, `gin_` or `ck_` with the table and column (4.3); over 63 characters, the first 54 and a hash | `ux_cms_article_i18n_slug` |
| Permission | dotted, lower case | `content.article.publish` |
| Route | the type name as declared, no pluralisation | `/api/delivery/v1/article` |
| Event | past tense, suffix `Event` | `EntryPublishedEvent` |
| Hook | `IBefore{Operation}Hook<T>` and `IAfter{Operation}Hook<T>` | `IBeforePublishHook<Article>` |
| Error code | `FCMS` plus four digits, grouped by area | `FCMS1001` |

**Name limits.** Names are held to PostgreSQL's identifier budget of 63 bytes, the smallest of the three providers, and to the 64-character columns that store type names and field paths. A type name or a field name has at most 64 characters, a content type's tables (`cms_{type}`, `cms_{type}_i18n`) and a column at most 63, and the path of a relation or media field (`seo.ogImage`) at most 64; schema validation refuses a longer one with `FCMS1218`, which names the type and the field. An index or check name over 63 characters is shortened instead (4.3, "Identifier budget"). This answers [#2873](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/2873) of the review of 7 Nov 2026 ([#2847](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/2847)).

### 3.4 Architecture tests

`FluentCMS.Architecture.Tests` asserts: `Api/*` never references `Storage/*` or `CmsDbContext`; plugins reference only `FluentCMS.Sdk` and `FluentCMS.Plugins.Abstractions` among FluentCMS packages, never Core; `FluentCMS.Sdk` references no implementation package; `FluentCMS.Plugins.Abstractions` references only the SDK, EF Core and the ASP.NET Core framework; nothing outside `Authorization/*` reads role permission tables; the MCP plugin references the two contract packages and the protocol package only, holds no database context and asks no permission (5.4); the AI assist plugin references the two contract packages and, of provider packages, `Microsoft.Extensions.AI` and `Microsoft.Extensions.AI.OpenAI` only, keeps no data but its usage log, and calls only the reads of the content and media services (5.5).

**GraphQL as built (B-75).** A plugin may also reference `FluentCMS.Plugins.GraphQL`, which holds `IGraphQLSchemaContributor`, since the SDK takes no HotChocolate dependency. Two rules hold the GraphQL plugin to 2.1: none of its types depends on EF Core or on a `DbContext`, and none but its request interceptor, which asks `IPermissionChecker` whether the caller may introspect, depends on `FluentCMS.Authorization`. Each rule is also run against a type that breaks it, so that it cannot pass by matching nothing.

**Multi-site as built (B-80).** The multi-site plugin references the two contract packages and nothing else of FluentCMS, and no type of it depends on a type of `FluentCMS.Core` or on `CmsDbContext`: it reaches sites through `ISiteAdministration` (4.6).

## 4. Core areas

Each area lists the specification rows it implements (category.row, for example 1.3 is content modeling row 3), the design, key types, storage, endpoints and open points.

### 4.1 SDK contract

Rows 8.3, 8.11, 8.14, 8.16 and every attribute and interface named below.

`FluentCMS.Sdk` is the only package plugins and host code compile against. Namespaces:

| Namespace | Holds |
| --- | --- |
| `FluentCMS.Schema` | content type and field attributes, `ContentItem` base class, `RichText`, `BlockList`, `Ref<T>`, `RefList<T>`, `IFieldType`, fluent `ISchemaBuilder`, rich text contracts `IRichTextRenderer`, `IRichTextNodeRenderer`, `IRichTextNodeContext`, `RichTextRenderOptions`, `RichTextFormats` |
| `FluentCMS.Content` | `IContentService`, `IContentService<T>`, `ContentDocument`, `ContentQuery`, filter AST, `PagedResult<T>`, `OperationContext`, `Origin`, `Principal`, the field types of the typed builders (`QueryField` and its kinds, 6.4) |
| `FluentCMS.Publishing` | `EntryStatus`, `VersionInfo`, `VersionChange`, `PublishResult<T>`, `PublishWarning`, `PublishPolicyAttribute`, `ScheduleRequest` |
| `FluentCMS.Hooks` | hook interfaces and `HookContext` |
| `FluentCMS.Events` | `IEventBus`, `IEventHandler<T>`, event records |
| `FluentCMS.Jobs` | `IJob<TPayload>`, `IJobScheduler`, `JobOptions` |
| `FluentCMS.Authorization` | `Permission`, `IPermissionProvider`, `IPermissionChecker`, `PermissionDeniedException`, `IRowRule<T>` |
| `FluentCMS.Plugins` | `IPlugin`, `FluentCmsPluginAttribute`, `IPluginServiceBuilder`, `IPluginAppBuilder`, `PluginException`, `IPluginState`, `IProblemDescriber` |
| `FluentCMS.Providers` | `IFileStorage`, `IEmailSender`, `ISearchIndex`, `IDistributedLock`, `IImageProcessor` |
| `FluentCMS.Media` | `MediaRef`, `MediaRefList`, `IMediaService`, `IMediaResolver` (contract only; implemented by the media plugin) |
| `FluentCMS.Sites`, `FluentCMS.Localization` | `SiteId`, `ISiteResolver`, `ILocaleResolver`, `FallbackChain` |
| `FluentCMS.Settings` | `SettingsAttribute`, `ISettings<T>`, `ResolvedSettings<T>` |
| `FluentCMS.Diagnostics` | `StartupReport`, `IInsecureDefaultSource`, `FluentCmsHealthCheckTags` |
| `FluentCMS.Agents` | `IMcpToolProvider`, `IMcpResourceProvider`, `IMcpPromptProvider`, their descriptors and `IMcpProviders` (contracts; implemented by plugins and served by the MCP plugin) |

The SDK has no dependency on EF Core or ASP.NET Core beyond `Microsoft.Extensions.*` abstractions, so plugin test projects stay light. The plugin contracts that need them, `IPluginModelContributor`, `PluginDbContext<TPlugin>` and the extension methods `AddModel`, `AddMigrations`, `MapGroup`, `MapManagement`, `MapDelivery` and `MapProtectedResource`, are in `FluentCMS.Plugins.Abstractions`, in the same `FluentCMS.Plugins` namespace ([ADR 0003](../decisions/0003-plugin-contribution-contracts.md)).

**Contract hazards settled before 1.0, as built.** The SDK is the semver-guaranteed contract (decision 14), so seven hazards found by the review of 7 Nov 2026 were settled while trimming is cheap ([#2868](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/2868)):

1. A failed entry of a bulk result carries `BulkItemResult.Problem`, a `BulkProblem` record (`Code`, `Message`, `Errors`, and `Current` for a version conflict), not the live exception, so no stack trace, inner exception or provider message rides in a result. The codes are those of the single call's problem details, and the Management API writes it as before.
2. `ScheduleRequest` no longer reads meaning from `DateTime.Kind`. It is made with `ScheduleRequest.At(action, DateTimeOffset)`, an instant, or `ScheduleRequest.AtLocalTime(action, DateTime, timeZone)`, a wall-clock time whose kind is ignored with an IANA zone; it has no public constructor and no settable property, so it cannot hold both. A Windows zone id is refused, since each platform finds the other's ids, so a zone means the same everywhere. The HTTP body is unchanged (4.10).
3. After hooks keep `HookContext<T>`, and its docs and those of every after hook say that `Entry` is a copy whose changes are discarded. A type that cannot be written is declined: `T` is a content class or `ContentDocument`, both writable, so a read-only view would need a second shape of every content class, and a separate after-hook context would still hand out the same `T` and change the signature of every after hook.
4. `IContentService<T>` has `Type` and `Documents`, the document service it maps to, and its members that map no entry (`DeleteAsync`, `PurgeAsync`, `ScheduleAsync`, `CancelScheduleAsync`, `GetVersionsAsync`, `DiffAsync`, `CountAsync`, `BulkAsync`) are default interface members over `Documents`, so a decorator or a fake implements them once, on `IContentService`, and a member added later that maps no entry comes the same way, without breaking implementers. Default members were chosen over a shared base interface, which cannot hold both forms because every document member takes the type name and every typed one returns `T`. The members that map an entry stay abstract, because the mapping (every system field, the fields the principal may read) is the engine's.
5. No SDK interface method takes an optional parameter other than a trailing `CancellationToken` or a single options record that defaults to `null`, whose defaults can never change. The locale and expected version of the version operations moved to `LocaleOptions` and `LocaleWriteOptions`, and the filter and status of a count to `CountOptions`. A flag of `IFieldBuilder` and `ITypeBuilder.Localized` is a parameterless default member that passes `true` and the overload with the flag; `ITypeBuilder.Field`, `IFieldBuilder.Relation` and `IPluginServiceBuilder.AddJob` have a short default member and the full overload; `IFieldBuilder.Display` takes a `FieldDisplayDescription`; the dead-letter lists take `offset` and `limit`, and `IJobScheduler.AddRecurringAsync` its `queue`, as required parameters. Overloads that each end in an optional token are refused by the public API analyzer (RS0026), which is why the content service takes records rather than overloads. Members of classes and records, such as their constructors, `SystemOperation.Begin` and `SchemaValue.Normalize`, keep their defaults: the item is about interface members, which every implementer must match.
6. `And.GetHashCode` and `Or.GetHashCode` combine the hash codes of their conditions in order, as their equality compares them.
7. `IRichTextNodeRenderer.Render` says the renderer escapes for the format everything it writes from `IRichTextNodeContext.Attributes`, which holds what the author typed.

**The public surface read before the first preview, as built (B-106, [#3051](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3051)).** `1.0.0-preview.1` is the first build that anyone outside compiles against, and M4 to M6 had added the agent contracts, `ITransferContributor`, `Origin.Import`, the field types of the typed builders and the member principal since the hazards above were settled. So both packages, `FluentCMS.Sdk` and `FluentCMS.Plugins.Abstractions`, were read namespace by namespace against them, with `PublicAPI.Unshipped.txt` as the index. Until 1.0.0 every entry stays in that file and no package validation baseline is set (10.4, H1), so a preview may still break the contract; `docs/releasing.md` says how the files are kept. What was found, and what was done:

1. **Optional parameters (item 5).** Six interface members broke the rule. Five now take the parameter as a required one, as the dead-letter lists do, or have a short default member: `IActivityQuery.QueryAsync(filter, page)`, which took two records; `IContentSurface.ReadableFieldsAsync(type, locale)` and `IMediaResolver.ResolveAsync(ids, locale)`, where a `null` locale is the operation's; `IMediaService.DeleteAsync(id, force)`; and `ITransferImport.Report`, whose `Report(section, id, outcome)` is a default member over `Report(section, id, outcome, code, message)`. `ITypeBuilder.PublishPolicy` took three optional parameters whose defaults of 50 versions and 30 days were compiled into every caller. It now takes one `PublishPolicyOptions`, a sealed record whose `init` properties take their defaults from the SDK when it is made, and `PublishPolicyAttribute.DefaultMaxVersions` and `DefaultTrashRetentionDays` are static properties rather than constants, so a caller that keeps the defaults compiles no number. `MaxTrashRetentionDays` stays a constant, a limit that core checks in patterns. Every other optional parameter of an interface member is a trailing token or one options record.
2. **Field type names** ([#3064](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3064)). `IFieldType.Name` is an ASCII letter followed by ASCII letters, digits, `.`, `-` or `_`, the names `generate` writes into code. `AddFieldType` refuses any other with `FCMS1528` when the host starts (4.13), and the field type registry refuses one too, with a duplicate, for a type registered as an `IFieldType` service without `AddFieldType`. A plugin type such as `acme:geo` would otherwise make `generate` refuse the snapshot of the whole instance.
3. **Host names** ([#3009](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3009)). `ISiteHostResolver` does not promise a bounded set of ids. Instead `SiteCache` keeps that the site of an id is gone for about 1,000 ids at the most (4.6), so the cache stays bounded whatever a resolver gives. The bound is soft: requests that store such an id at the same moment can pass it by their number.
4. **Public by accident, or showing an implementation.** None. `IJobRunner` and `ISeedRunner` are public so that a host can replace the runner and run the seeders when it chooses. `IContentSurface`, `IOperationContextAccessor` and `SystemOperation` are public because plugins use them. The SDK shows no type of core, and the Abstractions package shows EF Core and ASP.NET Core only where a plugin maps a model or endpoints.
5. **Live exceptions in results (item 1).** None. A bulk failure is a `BulkProblem`, and the failures of jobs, deliveries and the start (`JobFailedEvent.Error`, `DeadLetteredJob.LastError`, `FailedEventDelivery.LastError`, `StartupReport.Failure`) are text.
6. **Members that map no entry (item 4).** `IContentService<T>` keeps its eight default members, and nothing was added to it. `ISettings<T>.ResolveAsync` ([#3005](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3005)) has no default on purpose, since no default could say which secrets a site inherits, so an implementer of `ISettings<T>` adds it.
7. **Collections a caller can change, and open classes.** The schema model hands out read-only wrappers of its lists. What stays mutable is mutable on purpose: `ContentDocument`, the entry a caller writes; `FluentCmsPluginAttribute.DependsOn`, an array because an attribute argument must be one; and the `JsonObject` of `IContentSurface.ToJson` and the `JsonNode` of a `VersionChange`, which are new for each call. The fragment of `IFieldType.OpenApiSchema` goes the other way: core and the MCP plugin change it as they build a document, so they take a copy at each use, and a field type may hand out the same node every time. The classes open for inheritance are open on purpose: `CmsEvent`, since a plugin raises events of its own, with `EntryEvent` and `MediaAssetEvent`, the positional bases of the engine's events; `ContentItem`, the base of content classes; `FieldAttribute`, the base of a plugin's field attribute; and `QueryField` and `BlocksField`, which the builders a generator emits derive from. `ContentFilter` is closed by a `private protected` constructor, and every other class and record is sealed or static.
8. **Growth.** A record grows by an `init` property with a default. It never grows by a positional parameter, which changes its constructor and `Deconstruct`, nor by a `required` member, which breaks every object initializer. An enum grows by a value in a minor version, so a consumer that switches on one keeps a default branch. No enum is `[Flags]`. An enum that an implementation receives is checked before it grows: a new `ImageFit` comes with a default member of `IImageProcessor` that says whether the processor takes it, as `CanEncode` does for a type, so an old processor is never handed a fit it does not know.
9. **Names that differ for the same thing.** Each is kept for a reason:
   - A site is a `SiteId` named `Site` where a caller names the site to act in (`OperationContext`, `ContentQuery`, `JobOptions`, the parameters of the services), and a `Guid` named `SiteId` (`Id` in `SiteInfo`) in the records that report data and are written as JSON (the events and their `EventSource`, `ActivityItem`, `MediaAsset`, `MediaFolder`, `SiteInfo`), since `SiteId` is written as an object, not a bare id.
   - For the same reason `EventSource` writes the operation as the payload names it: `OccurredAt` is `OperationContext.Timestamp`, and `ActorId`, `ActorName` and `ActorKind` are `OperationContext.AuditPrincipal`, flat, where a `Principal` holds claims that the outbox and webhooks do not carry.
   - `IMediaService.GetAsync` throws a `PluginException` with `FCMS1585` for an asset it does not find, where `IContentService.GetAsync` gives `null`. Every member of the media service that names an asset or a folder, the reads included, refuses one it does not find with a code of the plugin, which its endpoints answer as a 404 and the MCP tools as that code, so a `null` from `GetAsync` alone would be the exception. The content service gives `null` from its reads of one entry (`GetAsync`, `GetVersionAsync`) and throws from its writes, so each service keeps to one form; aligning them would change every caller of one of them for no gain in safety.
   - A description record keeps the names of the schema file: `SchemaFieldDescription.Type` for `SchemaField.FieldType`, and `SchemaDescription.Types` for `SchemaModel.ContentTypes`.
   - `PageRequest` is the page of a query, so its members are `Number` and `Size` (`query.Page.Number`), where a result and a flat query record (`PagedResult`, `MediaQuery`) say `Page` and `PageSize`. The dead-letter lists have no pages and take an `offset` and a `limit`.
   - An event keeps the text its payload carries (`Action` of the schedule events, `Status` of `McpClientRegisteredEvent`), and the MCP client events name the row `Id` because `ClientId` is the OAuth client id, as in `EventSource`.
   - `IFieldBuilder.IsReadOnly` is not `ReadOnly`, a Visual Basic keyword that an interface member may not take (CA1716).
10. **Outside the two packages, and what is missing.** Core's `IDataProvider` is not part of the promise: `ContainsIgnoringCase` ([#3012](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3012)) has no default, so a data provider outside the repository adds it, built against the Core of its own version (3.2). The SDK has no list of a site's locales and no constant for `FCMS2211`, which the search plugin works around ([#3049](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3049)). Both would be additions, which a minor version makes without a break.
11. **The notes of the preview** (B-109) name what a plugin built on the code of M6 meets: the signatures of item 1, `Principal.IsInGroup`, which throws outside a row rule where it answered `false` (10.4, H7, [#3004](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3004)), and `ISettings<T>.ResolveAsync`.

### 4.2 Schema

Rows 1.1 to 1.13, 1.19 (snapshot), 1.14 to 1.18 as Should.

**Definition.** Attributes on plain classes are the primary form; the fluent builder is the second form and is what the attribute reader produces internally, so both yield the same `SchemaModel`. The builder also lets a host define types without a class, which the reference host uses to load a schema file (Q2).

```csharp
[Collection("article", Localized = true)]
public sealed class Article : ContentItem
{
    [Text(MaxLength = 200), Required, Localizable, Searchable]
    public string Title { get; set; } = "";

    [Slug(Source = nameof(Title)), Localizable, Unique]
    public string Slug { get; set; } = "";

    [RichText, Localizable]
    public RichText Body { get; set; } = RichText.Empty;

    [Relation(Reverse = "articles")]    // introspection only (4.4)
    public Ref<Author> Author { get; set; }

    [Relation(Ordered = true)]
    public RefList<Tag> Tags { get; set; } = [];

    [Media]
    public MediaRef? Hero { get; set; }

    [Blocks(typeof(HeroBlock), typeof(QuoteBlock), typeof(GalleryBlock))]
    public BlockList Sections { get; set; } = [];

    [Component]
    public Seo Seo { get; set; } = new();

    [Select, Indexed]
    public ArticleCategory Category { get; set; }

    [DateTime, Indexed]
    public DateTimeOffset? PublishedOn { get; set; }

    [VisibleWhen(nameof(IsVideo)), RequiredWhen(nameof(IsVideo))]
    [Url]
    public string? VideoUrl { get; set; }

    public static Condition IsVideo => Condition.Field(nameof(Category)).Equals(ArticleCategory.Video);
}

[Singleton("site-settings")]
public sealed class SiteSettings : ContentItem { /* ... */ }

[Component]
public sealed class Seo { [Text(MaxLength = 70)] public string? MetaTitle { get; set; } /* ... */ }

[Block("hero")]
public sealed class HeroBlock { [Text] public string Heading { get; set; } = ""; [Media] public MediaRef? Image { get; set; } }
```

`ContentItem` carries the system fields (`Id`, `Status`, `Locale`, `Version`, `CreatedAt`, `CreatedBy`, `UpdatedAt`, `UpdatedBy`, `PublishedAt`) with setters the engine uses. Their names are reserved, and so are `fallbacks`, which a response adds, `scheduledPublishAt` and `scheduledUnpublishAt`, which a Management read adds (4.4), and `deletedAt` and `deletedBy`, which a read of the trash adds (4.5) (`ContentItem.ReservedFieldNames`, compared without regard to case); a field with a reserved name fails schema validation. A write ignores `fallbacks` and the two schedule fields. The system tables of Appendix C are reserved too: a collection or singleton may not take a name that maps to one of them (`job`, `user`, `site`, `member`, `site-host` and the rest, the table name without `cms_` and with hyphens for underscores), and fails with `FCMS1206`. Components and blocks have no table and may use these names.

**Field types.** Each field type is an `IFieldType` descriptor: CLR type(s), storage kind (`Column`, `Json`, `Reference`), whether it supports `Multiple` (`SupportsMultiple`, `false` unless the type says otherwise), default validation, OpenAPI schema fragment, supported filter operators, whether it can be sorted or indexed. Core types that support `Multiple` are select, relation and component; the media plugin's `media` does too. Core registers text, longText, richText, integer, decimal, boolean, date, dateTime, email, url, slug, select (single and multi), json, relation, component and blocks. The media plugin registers `media`, which is the proof of the custom field type extension point (8.5): core without the media plugin reports `FCMS1203: field type 'media' used by article.hero is not registered`.

**Media field as built (B-67).** The media plugin adds `media` with `AddFieldType`: `MediaRef` and `MediaRefList` (`SupportsMultiple`), stored as `Reference`, so core keeps its values in `cms_reference` with `target_type = media` (5.1), filtered by asset id with `eq` and `in`, neither sortable nor indexable, with one setting, `accept` (5.1, "Accepted types as built"). A single field coerces to the asset id and a list to the ids in order, from an id, a `MediaRef`, or the reference or populated asset a read gave. Its OpenAPI fragment is the id a write sends, the reference `{ "id", "type": "media" }` or the populated asset (4.4), and an array of those for a list. A schema built by a host, such as the reference host's schema file (4.16), is built with the container's `IFieldTypeRegistry`, as the test host's is, so it knows the field types of plugins. Without the plugin, `[Media]` still stops the host with `FCMS1203`.

**Slugs as built.** A slug is made of lower case letters and decimal digits of any script, separated by single hyphens. Each character is a lower case (`Ll`), modifier (`Lm`) or other (`Lo`) letter or a decimal digit (`Nd`), and the text is in normalization form C. Fillers are not allowed: the Hangul fillers (U+115F, U+1160, U+3164, U+FFA0), which show nothing, and the Arabic tatweel (U+0640), which only stretches a stroke. The OpenAPI fragment states this as the pattern `^[\p{Ll}\p{Lm}\p{Lo}\p{Nd}]+(?:-[\p{Ll}\p{Lm}\p{Lo}\p{Nd}]+)*$`. A sent slug that breaks the rule is `InvalidValue`. When a write leaves a slug empty and the principal may read its `Source` field, the slug is made from that field in these steps:

1. Decompose the text (NFD).
2. Drop the combining marks of every script, and the fillers.
3. Transliterate the Latin letters that do not decompose: `ß` to `ss`, `æ` to `ae`, `œ` to `oe`, `ø` to `o`, `đ` and `ð` to `d`, `ł` to `l`, `ı` to `i`, `þ` to `th`.
4. Lower every other letter or digit (invariant culture) and keep it. A letter with no lower case form counts as a symbol.
5. Turn each run of anything else into one hyphen.
6. Compose the result again (NFC) and cut it to the field's maximum length, never inside a character or after a hyphen.

`Crème Brûlée` gives `creme-brulee`, `Привет, мир` gives `привет-мир` and `日本語` gives `日本語`. The slug is stored as it is, and a URL that carries it escapes it. A source with text but no letter or digit, such as `!!!`, gives no slug. For a `Required` slug, the save is refused with `EmptySlug` (`FCMS2112`, 422) on the slug field, rather than leaving the slug empty for `Required` to refuse at publish. An optional slug may stay empty, and a blank source leaves any slug empty.

**Components.** `[Component]` classes are reusable field groups. A single component flattens to prefixed columns (`seo_metaTitle`) so its fields are filterable and indexable; a repeatable component (`List<Address>`) is stored as JSON (Q6b).

**Blocks.** `[Block("name")]` classes form the union of a `BlockList` field. Blocks may contain components and nested block lists up to depth 3 (decision 20), validated at startup. Every block schema is emitted in OpenAPI as a `oneOf` with a `type` discriminator. A stored block also has an `id` beside `type`, unique within its top-level field at every depth ([ADR 0002](../decisions/0002-stable-block-ids.md)). The service gives a block that has none 12 random URL-safe characters before the entry is written, and returns the id on reads. A write may send any id of 1 to 64 of them (`A-Z`, `a-z`, `0-9`, `-`, `_`), so imported ids survive, and refuses one sent twice in a top-level field (`FCMS1407`). `type` and `id` are reserved block field names (`FCMS1206`). A block class keeps its id through `IContentService<T>` with a `string? Id` property and no field attribute. A block stored before ids existed gets one on the entry's next write. Repeated components have no ids.

**Rich text editor contract.** A `RichText` value is a ProseMirror JSON document whose node and mark names follow Tiptap, which is what the admin editor emits. Stored node types are `doc`, `paragraph`, `heading` (`level` 1 to 6), `bulletList`, `orderedList` (optional `start`), `listItem`, `blockquote`, `codeBlock` (optional `language`), `horizontalRule`, `hardBreak`, `text`, `media` and `inlineBlock`. Stored mark types are `bold`, `italic`, `underline`, `strike`, `code`, `link` (`href`, optional `title` and `target`) and `entryLink`. Validation reports any other name, including the stock ProseMirror basic schema names `image`, `bullet_list`, `ordered_list`, `code_block`, `hard_break`, `horizontal_rule` and `strong`, with the JSON path; nothing is dropped silently. FluentCMS adds three types to Tiptap:

- `media` node: `mediaId` (GUID), optional `alt` and `title`. It replaces `image`.
- `inlineBlock` node: `blockType` (the name of a registered block), `data` (an object) and `id` (as on a block in a list), the typed embed rendered by a plugin `IRichTextNodeRenderer`.
- `entryLink` mark: `entryId` (GUID) and `entryType` (content type name), a link to an entry that is resolved when rendered.

The `data` of an inline block has the rules of the same block in a block list. A write coerces and validates it against the block's fields, with errors at its path in the document (`body.content[0].content[1].attrs.data.label`), and refuses a hidden or read-only field or a key that is not a field from anyone but the system principal, except a read-only value sent back as the read showed it (**Read-only values sent back**, 4.5). A read leaves its hidden fields out, in JSON, HTML and Markdown, in versions and diffs, and in populated entries. A client that sends the document back keeps what it could not see: inline blocks pair up as the blocks of a list do (4.5), by `attrs.id` anywhere in the top-level field and otherwise over the whole document. An inline block of a type the schema does not know is left as written.

**Link URLs as built ([#3269](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3269)).** A URL in rich text is allowed when it is a string with a scheme of `FluentCms:RichText:LinkSchemes`, in any case, or with no scheme at all (`/about`, `about`, `#top`, `?page=2`, `//cdn.example.com`, `javascript%3A`, which a browser reads as a path), or when it is null. The list is `http`, `https`, `mailto` and `tel` unless the host sets it, and it replaces that default; `javascript`, `data`, `vbscript`, `blob`, `file` and `about` in it stop the host (`FCMS1101`). Leading and trailing whitespace is ignored; a control character anywhere (`java\tscript:`) and a character reference before the scheme ends (`&#106;avascript:`, `javascript&colon;`) make the URL unsafe, and so does any other character in the scheme, such as a zero-width space or a bidi mark, since no scheme of the list has one. A value that is not a string, such as `["javascript:…"]`, a number or an object, is unsafe, since a front end that binds it reads it as one. The rule covers every key that holds a URL by its name, `href`, `src`, `url`, `cite`, `action`, `formaction`, `poster`, `background` and `xlink:href` in any case, at any depth in the `attrs` of every node and mark (a `link`'s `href`, and the same names on `entryLink`, `media` or any node, and inside objects and arrays under any attribute, since the model keeps attributes it does not name), the `data` of an inline block of a known type aside, which has its block's rules; the data of an inline block of a type the schema does not know is covered. Attributes outside the model's names are kept, not refused, since the editor writes some (Tiptap's `rel` and `class` on a link). A write refuses an unsafe URL with `UnsafeUrl` (`FCMS2114`, 422) at its path in the document (`body.content[0].content[0].marks[0].attrs.href`), wherever the document is: a top-level field, a component, a repeated component, a block, or the data of an inline block. Every write of an entry, a hook's change included, reads its rich text through `EntryCanonicalizer`, so this holds for create, update, patch, bulk, import, publish, restore, discard, seeding and the MCP tools. The HTML and Markdown renderers drop a link whose `href` the rule refuses and keep its text, and so do the URLs `entryLink` and `media` render to.

A URL stored before the rule, or with a scheme taken off the list since, stays in the row and is dropped where entries are read from the database: in `EntryStore`, in the reader of the query engine and in version snapshots. A `link` mark goes and its text stays; any other key goes on its own. Every read of an entry goes through one of them, so none returns it: JSON, HTML and Markdown, every locale and a locale's fallback, populated entries, lists, versions and diffs, transfer exports, and the MCP and GraphQL reads. A write that only carries it on, a patch of another field, a restore or a bulk update, stores the value without it instead of failing, and logs it at Warning with event id 9405, the entry, the locale, the field and the URL's scheme when it is well formed (a letter, then up to 31 letters, digits, `+`, `.` or `-`), else `(invalid)`, never the URL. A publish, scheduled or not, succeeds and copies the draft row as it is, so a published row keeps such a URL until the next save of the draft. Outside entry reads the value is not cleaned: a raw backup of the database keeps it, a response that an output cache store already holds is served until its lifetime ends, and event payloads and search documents written before the upgrade keep what they had. No migration rewrites stored rows; a cheap scan of the stored text decides whether a value is parsed for this, so ordinary content pays nothing.

**Validation.** Declarative: `Required`, `Length`, `Range`, `RegularExpression`, `Unique`, enum membership. Custom: `IFieldValidator<TField>` and `IEntryValidator<T>` receive a `ValidationContext` (entry, sibling values, principal, operation, locale). Draft saves skip `Required` and `RequiredWhen`; publish runs everything.

**Conditions.** `VisibleWhen` and `RequiredWhen` reference a static `Condition` expressed with a small fluent API (`Field(...).Equals/In/IsEmpty/And/Or/Not`). Being data rather than arbitrary C#, a condition is serialised into the introspection document so the admin and agents can evaluate it, and the server enforces `RequiredWhen` at publish. Arbitrary C# predicates stay available through `IEntryValidator<T>` for server-only rules.

**Conditions as built.** A field the entry does not have reads as null. A field that holds a list, such as a multiple select, equals a value it contains, and an empty list equals null, for `Equal` and `In` alike, so `In` holds when the list shares a value with its operands. `ConditionDescription` and `ConditionOperator`, the records the introspection document is made of, state this for the admin and agents that evaluate conditions themselves, and there is no separate `AnyOf` operator. A field that `VisibleWhen` hides is kept and skipped: a save stores its value as sent and a read returns it, but neither the declarative rules nor a custom validator check it while it is hidden, so it is there again when the condition shows it. Clearing it on save was declined, since a condition that flips back would lose what the editor wrote. This answers item 6 of [#2869](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/2869).

**Metadata.** `Display(Label, Help, Placeholder, Group, Tab, Width)`, `DefaultValue`, `ReadOnly`, `Hidden`, `Localizable`, `Indexed`, `Searchable`. All of it appears in introspection; none of it affects storage except `Indexed`, `Unique` and `Localizable`.

**Reverse relations and `Searchable` as built.** Two pieces of metadata have no reader in core. A reverse side, declared with `Relation(Reverse = ...)`, is introspection only in v1: `reverseRelations` lists it on the type pointed to, and a query path that names it is refused (4.4, "Reverse relations as built"). `Searchable` is reserved for the search plugin: core stores it and shows it in introspection, and the `ISearchIndex` that fills the provider slot decides how it indexes them, and matches only the fields marked `Searchable` that are not `Hidden`, whatever rows it holds (4.4, "Search as built"; 5.7). Neither changes storage, and reverse traversal and core storage hooks for search can come later without breaking a client. This answers [#2870](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/2870) and [#2871](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/2871).

**Validation at startup.** Reserved names, nesting depth, relation targets exist, slug sources exist, `Localizable` only on localized types, field types registered, `Multiple` only on field types that support it, duplicate type names, duplicate reverse relation names, name lengths (4.3, "Identifier budget"), former names that another field of the type has or lists too (`FCMS1219`, below), a title field that is not a single visible text field of its type (`FCMS1220`, "Display name and title field as built"). Errors carry `FCMS12xx` codes and stop the host.

**Introspection.** `GET /api/management/v1/schema` and `GET /api/management/v1/schema/types/{type}` return the model filtered by the caller's permissions (4.17 in the identity category): types, fields, field types, validation, conditions, metadata, relations, block unions, localized flags. The same DTOs feed the MCP schema resources and the CLI's schema diff.

**Snapshot and evolution.** `SchemaModel` serialises to a deterministic JSON snapshot; its SHA-256 is the schema hash. The hash is stored in `cms_schema_history` whenever content migrations are applied, so startup can report drift. Field renames are declared (`[FormerNames("subtitle")]`) so the migration generator emits a rename instead of drop and add.

**Snapshot as built.** The snapshot is the canonical form `SchemaJsonWriter` writes, which `schema.json` reads back: UTF-8 JSON with LF newlines, types ordered by name, fields in declaration order, settings by key, defaults left out, and neither the CLR class nor any runtime value in it. Two builds of one schema give the same bytes. The schema hash is the SHA-256 of those bytes as lowercase hex, and it also keys the EF model cache. `cms_schema_history (id, hash, snapshot, applied_at, fluentcms_version)` holds one row per schema the content tables were brought up to. `id` is a `bigint` identity, and the newest row is the one with the highest `id`, because the clocks of nodes disagree; `applied_at` is for people. `hash` is not unique because a rollback adds a row with an older hash. `snapshot` is plain text (not `jsonb`, which would reorder keys and break the hash) and `fluentcms_version` is the informational version without build metadata. The runner adds a row, unless the newest row has the same hash, for a host with content migrations after the content stream is applied and checked at startup and after `MigrateAsync` (`fluentcms migrate`) if the database has every table and column of the schema, and after a `SchemaSync = Auto` sync that left nothing unapplied, at startup or through `SyncContentSchemaAsync`. For a host without content migrations only schema apply and schema sync add one, and sync only when the history counts nothing as lost (4.3). The read of the newest row and the insert are one serializable transaction, and a node that loses a deadlock or a serialization failure reads again, so nodes that start together add one row between them. A change the database does not store, such as `Required`, is recorded when startup or `MigrateAsync` runs for a host with content migrations, and by schema apply or schema sync for one without. A start that records no row (one that does not migrate, a start of a host without content migrations, or a sync that left something undone) compares the code with the newest row and logs one warning that names the types and fields that differ, never their values: `type 'x' added`, `field 'article.title' changed (required)`, `field 'article.subtitle' renamed to 'summary'`; a component or a block is named as such (`block 'hero' removed`, `field 'component seo.title' changed (maxLength)`), because it may share a name with another kind. Collections and singletons share one namespace, components and blocks have one each. A new field that lists a field that has gone in its `formerNames` is a rename; without that it is a drop and an add. A missing history logs information; a stored snapshot of any other shape than the writer's logs the drift with "details are unavailable". Neither stops the host, and neither does a history that cannot be written or read (a deadlock that outlasts four attempts, a missing table): that logs a warning with the exception type and the host starts, since only a failure of the migrations themselves may stop it. The check reads one row, so it adds no noticeable time to boot. During a rolling deploy an older node boots against columns a newer node added and records its own hash, so alternating boots add rows and the drift warning fires on the older node; that is expected, and the history then shows the order the schemas were applied in.

**Destructive changes and renames as built (B-97, [#3020](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3020)).** The startup drift check and the schema tools of 4.14 share one comparison, `SchemaSnapshotDiff`, which also gives the report the tools print (`SchemaDiffReport`). A change is destructive when a database that follows it loses stored values: a type or a field removed, a field whose `type`, `multiple` or `localizable` changed, since each moves or retypes its column, and a type whose `kind`, `localized` or `scope` changed, since each drops or rekeys its tables. A field removed beside a field added in the same type with the same `type` and `multiple` is a possible rename, reported with the attribute that would make it one (`possible rename: field 'article.subtitle' to 'summary'; add [FormerNames("subtitle")] to 'summary' to make it one`); each removed field is offered to one added field, in declaration order, and nothing is rewritten. With `[FormerNames]` the change is `renamed` and not destructive, and the content migration generator carries it (below). The content model carries the snapshot of its schema as the model annotation `FluentCMS:Schema`, so the model snapshot and the target model of every content migration record the schema they were generated from; an annotation makes no migration operation.

**Renames in content migrations as built ([#3059](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3059)).** `migrations add` scaffolds with `ContentModelDiffer`, which EF's scaffolder takes in place of EF's differ for that call only. It reads the schemas on the two models, the snapshot's and the host's, and gives `SchemaSnapshotDiff`'s renames to `ContentRenames`, which follows each leaf of a content type through the fields and the single components above it to the path and the column it had: a rename of the type's field, of a component's field or of both turns `meta_meta_title` into `seo_page_title`. A column of a content table is then the same column by its name or by that former column, in the shared table or the `_i18n` table, and EF's own `Diff` of a column pair gives the `RenameColumn`, and an `AlterColumn` when the store type or nullability changed too. EF's guess of a rename by store type alone is not taken, so a field removed beside one added without the attribute is a drop and an add, as the review says, and a removed field's values never reach an unrelated new one. The indexes follow the column: one that is otherwise the same is renamed (`ix_cms_article_summary`), one whose filter names the column, a `[Unique]` index on a nullable column, is dropped and made again, and a JSON check constraint is dropped and added under the new name; EF orders the drops before the rename and the creates after it, on every provider, and on SQLite a renamed index is made again since SQLite cannot rename one. `cms_reference` keeps references under the field's path (`seo.ogImage`), those of a relation or media field and those that a rich text, blocks or repeated component field holds, so the migration ends with an `UpdateData` per moved path, `field` from the former path to the new one where `source_type` is the type, with the store types of the provider's `cms_reference`, and its `Down` moves them back. A field of a block, or of a component that is repeated or sits in a block (a component is kept in JSON when any field that holds it is), keeps its values under its name inside the JSON of another field, which no migration rewrites; `migrations add` names such a rename as a loss and refuses it without `--allow-destructive` (6.1). The versions in `cms_version` keep a renamed field under its former name, where a read finds it (below). `ContentModelDiffer` derives from EF's internal `MigrationsModelDiffer`, since EF has no public seam for how columns are paired, and runs only in the tool, with the tool's own EF Core.

**Former names in versions and validation as built ([#3085](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3085)).** A version keeps an entry's fields under the names they had when it was written, and no migration rewrites it. Reading a snapshot (`VersionSnapshot`) takes each field's value under its name, and else under each of its former names in order, at the top level and in a single component, through the components above it, as a migration renames their columns; a key no field takes stays as it was. The version read, the diff and a restore read it so, so a version from before a rename shows and restores the renamed field, a required, localizable or component field included. A field of a block or of a component kept in JSON keeps its former name in the snapshot as in the stored entry, which is the loss `migrations add` names. Schema validation refuses a former name that is the name of another field of the same type, component or block, compared without regard to case, with `FCMS1219`, which names both fields (`former name 'subtitle' of article.summary is the name of field article.subtitle; a former name must not be the name of another field of collection 'article'`): `SchemaSnapshotDiff` does not take such a name for a rename, so the migration would leave the old values under the field that has the name and none under the renamed one, while a version read would move them to the renamed field. Two fields that list each other's names, a swap, are one error naming both. Two fields that list one former name are refused with `FCMS1219` too, naming both (`former name 'subtitle' of article.teaser is also a former name of article.summary; a former name must belong to one field of collection 'article'`), since a version would give the old values to the first of them declared, and a reorder of the two would move them. A field allow-list of a grant keeps the former name, so a renamed field drops out of it until the role names the field again; that fails closed and is left so.

**Display name and title field as built ([#3150](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3150)).** A collection or a singleton may have a `displayName`, its name for people (`Blog post`), and a `titleField`, the name of the field whose value labels an entry (admin specification 4.1, AQ10). Both are optional metadata that changes no storage, and a type without them is as before: `null` in `SchemaType` and `SchemaTypeDescription`, left out of the snapshot and of the JSON of `GET /schema`, which writes no null. They are set by `DisplayName` and `TitleField` on `[Collection]` and `[Singleton]`, where `TitleField` takes the property name (`nameof(Headline)`) as `Slug.Source` does, by `ITypeBuilder.DisplayName` and `ITypeBuilder.TitleField`, which throw `ArgumentException` on a component or a block, and by `displayName` and `titleField` in `schema.json`, which refuses them on a component or a block and as anything but a string (`FCMS1213`). Validation refuses a title field that is not a single `text` field of the type itself, or that is `Hidden`, since a read leaves a hidden field out and no client could show the label, with `FCMS1220`, which names the field and the type (`title field 'title' of collection 'article' is not a field of it; a title field must be a single visible text field of the type`), and a blank display name with `FCMS1215`. Only `text` is taken, the field type the admin's derivation reads first, since a rule can be widened later without breaking a schema and not narrowed. `GET /schema`, `GET /schema/types/{type}`, the OpenAPI document and the MCP schema resources show both, and the snapshot writes them after `trashRetentionDays`, so a change of either changes the schema hash and is a `type changed (displayName, titleField)` in `schema diff`, which loses nothing. Introspection keeps the display name and leaves out a title field the caller may not read, as it does a slug source. The emitters of `generate` carry both as metadata, not as fields (6.1, "Generate as built"), and the MCP tool descriptions name them (5.4, "Types"). A client that labels types or entries uses them when present and falls back to the derivation of admin specification 4.1.

Related decisions: Q2 (schema file for the reference host), Q6b (component storage).

### 4.3 Storage and migrations

Rows 1.12, 8.13, 10.1, 10.4, 10.10; decisions 8, 19, 39, 43, 48, 54, 55, 60.

**Model building.** The EF Core model is generated from `SchemaModel` using shared-type (property bag) entity types, so there is no CLR entity class per content type. This supports attribute-defined and builder-defined types alike, lets one typed `Article` project out of two tables (shared row plus locale row), and keeps the user's class a plain projection. Source-generated row classes were the alternative (Q5); the M1 storage spike (issue B-10) validated the choice on SQLite only (Q19), and [#2711](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/2711) validated it on PostgreSQL 15 and SQL Server 2019 and 2025.

**Tables per content type.** For `article` (localized, prefix `cms_`):

| Table | Key | Holds |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `cms_document` | `(document_id, locale)` unique; `id` v7 PK | One row per document and locale, any type: `type`, `site_id`, `status`, `draft_version`, `published_version`, `published_at`, `first_published_at`, `deleted_at`, `deleted_by`, `created_*`, `updated_*`, `schedule_publish_at`, `schedule_unpublish_at`. `locale` is `''` for a non-localized document, never null, because a null `locale` defeats the unique key. The type-independent state table used by lists across types, trash, scheduling and the MCP `query` tool. |
| `cms_article` | `(document_id, state, site_id)` unique; `id` v7 PK | Non-localized fields, one row per document and state (0 draft, 1 published). `site_id` denormalised for partial unique indexes and future partitioning. |
| `cms_article_i18n` | `(document_id, locale, state, site_id)` unique; `id` v7 PK | Localized fields and `site_id`, one row per document, locale and state. `site_id` is repeated here because an index cannot span tables and a localized `[Unique]` field needs it beside the field. Absent for non-localized types, whose fields all live in `cms_article`. |
| `cms_reference` | `(source_type, source_document_id, state, locale, field, position)` unique; `id` v7 PK; index on `(target_type, target_id)` | Every relation and media reference in one polymorphic table. `locale` is `''` for non-localized values, never null, because a null `locale` defeats the unique key. Gives "what references X" for media usage tracking, reference integrity at publish and delete warnings in one query. Polymorphic relations (1.14, Should) need no new table. |
| `cms_version` | `(document_id, locale, number)` unique; `id` v7 PK | Full JSON snapshot per save plus `status_after`, `author_id`, `origin`, `client_id`, `message`, `created_at`. |

**Column mapping.** text `varchar(n)` (unbounded `TEXT` on SQLite, which does not enforce `n`, so the validation pipeline is the only length limit); longText `text`; richText, json, blocks, multi-select and repeatable components `jsonb` (PostgreSQL), `nvarchar(max)` with `CHECK (ISJSON(col) = 1)` (SQL Server, every version: the native `json` type of 2025 is not used in 1.0), `text` with JSON1 and `CHECK (json_valid(col))` (SQLite); integer `bigint` (`long`, always, because widening a populated column later is a migration); decimal `numeric(p,s)` (text on SQLite, which EF compares and sorts numerically without a conversion); boolean; date `date`; dateTime `timestamptz` (PostgreSQL), `datetimeoffset` written in UTC (SQL Server), UTC ticks in an `integer` column (SQLite, through a provider convention that also covers system columns such as `schedule_publish_at`, so that scheduling and `sort=-publishedOn` can compare and order; SQLite supports only equality on `DateTimeOffset`, and the conversion drops the offset); email, url, slug `varchar`; single select the enum name as `varchar`; relations and media as `cms_reference` rows only, no denormalised id column.

**Nullability.** The storage model, the content service and the validation pipeline share these rules. A draft may be incomplete, so a column is `NOT NULL` only where there is an honest placeholder:

- `text` and `longText` are `NOT NULL` when `[Required]`; `''` stands in for a missing draft value.
- `slug` is `NOT NULL` always, because it is derived.
- `richText` is `NOT NULL` always; the empty document stands in for a missing value. There is no store default.
- Lists (`blocks`, multi `select`, repeatable components and any other field of a type that supports `Multiple` and stores JSON) are `NOT NULL DEFAULT '[]'`.
- Everything else is nullable even when `[Required]`: `email`, `url`, single `select`, `integer`, `decimal`, `boolean`, `date`, `dateTime` and `json`. A draft has no honest placeholder for them, and format checks would reject `''`.
- A column flattened from a single component is nullable even when its field is `[Required]`, `slug` or `richText`, because the whole component may be absent. A list inside a component keeps `NOT NULL DEFAULT '[]'`.
- `site_id` is `NOT NULL` except on `ContentScope.Global` types, where it is null. `locale` is always `NOT NULL`.

**Default lengths.** When a field declares no maximum length, `slug` is 200, `url` 2048, `email` 254 and a single `select` 64. `text` and `longText` without a limit stay unbounded. The column is sized with that length, and the validation pipeline refuses a longer value with `FCMS2103` before the write, wherever the field is (in a block or a repeated component too), so no provider sees one: SQLite would store it, and PostgreSQL and SQL Server would fail the write.
