*Groups and permissions.* `cms_member_group (id, name, description, role_id, created_at)` and `cms_member_group_member (group_id, member_id)`. A group has a name of 1 to 64 lower-case letters, digits, `-` and `_`, unique in the instance, so that it is one name on every database and one word of the principal's groups, and is bound to one role, of the instance or of a site; a group always has a role, and one that only names its members for a row rule may be bound to a role without grants. The membership of such a group is managed with `members.manage` alone, in both directions (4.10), so a row rule that must hold against those callers, a negated one above all, reads a group whose role holds a grant they lack. A member holds the Public role of the site of the request, the site's own or else the seeded one as for a visitor, the built-in Member role, and the roles of its groups. The evaluator reads them with their stamps, and the names of the groups, in one query on every request, so a change holds from the next request; a member that is deactivated or deleted holds nothing, not even what a visitor reads, and nothing is taken from the token, whose roles or groups claim is never read. The principal a row rule sees carries the group names in the claim `fcms_groups`, and `Principal.IsInGroup(name)` (`FluentCMS.Content`, in the SDK) reads it, as in `ctx => perk => perk.Tier == (ctx.Principal.IsInGroup("gold") ? "gold" : "standard")`. Only that principal carries them, and `IsInGroup` throws on any other (4.8). A member's token has no scope that manages, so its grants give it published reads and nothing else, a group bound to a role with `*` included (4.8). A role is not deleted while a group is bound to it (`FCMS3044`, 4.10); the foreign key cascades, so a site that is deleted takes the groups bound to its roles along, as it does the API keys bound to them.

*Clients and tokens.* A client of `cms.Clients.Applications` names its pool with `Pool`, `Admins` (the default) or `Members`. A client of the member pool asks for `openid`, `profile`, `email` and `offline_access` only, names no roles, has no consent step, needs redirect URIs or `AllowDirectLogin`, and is not the admin application's client (`FCMS3021`); it may have a secret, and is never a service account: it holds neither the client credentials grant nor introspection. The authorize endpoint answers such a client from the member cookie and never from the admin's, challenging to `/account/members/login`, with `prompt`, `max_age` and the absolute session lifetime as for an admin user. Its tokens carry the audience `fluentcms:members`, `fcms_kind` `Member`, `sub`, `name` (the display name, or the address) and the scopes asked for among those four, whatever else was asked; they carry no roles and no groups. The token endpoint reads the member again for a code or a refresh token and refuses one that is deactivated, deleted or whose security stamp changed (`invalid_grant`); lockout holds for a new sign-in only, as for admin users, and ends neither a refresh token nor the member cookie. A code or refresh token says whose it is by its kind and is redeemed only through a client of the pool it was issued in, so a client the configuration moves to the other pool redeems nothing. The bearer scheme accepts both audiences, and a member's token is kept out of the Management API, of drafts and of `/mcp` by its scopes: it never holds `management`, `draft` or `mcp`. `/connect/userinfo` answers a member's token from the member. `/connect/logout` ends the sign-in of the pool of the client the request names, by its `client_id` or the audience of its `id_token_hint`, and the admin's when it names none; the two logout pages share an antiforgery token, so the member page marks its form (`fcms_pool`), neither page passes the mark on from a link, and the endpoint takes a confirmation only from the page of the pool it signs out. The member logout page, which ends a member's sign-in itself when no client sent the member there, revokes the authorizations of its server session as the endpoint does. Deactivating or deleting a member, a reset and a change of password revoke its authorizations and every token of its subject (`MemberSessions`), so an access token stops on the next request, one of a direct login without a session included. `UserSessions` lists and ends a member's sessions for the Management API, since a member's id is the subject of its tokens.

*Direct login* (Q4). `grant_type=member_password` with `username`, `password` and `client_id` at `/connect/token`, a custom grant of OpenIddict, for a client of the member pool with `AllowDirectLogin` while `cms.Members.AllowDirectLogin` is on; both are off by default. Every refusal is `invalid_grant` with one description: the option off, a client without the flag, an unknown address, a wrong password, an address that is not verified, a member that is deactivated or locked out. The reason is in the log (event 9075, with the client and the client address, never the address typed or the password), and an address or a password that was refused is a `member.login_failed` activity row. A wrong password counts towards the lockout, and the hash runs for an unknown address and for a member that may not sign in, as on the login page. Every client of the member pool holds the grant's permission, so that the endpoint answers; a client of the admin pool does not, and OpenIddict refuses it (`unauthorized_client`). The discovery document names the grant only while the option is on, and the startup report lists `member-direct-login` outside Development as the less secure way in (4.15).

*Member pages.* Razor Pages of the `FluentCms` area at `/account/members/login`, `/logout`, `/register`, `/verify-email`, `/forgot-password` and `/reset-password`, and `/external-login` and `/logins` for the providers (member providers as built), with the layout, branding, headers, content security policy and `auth` rate limit of `/account` (hosted pages as built), and public models (`MemberLoginModel` and the others in `FluentCMS.Core.Api.Account`) for a host that overrides a page. They sign in with the cookie scheme `FluentCms.Members` (cookie `.FluentCms.Members`), apart from Identity's application cookie, so an admin session and a member session in one browser neither sign each other in nor out; `MemberSignInManager` signs out that cookie alone. The login page takes the address and the password with lockout, gives every refusal one answer, an address that is not verified included, and returns to a local `ReturnUrl` only. The register page is there only while registration is on. Opening the verification link changes nothing: the page has a button, so a mail scanner that follows links does not spend the token, and without a link it sends the mail again. The reset page is also where a member an administrator created chooses its first password. Every form carries an antiforgery token.

*Member providers as built* ([#2993](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/2993)). `cms.Members.Providers`, bound from `FluentCms:Members:Providers:{name}`, lists the external providers members sign in with, apart from `cms.Sso`. A provider has `Type` (`Google`, `Microsoft`, `GitHub`, `Apple` or `Oidc`, in any case), `ClientId`, `ClientSecret`, `Scopes` and `DisplayName`; `Oidc` takes `Authority` (https, unless `RequireHttpsMetadata` is off) and may have no secret, and `Apple` takes `TeamId`, `KeyId` and `PrivateKey`, the PEM text of the key, and no `ClientSecret`. A name is 1 to 64 lower-case letters, digits or `-`: the options find a provider whatever the case, but its logins are kept under the name as it is written, so a name has one way to be written and a later change of case cannot part the members from their logins. It is none of the sign-in methods of the pool (`password`, `direct_login`), since a sign-in event names the provider as its method. A provider that is not valid stops the host with `FCMS3180` and its key: a name that is not one, an unknown or missing type, a missing client id, a missing secret for Google, Microsoft or GitHub, an Apple key that does not import as a private key, a scope that is not one word, a display name of more than 128 characters, `LinkVerifiedEmail` on Microsoft, a name whose scheme is the name of another authentication scheme, and an `Oidc` provider whose `Events` were replaced or `EventsType` set after FluentCMS set its events (#3526); for the last two, the authentication options and the options of the providers' schemes are built when the host starts, the latter apart from the former. Secrets come from configuration only, and no message or log line repeats one. *Handlers.* The name is the login provider in `cms_member_login` and the callback path, `/account/members/sso/{name}`; the scheme is `member-{name}`, added to the authentication options at start as the providers of `cms.Sso` are, after them, so that a member provider whose scheme is the name of an admin provider is the one refused. A scheme of a member provider has no display name, which is what keeps it off the admin login page, which lists the schemes that have one; the member pages list `cms.Members.Providers` and challenge nothing else, so a provider of the same name in both sections is two schemes that never meet. Google and Microsoft are the in-box handlers (`Microsoft.AspNetCore.Authentication.Google` and `.MicrosoftAccount` 10.0.15), GitHub and Apple those of aspnet-contrib (`AspNet.Security.OAuth.GitHub` and `.Apple` 10.1.0, Q10), and `Oidc` the OpenID Connect handler with the code flow, PKCE and the token's own claim names. An OAuth handler builds the `redirect_uri` from the request, when it challenges and again when it redeems the code, with no event between the two, so each is subclassed (`MemberGoogleHandler` and the others) to build both from the public origin when one is set (4.15); the OpenID Connect provider does it in its events' `RedirectToIdentityProvider`, before a delegate the host set. The Apple handler signs its client secret with the private key (ES256, in the name of the team) and validates the id token against Apple's keys. A host tunes a provider with `services.Configure<GoogleOptions>("member-{name}", ...)` and the like. Neither the remote sign-out path nor the signed-out callback path of an `Oidc` provider is served (404): a provider's front-channel logout ends no member session, and no logout at the provider is started here. The events of an `Oidc` provider are kept as an admin provider's are (admin SSO as built, [#3526](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3526)): FluentCMS's `TokenValidated` (the login's key from `sub`), `RemoteFailure` (back to the member login page), `TicketReceived` (the provider's name in the ticket), `RemoteSignOut` and `SignedOutCallbackRedirect` (not served) run first and then the host's, set as `Events`, as `EventsType` or as a delegate, and a host that replaces `Events` or sets `EventsType` after `AddFluentCms` does not start (`FCMS3180`). The OAuth providers keep their events as before: a host that replaces them drops the provider's name from the ticket, which the external login page then refuses, and the way back to the login page after a failure. The Apple package writes the client secret it signs and the tokens of a sign-in to the log at Trace; a handler's log is named after its type, so `MemberAppleHandler` keeps the name of the package's handler, and all of it is under the category `AspNet.Security.OAuth.Apple`, and none under a category of FluentCMS ([#3006](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3006)). `AppleLogLevel`, a post-configuration of `LoggerFilterOptions`, holds that category at `Information` or above unless the configuration names it or a category under it: it adds a rule of the category for every provider and one for each logging provider that has rules of its own, since a provider's own rule outranks a rule of every provider whatever its category, at the level named for every provider when there is one. A rule only raises the level the category had and keeps the filter of the rule it replaces, so a provider held above `Information`, as the event log is on Windows, stays there. A default of `Trace`, for every provider or for one, does not reach the category; naming it lower does, and the startup report lists `member-apple-logging` while a logger of the package's categories writes below `Information` outside Development with an Apple provider configured, which also catches a logger factory of the host's own. The request log of ASP.NET Core writes the URL of each request at `Information`, query included. The callback of a Google, Microsoft or GitHub provider is a GET, so its line holds the one-time `code` and the `state`; Apple and `Oidc` providers, and the providers of `cms.Sso`, post their answer (`form_post`), so theirs holds the path only. The query is left in the log: a code is redeemed once, by the handler as the callback arrives, and only with the client secret, and with the PKCE verifier of the browser's correlation cookie for Google and Microsoft. *The external cookie.* The handlers sign into `FluentCms.Members.External` (cookie `.FluentCms.Members.External`, five minutes), apart from Identity's external cookie, which is the admin pool's, so a sign-in at a provider of one pool is never read by the pages of the other. The ticket names the provider that issued it, written when the provider answers, and `MemberSignInManager` is never asked for Identity's external sign-in, which would end the admin's cookie. `/account/members/external-login` reads the cookie, ends it whatever follows, and asks `MemberExternalSignIn`. *Who signs in.* A member linked to the login signs in, with the provider as its authentication method and no `amr`, unless it is deactivated, locked out or not verified. Anyone else gets a member only while `AllowRegistration` is on, named by the provider's address and display name, with no password, the member and its login in one change; with registration off the sign-in is refused (`FCMS3187` in the log). *Verified addresses.* The address of a new member counts as verified only when the provider says so: `email_verified` of Google's user information, of Apple's id token and of an `Oidc` id token. The GitHub handler would take the primary address whether or not GitHub verified it, and the public address of the profile before that, so `MemberGitHubHandler` takes the primary address only when it is verified and the profile's address is not mapped: a GitHub sign-in carries a verified address or none, `user:email` is always asked for, and an account without a verified primary address makes no member (`FCMS3181`). Microsoft gives no such flag, so its address never counts as verified. A member made with an address that is not verified gets the verification mail of the pool and is not signed in; the mail names the provider, so that the owner of the mailbox knows which account its link lets in, the page says that a mail is on its way, and a later sign-in through the provider, or `resend-verification`, sends it again until the address is verified. *An address that is a member's.* It is never taken over: the sign-in is refused (`FCMS3182`), with no member named in the event, and the member links the provider after signing in. `LinkVerifiedEmail` on a provider, off by default and refused for `Microsoft`, links such a member at its first sign-in through the provider when the provider says the address is verified, the member may sign in now, which needs its own address verified, and it has no login of that provider yet. A sign-in with an address the provider did not verify is answered alike whether or not the address is a member's, with the page of the mail, and the member gets the mail that says it has an account. *An address that is not verified yet.* A sign-in through a provider with the address of a member whose address is not verified, by a login that is not that member's, is a second claim of the address, and `MemberAccountService.ContestAsync` treats it as it treats a second registration (registration and mail), whatever the provider says of the address: the password or the logins the first claim left are removed with one new security stamp, which ends the links sent so far, the member leaves its groups, and the mail carries the link that chooses a password. Nothing is linked and nobody is signed in, also with `LinkVerifiedEmail` and an address the provider verified, since the member is not shown to be its owner's yet; the answer is the page of the mail, or the refusal while registration is off, which is what a visitor without a member gets there. Without this, whoever claimed the address first, with a password or through a provider that does not verify addresses, would keep that way in once the owner, who was told that a mail is on its way, used a verification link from its inbox. A member with neither a password nor a login is left as it is, so a repeat changes no stamp, and an address still gets one mail a minute. A login that a provider brought without verifying the address does not outlive the owner of the mailbox: it is removed when the address is registered again and when a reset link sets the password, before the address was verified (registration and mail), so whoever made the member first cannot sign in as its owner later. Verifying with the verification link keeps the login, as it keeps a password the registration set. *Linking.* `/account/members/logins` shows the signed-in member its logins. Linking starts there with a form post, goes to the provider and comes back to the page, which names the account at the provider and links it only on a second form post; the sign-in at the provider carries the id of the member that started it and is not taken by another member or by the external login page. A login belongs to one member and a member has one login of a provider (`FCMS3186`). `MemberLogins` lists `{ "provider", "displayName" }`, without the provider's key, and removes the member's logins of a provider (`FCMS3185` when it has none) in one save with a new security stamp, and then ends the member's sessions, access tokens included, as a new password does; the page that removed it keeps its own cookie. The last way to sign in is not removed (`FCMS3184`, 409): a login counts while its provider is in `cms.Members.Providers`, and a password while the member has one. `GET /me/logins` and `DELETE /me/logins/{provider}` are the member's routes and `GET` and `DELETE /members/{id}/logins` and `/logins/{provider}` the administrator's, with `members.manage` (4.10). *Failures.* An error at the provider, or a callback that belongs to no sign-in started in that browser, goes back to the member login page as a failed sign-in (`invalid_credentials`, event 9091) with the return URL it started with; one that was started to link goes back to the logins page and is no sign-in. The callbacks are answered by the authentication middleware, before the endpoints and the rate limiter of their policies, as those of admin SSO are; the check before authentication counts them with the `auth` limit by client address, as it counts the pages, and each failed callback is a `member.login_failed` row. Every form of these pages carries an antiforgery token, and the return URL is a local one. The `member_password` grant takes no external login: a member a provider made has no password. Codes `FCMS3180` to `FCMS3187`; event ids 9090 to 9094.

*Events.* `MemberRegisteredEvent`, `MemberVerifiedEvent`, `MemberUpdatedEvent` (with the names of what changed, `MemberChanges`), `MemberDeactivatedEvent`, `MemberDeletedEvent`, `MemberGroupChangedEvent`, `MemberLoginLinkedEvent` and `MemberLoginUnlinkedEvent` (with the provider) from the store and the services with the change, `MemberGroupCreatedEvent`, `MemberGroupUpdatedEvent` and `MemberGroupDeletedEvent` for a group, and `MemberLoginSucceededEvent` and `MemberLoginFailedEvent` from `MemberSignInManager` with the way in as their method (`MemberLoginMethods`: `password` on the page, `direct_login` at the token endpoint, and the name of a provider for a sign-in through it). They are the activity rows `member.registered`, `member.verified`, `member.updated`, `member.deactivated`, `member.deleted`, `member.group_changed`, `member.login_linked`, `member.login_unlinked`, `member_group.created`, `member_group.updated`, `member_group.deleted`, `member.login_succeeded` and `member.login_failed`, of no site, and the end of a member's sessions is `member.sessions_ended` (`MemberSessionsEndedEvent`, sessions that end as built, 4.9). What a member does to its own account names the member as the actor, what an administrator does the administrator, and a failed sign-in the anonymous principal, without the address that was typed. Codes `FCMS3160` to `FCMS3174`, and `FCMS3180` to `FCMS3187` for the providers; event ids 9070 to 9076 and 9090 to 9094.

**From the command line (B-91).** `fluentcms user create` makes an active administrator, with a password read at a prompt or from standard input and never from an argument, in the host only; `user invite`, `user add-role`, and `apikey create`, `list` and `revoke` call the services of the endpoints, inside the host as the system or with `--url` as a management key (6.1).

**Security defaults.** Closed by default: no anonymous Delivery access until the Public role grants it; CORS allow-list with no wildcard; secure cookie flags; strict rate limit on `/connect/*` and `/api/members/v1/*`; secrets only from configuration providers; every environment but Development, Staging and custom names included, refuses to start with a generated signing key (`FCMS1102`), without a public origin (`FCMS1105`) or with the Data Protection ring unencrypted (`FCMS1106`), unless `Security:AllowGeneratedSigningKey`, `Security:AllowRequestOrigin` or `Security:AllowUnencryptedKeyRing` is set for it. The last two were report lines until the code review of 7 Nov 2026, which made them refusals like the first (#2847, decision 1; #2848, #2849): an unset origin lets a forged `Host` receive a real user's reset link, and an unencrypted ring lets a reader of the database forge the sign-in cookie. A setting accepted by its opt-in stays in the startup report (4.15), and other environments start as before.

### 4.8 Authorization

Rows 4.3 to 4.8, 4.12, 4.17, 8.14; decisions 31, 32.

**Permission catalogue.** Core permissions (`users.manage`, `roles.manage`, `apikeys.manage`, `sites.manage`, `settings.{area}.manage`, `jobs.manage`, `activity.read`, `schema.read`, `plugins.manage`, `transfer.manage`, `system.read`, `members.manage`), generated per type (`content.{type}.create|read|update|delete|publish|unpublish|restore|purge`), and plugin-declared ones through `IPermissionProvider`. All stored in `cms_role_permission (role_id, permission, constraints json)`.

**Constraints.** Additive only, no deny rules. A grant may be constrained to sites, locales, a field allow-list (`fields: ["title", "slug"]`, null means all), a row rule name and a plugin's resources (`resources`, such as media folders, [ADR 0004](../decisions/0004-folder-limited-media-grants.md)). A grant with `resources` counts only in `IPermissionChecker.ResourcesAsync`, which answers null when a grant with no resource limit holds the permission and otherwise the union of the resources of the grants that hold it; every other check, core's and content's included, leaves it out, so `*` limited to resources gives no core or content permission. A grant with a row rule gives content permissions alone ("Row rules on grants as built", below). A principal's effective permission set is the union of its roles' grants. Field lists and row rules are tied together where the union would otherwise give access no grant did: a write is decided per grant against the entry, and until responses can project per row, the rows of a read stay the union while a field is readable only if a grant with no row rule allows it or every applying grant does.

**Global types.** A write to a type with `ContentScope.Global` (create, update, patch, delete, restore) needs a grant with no site limit, from a role with no `site_id`, because a global entry is shared by every site. Reads are unchanged. The seeded instance-wide roles are unaffected.

**Shared fields.** A non-localizable field of a localized type is shared by all locales, so writing it needs a grant with no locale limit, or one that covers every locale of the entry, including a locale being added. Two grants that cover the locales only together do not count. Otherwise the field is unwritable and keeps its value.

**Roles.** `cms_role` has an optional `site_id` (null is instance-wide). Built-ins seeded by the first migration: Super Admin (everything), Editor, Author (own entries row rule), Public (the anonymous principal per site, which is how public access configuration is stored), Member (default for the member pool).

**Last Super Admin.** The instance always keeps one active user who holds `*` with no constraints through an instance-wide role (#2847, decision 5; #2850). Super Admin's own grants do not change (`FCMS3043`), and an edit of another instance-wide role that drops that grant is refused with `FCMS3052` when no other active user would hold it. Deactivating a user and taking roles from it follow the same rule (admin users as built, 4.7).

**Default grants of plugins as built ([#3103](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3103)).** Core's seed cannot name a plugin's permissions, so a permission names the built-in roles that get it by default: `Permission.DefaultRoles` (SDK), a list of `BuiltInRole`, which is `Editor` or `Author`, empty unless set; a value that is not one throws `ArgumentOutOfRangeException`. Super Admin holds `*` already, and Public and Member are left out, so that no plugin opens anonymous or member access by itself. At each start, after the migrations and before the seed, the host applies every default of every `IPermissionProvider` that the instance has not applied yet: it gives the instance-wide role the permission with no constraints, unless the role holds a grant whose pattern matches it, limited or not, or a limited grant of any permission of the same plugin (of the same provider, for one the host registered), and records the default in `cms_default_grant (role_id, permission, plugin, applied_at)`, keyed on role and permission, whether it gave a grant or not. A recorded default is not applied again, so a grant that an operator takes away stays away at the next start; a plugin added to an instance that runs already gets its defaults at its first start there, once; a default that a later version of a plugin names is applied once, and the earlier ones are not given again; and a role whose grants of a plugin an operator limited before its first start, to sites, locales, fields or folders, gets none of that plugin's defaults, so a default never widens the reach of a limited role: an Editor with `media.delete` limited to a folder gets neither `media.read` nor `media.upload` nor `media.update`, while a wildcard such as `media.*` with no limit only means the role holds them already. A default is checked against the catalogue: one whose permission the catalogue does not hold as a plugin permission of the provider that names it, which only a provider that answers differently after the catalogue was built can give, is not applied, not recorded and logged as event 9239. The built-in roles have fixed ids and can be neither deleted (`FCMS3043`) nor renamed (`FCMS3041`), so a default names its role by id, and they are instance-wide, so a default grants in every site; a site where the plugin is disabled answers its routes with 404 (4.13). `plugin` names the plugin whose provider declares the default, and is null for a provider the host registered on the container itself, whose defaults apply the same way. The grants and their records are one save through `CmsDbContext`, so each changed role's stamp rises once and `RolePermissionsChangedEvent` is staged for it as for any edit of a role, which evicts every site's Delivery copies once for each role that got grants; of two instances that start together, the one that saves second fails on the key, reads again and finds nothing left to apply. A save that keeps failing, as when the roles keep changing, is tried five times and then stops the start with an `InvalidOperationException` that says nothing was given, and the next start tries again. Each role that got grants has an activity row `role.updated` by the system with the grants under `added`, and log event 9238. A start with no default to apply writes nothing, and one whose providers name none reads nothing. The record is a table rather than a settings area, as seeding's is (4.16), because an area would add a `settings.{area}.manage` permission and a Management route through which the record could be rewritten, and a settings write cannot share the save of the grants (`FCMS2807`). No tag of a preview existed, so the table went into the baseline of each provider (4.3, 10.4, H5).

The defaults as built:

| Plugin | Editor | Author |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Media (5.1) | `media.read`, `media.upload`, `media.update` | `media.read`, `media.upload` |
| AI assist (5.5) | `ai.use` | `ai.use` |

**Row rules.** Registered in code with a name and used by roles as data:

```csharp
cms.Authorization.AddRowRule<Article>("own-entries",
    ctx => article => article.CreatedBy == ctx.Principal.Id);
cms.Authorization.AddRowRule<Article>("region",
    ctx => article => article.Region == ctx.Principal.Claim("region"));
```

The evaluator compiles the predicate into the list query and checks it on single-entry operations. JSON-authored rules are a Should (decision 32).

**Row rules on grants as built ([#3443](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3443)).** A rule limits the rows of content, so a grant names one only beside a pattern of content permissions alone, `content.{type}.{operation}` with `*` in place of the type, the operation or both, and only a rule registered for the type; for a pattern of every type, for each content type of the schema, which a rule registered on `ContentItem` is. Anything else is refused with `FCMS3042` on `grants[n].constraints.rowRule` wherever a role is written, `POST /roles`, `PUT /roles/{id}` and the import of a package's roles: `*`, `*.*.update` and every core, settings and plugin permission take no rule (`content.*.*` with the rule is what `*` with it gave), and `content.*.update` with a rule of `page` alone is refused rather than read as `page`, since it would grant nothing on the other types. The check runs with the other checks of the grant, as for an unknown rule. On `POST /roles` and `PUT /roles/{id}` that is after `roles.manage` and before the check that the caller holds every grant, and anyone who gets that far may read the rules and their types in `GET /permissions/row-rules`. An import validates each role it would create or change before it checks `roles.manage` and the grants, so a caller with `transfer.manage` alone gets `FCMS3042` for that role in the report, in a dry run too, with the message naming the types the rule lacks. A grant stored before this check is read and exported as it is, and gives no more than before: one with a rule beside a permission outside content gives that permission nowhere, as a grant limited to resources gives no core permission (`Allows` and `ResourcesOf` leave it out), while the content that a `*` with a rule reaches stays under the rule; one with a rule on a type it is not registered for matches none of that type's rows, as it always did. An edit that keeps such a grant is refused until the grant or its rule is dropped, and so is an import that would change its role; an import of a role identical to the stored one, grants and constraints included, is skipped without validation, so an instance still imports its own export. The same holds for a type added to the schema after a grant of every type was written with a rule registered type by type: the grant gives no row of the new type, and every `PUT` of its role, a rename or a change of `requireTwoFactor` too, is refused with `FCMS3042` until the developer registers the rule for the new type, or the administrator drops the grant or gives one grant per type the rule has. Super Admin's `*` has no constraints, so the guard of the last Super Admin (above) is not affected.

**Evaluator.** `IAuthorizationEvaluator` caches the effective permission set per principal for the request; a `permissions_version` stamp on roles invalidates it across instances. Scopes narrow but never widen: a token with `draft` may read drafts if its roles allow; `management` tokens are bounded by roles. The set keeps its answers about content (the grants behind an operation, the readable fields and the row rule) per type, operation, site and locale for the request, so filtering the fields of a list goes through the grants once per type, not once per entry and field. A grant's pattern is split once, when its role's grants are loaded into the cache across requests. `RoleService.DeleteAsync` takes a deleted role out of that cache on its instance; another instance keeps the entry, which no one asks for again, until it restarts, bounded by the number of roles (#2854). Without an operation nothing is answered (2.3).

**Member groups on the principal as built** ([#2992](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/2992), [#3004](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3004)). The evaluator reads a member's groups with its role stamps (4.7), and `EffectivePermissions` gives every row rule the operation with a copy of its principal that carries them: their names in the claim `fcms_groups` for a member, and none for any other kind, a groups claim the principal had being dropped. The copy is made once per operation, when the first rule is read. Only that copy carries the groups, and `Principal.IsInGroup` throws `InvalidOperationException` on any other principal, with a message that names the call and says that groups are read inside a row rule only. The principal of the operation context, which a hook, an event handler, a job or a plugin's endpoint reads, is such a principal, for every kind, so a check outside a row rule fails when it is first run instead of answering false, which would let a check that refuses by group pass. Putting the groups on the operation's principal would cost a query on every request of a member, also on those that check no permission, so it is left out (10.4, H7). Taking a member out of a group and deleting a group need every grant of the group's role, as putting a member in one does, and are refused with the same 403 `FCMS3103` (4.10), since a row rule that negates a group lets a member read more once it is out of the group. The check covers only what the role grants: the membership of a group whose role holds no grant is managed with `members.manage` alone, in both directions, so a row rule that must hold against such callers, a negated one above all, reads a group whose role holds a grant they lack.

**Role stamps on every request as built.** The stamps of the caller's roles are read on every request, in one query, and are what carries a role change to every instance: the grants are kept per role and stamp, and the role an anonymous caller holds, its site's own Public role or else the instance-wide one, is kept per site with the stamp it had when it was found. A warm anonymous request reads that one stamp; a stamp that moved, or a role that is gone, finds the Public role again in the query that reads the stamps. An API key's role comes from the row the authentication handler read and checked for the request (it is kept in `HttpContext.Items` until the request ends, and its expiry is checked again), so the key is read once per request; a job, which has no request, reads the key again, so a key revoked since the job was scheduled holds no role (#2857).

**Client roles as built** ([#2978](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/2978)). An MCP client that registered itself has a role (4.7, "Dynamic client registration as built"), and a call through it is allowed what both the user's roles and that role allow. The authorize endpoint puts the role's id on the code as the claim `fcms_client_role`, and the token endpoint reads the client again for a code or a refresh token and writes the claim anew, so the access token carries the role the client has now; a token of any other client has no such claim. The evaluator reads the claim from the principal, loads the grants of that role with the principal's own, in the one query that reads the role stamps, and gives `EffectivePermissions` a second set, which is the role's grants worked out as for a principal of its own with the same scopes and bindings. Every answer is then what both sets give: a permission and a content operation need a grant in each, the readable and writable fields, the readable locales and a plugin's resources are those both allow, the row rules of both apply together, and handing out a grant (`Covers`) needs both to hold it. Since it is the evaluator, it holds on every surface that takes the token, Delivery included, and for a plugin's `IPermissionChecker`. A claim that names no role, or a role that was deleted since, is a set with no grant, which leaves the token nothing rather than the user's own reach; a role is therefore not kept from deletion by a client that has it. The role only removes: a client with Super Admin gives a user nothing the user's roles do not, so approving a client with a role, registering one with it or changing a client's role to it needs no grant of that role, unlike giving a role to a user or a key. `IEffectiveAccess`, which the MCP tool list and the input schemas of its tools are built from (5.4, "Tools as built"), describes the same: the types and their readable fields, the operations of a type, the fields a create or an update takes and the permissions held are those both sets give, so a tool is listed through a client only when a call through it would be allowed.

**Media references on the Delivery API as built** ([#3109](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3109)). `ScopeAllows` leaves a Delivery principal, one without the `management` scope (the anonymous caller as the Public role, a Delivery or preview key, a preview token, a bearer token without the scope), no permission but reading content, so it never holds `media.read`, whatever its role grants. A media reference is therefore not resolved by a grant on the Delivery API. There, a media field with `populate` and an image node with `richText=html` or `markdown` resolve to an asset that is public, not deleted and in the library of the operation's site, whoever reads, which is what `GET /api/delivery/v1/media/{id}` serves to anyone, with the same URLs; a private asset never resolves there, for a managing principal that calls the Delivery API too, and in a site where the media plugin is disabled (4.13), whose media routes answer 404, none does. The Management API resolves by the caller's folder grants of `media.read`, as before (5.1). The rule is the media plugin's resolver's, by the surface of the operation (`IsDelivery`, 2.2); the evaluator is unchanged and grants nothing more, and a preview token takes the same rule as any Delivery read. Outside the Delivery API, on `/graphql` say, the same rule holds for a principal that may not manage, which `IPermissionChecker.CanManageAsync` tells from the evaluator's `CanManage`, and a managing one resolves by its folder grants (5.1, [#3112](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3112)).

**Introspection and MCP.** Both call the evaluator to drop types and fields the principal cannot read, so generated admin forms and agent tools match permissions (4.17). As built, the MCP plugin asks through `IEffectiveAccess`, which core answers from the evaluator (5.4), and so does `GET /me/access` for the admin (4.10).

### 4.9 Activity log

Row 4.14; the backend half of 3.26 and the data for 4.20 (Should).

`cms_activity (id v7, site_id, occurred_at, actor_type, actor_id, actor_name, origin, client_id, action, target_type, target_id, target_label, locale, details json, correlation_id, ip)`. Written by event subscribers, so plugins get logged for free when they raise events. Retention job, default 30 days, configurable per site. `GET /api/management/v1/activity` with filters on actor, origin, action, target, date range; permission `activity.read`.

**As built.** Every event of the catalogue that exists has an outbox handler named `activity.{action}`, registered with `cms.Events.AddActivity<TEvent>(name, describe)`, which a plugin calls for its own events. The row says what the event says: actor, origin, client id, correlation id, site and time come from its `EventSource`, not from the job that delivers it, and the id of the row is the delivery key, so a repeated delivery writes nothing. A row names its target by type and id and never by a field of content, and text is passed through `SecretScrubber` and cut to its column; `IActivityLog` is the direct writer for what no event covers: it writes on a context of its own, refuses inside a transaction, and stores actions in lower case; `ip` is filled only by a writer that knows it. `IActivityLog.ActorName` gives a name as a row's actor and a version's author are stored, scrubbed and cut to 128 characters outside a surrogate pair, for a plugin that keeps a principal's name in a row of its own, as AI assist does (5.5, [#3449](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3449)); it writes nothing. `IActivityQuery` reads the site of the current operation, newest first, filtered by actor, origin, action (exact, or a prefix ending in `*`), target and date range (`From` inclusive, `To` exclusive), and leaves the permission check to the endpoint. Rows of no site (the failures of jobs, whose text can name internals of the host) are returned only with `IncludeInstance`, which is for instance-wide readers only: the query cannot tell a reader of one site from a reader of the instance, so the Management endpoint must check an instance-level permission before it sets it. Retention is `cms.Activity.Retention` (30 days) with `SiteRetention` by site slug until sites have settings of their own; the instance-wide rows use the default. The job is `activity.retention`, daily at 03:30 UTC, deleting in batches of `PruneBatchSize` (at most 1000), served by an index on `occurred_at`. `cms.Activity.Enabled = false` stops new rows and stores no schedule, leaves a stored one alone (the job returns at once while the log is off), and drops the rows already waiting in the outbox. `role.updated` has the details `added` and `removed`, the grants the update gave and took, each its permission followed by its constraints as JSON when it has any, separated by semicolons and cut to 256 characters like every detail (#2854). A refused API key writes no row, except a valid key sent from an address it is not allowed from (4.7).

**Sessions that end as built** ([#3207](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3207), [#3531](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3531)). The ends of sessions listed here write an activity row: `user.sessions_ended` (`UserSessionsEndedEvent`) for an admin user and `member.sessions_ended` (`MemberSessionsEndedEvent`) for a member, of no site, targeting the user or member, with the reason (`SessionEndReasons`) and, for a session ended by its id, that id in `details`; never a token, a cookie or the id of a server session. One row is written per session ended by its id and one per sign-in or user whose sessions ended together. The actor is the one who ended them: in an operation its caller, the user or member itself or an administrator; on the hosted pages and the logout endpoint the user or member whose sign-in ended, or the system when something else ended it. The reasons are `signed_out` (the logout endpoint and the member logout page, the user or member, written whether or not the sign-in had issued tokens), `revoked` (`DELETE /me/sessions/{id}`, `DELETE /users/{id}/sessions/{sessionId}` and `DELETE /members/{id}/sessions/{sessionId}`, in the transaction of the revocation), `replaced` (another user or member signing in over a live sign-in of the pool, the system), `second_factor` (a sign-in with the second factor ending what its server session was issued without it, the user, only when something ended), `remote_sign_out` (a provider's front-channel logout, the system), and, for the ends of every session of a user or member, only when one was live: `password_changed`, `deactivated`, `login_removed` and, for a user, `security_changed` for any other change of its security stamp, and `deleted` for a member. A live session is a valid authorization of the user or member, and for a member also a valid token that has not expired, as those of a direct login, which has no authorization, are; every authorization and token of the member is revoked all the same, and the counts of OpenIddict's revocations by subject, which take in what was revoked, redeemed or expired already, decide nothing. A change made on the admin's hosted pages runs outside any operation, so the change of a password there has the system as its actor, as its `user.updated` row does; the member pages run a change of the member's own account as the member (`MemberOperation`), so their rows name the member. A row of an operation commits with the end in its transaction, so a row that cannot be written ends nothing and the request fails as any failed write does. A row of a sign-in that ended outside an operation (`signed_out`, `replaced`, `second_factor`, `remote_sign_out`) is written after the end, in a transaction of its own. Two failures of that write are faults of the code and fail the request as before: the row written inside a transaction someone else began (`FCMS3009`, refused by `IdentityChanges` before the database is asked) and the event published inside one (`FCMS2602`). Every other failure of the write, whatever the provider throws for it (an exception of ADO.NET or EF Core, a timeout, SqlClient's bare `InvalidOperationException` for a pool with no free connection or a severed one, a socket error), except `OutOfMemoryException`, lets the end finish: a sign-out whose tokens were revoked still deletes its cookie and answers as it otherwise does, and a sign-in still signs in. The failure is logged as an error (event 9012, with the pool, the id, the reason, the type of the failure and, for a `DbException`, its `SqlState` and `ErrorCode`, but neither its message nor its stack, which with EF Core's sensitive data logging could hold the row, and no token or cookie), and that line is then the only trace of the end. A sign-out needs no write of its own beyond the revocation, so it is not made to depend on the row. These ends write no row of this kind: `/connect/revoke`, which a client calls for one of its own tokens and which is the only sign-out of a member signed in through the direct login, since OpenIddict answers it alike for any token, live or not, and it names a token rather than a sign-in; a refresh token used again after the leeway, which revokes the session's tokens inside OpenIddict's handling of the token request; a new role for an MCP client and its revocation, which end every session of the client and are its rows `mcp.client_role_changed` and `mcp.client_revoked` (dynamic client registration as built, 4.7); a client that `cms.Clients` no longer lists, deleted with its sessions and tokens when the host starts, which has no actor and logs event 9002; the consents of a client whose redirect URIs changed in configuration, revoked when the host starts for the same reason; and a session whose tokens expire, which nothing ends.

### 4.10 REST APIs

Rows 2.1, 2.2, 2.6 to 2.9, 2.12 to 2.20, 10.8, 10.12.

**Delivery API.** Read-only, published by default, under `/api/delivery/v1`:

| Route | Notes |
| --- | --- |
| `GET /{type}` | list with the query grammar; singletons return the single entry |
| `GET /{type}/{id}` | by id |
| `GET /{type}/by/{field}/{value}` | by a unique field such as slug |
| `GET /media/{id}` | asset metadata and URLs (media plugin; as built in 5.1, B-66) |
| `GET /openapi/v1.json`, `GET /scalar` | document and UI (host-level, not versioned) |

`status=draft` requires a preview key, a bearer token with the `draft` scope, or a preview token for the entry (4.7). Responses carry `ETag` (hash of version, locale and query), `Last-Modified` and a configurable `Cache-Control` (default `public, max-age=60, stale-while-revalidate=300`). Output caching (decision 56) tags responses with `site:{id}`, `type:{site}:{type}` and `entry:{id}`; publish, unpublish and media events evict by tag and raise `CacheInvalidatedEvent` for webhook-driven CDN purges.

**As built.** A request bridge in `FluentCMS.Core.Api.Common`, shared with the Management API, sets the `OperationContext`. It reads the principal from the `FluentCms` scheme that `AddFluentCms()` registers, which goes to the API key handler when a key is sent and otherwise to the configured bearer scheme, never to the host's default. A host without that scheme uses the request's user. A request without one is the anonymous principal. An authenticated principal without a usable `fcms_kind` or `sub`, or one whose credentials the scheme refuses, is 401 (`FCMS3102`), never anonymous. The site follows 4.6. A key bound to one site reads it. A key bound to several reads the one named, or the site of the request's host name when it is among them, which is the default site unless a host resolver names another (4.6), and is otherwise refused (`FCMS3122`, 400). A named site outside the binding is 403 (`FCMS3121`) whether it exists or not, and an `X-Cms-Site` header and a `site` parameter that name different sites are refused (`FCMS2201`). Delivery reads drafts only with the `preview` or `draft` scope, even for a principal whose `management` scope lets the engine read them. A preview token (4.7) is taken by `GET /{type}/{id}` with `status=draft` only: the list, `by/`, another type or entry, a read without `status=draft` and, for a token bound to a locale, another locale or `locale=all` are refused with `FCMS3028` (403), whose `errors[].path` is `id`, `type`, `status` or `locale`; another site is `FCMS3121` as for a bound key. A draft read is `no-store`, and the output cache takes no request that carries a preview token whatever it asks for, so no cache keeps what a token read, its populated targets included. An unknown type and one the principal cannot read are the same 404 (`FCMS3104` on `type`). A problem's `errors[].path` names the parameter the client sent (`filter[publishedOn][gt]`, `limit`, or `field` and `value` on `by/`), not the field the engine reports. `by/{field}` needs a unique field (`FCMS3123`), which is checked before the query runs, so a field that is not unique costs no query; a field the principal may not read is refused as the unknown field the query would report (`FCMS2202`), so that no field is shown to exist by its error. Under `locale=all` an entry is `{ "id", "locales": { code: entry } }`, and a type that is not localized has one entry, keyed by the empty string, as its `locale` field is.

**Caching as built.** The `ETag` is a hash of the body, which holds the version and locale of every entry, populated ones included, and follows from the query, so it also changes when a populated target or what the caller may read changes. There is no `Last-Modified`, and only `If-None-Match` decides a 304: no date in the body moves when an entry leaves a list, a populated target is unpublished or a role changes what the caller may read, so a date would let a client or a CDN keep a stale copy. `If-Modified-Since` is ignored, as RFC 9110 (13.1.3) asks of a resource with no modification date, and the Delivery policy takes it off the request before the output cache looks, which would otherwise answer it by the date it stored the copy; such a request is answered in full, from the cache when it holds a copy, rather than running the endpoint each time. `FluentCms:Delivery:Cache:CacheControl` is sent as written to a published read without credentials, in its `private` form to one with a key or a token (a CDN keys by URL, not by credentials), `no-store` on a draft read, and an error keeps the exception handler's `no-cache,no-store`; responses vary on `Authorization`, `X-Api-Key`, `X-Cms-Site` and `X-Cms-Locale`. The three routes answer `HEAD` as `GET` without the body, `Content-Length` included, and the output cache keeps a `HEAD` apart from its `GET`. `UseFluentCms()` adds the output cache last, after the rate limiter, authentication and authorization, so a policy the host puts on `MapFluentCms()` holds for a cached answer too, and the Delivery group carries its policy: one copy for every request without credentials and one per API key, keyed by a hash of its claims (role, sites, scope), for `OutputCacheDuration` (60 seconds; `OutputCache`, `cms.DeliveryCache` in code, turns it off, and with it the evictions and their event unless `CacheTagHeader` is set). The key also holds the schema hash, as the OpenAPI document's does, so a store that outlives the process or that instances on another schema share serves none of them a copy made for another schema. Beside the host name, which the framework puts in the key, it holds the site that host name resolves to (4.6, B-80), so the copies of two sites are apart whatever their host names, and a host name that moves to another site reads none of the copies it left, on the instance that moved it at once and before any eviction has run. That site is resolved once for the request and the endpoint reads it, so a copy is never stored under a site other than the one it holds. The schema is built once per process, so a schema change is a new process: the in-memory store starts empty, and the hash keeps a shared one right during a rolling deployment, where evicting at start would not, since an instance still on the old schema would write old copies again. A bearer token, a preview token, a draft read, refused credentials and anything but a 200 are not cached, and the other FluentCMS groups say so, so a host's base policy leaves them alone. Response compression goes before `UseFluentCms()`: the cache then keeps the body the endpoint wrote and compression encodes each answer for its request, so the key need not vary on `Accept-Encoding`. Compression inside the cache would keep a body encoded for one client, so a response that has a `Content-Encoding` while no compression middleware wraps the cache is not kept, by the Delivery and the OpenAPI policies alike. A cached read is tagged `site:{id}`, `type:{site}:{type}` (`type:global:{type}` for a global type, whose entries every site serves) and `entry:{id}` for every entry in the body, populated ones and references included. An outbox handler evicts `entry:{id}` and the type's tag on `EntryPublishedEvent`, `EntryUnpublishedEvent`, `EntryTrashedEvent` and `EntryRestoredEvent`, and `site:{id}` on `SiteUpdatedEvent`, which a change to the site or to one of its locales raises (4.6), since a site's locales decide its fallbacks, and raises `CacheInvalidatedEvent` with those tags. A change to a role or its grants evicts `site:{id}` the same way, because the Public role decides what the shared copy holds and a key's role what its own holds: `RoleVersionInterceptor`, which raises the role's permissions version in the same `SaveChanges`, stages an internal `RolePermissionsChangedEvent` in the outbox there, with the role's site, or every site for a role of the instance, so that `RoleService` and anything else that writes roles through `CmsDbContext` evict with the commit, and a crash after it does not leave the copies. A newly published relation target shows when the copy expires. The store is the provider slot `IOutputCacheStore` (4.13), the framework's in-memory store by default, which the startup report lists; an eviction reaches only the store of the instance that delivers the event, so with several instances on the in-memory store the others serve their copies until they expire, and a shared store, such as Redis, evicts for all. Lookups count in `fluentcms.cache.requests` as cache `delivery`. This answers items 6, 7, 13 and 15 of [#2860](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/2860) of the review of 7 Nov 2026.

**Cache tag header as built.** `FluentCms:Delivery:Cache:CacheTagHeader`, unset by default, names a header that carries a published read's tags for a CDN that purges by tag: `Cache-Tag` (Cloudflare), `Surrogate-Key` (Fastly), `Edge-Cache-Tag` (Akamai) or any other HTTP token; a name that is not a token, or one the response writes itself (`Cache-Control`, `ETag`, `Vary`, `Set-Cookie` and the like), stops the host with `FCMS1101`. A published read, anonymous or with a key, from the cache or not, a 304 included, carries the tags the output cache gives it, within the budget below: `site:{id}`, `type:{site}:{type}` or `type:global:{type}`, and `entry:{id}` for every entry in the body; the media route carries `site:{id}`, `media:{id}` and `media-presets:{library}`. A draft read is `no-store` and carries none, and without the option no response carries them. The tags are separated by a space for `Surrogate-Key`, as Fastly reads it, and by a comma for any other name. Every `CacheInvalidatedEvent` names tags of that same set, and the evictions and their event run while either the output cache or the header is on, since a CDN holds what the header tagged though the server kept nothing. The purge is the webhooks plugin's (5.2): a webhook on `cache.invalidated` receives `data.tags`, and a receiver checks the signature and calls the CDN's purge by tag, for Cloudflare `POST /client/v4/zones/{zone}/purge_cache` with `{ "tags": [...] }`. The core takes no CDN dependency and sends no purge itself. This answers [#2935](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/2935).

**Tags of resolved targets as built** ([#3109](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3109)). A Delivery read also carries, on its copy in the output cache and, within its budget (below), in the cache tag header, the `CacheTags` of every target of another kind it resolved (4.4). An asset of a media field, a block or a rich text node carries those of the media route, `media:{id}` and `media-presets:{library}`, so the outbox handlers that evict the route's copy when an asset changes, turns private, moves, is replaced or is deleted, and a preset's change, evict the entries that show it too, and raise `CacheInvalidatedEvent` with those tags for a CDN. Core keeps only tags of visible ASCII without spaces or commas, which the header can carry. A target that becomes readable shows in a copy that held it out when the copy expires, as a newly published relation target does.

**Tag header budget as built** ([#3113](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3113)). The header had no cap, so a page of 100 entries could pass the 16 KB that Cloudflare and Fastly take and the 4 KB in which nginx buffers a response's headers by default (`proxy_buffer_size`), which then answers 502. Its value is held to 2,048 bytes (`CacheTags.HeaderBudget`; every tag is ASCII): the Delivery API's other headers (`Content-Type`, `Content-Length`, `Date`, `ETag`, `Cache-Control`, `Vary`, the rate limit and security headers) take about 500 bytes, so the status line and every header fit nginx's 4 KB with room left for a host's own (CORS, HSTS, a request id), and the budget holds the site's and type's tags with some 45 entries'. Within it the header lists every tag, as before. Past it, the tags of the entries give way to the type tag of every content type the read shows entries of: its own, and that of every relation target and entry link the caller reads in it, at every level, which the query engine records in a scoped `TargetTypes` as it asks whether the caller may read them, as `ReferenceResolvers` records the resolvers' tags (a type the caller cannot read, or none of whose targets it reads, is not named); the site's tag and the tags of resolved targets stay. A publish, unpublish, trash or restore evicts the entry's type with the entry, so every change that purged the copy by an entry still purges it, and a change to any other entry of those types purges it too. A forced delete of a referenced asset (`IContentReferences.RemoveAsync`) evicts the entries' tags alone, so with the header on its `CacheInvalidatedEvent` also names the tags of their types; the server's store is still evicted by the entries' tags only, since its copies keep every tag. When even those tags pass the budget, as for a read that resolves fifty assets, no set the header can hold reaches every change, so the header names only the site and the read is sent with the `private` form of `CacheControl`, as a read with credentials is: a browser keeps it until it expires, and no CDN stores a copy it could not purge. The copy in the output cache keeps every tag whatever the header holds, so the server evicts it as precisely as before. The budget is a constant, not an option, and the media route, whose header holds three tags, is unchanged.

**Round trips as built.** Before its content queries a request used to read the default site or the named one, a site's default locale and its locales (once per lookup), the site's Public role by name or an API key's row a second time, and the role stamps. Now the site and its locales come from `SiteCache` (4.6), the Public role and the grants from the cache keyed by the stamps (4.8), and a key's role from the row its authentication read. A warm anonymous Delivery read of a public entry sends the stamp of the Public role and its content queries only (for a type with a relation: the entry with its document, and its references), three queries where it sent nine, and one with an API key adds the key's lookup by the handler, four where it sent ten (`DeliveryRoundTripTests`). The stamp read stays because it is what makes a role change visible on the next request on every instance; a change to an existing site or its locales reaches another instance within 30 seconds (#2857).

**Plugin Delivery groups as built.** A plugin mounts a group under `/api/delivery/v1` with `MapDelivery(prefix)` (4.13), beside `MapManagement`. Its endpoints get what core's Delivery group has, applied by the same code: the Delivery surface, the output cache policy, rate limit and CORS policy (`GET` and `HEAD`), the FluentCMS authentication with an anonymous caller read as its site's Public role, the request bridge's site and locale, and a place in the OpenAPI document. A copy of an answer is tagged with its site only, so it goes when the site is updated, a role changes or the plugin is disabled in the site (an outbox handler evicts `site:{id}` on `PluginDisabledEvent`, since a copy is served before the plugin's per-site gate); an endpoint whose answer changes on anything else opts out with `CacheOutput(policy => policy.NoCache())`, and sets its own `Cache-Control`. A prefix whose first segment is a route parameter or the name of a content type would take core's `/{type}` routes and stops the host (`FCMS1524`), and `MapGroup` still refuses a raw prefix under a group of core. The media plugin's route of the table above is `GET /api/delivery/v1/media/{id}`, mapped with `MapDelivery("/media")`; it sets the `ETag`, `Cache-Control` and `Vary` of content itself and tags its copies `media:{id}` and `media-presets:{library}` beside the site's, which its own outbox handlers evict (5.1, B-66). This answers [#2888](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/2888) of the review of 7 Nov 2026.

**Management API.** Under `/api/management/v1`, bearer or management key:

| Group | Routes |
| --- | --- |
| content | `/content/{type}` list and create; `/content/{type}/{id}` get, patch, delete; `/publish`, `/unpublish`, `/discard`, `/schedule`; `/versions`, `/versions/{n}/restore`, `/versions/{a}/diff/{b}`; `/content/{type}/bulk`; `/content/{type}/trash` list, `/trash/{id}/restore`, `/trash/{id}` purge |
| schema | `/schema`, `/schema/types/{type}`, `/schema/snapshot` |
| identity | `/users`, `/users/{id}/sessions`, `/users/{id}/two-factor/reset`, `/invitations`, `/roles`, `/permissions` (catalogue), `/permissions/row-rules`, `/api-keys`, `/members`, `/member-groups` |
| sites | `/sites`, `/sites/{id}/locales`; `/sites/{id}/hosts` (multi-site plugin, 5.6) |
| settings | `/settings` lists the areas; `/settings/{area}` and `/settings/{area}/defaults` get, put and delete (reset), with the JSON schema of the settings type |
| jobs | `/jobs`, `/jobs/{id}`, `/jobs/{id}/retry`, `/jobs/{id}/cancel`, `/jobs/dead-letter` |
| events | `/events/dead-letter` list and purge by handler, `/events/dead-letter/{id}` remove, `/events/dead-letter/{id}/retry` |
| activity | `/activity` |
| plugins | `/plugins`, `/plugins/{name}/sites/{site}` enable and disable |
| transfer | `/transfer/export` (job, then download), `/transfer/import` (upload, dry run, apply) |
| system | `/system/diagnostics`, `/system/health` (detailed; `system.read`) |
| me | `/me`, `/me/sites`, `/me/access`, `/me/preferences`, `/me/sessions`, `/me/two-factor` |

**Content group as built.** The routes need a bearer token or a key with the `management` scope: a request that is not signed in is 401 and one without the scope, such as a Delivery key, is 403; what the principal may then do is the content service's to decide, through the request bridge as on Delivery. `GET /content/{type}` and `GET /content/{type}/{id}` take the Delivery query grammar and read the draft unless `status=published` is asked. Every entry the group answers carries the pending schedules of its locale (4.4). `POST /content/{type}` creates and answers 201 with `Location`, which keeps the `site` parameter the request named; `PUT /content/{type}/{id}` replaces the draft (`UpdateAsync`) and `PATCH` applies a JSON merge patch, both in the request's locale, where `If-Match: "0"` adds a locale the entry does not have. `DELETE` moves the entry to the trash. `POST .../publish` answers `{ "entry", "warnings" }`, `.../unpublish` and `.../discard` the entry; `POST .../schedule` takes `{ "action": "publish" | "unpublish", "at", "timeZone" }`, where `at` has an offset without a zone (`2027-03-01T09:00:00Z`, an instant) and is the wall-clock time there with one (`2027-03-01T09:00:00` with `Europe/Berlin`), and the other form is refused (`FCMS2108` on `at`), and `DELETE .../schedule/{action}` cancels. `GET .../versions` lists `{ "items" }` newest first, each with `number`, `locale`, `status`, `authorId`, `authorKind`, `authorName`, `origin`, `clientId`, `message` and `createdAt`, `GET .../versions/{n}` reads one, `POST .../versions/{n}/restore` restores it and `GET .../versions/{a}/diff/{b}` answers `{ "from", "to", "changes" }`, all in one locale. `POST /content/{type}/bulk` takes the list's `filter[...]` parameters in the query string and `{ "operation", "entries", "items", "cap", "patch" }` in the body, and answers `{ "items", "cancelled" }` with an entry's status, id, version, warnings and, for one that failed, the problem the single call would have answered. `POST .../preview-token` mints a preview token for the entry and answers it with the type's preview URL, from the settings area `preview` (4.7). `GET /content/{type}/trash` lists the trash with the query grammar, `POST /trash/{id}/restore` restores and `DELETE /trash/{id}` purges. A save needs `If-Match` with one tag, `"7"` (`FCMS3130`, 428, when it is missing; `FCMS3131`, 400, for `*`, a list or anything else); publish, discard and version restore take it optionally, with `*` as none. The tag is strong, but a weak one, `W/"7"`, is read by its opaque value: a proxy that compresses responses, such as nginx with `gzip on`, weakens the `ETag` it passes on, and a client that echoes it would be refused on every save. This departs from RFC 9110, whose `If-Match` compares strongly and so never matches a weak tag; the tag names a version rather than the bytes of a representation, so a weakened one still names it. A response that holds a draft gives its version as `ETag`, unless a value came through the fallback chain, which can carry another locale's version, and a conflict is 412 (`FCMS3105`) with the current draft in `current`, in the response shape. A body is a JSON object that names a key once, read before the engine sees it (400 when it cannot be, 415 and `FCMS3107` for another media type), and a key a schedule or bulk body does not use is refused (`FCMS2108`). An unknown type is 404 on every route, and one the principal cannot read is the same 404 on a read; a write leaves it to the content service, since a principal may write without reading. A write and a read of versions take only `site` and `locale` from the query string, bulk its filter too, and `locale=all` is refused where one locale is needed. `IfMatch` in `FluentCMS.Core.Api.Common` is for the other groups that take a version.

**Idempotency keys as built** ([#2933](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/2933)). `POST /content/{type}` and `POST /content/{type}/bulk` take an optional `Idempotency-Key` header: one value of 1 to 255 visible ASCII characters (`!` to `~`), compared exactly, or 400 (`FCMS3140`). A key belongs to the principal (kind and id) and the site of the request, so two callers never share one. It is kept in `cms_idempotency_key` as its SHA-256, with a SHA-256 of the request (method, path, query string, asked locale, content type, principal, site and body) and the response. The first request claims the key by inserting its row, so of two that send it at once one runs and the unique index refuses the other, on every database; a key already in use is answered from its row without a write. A repeat of the same request answers the stored status, `Content-Type`, `Location`, `ETag` and body with `Idempotent-Replayed: true`, and runs nothing. The same key with another request is 422 (`FCMS3141`), and a repeat while the first still runs is 409 (`FCMS3142`). Only a response below 500 is kept: a request that is refused with a problem, or fails, releases the key, so a corrected or later retry runs (a request whose client went away before its work answered keeps it until the lease ends, since its work may have committed), while a bulk request answers 200 when items fail, and that answer is kept. The route's own checks (type, parameters, locale) come first, so a malformed request claims nothing. `FluentCms:Idempotency:Window` (24 hours, at most 30 days) is how long a key is kept from its first request; a key past it is claimed afresh, and the recurring job `idempotency.retention` (hourly at minute 41) removes expired rows, but neither while a request still holds the key's lease, so a window shorter than a request never lets a repeat run beside it: a repeat, or another request with the key, is 409 (`FCMS3142`) until the request answers, and once it has, the expired key is claimed afresh and the same request runs again, which the 409's message says ([#3523](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3523)). A takeover matches the lease it read, so a renewal between its read and its update makes it lose and answer `FCMS3142`. A request whose instance stops before it answers holds the key until `InProgressTimeout` (5 minutes, at most the window) runs out; a repeat then takes it over and runs (event 9140). The work and the stored response are not one transaction, so a response that cannot be stored (event 9141) leaves the key held until the lease ends, and a repeat then runs again. The Management CORS policy allows the header and exposes `Idempotent-Replayed`, and the OpenAPI document lists both on the two routes. While a request runs, its lease is renewed every third of `InProgressTimeout`, so a request that runs longer is not taken over; a renewal that fails is tried again (event 9144). Should renewals fail for a whole lease and a repeat take the key over, the next renewal finds the key in the repeat's hands (event 9145) and cancels the request, whose uncommitted write rolls back and which answers `FCMS3142`; a request whose work had already answered, its write committed, as when the key is lost while the entry is read back, answers what its work answered instead (201 with the entry, or the bulk result, without `Idempotent-Replayed`), so its client sees the write. That request keeps nothing under the key and frees nothing, since the repeat holds it. A renewal whose row is gone, removed past its window once its lease ran out, puts the claim back with the same id and owner: when the unique index refuses it, another request has the key and the request is cancelled as above; otherwise the request holds the key again (event 9146), a repeat is 409 meanwhile, and its response is kept as usual ([#3523](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3523)). On SQLite neither the renewal nor a takeover writes while the request holds the writer, so there the key is lost only between its transactions ([#3307](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3307)). An answer is kept after the client went away only when the work answered before it went, its token not cancelled, so that the answer is of work that ran whole ([#3485](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3485)). A client that goes away while that answer is being written does not stop it being kept: the answer is written to memory, which needs no client, so it is captured and stored as for a client that stayed, and a retry with the key is answered it with `Idempotent-Replayed: true` and runs nothing. When the client went away before the work answered, nothing is kept and the key stays held until its lease ends, as before: the work stopped on its token, or answered cut short, as a bulk does when it reports the items after the cancellation as `notRun` with `"cancelled": true` and 200, and a single create whose client went away after its commit but before the service returned is among these, since nothing tells the two apart there; a retry after the lease runs the request again, the bulk's items that ran included. The capture is bounded: once the client has gone it runs for `InProgressTimeout` at most; an answer that takes longer is not kept (event 9147, which no endpoint of FluentCMS gives, since each answer is a value written at once). A request whose client went away while its answer was written can so hold its key for up to twice `InProgressTimeout`: the capture's bound, with the lease renewed meanwhile, and then a lease.

**Identity, sites, settings, jobs, events, activity, plugins and me as built.** These groups, and the system group (4.15), need the `management` scope as the content group does. The routes read the request, and the in-process services check the permission through `ManagementAuthority` (`FluentCMS.Core.Authorization`), so a caller without it is 403 (`FCMS3103`), or 401 when not signed in. A permission for the instance is one that no site limits: a role of a site, a grant limited to sites and a key bound to sites do not hold it. Paged lists take `page` and `pageSize` (50, at most 100) and answer `{ "items", "total", "page", "pageSize" }`, other lists `{ "items" }`. A query parameter a route does not take is refused (`FCMS2201`), and so is a body key (`FCMS2108`). No caller hands out more than it holds: a role, a user's role or a key's role must give nothing beyond the caller's own grants, compared grant by grant with the pattern, sites, locales, fields and row rule (a grant that reaches several sites may be held through a different grant in each).

- Users, with `users.manage` for the instance, since a user belongs to no site: `GET /users` (`search` on user name and email), `GET` and `PATCH /users/{id}` (`userName`, `email`, `mustChangePassword`, which is refused for a user without a password), `POST /users/{id}/deactivate` and `/reactivate`, `PUT` and `DELETE /users/{id}/roles/{roleId}`, `GET /users/{id}/sessions` and `DELETE /users/{id}/sessions/{sessionId}` through `UserSessions`, `GET /users/{id}/logins` and `DELETE /users/{id}/logins/{provider}` (4.7), and `POST /users/{id}/two-factor/reset`, which answers the user and needs a caller signed in with a second factor (4.7). Changing, deactivating or reactivating a user, ending its session, removing its login or resetting its two-factor needs every grant of the user's roles too, and giving or taking a role every grant of that role. A user does not deactivate itself or reset its own two-factor. Deactivating the last active user who holds `*` with no constraints, or taking from it the role that grants it, is refused (`FCMS3052`, 4.7).
- Invitations: `GET /invitations` lists the pending ones, `POST /invitations` `{ "email", "roleIds" }` sends one linked to `/account/accept-invitation` on the request's origin, and `DELETE /invitations/{id}` revokes; `InvitationService` needs `users.manage` for the instance and every grant of each role named, for revoking as for sending (#3262). Since a new invitation to an address ends the pending ones to it, matched by the normalized address, sending one also needs every grant of the roles of those that could still be accepted, read in the transaction that ends exactly the rows it read, and is refused with 403 otherwise; an expired one is ended unchecked. Sending and revoking check `users.manage` and every grant of the roles again in the transaction of the write, the roles of the new invitation and of those it ends when sending, with the caller's grants read there and a calling key read again as for a change of a key, so that grants cut, or a key revoked, rotated or narrowed, after the checks before the transaction refuse it with 403 and nothing is written or mailed; revoking reads the row, checks and writes in that one transaction ([#3481](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3481)). `GET /invitations` leaves out an invitation that accept would refuse for its sender, by the check accept and the accept page make (4.7), made once for each sender and set of roles; it only reads, so such a row is neither revoked nor changed, and the list has no paging or count that a row left out could put off (#3391).
- Roles, with `roles.manage` where the role applies (the instance for an instance-wide role, its site otherwise): `GET /roles` and `GET /roles/{id}` (also for `users.manage`, `apikeys.manage` and `members.manage`), `POST /roles` `{ "name", "description", "siteId", "requireTwoFactor", "grants": [{ "permission", "constraints" }] }`, `PUT /roles/{id}`, which replaces the name, the description and the grants, and `requireTwoFactor` when the body gives it, and `DELETE /roles/{id}`. A grant names a pattern the catalogue matches, sites (only its own for a role of a site), locales, fields and a registered row rule, the rule only beside a content permission and for the types it is registered for (4.8, [#3443](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3443)), and an empty list is refused. What a change adds and what it replaces or deletes must both be within the caller's reach. Built-in roles keep their names and are not deleted, and of Super Admin only `requireTwoFactor` changes (`FCMS3043`).
