An edit that drops `*` with no constraints from an instance-wide role is refused when it would leave no active user who holds it (`FCMS3052`, 4.8). `RoleService` writes through `CmsDbContext`, whose interceptor raises the permissions version, and deletes a role in one statement only while no user holds it, no key that is not revoked uses it and no member group is bound to it (`FCMS3044`), so the cascade drops no holder without its `UserRoleChangedEvent`, no live key without `api_key.revoked` and no group without its event. `role.created`, `role.updated` and `role.deleted` are activity rows of the role's site. `GET /permissions` lists the catalogue. `GET /permissions/row-rules` lists the registered row rules a grant's `rowRule` may name, for the same callers and by the same check as `GET /permissions` (`roles.manage`, `users.manage`, `apikeys.manage` or `members.manage` in the site of the request, 403 `FCMS3103` otherwise): `{ "items": [{ "name", "typeNames" }] }`, by name, with the content types each rule is registered for by name, or `typeNames: null` for a rule on `ContentItem`, which holds for every type, as `own-entries` does. It names the rules only, nothing of what they compare, and is a route of its own so that the answer of `GET /permissions` stays as it was ([#3417](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3417)).
- API keys: `GET /api-keys`, `GET /api-keys/{id}`, `POST /api-keys` `{ "name", "class", "roleId", "siteIds", "typeNames", "expiresAt", "rateLimit" }`, which answers 201 with the key and its `secret` once, `DELETE /api-keys/{id}`, which revokes and keeps the row, `POST /api-keys/{id}/expire` `{ "at" }`, `POST /api-keys/{id}/rotate` `{ "overlapSeconds" }` (optional), which answers the key and its new `secret` once, and `PUT /api-keys/{id}/allowed-addresses` `{ "allowedAddresses" }` (4.7). `POST /api-keys` also takes `allowedAddresses`. `ApiKeyService` needs `apikeys.manage` for the instance for a key bound to no site and in each of its sites otherwise; a key out of reach is 404 and left out of the list, and a new key's role, as its binding narrows it, holds only what the caller holds, so a key bound to sites mints no key that reaches further. Rotating, revoking and expiring a key need the same of the key's role, so a caller below it neither takes its secret nor cuts off its clients (#3262). Creating a key checks `apikeys.manage` for the sites it names, or in the site of the request when `siteIds` is empty, before whether its role and sites exist, so a caller without the permission gets 403 for real and made-up ids alike (#3297). Revoking a key, or expiring it now, also revokes the invitations it sent that are still pending; an expiry set later leaves them to the check at accept. Each change of a key, creating, rotating, revoking, expiring and setting its addresses, checks `apikeys.manage` where the key reaches and the ceiling of its role again in the transaction of the change, before its write, with the caller's grants read there rather than taken from what the request resolved before, and a calling key is read there again too, since the request holds the row it authenticated; the checks before the transaction stay, so a caller whose grants did not change gets the same answers in the same order. The calling key is held there to what the authentication handler would take now ([#3483](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3483)): the secret the request presented must still be the key's current one, or the one a rotation replaced while its overlap lasts, and the request's client address must still be allowed by the key's `allowedAddresses`; a key that no handler verified for the request, as in a command or a job, has no secret or address to compare. A change of the caller's grants, or a revocation, expiry, rotation without overlap or narrowing of the addresses of the calling key, committed before that transaction opens refuses the change with 403, with one message whatever the cause; on PostgreSQL and SQL Server, at read committed, one committed after the check and before the write is not seen, as with any check of grants (#3391).
- Members, with `members.manage` for the instance, which is apart from `users.manage`: `GET /members` (`search`, `group`, `verified`, `active`, with paging), `GET /members/{id}`, `POST /members` `{ "email", "displayName" }`, which makes a member without a password whose address is not verified and mails it a link to choose one (409 `FCMS3173` while the link has nowhere to go, 4.7), `PATCH /members/{id}` `{ "displayName" }`, `POST /members/{id}/deactivate` and `/reactivate`, `DELETE /members/{id}`, `GET /members/{id}/sessions` and `DELETE /members/{id}/sessions/{sessionId}`, `GET /members/{id}/logins`, which lists the member's external logins as `{ "provider", "displayName" }`, and `DELETE /members/{id}/logins/{provider}`, which removes one and ends the member's sessions (404 `FCMS3185` when it has none, 409 `FCMS3184` for its last way to sign in, 4.7), and `PUT` and `DELETE /members/{id}/groups/{groupId}`. `search` finds the members whose display name or address holds the text, in any case, with `%`, `_` and `[` taken as they are ([#3012](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3012)): the address in its normalized form, and the display name as the database compares text without regard to case, through `IDataProvider.ContainsIgnoringCase`: `LIKE` on SQLite, which folds ASCII letters only, `ILIKE` on PostgreSQL, and `LIKE` under `Latin1_General_100_CI_AS` on SQL Server, whatever the default of the database. A match on a part of a text can use no B-tree index, so none was added and a search reads every member the other filters leave. Deactivating and deleting end the member's sessions. A member is `{ "id", "email", "emailVerified", "displayName", "createdAt", "lastLoginAt", "deactivatedAt", "lockoutEnd", "groups" }`. `GET`, `POST`, `PUT` and `DELETE` on `/member-groups` and `/member-groups/{id}` take `{ "name", "description", "roleId" }`. A group hands the grants of its role to its members, so creating a group, changing one (with the role it has, since a new name changes what a row rule gives its members) and putting a member in one need every grant of that role, as handing a role to a user does (403 `FCMS3103`). Deleting a group and taking a member out of one need them as well and are refused the same way. So the membership of a group whose role holds no grant is managed with `members.manage` alone, in both directions, and a row rule that must hold against such callers, a negated one above all, reads a group whose role holds a grant they lack (4.8). Each change raises its event and is an activity row of no site (4.7).
- Sites, with `sites.manage` for the site: `GET /sites`, `GET` and `PATCH /sites/{id}` (`name`, and `defaultLocale`, an enabled locale of the site; `SiteUpdatedEvent`), and `GET`, `POST` `{ "code", "position", "enabled", "fallbacks" }`, `PATCH` and `DELETE` on `/sites/{id}/locales/{code}` (`SiteUpdatedEvent`, 4.6), where a fallback is another locale of the site and `fallbacks: null` is the implicit chain. The default locale stays enabled and is not removed, and a locale with entries, trashed ones included, is disabled rather than removed (`FCMS3136`). A locale that another locale of the site names in its fallbacks, enabled or not, is not removed either (`FCMS3143`, 409, with one error for each such locale at `locales.{code}.fallbacks`), so a removal changes no chain; a locale with the implicit chain names none ([#3517](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3517)). `site.locale-added`, `site.locale-updated` and `site.locale-removed` are activity rows of the site. Creating and deleting sites, and their host names, are the multi-site plugin's routes, with `sites.manage` for the instance (5.6).
- Settings: `GET` and `PUT /settings/{area}` for the site of the request and `/settings/{area}/defaults` for the instance defaults, with `settings.{area}.manage` there for both, since the read is what the admin edits. A read answers `{ "area", "siteId", "source", "version", "value", "secretsSet", "secretsInherited", "schema" }` from the redacted resolve, with the version as `ETag`; a write takes the whole document and needs `If-Match` with the version read (`"0"` for no row), and a row that has moved is `FCMS2808` (412). `DELETE /settings/{area}` and `DELETE /settings/{area}/defaults` reset the area ([#3098](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3098)): the row goes, so the level below shows through again and a site inherits every secret of the defaults again (4.13). A reset needs `settings.{area}.manage` there and `If-Match`, as a write does, and answers 204. With `"0"` and no row it changes nothing and raises nothing; a row that has moved, or a version other than `"0"` when there is no row, is `FCMS2808` (412). It raises `SettingsChangedEvent`, so its activity row is `settings.changed`, as for a write. `GET /settings` lists `{ "area", "plugin", "site", "defaults", "instanceOnly" }` for each area the caller may manage in the site of the request (`site`) or for the instance defaults (`defaults`), by area name, with the plugin that registered it (null for core's and the host's) and whether it is read from the instance defaults only, as its registration says (`instanceOnly`, [#3499](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3499)), so that the admin can draw its settings menu; an area the caller may manage nowhere is left out, and a caller with no settings permission gets an empty list. Neither it nor the routes of an area look at whether the area's plugin is enabled in the site, which the admin reads from `/plugins`. An area core keeps for its own state is not listed (4.13).
- Jobs, with `jobs.manage` for the instance: `GET /jobs` (`status`, `type`, `queue`), `GET /jobs/{id}` with its attempts as `history` and never its payload, `GET /jobs/dead-letter`, `POST /jobs/{id}/retry` for a dead-lettered job (`FCMS3133` otherwise) and `POST /jobs/{id}/cancel` for a pending one (`FCMS3134` otherwise; a running job is left to finish). A cancelled job is dead-lettered with its `JobDeadLetteredEvent` in one transaction, so a schedule it carried closes as for any dead letter, and it can be retried.
- Events, with `jobs.manage` for the instance, as for jobs, since an error can name internals of the host whatever site its event came from: `GET /events/dead-letter` lists the failed outbox deliveries, newest first, as `{ "id", "eventId", "eventType", "handler", "attempts", "lastError", "occurredAt" }` and never the event. `POST /events/dead-letter/{id}/retry` queues one again with fresh attempts and `DELETE /events/dead-letter/{id}` removes it, both answering 204; a delivery that is not failed is `FCMS3139` (409) and an unknown one `FCMS3138` (404). `DELETE /events/dead-letter?handler={name}` removes every failed delivery to that handler, in batches, and answers `{ "handler", "removed" }`; without `handler` it is `FCMS2101`. `EventAdministration` checks the permission and writes the activity rows, of no site: `event_delivery.retried` and `event_delivery.removed` with the target type `event_delivery`, the delivery's id and the handler as label, and `event_delivery.purged` with the target type `event_handler` and the count, when it removed any.
- Activity: `GET /activity` with `actorId`, `actorKind`, `origin`, `action` (a trailing `*` for a prefix), `targetType`, `targetId`, and `from` and `to` with an offset, needs `activity.read` in the site of the request, and adds the rows of no site only for `activity.read` for the instance.
- Plugins: `GET /plugins` lists every plugin in plugin order with `name`, `version`, `sdkRange`, `dependsOn`, `enabled` for the site of the request, `contributions` (`fieldTypes`, the `permissions` of its permission providers, the `endpoints` of its groups as `{ "method", "route" }`, `jobs`, and `tools`, the names of the MCP tools it offers the caller in that site, 5.4) and `sites`, its enablement `{ "siteId", "slug", "enabled" }` in each site where the caller holds `plugins.manage`. Any caller with the `management` scope reads it, because the admin hides a disabled plugin's UI for every user. `PUT /plugins/{name}/sites/{site}` `{ "enabled" }` needs `plugins.manage` for that site and answers `{ "siteId", "slug", "enabled" }`; an unknown plugin is `FCMS1551` and an unknown site `FCMS2301`, both 404 (4.13).
- Me: `GET /me` is the caller, and for a user also its user name, email, `mustChangePassword`, `twoFactorEnabled` and roles; `GET /me/sites` lists the sites in which the caller holds a grant (4.6); `GET /me/sessions` and `DELETE /me/sessions/{id}` are a user's own sessions. `GET /me/two-factor` is `{ "enabled", "required", "pending", "recoveryCodesLeft" }`, `POST /me/two-factor/enrol` answers `{ "sharedKey", "authenticatorUri" }` (an `otpauth` URI named after the brand of the hosted pages), `POST /me/two-factor/verify` `{ "code" }` turns two-factor on and `POST /me/two-factor/recovery-codes` `{ "code" }` replaces the codes, both answering `{ "recoveryCodes" }`, and `POST /me/two-factor/disable` `{ "code" }` turns it off (4.7); `enrol` and `verify` answer `FCMS3065` (403) to a token whose sign-in is more than ten minutes old; `GET /me/logins` and `DELETE /me/logins/{provider}` are its external logins (4.7); a key or a service account is 403. *Access and preferences as built* ([#3096](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3096)). `GET /me/access` is what the caller may do in the site of the request, for the admin's sidebar, buttons and forms: `{ "site", "locale", "permissions", "instancePermissions", "limitedPermissions", "search", "types" }`. `permissions` and `instancePermissions` are the permissions of the catalogue that are not generated per type, core's, the settings areas' and the plugins', that the caller holds in the site and for the instance through a grant with no resource limit, as a core route asks, so a key bound to sites holds none for the instance and one bound to types holds only their content permissions. `limitedPermissions` are those it holds in the site only through grants limited to resources, which a plugin narrows call by call and a core route refuses. A plugin disabled in the site answers 404 there, so its permissions are left out of both lists of the site. `types` maps each content type the caller may read in the site and locale to what `IEffectiveAccess.ContentAsync` describes for the MCP tool list (4.8): `drafts`, `create`, `update`, `delete`, `publish`, `unpublish`, `creatableFields` and `updatableFields` (readable fields only, in schema order), and `conditional`, the operations among those that a single entry may still refuse: those no grant gives for every entry, since each grant behind them has a row rule or a locale limit, and a create or an update whose listed fields such a grant widens, since the service lets those fields through on some entries only. A create with a row rule counts as conditional although a new entry is not asked the rule, since adding a locale to an existing entry is. The answer comes from the evaluator the content service asks, through the role of an MCP client too, so it cannot say more than a call would be allowed; it holds no other principal's grants, no type the caller may not read and no field it may not read, and any caller with the `management` scope reads its own, a key and a service account included. Restoring and purging are not described, as the MCP tool list does not describe them. `GET`, `PUT` and `PATCH /me/preferences` keep a user's admin preferences in `cms_user_preference`, one row per user that goes with the user, so they follow it to every session and device: `{ "culture", "timeZone", "dateFormat", "numberFormat" }`, each null until chosen. `culture` is the interface culture, and `dateFormat` and `numberFormat` are the cultures whose conventions dates and numbers follow, each a culture the server has data for, not the invariant one, at most 32 characters and stored as the platform writes it (`de-DE` for `de-de`); `timeZone` is an IANA id the server knows, as a schedule takes (4.5), and only its exact id is taken: another case or a Windows id is refused, although the platform finds a zone it has already found in any case. A `PUT` replaces every value, one left out becomes null; a `PATCH` ([#3450](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3450)) changes the members its body names and keeps the others as they are stored, whatever the caller last read, with null for not chosen, as `fallbacks` in `PATCH /sites/{id}/locales/{code}`, and writes only those columns, so two changes of different preferences sent at once are both kept; it is answered the row as its update left it, read again in the update's transaction, which `IdentityChanges` opens as for the other identity tables, so a change of other members that landed after its read shows in the answer and its tag, and one that names no member writes nothing, not even an empty row. A refused value is a 422 on its path that keeps nothing. The read and both writes answer an `ETag`: the row has no version, so it is a hash of the four values (the first 16 bytes of their SHA-256, in hex), which changes with any of them and holds nothing but what the body shows. Both writes take an optional `If-Match` with it (one tag, weak or strong, or `*` for whatever is stored; anything else is `FCMS3131`, 400) and are refused with `FCMS3105` (412) once another write has changed the values, checked against the values in the update itself; without it they apply to whatever is stored, as before, so no caller has to send it and the API answers no 428 here. A 412 carries no `ETag` and no `current`, as a settings 412 does not, and the read answers no 304 to `If-None-Match`, as no Management route does. They are the admin's own settings, so they raise no event and write no activity row. A key or a service account is 403.

Codes `FCMS3040` to `FCMS3052`, `FCMS3132` to `FCMS3139` and `FCMS3143`; the member routes have `FCMS3160` to `FCMS3174` and `FCMS3184` to `FCMS3186` (4.7).

**Members API as built** ([#2992](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/2992)). `/api/members/v1` is a group of its own (`Members`), with the `auth` rate limit by client address, which the check before authentication counts as for `/connect` and `/account`, the CORS policy of `FluentCms:Security:Cors:Members:AllowedOrigins` (empty by default; `GET`, `HEAD`, `POST`, `PATCH` and `DELETE`, which removes an external login, with `Authorization` and `Content-Type`, and no credentials, so a browser sends no cookie), no output cache, and the surface `members`; it is not in the OpenAPI document. `/connect` and `/.well-known` answer the Members origins beside the Management ones. The routes read the request and `MemberAccountService` decides (4.7). `POST /register` `{ "email", "password", "displayName" }`, `POST /resend-verification` `{ "email" }` and `POST /forgot-password` `{ "email" }` answer 202 with no body whatever the address; `register` answers 404 (`FCMS3104`) while registration is off. `POST /verify-email` `{ "email", "token" }` and `POST /reset-password` `{ "email", "token", "password" }` answer 204, or 422 with `FCMS3166` for a token that is wrong, used or expired and for an unknown address alike. With a member's token, `GET /me` answers `{ "id", "email", "emailVerified", "displayName", "createdAt", "lastLoginAt", "groups" }` with `Cache-Control: no-store`, `PATCH /me` `{ "displayName" }` changes the display name (left out it stays, and an empty text clears it), and `POST /me/change-password` `{ "currentPassword", "newPassword" }` answers 204 and ends the member's sessions. `GET /me/logins` lists the member's external logins as `{ "items": [ { "provider", "displayName" } ] }` with `Cache-Control: no-store`, and `DELETE /me/logins/{provider}` removes one and ends the member's sessions, the token of the call included (404 `FCMS3185`, and 409 `FCMS3184` for the last way to sign in; #2993). Another caller is refused there: an admin user's token or a key with 403 (`FCMS3103`), no credentials with 401. A body is JSON (415 otherwise), a key the route does not take is refused, and a request that is wrong in itself is a 422: `FCMS3164` for an address that is none, `FCMS3165` for a password the policy refuses, `FCMS3167` for a wrong current password. A member's token on the Delivery API reads published content as far as its roles allow and is never cached: the output cache keeps a copy for a request without credentials and for an API key only, so a read that a row rule narrowed for one member is not served to another caller.

**Schema group as built.** `GET /schema`, `GET /schema/types/{type}` and `GET /schema/snapshot` need the `management` scope and `schema.read` in the site of the request, checked before any type is looked up (403, `FCMS3103`). `SchemaIntrospection` (`FluentCMS.Core.Schema`) then asks the evaluator what the caller may read in the site and locale of the request, which is what `TypeAccess` asks on a content route. A type it may not read is left out of `/schema`, and `/schema/types/{type}` answers it with the same 404 as an unknown type (`FCMS3104` on `type`). A field outside its allow-lists is left out, not blanked, and so is a relation to a type it may not read and the reverse side of a field that went, so that no name of such a type shows. A component or block that only what went used goes too, one that nothing uses stays, and a readable rich text field keeps every block, since an inline block may be of any. A condition or a slug source that reads a field that is left out goes too, so no name of such a field shows either. A condition goes whole, even when only one of its clauses reads such a field, because what is left of it would state a rule other than the one declared, and publishing still enforces a `requiredWhen` as declared. `/schema` answers `{ "types", "components", "blocks" }` and `/schema/types/{type}` one type, as the `SchemaDescription` records of the SDK, which a client can read back; `ISchemaIntrospection` there is the seam for the MCP plugin's schema resources. A type has `kind`, `localized` and `previewable`, and a collection or singleton also `scope`, `unpublishedReferences`, `maxVersions` and `trashRetentionDays`. A field has its flags, `formerNames`, `display`, `defaultValue`, `validation` (`required`, `unique`, the lengths, the range and `pattern`), `visibleWhen` and `requiredWhen` as `{ "operator", "field", "values", "operands" }`, `relation`, and the field type's `settings`, where a blocks field names its union in `blocks` and a select its `options`. Members that are null are left out. `previewable` is true for a type whose template of the settings area `preview` in the site fits the type as the caller sees it, when the caller may mint a preview token of it: a principal with the `management` scope that may read its drafts. A template that reads a field the caller may not read is none, as a title field that went is, and so is one the schema no longer fits; a component, a block and a call outside an operation are never previewable ([#3432](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3432)). `/schema/snapshot` is the canonical `schema.json` text of what the caller sees, with its SHA-256 as `ETag`, so for a caller that reads everything it is the instance's snapshot and schema hash.

**Error model.** RFC 9457 `application/problem+json` on every error: `type` (`urn:fluentcms:error:{code}` until a docs domain exists), `title`, `status`, `detail`, `code` (`FCMS...`), `errors[]` of `{ path, code, message }` for validation, `current` on 412, `traceId`. **As built.** `FluentCMS.Core.Api.Common` maps the engine's exceptions to a status, a code and this body in one place (`ProblemMapper`) and writes it through an `IExceptionHandler` and `IProblemDetailsService` that `AddFluentCms()` registers; the host adds `app.UseExceptionHandler()`. `ErrorCodeRegistry` lists every `FCMS` code once with its status and title, and a test fails when a code in `src` is missing from it. A validation exception is 422 with `errors[]`, or the status its codes share in the registry (400 for query errors, 404, 409 or 412 for the few codes that say so) and `FCMS2100` when its errors have different codes. Access denied is 401 for a request without a signed-in principal and 403 for one with it. A hook's own code is kept, 422 unless the registry says otherwise. A write that gave up at its lock timeout is 503 `FCMS2411` with `Retry-After` (4.5, "Lock waits as built"). A code raised only while the host starts or a job runs is 500, and so is any other exception, with a generic `detail` and `FCMS3199`. The HTTP layer's own codes are `FCMS2100`, `FCMS3101` to `FCMS3105`, `FCMS3107` for a body of another media type (415), `FCMS3108` for a hosted page asked for over plain http (403), `FCMS3110` and `FCMS3111` for the rate limit and the body cap, `FCMS3121` to `FCMS3123` for the request bridge and the Delivery API, `FCMS3130` and `FCMS3131` for `If-Match`, and `FCMS3140` to `FCMS3142` for `Idempotency-Key`.

**OpenAPI 3.1.** `Microsoft.AspNetCore.OpenApi` with document transformers that add one schema per content type and per block, block unions as `oneOf` with discriminator, the filter grammar as documented parameters, security schemes (`oauth2` authorization code, `apiKey`), examples from the sample pack. Served per deployment at `/openapi/v1.json`; Scalar UI at `/scalar` (dependency approved in Q9).

**OpenAPI as built.** `AddFluentCms()` registers the document `v1` of `Microsoft.AspNetCore.OpenApi` with the Delivery and Management routes only, and the host maps it at `/openapi/v1.json` and Scalar at `/scalar` with `app.MapFluentCmsOpenApi()`, which returns a builder whose conventions, such as an authorization policy, apply to both (Appendix B leaves their authentication to the host). Both are FluentCMS endpoints of the `docs` surface: they carry the security headers, the coarse limit counts them before authentication and the `delivery` policy by client address, and the FluentCMS scheme does not run on them, so a key sent there is never looked up; they are still not held to the path base. The document is built once and kept by the output cache for a day: one copy in all with a public origin, and without one a copy per path base (the pipeline's, so with `PathBase` set a request under it and one without; no request header reaches the key), keyed also by the schema hash, so that a store which outlives the process serves no copy made for another schema. The schema is built once per process, so there is no reload to evict on. `UseFluentCms()` runs authorization before the output cache, so a route that needs authorization, by its own requirement or the fallback policy, keeps its copy too and hands it only to a caller the policy lets through. Scalar.AspNetCore serves its bundle and helper beside the page from its embedded assets, so the page's `Content-Security-Policy` allows scripts from its own origin and, by a nonce made per request, the inline script that starts them, and styles from its own origin and inline, which the bundle injects (`default-src 'none'; script-src 'self' 'nonce-…'; style-src 'self' 'unsafe-inline'; img-src 'self' data:; font-src 'self' data:; connect-src 'self'`, the public origin added when it is set, `base-uri 'none'; form-action 'none'; frame-ancestors 'none'`). Scalar's CDN fonts, telemetry and agent chat would leave the origin, so they are off. This answers [#2856](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/2856) of the review of 7 Nov 2026. They are mapped in Development, and elsewhere only with `FluentCms:OpenApi:Enabled = true`, because the document names every type and field of the schema to whoever can reach it; this spec did not settle it, so the default is the safer one. The routes come from endpoint metadata. A route under `{type}` carries a `ContentOperation` that says what it reads and answers, and a document transformer writes it once per content type in place of its `{type}` form, so a new content route needs only that metadata; any other route gets a tag from its group, an operation id from its method and path (a parameter without its constraint, so `{id:guid}` is `ById`, and a Delivery route prefixed `delivery`, as content's `deliveryListArticle` is, so a plugin's `GET /media/{id}` in both groups gives `getMediaById` and `deliveryGetMediaById`), its security and the problem response. `entry-{type}` holds the system fields as read-only, `scheduledPublishAt` and `scheduledUnpublishAt` described as the Management API's alone, `deletedAt` and `deletedBy` as a read of the trash's alone, and every field that is not hidden, each from its field type's fragment and nullable unless its storage has an empty value (4.3), a relation also as its populated entry and rich text also as a string. `input-{type}` is what a write sends: the same fields without the system ones, a relation as ids and rich text as a document. No field is required, since a draft may leave any out and `fields` selects. A block is `block-{name}` with `type` as a `const` and an optional `id`, and a block list is the `oneOf` with the `type` discriminator that `BlocksFieldType` writes. The query grammar of 4.4 is shared parameters, and each type has `filter-{type}`, a `deepObject` with the operators of each field it can filter, the fields of its single components as `component.field` and the system fields, and `sort-{type}`, which lists its sort keys. `oauth2` has the authorization code flow, with `x-usePkce` for PKCE, and client credentials, at the document's server, which is `FluentCms:PublicOrigin` when it is set, as the issuer is, and otherwise the request's path base as a relative URL (`/cms`, or `/`), so that a forged `Host` neither reaches the document nor costs a copy of its own in the shared output cache, which varied by host before [#3298](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3298); `apiKey` is the `X-Api-Key` header; a Delivery route also accepts no credential. Examples come from `cms.OpenApi.AddExample<T>()`, which `AddBlogSample()` calls. The schema group answers the SDK's `SchemaDescription` records. The identity, site, settings, job, activity and me groups declare their bodies on each route, `Produces<T>` for the response and `Takes<T>` for a request body, which the route reads key by key, so its record only describes it, with `additionalProperties: false` since the route refuses a key it does not take; `Takes<T>` tells routing no media type, because with one, as `Accepts<T>` gives, routing would refuse another with a bare 415 before the route answers its problem. A record is the service's (`UserInfo`, `SiteInfo`, `ApiKeyInfo` and the like) or, where a route wrote an anonymous object or a JSON tree, one of the same JSON in `AdminBodies`: `ItemList<T>` and `ItemPage<T>` for the lists, and `RoleResponse`, `CreatedApiKeyResponse`, `SettingResponse`, `JobResponse`, `MeResponse` and `RecoveryCodesResponse`. The settings read is `SettingResponse`, whose `value` follows the area's JSON schema under `schema`, and a write takes the document as an object; a write and a reset declare the `If-Match` they require as the header `settings-if-match`, `"0"` when nothing is stored. The routes of the first-party plugins (media, webhooks, MCP, AI assist, multi-site and search) are described the same way, so the document holds every Delivery and Management route of the reference host: `Produces<T>` for a response, `Produces(204)` for a route that answers nothing, and `Takes<T>` of `FluentCMS.Plugins.Abstractions`, which core's routes use too, for a body a route reads by itself; its `mediaType`, such as the `multipart/form-data` of a media upload and replace, is for the document alone, so routing still lets another type reach the route's problem. A body bound to a parameter and the query parameters a route binds are what the API explorer reads from the route. Where a plugin route wrote an anonymous object, a record of the plugin's own writes the same JSON: `WebhookList`, `WebhookDeliveryQueued`, `McpClientList`, `McpInstructionList`, `McpPromptList`, `SiteHostList`, `MediaAssetList`, `MediaUsageList`, `MediaFolderList` and `MediaPresetList`, and the Delivery read of an asset, which a result of its own writes, declares that JSON as `DeliveredAsset`. An AI action names its `text/event-stream` answer in its description. A test of the reference host fails when a Delivery or Management route of a first-party plugin is missing from the document, declares a success other than 204 without a body or a 204 with one, or takes a body it does not declare. Enums are camel case names and numbers are numbers whatever the host's `JsonOptions` say, since FluentCMS writes them so and reads a body's numbers only as numbers. The system group answers `HealthReportResponse` at `/system/health`, its checks in order of name with the names of `HealthStatus` as they are, and the SDK's `StartupReport` at `/system/diagnostics`. A route of these groups declares the query parameters it reads with `Query(...)`, with their types, defaults and limits (`page` from 1, `pageSize` from 1 to 100 and 50 by default), and `ManagementQuery` accepts and reads only those beside the bridge's `site` and `locale`, so a parameter read without being declared fails every request to the route. Every route beside content also lists the bridge's `site` and, as `bridge-locale`, its `locale`, which such a route checks against the site's locales and reads nothing by.

**Rate limiting.** ASP.NET Core rate limiting with policies `delivery` (partition by key or IP), `management` (by principal), `auth` (strict, by IP). `RateLimit-*` headers and `Retry-After`. Per-key overrides from `cms_api_key.rate_limit`.

**CORS and headers.** Separate allow-lists for Delivery and Management from configuration and settings; no wildcard by default; security headers middleware (`X-Content-Type-Options`, `Referrer-Policy`, `Content-Security-Policy` with `frame-ancestors` for the hosted pages).

**Request limits.** Body size, page size cap (default 100), populate depth cap, filter node cap, upload caps (media plugin).

**HTTP protections as built.** `app.UseFluentCms()` adds the exception handler, routing, security headers, CORS, the gate that holds FluentCMS routes to the path base, the refusal of a hosted page over plain http (`FCMS3108`), a coarse limit per client address, the request limits, authentication, the rate limiter of the endpoints, authorization, the step that drops the body of a `HEAD` and the output cache in that order, and `MapFluentCms()` refuses to run without it (`FCMS1104`). A host does not call `UseAuthorization()` itself; one that still does runs its policies a second time, which changes nothing but the cost, and `AddFluentCms()` registers the authorization services the step needs. The policies are token buckets per caller with limits from `FluentCms:RateLimits` (delivery 600, management 300, auth 20) as the rate per minute: a bucket holds the limit and fills again over a minute, so a quiet caller may send the limit at once and then one request per minute divided by the limit, and a burst at the turn of a minute is not let through twice, as it was with fixed one-minute windows. A bucket is kept as the time it is next full (the generic cell rate algorithm), so one number is its state and no timer runs per caller, and a clock that moves back moves the bucket with it; the coarse limit (`PreAuth`, 6000) runs before authentication on every FluentCMS endpoint, which routing has found by its metadata, so a plugin's routes are covered without registering their prefixes, and on any path under a group of core that no endpoint answers, such as `/connect/token`; `/health` counts in a partition of its own (4.15), and so do the endpoints a surface marks as its static files (`StaticFiles`, 15000; 4.15); a key's `fcms_rate_limit` claim replaces the delivery limit up to `Delivery:MaxKeyLimit`; the caller is read from the `FluentCms` authentication scheme when there is one, and IPv6 addresses are limited by their /64. Responses carry the draft fields `RateLimit-Limit`, `RateLimit-Remaining` (what may be sent now) and `RateLimit-Reset` (seconds until the bucket is full), as the bucket was right after it counted the request: the bucket records them for the request when it grants or refuses it, and they are not looked up again when the response starts, since the partitioned limiter disposes a bucket that has been full for ten seconds and a slow request would lose them ([#2965](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/2965)). A refusal is a 429 problem (`FCMS3110`) with `Retry-After`, the seconds until the next request is let through, which the refusing lease carries. The caller is read once per request into an `ApiCaller` when the FluentCMS scheme has run: its kind, `sub`, rate limit claim and, for a key, the hash of its claims that the Delivery output cache keys its copy by, computed on first use; the client address is read once too and shared by the limit before authentication and the endpoint's. A partition is the caller alone, with its limit only when a key's own limit replaces the policy's, so a request builds no partition key or limiter factory of its own. A `/connect` or `/account` request, which the check before authentication has counted under the auth limit, passes the endpoint's limiter in a partition of its own that has no limiter: that limiter keeps one limiter per partition key, so sharing the caller's key would let the pass-through stand in for the caller's bucket on a hosted page, or the bucket count the request a second time ([#2968](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/2968)). This answers items 8 and 10 of [#2860](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/2860). The allow-lists come from configuration only; the settings half is not built. `/connect` answers the Management origins. Both policies allow the `X-Cms-Site` and `X-Cms-Locale` headers and expose `ETag`, `Location`, `Retry-After` and the `RateLimit-*` fields. A FluentCMS response other than Delivery's that sets no `Cache-Control` gets `no-store`, so a key's secret, recovery codes, `/me` and the diagnostics are kept by no browser or proxy. The body cap is `FluentCms:Limits:MaxBodySize` (2 MiB, `FCMS3111` on 413). It runs before authentication, where the authorization server reads the body of a token, introspection or revocation request, and holds a path under a group of core that no endpoint answers by its prefix, as the coarse limit does. The page size, depth and filter caps stay the engine's. Details are in `docs/host-configuration.md`.

**Rate limit by caller class as built (B-75).** `RequireCallerRateLimit()` (`FluentCMS.Plugins.Abstractions`) puts an endpoint or a group of a plugin under the policy `caller`, in place of the Delivery limit of `MapGroup`: a caller whose principal has the `management` scope is counted per principal at the Management rate, and any other per API key or client address at the Delivery rate, a key's own limit included, with the headers and the 429 problem of those policies. The rate limiter keeps its buckets per policy, so these endpoints count apart from the REST APIs. The GraphQL endpoint uses it (5.3).

### 4.11 Events and hooks

Rows 8.4, 8.16, 6.10.

**Event bus.** `IEventBus.PublishAsync<TEvent>` with `IEventHandler<TEvent>` subscribers, in-process. Events raised during a transaction are buffered and dispatched after commit, in `[HookOrder]` order; a failing handler is logged and does not affect the write. Handlers that must not be lost (webhooks, activity log, cache invalidation) subscribe through the outbox: the event is also written to `cms_outbox` in the same transaction and a runner job drains it, so a crash between commit and dispatch loses nothing.

**In-process handlers, as built.** The handlers of `IEventHandler<T>` stay awaited after the commit, because a caller that reads after a write expects their side effects, such as a cache eviction, to have run; moving them behind the response is declined. Each one is bounded instead. It runs off the caller's thread, so that one that blocks before its first await is cut off too, and the caller waits for it at most `FluentCms:Events:Handlers:Timeout` (default 5 seconds, the default of `OutboxOptions.JobEventTimeout`), with the `WaitAsync(timeout)` that bounds the handlers of a job event. Its token is the host's shutdown, and the dispatch cancels it when the timeout passes, before the next handler runs, so a cooperative handler stops then; one that does not is logged as abandoned (event 9615), left to end on its own, and the next handler runs. The handlers of an event run in a scope the dispatch creates, as the handlers of a job event do, never in the caller's, so a handler left running shares no `DbContext` or other scoped service with the request, which goes on to read its response. The abandoned handler keeps its scope until it ends, and the handlers after it run in a fresh one. Whatever it does after the caller went on, such as using the request's services through `HttpContext.RequestServices` once the request has ended, is logged (9617) and never thrown, so it neither reaches the caller nor surfaces as an unobserved task exception; a scope that fails while it is disposed is logged too (9618). The bound is per handler, so an event with several slow handlers waits up to the bound for each. A handler that ends after `FluentCms:Events:Handlers:SlowThreshold` (default 1 second) is logged as slow (9616), and every run is recorded in `fluentcms.event.handler.duration` (histogram, seconds) by `fluentcms.operation` (the event type), `fluentcms.handler` (the handler's class) and `fluentcms.outcome` (`succeeded`, `failed` or `timed-out`). This answers [#2883](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/2883) of the review of 7 Nov 2026.

**Publishing inside a transaction, as built.** Outside a transaction of the content service, `IEventBus.PublishAsync` is refused with `FCMS2602` while another transaction is open in the scope, whether on `CmsDbContext`, on `SystemDbContext` or on a plugin's `PluginDbContext<TPlugin>`, because its end cannot be seen and an event that may yet roll back is never delivered. Core adds a transaction interceptor to every plugin context it registers, which remembers the contexts of the scope that began or joined a transaction, and the transaction probe asks whether one of them still has it open. A plugin publishes after its own transaction commits. An explicit `IEventBus.StageAsync(DbContext, ...)`, which would write the outbox rows into the plugin's transaction, is declined before 1.0 to keep the SDK small. The activity log (`FCMS2704`), settings (`FCMS2807`) and the Super Admin guard read the same probe, so they refuse inside a plugin's transaction too. This answers [#2885](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/2885) of the review of 7 Nov 2026.

**Outbox dead letters and retention, as built.** A delivery that runs out of attempts is left as failed and stays in the dead-letter list, which the Management API lists, retries, removes and purges by handler (4.10). A row whose handler is missing on the instance that claims it, as when its plugin was removed, or whose plugin is disabled in the row's site (4.13), is put back without using an attempt for `MissingHandlerDelay` (5 minutes) at a time, but only until `OutboxOptions.OrphanAfter` (7 days) has passed since its event happened. Then it is moved to the dead-letter list with an error that names the handler, and the plugin when it is disabled, and is logged (9619); it is never deleted unseen. A failed row's `available_at` is when it failed. `OutboxOptions.FailedRetention` (30 days) removes failed rows that ran out of attempts that long after they failed, as `jobs.retention` removes dead-lettered jobs (4.12); set to null it keeps them until they are retried or removed. A row moved to the list for a missing handler or a disabled plugin never used up its attempts (`attempts < MaxAttempts`), so the retention leaves it, and it stays until it is retried or removed; a `MaxAttempts` lowered below a row's attempts counts it in. The dead-letter list walks `ix_cms_outbox_failed`, on `(sequence DESC, id)` filtered to `status = 'failed'`, the whole order of the list, so it never reads delivered or pending rows and never sorts. The prune seeks `ix_cms_outbox_failed_available_at`, on `(available_at, attempts)` filtered to `status = 'failed'`, so it reads only the rows that failed before its time, and passes over the ones it keeps by their attempts in the index; a burst of recent failures within the retention is never read. The purge by handler seeks `ix_cms_outbox_failed_handler`, on `handler` filtered to `status = 'failed'` ([#3411](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3411)). The drain reads the candidates of one handler from `ix_cms_outbox_handler_pending`, on `(handler, sequence, id)` filtered to `status = 'pending'`, so the claim of one owner's handlers seeks their rows in event order and never walks another handler's backlog ([#3285](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3285)). Delivered rows go after `Retention` (7 days). Both are removed by the recurring job `events.outbox.prune`, hourly on the default queue with a 10-minute attempt timeout, in batches of `PruneBatchSize` (500, at most 1000) as `jobs.retention` does, and no longer by the drain. A batch is claimed with one conditional `UPDATE ... WHERE id IN (...)` that checks every condition of the read again, under an owner of that claim alone, and read back by that owner, so two drains of one instance never take each other's rows. A handler that is cut off at `DeliveryTimeout`, or left by a stopping host, may still be running: the drain cancels its token before it settles the delivery, whatever the timer of the handler's own limit has done, and its scope and its token sources are disposed in a continuation of its task, once it ends, and a fault it ends with is logged (9620), as is a scope that fails while it is disposed (9621). This answers [#2884](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/2884) and items 1 and 2 of [#2887](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/2887) of the review of 7 Nov 2026.

**Outbox drain, as built.** The drain delivers in a lane per owner of outbox handlers: each plugin is one owner, and core's and the host's handlers together are another; the rows of a handler this instance does not have share one lane more. A lane claims and delivers the rows of its owner's handlers one after another in the order of their events, whatever their types, so on one instance a plugin gets an entry's updated, published and trashed events in that order, and the lanes of different owners run side by side, so an owner whose handler is slow or hangs until `DeliveryTimeout` holds back only its own deliveries. That order holds per owner and per instance only: another instance's drain may claim the owner's next batch at the same time, and a row that fails waits for its retry while later rows of the owner go on. The order between owners is not kept; the rows of one event are still recorded in `[HookOrder]` order. There are as many lanes as owners, a few, so their number is not capped. One drain runs on an instance at a time: a drain job that finds one running, as a second worker of the `outbox` queue would, only signals it and ends, so the order of an owner holds however many workers the queue has. A drain cancelled without a stop of its host, as when its job lost its lease, returns at the end of `JobRunnerOptions.ReleaseTimeout` while a lane may still wait for a write that its provider does not let go of; until those lanes end, a drain job of that instance also only signals and ends, so that no new lane claims an owner's later rows while its earlier ones are still leased. The instance drains again only once those lanes have ended, and then at the next kick or sweep, since nothing kicks a drain when they end. The drain logs that return at Warning (9628) ([#3548](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3548)). A drain starts a lane for each owner with rows due, and looks again, at the owners with no lane only and with one seek per handler, when a lane ends or when a kick of its own instance signals it, so a new event for an idle owner is delivered while another lane is busy and a burst of kicks while a slow lane runs costs a few reads each. The rows of handlers this instance does not have are looked for once a drain, since that walks the pending rows. A claim that finds the database busy, as lanes claiming together on SQLite do, is tried again in the lane after 50 ms, 200 ms and 1 second. A lane whose read or claim still fails is left out until the next signal or sweep, while the others go on, and logged: a busy database at Warning (9627), anything else at Error (9623). A claim reads the candidates handler by handler, each the first `BatchSize` of that handler, merges them in event order and claims the first `BatchSize`, so the claim of one owner never walks another's backlog; `ix_cms_outbox_handler_pending` on `(handler, sequence, id)` over pending rows serves those reads ([#3406](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/pull/3406)). The claim counts the attempt of every row it takes (`attempts + 1` in the claim's `UPDATE`), and a row that is not run to an end gives it back (a handler that is missing or disabled, a hand-off that never started, a stopping host, a renewal that failed, an orphan, and a claim that fails or is stopped after its update and before its rows are read back, [#3537](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3537)), so a delivery that kills its process still uses up its attempts. A row that still names the owner of a lapsed lease of another process is claimed alone, so such a delivery costs the rows after it one attempt at most; one that names a claim of the instance that claims it, which is still running, was given up without a write, as when a release failed, and is claimed with the rest of its batch. The lease of a batch is renewed only when what is left of it is shorter than `DeliveryTimeout` plus half the gap between it and `LeaseDuration`, with that of the rows given back so far, and the rows delivered are settled with one `UPDATE` then and at the end of the batch. The rows given back go with one `UPDATE` at the end of the batch, those that wait `MissingHandlerDelay` (a missing handler, a disabled plugin) with their `available_at` moved and the rest (a hand-off that never started, a stopping host, a renewal that failed) as they were ([#3548](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3548)); a failure or an orphan is still written on its own. A stopping host settles the rows delivered and gives back what is left of the batch, also when the stop comes during the read of which plugins are disabled, during the read of the site's default locale, which is shared and which the delivery stops waiting for, or during the disposal of a delivery's scope, which goes on without it ([#3548](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3548)). No handler starts once its lane is told of the stop, also when the stop came during a renewal or just before the hand-off ([#3537](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3537)). The writes of a stop share `JobRunnerOptions.ReleaseTimeout` (5 seconds) in all, counted from when the job runner cancels the drain, which tells each lane on a thread of its own. Each lane settles its delivered rows first, and what it writes before the release (that settle, a failure or an orphan, which is given up and written after the settle when the stop comes during it, and a renewal) has half of that time at most. The release, the one `UPDATE` of every row the batch gives back, has what is left. A write past its time is cancelled and not waited for, even one that its provider cannot cancel, as SQLite cannot while it waits for the write lock, and at the end of the time the drain returns, whatever it still waits for. The rows of a write that does not fit keep their lease and the attempt of their claim. When the lease ends they are delivered, one per claim by another instance as after a crash, and again for those already delivered, which is at-least-once. The sweep fails instead a row that was on its last attempt: one whose own write did not fit, even if it was delivered, and, when its release could not be written, one no handler saw. It is in the dead-letter list and can be retried ([#3521](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3521)). Each row of a write that failed or was given up is logged at Warning (9603), saying that a write given up may still land and that a row on its last attempt is left as failed rather than tried again ([#3548](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3548)). A renewal that fails ends the batch and the lane, and gives back the rows not tried without costing them an attempt (logged at Warning, 9626). A crash before the settle delivers the batch again. A batch of N events whose handlers answer in time costs two writes to `cms_outbox`, one to claim it and one to settle it, besides the kick and the drain job; one event with handlers of K owners costs K claims and K settles. A row whose hand-off never started is left out of the drain for `MissingHandlerDelay`, then tried again. When which plugins are disabled cannot be read, the batch is given back with one `UPDATE`, left out of the drain for `MissingHandlerDelay`, and its lane waits for the next signal, so the read is tried once a batch and not once a row. The drain leaves out at most `BatchSize` such rows, the latest, since their ids go into every claim; one left out early is only tried again sooner ([#3410](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3410)). The operation of a delivery, and of a job run, reads the site's default locale through `SiteCache`, so a batch reads it once per site. There is no recurring drain: the hosted `OutboxScheduleService` sweeps every minute, leaving rows whose attempts are used up and whose worker is gone as failed (9607) and kicking a drain when rows are due, and it reads before it writes, so an idle instance adds no job, attempt or lock; an error in it is logged (9625), as is a failure to store the prune's schedule (9624). The ids 9605 and 9610, of the recurring drain's registration and errors in `1.0.0-preview.1`, are retired and not given again. It removes the `events.outbox.drain` definition that earlier versions stored. The kick counts only a pending drain job that is due, so a drain whose retry waits for its backoff no longer holds back delivery. This answers [#3303](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3303) and [#3282](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3282) of the review of 17 Nov 2026.

**Events of an import, as built (B-92).** Everything an import writes carries `Origin.Import`, and the events of one run share a correlation id: `import:{run}`, or `import+webhooks:{run}` when the import was started with `webhooks=true`. The webhooks plugin enqueues no delivery for an event of `Origin.Import` whose correlation id does not start with `import+webhooks:`. The correlation id travels with the event through the outbox, so the choice needs no column, no SDK member and nothing kept beyond the request. The other handlers, such as cache invalidation and the activity log, hear the events of an import as they hear any.

**Catalogue.** Content: `EntryDraftSavedEvent`, `EntryPublishedEvent`, `EntryUnpublishedEvent`, `EntryDraftDiscardedEvent`, `EntryVersionRestoredEvent`, `EntryTrashedEvent`, `EntryRestoredEvent`, `EntryPurgedEvent`, `EntryScheduledEvent`, `EntryScheduleCancelledEvent`. Media (plugin): asset uploaded, updated, replaced, moved, deleted. Identity: user created, updated, deactivated, role changed, login linked, login unlinked, key created, key revoked, key expiry changed, key rotated, key allowed addresses changed, key address refused, login succeeded, login failed, member registered. Settings: `SettingsChangedEvent`. Sites: created, updated. Plugins: `PluginEnabledEvent`, `PluginDisabledEvent`. Infrastructure: `CacheInvalidatedEvent`, `JobFailedEvent`, `JobDeadLetteredEvent`.

**Hooks.** `IBeforeValidateHook<T>`, `IBeforeCreateHook<T>`, `IAfterCreateHook<T>`, the same pairs for Update, Delete, Publish, Unpublish, Restore; `T` is a content class or `ContentDocument` for all types. `HookContext` carries the `OperationContext`, the entry, the previous state, the changed field paths, `Locales` and `Cancel(code, message)`. A before hook may change the entry; an after hook gets a copy of what was written, and its changes are discarded (4.1). `Locales` lists the locales the operation acts on: every locale of the entry for a delete or a restore, whose entry holds one of them, and the entry's own for any other operation; a type that is not localized has the one locale `""`, as its events do. A purge runs no hooks (item 5 of [#2869](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/2869)). Ordering: `[HookOrder(int)]` with plugin dependency order as the tiebreak (decision 49).

### 4.12 Job runner

Rows 6.6, 8.10, 10.7 (locks), 10.20 (Should); decision 40.

**Tables.** `cms_job (id, type, payload json, queue, site_id, run_at, priority, status, attempts, max_attempts, lease_until, lease_owner, last_error, created_at, created_by, origin, completed_at)`, `cms_job_attempt (job_id, attempt, started_at, finished_at, outcome, error)`, `cms_recurring_job (name, type, cron, payload json, queue, next_run_at, enabled)`. A recurring job has a `type` because a schedule must say which job it fires. Job `status` is `pending`, `running`, `succeeded` or `dead_lettered`. The claim is served by `ix_cms_job_claim` on `(queue, priority DESC, run_at, id)`, filtered to live jobs (`pending`, `running`), so finished jobs are never walked. `payload` is the payload as JSON (`JsonSerializerOptions.Web`); one whose type writes a scalar, such as the `int` of an `IJob<int>`, is stored as `{"value":12}`, because the `ISJSON` check of SQL Server takes only an object or an array, and the payload type tells the reader to unwrap it ([#2711](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/2711)).

**API.** `IJob<TPayload>.ExecuteAsync(payload, JobContext)`; `IJobScheduler.EnqueueAsync`, `ScheduleAsync(runAt)`, `AddRecurringAsync<TJob, TPayload>(name, cron, payload, queue)`, which is safe to call at every startup on every instance. `IJobDeadLetters` lists dead-lettered jobs and retries one; it checks no permission, so `jobs.manage` is enforced by the caller. Jobs are registered with `cms.Jobs.AddJob<TJob, TPayload>("stable-name")`, and that name is what `cms_job.type` stores.

**Principal and actor.** A job always runs as `Principal.System` with `Origin.Job`. An actor in the payload (a payload that implements `IJobActorPayload`) does not replace the principal: it goes to `OperationContext.Actor`, which authorization never reads, so a payload cannot borrow a user's roles. The actor is built from kind, id and name only, with no claims. `OperationContext.AuditPrincipal` is `Actor ?? Principal`, and audit columns (`created_by`, `updated_by`, `deleted_by`, `author_id`) use it, so a scheduled publish records who scheduled it. A `System`, `Anonymous` or empty actor fails the attempt with `FCMS2507`.

**Runner.** A hosted service with a configurable worker count per queue. Claiming uses the provider dialect: `FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED` (PostgreSQL), `UPDLOCK, READPAST` (SQL Server), optimistic `UPDATE` that rechecks every condition of the read (SQLite). Leases with heartbeat; retries with exponential backoff and jitter; dead-letter after `max_attempts`; graceful shutdown; multi-instance safe without extra infrastructure. **Stop.** A stop ends the claims, waits `ShutdownGracePeriod` (20 seconds) for the running jobs, then cancels their tokens and waits `ReleaseTimeout` (5 seconds) for their releases. Past it, event 9514 is logged and the heartbeats stop, so a job whose release was not written keeps its lease until the lease ends and is then taken over, never marked done. Each worker's job is told in a thread-pool work item of its own, which runs the callbacks of that worker's cancellation, and the time runs from the cancellation, not from the end of its callbacks: a job's await can end inside the cancellation, and what follows it runs there, such as its release, which SQLite writes synchronously for as long as its command waits for the lock. So such a release neither holds the stop past `ReleaseTimeout` nor delays telling the job of another worker, such as the outbox drain ([#3538](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3538)). That bound has a cost: a release that needs longer than `ReleaseTimeout` but would have succeeded used to finish before the stop returned; now the stop returns and logs 9514, and unless the release still lands before the process ends, the job waits for its lease to end with its attempt spent. What a callback of the cancellations throws within the waits is thrown from the stop; what one throws after the stop has given up waiting is logged (9522). The drain bounds its own writes by the same `ReleaseTimeout`, so a drain that uses all of it can leave its job's outcome unwritten when the stop returns, and the job then waits for its lease. **Attempt timeout.** Off by default. `JobRunnerOptions.AttemptTimeout` sets a limit for every job, and `AddJob<TJob, TPayload>("name", attemptTimeout)` overrides it for one (`Timeout.InfiniteTimeSpan` turns it off). When an attempt passes its limit the runner stops renewing the lease and cancels `JobContext.CancellationToken`. A job that stops by throwing `OperationCanceledException` fails the attempt with `FCMS2508`, which is retried with backoff or dead-lettered by the usual rules. The job runs off the worker's thread, so that one that blocks before its first await is cut off too. A job that ignores the token is abandoned 30 seconds after its limit: its attempt fails with `FCMS2508` as above, the worker is free for the next job, event 9515 is logged and the counter `fluentcms.job.abandoned` (by `fluentcms.operation`, the job type) counts it. The invocation keeps its scope and its token until it ends, and nothing of how it ends is written to the job; what it throws then is logged (9516). It is not stopped, though: it may still commit its own writes, and it can run beside the retry of its job. An instance leaves at most `JobRunnerOptions.MaxAbandonedJobs` (4) running, counted by its job executor, while the gauge `fluentcms.job.abandoned_running` reports those of the whole process; past that, a further job that ignores its token keeps its worker until it returns, with a Warning that names its type (9519). A job that ended as the cut-off came keeps its own outcome, and one that returns normally within the grace is a success ([#3266](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3266)). Abandoning needs a limit, so every job core registers has one but the outbox drain, which runs for as long as rows are due and cuts off each delivery itself at `DeliveryTimeout`: scheduled publish and unpublish and the two mail jobs have five minutes, far past what they take ([#3408](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3408)). So does every job of the first-party plugins. The five that had none have, each far past what a run takes ([#3529](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3529)): `mcp.client-cleanup` and `webhooks.deliver` 10 minutes, the second past the longest send timeout the options allow (five minutes) and the minute after which its claim is taken over, `webhooks.queue` 5 minutes, `media.dimensions.backfill` 30 minutes and `media.variants.cleanup` an hour. A resumable job that ignores its token past the grace is abandoned and failed like any other, since it did not stop. **Resumable jobs.** Every job core registers that deletes in batches, the outbox prune aside (job, activity and idempotency retention, trash purge, the prune of the authorization server and the removal of unverified members, each with a 10 minute timeout) is resumable: one that stops at its timeout has succeeded (event 9520), and what is left waits for its next run, so a long backlog is never dead-lettered for its size ([#3304](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3304)). A plugin, which cannot see the internal marker, registers a job as resumable with `IPluginServiceBuilder.AddJob<TJob, TPayload>(type, attemptTimeout, resumable: true)`, as the retention jobs of the AI Assist and Webhooks plugins and the media variant cleanup do; the default implementation of that overload hands a job that is not resumable to the one without `resumable` and refuses a resumable one with `NotSupportedException`, so a builder other than core's builds as before ([#3408](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3408)). A host registers one with `cms.Jobs.AddJob<TJob, TPayload>(type, attemptTimeout, resumable: true)`, the registration core's plugin builder makes ([#3529](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3529)). Either way the job stops on its token wherever it is with what it did kept, and something runs it again, such as a schedule, since a run stopped at its limit has succeeded and is not retried. The outbox prune has the same timeout but is not resumable yet. Tests list the jobs core and the first-party plugins register without a timeout, and the resumable ones. **Unknown types.** A job whose type the instance has not registered, as on an older instance during a rolling deploy or after a plugin is removed, is put back for an instance that knows it (event 9517), first for one poll and then for as long as it has waited since its first release, at most five minutes (`JobExecutor.LongestOrphanWait`), so that a backlog of them is claimed a few times an hour rather than every poll and jobs this instance knows are not held up behind it. When how long it has waited cannot be read, it is put back for one poll without using an attempt, as when which plugins are disabled cannot be read (event 9521). The first such release is kept as an attempt, marked with `FCMS2501`, and given back; a later one removes its attempt, so neither the job's attempts nor the attempt table grow while it waits. Once an hour has passed since that first release, it is dead-lettered with `FCMS2501` at once, whatever attempts are left. A recurring row of an unknown type is not fired and its `next_run_at` stays, so an instance that knows the type fires the occurrence, and a removed plugin's schedule makes no jobs; event 9518 is logged once per row and type in a process, and again if the row's type changes, and a row that stays due shows only there and in `cms_recurring_job`. When the type is known again, the row fires one occurrence at once, at its stored `next_run_at`, and moves to the next after now, as after downtime ([#3276](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3276)). `IJobRunner` is an interface so a Hangfire adapter can replace it (**Adapters** below).

**Built-in jobs.** Scheduled publish and unpublish, trash purge, version pruning, activity retention, idempotency key retention, outbox dispatch (its own `outbox` queue), the mail a visitor asks for with an address, the reset of either pool and the verification mail of a member again (`members.send_mail` and `users.send_password_reset`, on their own `mail` queue with two workers an instance, so that the answer never waits for a send), webhook delivery (plugin, its own `webhooks` queue), media variant cleanup (plugin).

**Job retention.** A recurring job (`jobs.retention`, daily) removes succeeded jobs and their attempts once `JobRunnerOptions.SucceededRetention` (7 days) has passed since `completed_at`, and dead-lettered jobs and their attempts once `DeadLetteredRetention` (30 days; null keeps them until they are retried) has passed since they were dead-lettered, in batches of at most `RetentionBatchSize` (500, at most 1000), each batch in one transaction. Pending and running jobs and recurring definitions are never removed. Before a batch of dead-lettered jobs goes, a pending schedule that still names one of them, left so by a crash before `ScheduleDeadLetterHandler` heard the job, is failed with the error that its job was dead-lettered and then removed, and its mirror cleared, so that no schedule waits for a job that is gone ([#3407](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3407)). Both removals, and the dead-letter list, which pages newest first by `completed_at` and then by `id`, use `ix_cms_job_status_completed` on `(status, completed_at DESC, id)`, the whole order of the list ([#3285](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3285)). It sets an attempt timeout, and it is safe to run on several instances and to run again. Each run logs how many succeeded and how many dead-lettered jobs it removed (9512, Information), and a schedule that cannot be stored at startup is logged (9513, Error) and tried again every 30 seconds.

**Wake.** A worker that finds nothing to claim waits `PollInterval` (5 seconds), or until its own instance adds a job of its queue that is due now: `IJobScheduler` wakes an idle worker of the job's queue after the insert, and the outbox kick wakes the `outbox` worker when it finds a drain job already pending and due. Each queue holds at most one wake per worker of the queue on the instance, so a burst wakes every idle worker once and no more. The wake is process-local, and the workers of other instances find the job on their next poll. An event with a webhook on an idle instance is then sent as soon as the drain and the send allow, rather than after two polls of about 5 seconds each. This answers item 5 of [#2887](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/2887).

**Queue ages.** Each maintenance turn (`MaintenanceInterval`, 15 seconds) also measures how long the oldest job of each queue that is due and held by no worker has waited, `now - run_at` over the jobs of every instance, and keeps it in memory. The `job-runner` health check carries the ages in its data, in seconds by queue, and reports Degraded when one is above `FluentCms:Health:PendingJobAgeLimit` (15 minutes; `Timeout.InfiniteTimeSpan` for no limit), which leaves the instance in rotation and logs event 9104. The observable gauge `fluentcms.job.oldest_pending_age` (seconds, by `fluentcms.queue`) reports the same, with every queue of the instance's options at zero when nothing of it waits. So workers that all hang on jobs that ignore their tokens, while the maintenance loop keeps beating, show in both. This answers item 8 of [#2887](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/2887).

**Errors.** The error an attempt stores and logs has every string, number and property name of the payload taken out, one of four or more characters wherever it appears and a shorter one as a whole word, since an exception message can quote the job's input. The names of the schema's content types, components and blocks and of their fields are kept, because they are not secret and an error such as "Content type 'article' not found" says little without them. This answers item 9 of [#2887](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/2887).

**Adapters.** A Hangfire adapter replaces `IJobRunner`, and `IJobScheduler` too when it keeps its jobs elsewhere. Three things of core assume the built-in runner. The outbox kick reads `cms_job` for a pending `events.outbox.drain` job that is due before it enqueues another, so with jobs kept elsewhere every commit that records outbox rows enqueues a drain job; the adapter dedupes them itself, or accepts them, since a drain that finds nothing is cheap. The wake belongs to the built-in runner and its scheduler, so the adapter's own polling sets the latency. The queue ages are measured by the built-in runner's maintenance loop, as its heartbeat is. This answers item 10 of [#2887](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/2887).

**Management.** `/jobs` endpoints (4.10), permission `jobs.manage`. Cron parsing uses Cronos (MIT), approved in Q9. `fluentcms jobs list`, `run` and `retry` (6.1, B-91) go through `JobAdministration`; `jobs run <name>` adds a run of a recurring job due now, and with `--inline` runs it in the command's process as a job that process holds from the start (`JobStore.AddClaimedAsync`), with one attempt.

### 4.13 Plugin host, server side

Rows 8.1 to 8.4, 8.6, 8.10 to 8.16; decisions 11, 13, 14, 46 to 49.

**Shape.**

```csharp
[FluentCmsPlugin("media", Version = "1.0.0", SdkRange = "[1.0.0,2.0.0)")]
public sealed class MediaPlugin : IPlugin
{
    public void ConfigureServices(IPluginServiceBuilder plugin)
    {
        plugin.Services.AddScoped<IMediaService, MediaService>();
        plugin.AddFieldType<MediaFieldType>();
        plugin.AddPermissions<MediaPermissions>();
        plugin.AddSettings<MediaSettings>();
        plugin.AddModel<MediaModelContributor>();      // entities with prefix cms_media_
        plugin.AddMigrations(typeof(MediaPlugin).Assembly);
        plugin.AddJob<VariantCleanupJob, VariantCleanupPayload>("media.variant-cleanup");
        plugin.AddEventHandler<EntryPurgedEvent, ReleaseReferencesHandler>();
    }

    public void Configure(IPluginAppBuilder app)
    {
        app.MapGroup("/media").MapMediaEndpoints();   // under the CMS route group, CMS auth by default
        app.MapDelivery("/media").MapMediaDeliveryEndpoints();   // under /api/delivery/v1
        app.MapManagement("/media").MapMediaManagementEndpoints();
    }
}
```

**Registration.** Explicit `AddPlugin<T>()` wins and fixes order; `[assembly: FluentCmsPlugin(typeof(MediaPlugin))]` on referenced assemblies is discovered through the compile-time dependency context. No folder scan, no runtime loading.

**Validation at startup.** SDK range against the running SDK version, dependencies present, duplicate names; failure stops the host with `FCMS15xx` errors (`FCMS14xx` is authorization's).

**Plugin host as built.** The manifest is `[FluentCmsPlugin("name", Version = "1.0.0", SdkRange = "[1.0.0,2.0.0)", DependsOn = ["other"])]` on the plugin class, and the same attribute on an assembly, `[assembly: FluentCmsPlugin(typeof(T))]`, names a plugin class for discovery, as many as the assembly has. A name is lower kebab-case like a content type or a settings area, at most 32 characters, so that `cms_{name}__migrations` and the plugin's tables fit a PostgreSQL identifier. A plugin's name is also its stream's, so `system` and `content`, core's streams, are refused with `FCMS1502`, for a plugin and for a stream registered through `IPluginMigrations` alike. `Version` is a semantic version and, left out, the informational version of the plugin's assembly; `SdkRange` is required. `IPluginServiceBuilder` has the plugin's `Name` and the host's `Services`, and `IPluginAppBuilder` its `Name` and the root `Services`; core implements both and plugins only call them, so the contributions of B-61 are new members that break no plugin. For `IServiceCollection` the SDK references `Microsoft.Extensions.DependencyInjection.Abstractions`, and nothing of EF Core or ASP.NET Core. `cms.AddPlugin<T>(configure)` creates the plugin with its parameterless constructor and hands it to `configure`. Discovery reads the app's deps file (the first of `APP_CONTEXT_DEPS_FILES`, else `{entry}.deps.json`) once per process, loads the runtime assemblies of the libraries that depend on `FluentCMS.Sdk`, directly or through other libraries, and reads their assembly attributes; an app with no deps file on disk, such as a single-file one, discovers nothing and adds its plugins with `AddPlugin`. So does an app that sets the `AppContext` switch `FluentCMS.Plugins.DisableDiscovery` (`<RuntimeHostConfigurationOption Include="FluentCMS.Plugins.DisableDiscovery" Value="true" />` in its project file), which is for an app that references plugins it adds to some of its hosts only, as the data tests do (B-80); such a host says so in the log when it starts (event 9237). A plugin that is added and discovered is registered once, as added and where added; one added twice is a duplicate. **Order.** The plugins are taken as they came, the added ones in the order of their `AddPlugin` calls and then the discovered ones in the order of the deps file, and each is placed after the plugins it depends on, which move ahead of it when they come later; plugins with no dependency between them keep that order unless a dependency pulls one of them forward. `ConfigureServices` runs in this order inside `AddFluentCms`, after the host's callback and before core adds its own services, so a plugin's service replaces one that core adds only when none is there, and `Configure` runs in this order inside `MapFluentCms`, after the endpoint groups of core. The plugin host is the `IHookTiebreaker` (a host that registers its own keeps it): among hooks and event handlers of equal `[HookOrder]`, one of a plugin ranks by the plugin's place, and one of no plugin, the host's or core's, after every plugin's, keeping its registration order. A type belongs to the plugin whose `ConfigureServices` registered it, by implementation type or instance, or else to the plugin whose class is the only plugin class in the type's assembly, which covers a hook registered with a factory; the app's own assembly is left out of that, since its other types are the host's. `Configure` runs once, however many times `MapFluentCms` is called, and a discovered plugin whose constructor throws stops the host with its own exception. **Codes.** `FCMS1501` the type cannot be a plugin (no manifest or several on the class, an assembly attribute with a name instead of a type or one that cannot be read, or a discovered class that is abstract, does not implement `IPlugin` or has no public parameterless constructor), `FCMS1502` the name, `FCMS1503` the version, `FCMS1504` an SDK range that is missing or not interval notation, `FCMS1505` a running SDK outside the range, `FCMS1506` two plugins with one name, `FCMS1507` a missing dependency and `FCMS1508` a dependency cycle, whose message lists the cycle; each message names the plugin and its class. `FCMS1520` to `FCMS1529` are the contributions' (below), `FCMS1540` to `FCMS1544` the provider slots', `FCMS1550` to `FCMS1559` per-site enablement's (below), `FCMS1560` to `FCMS1579` the webhooks plugin's (5.2), `FCMS1580` to `FCMS1599` the media plugin's (5.1), `FCMS1600` to `FCMS1639` the MCP plugin's (5.4), `FCMS1660` to `FCMS1679` the multi-site plugin's (5.6), `FCMS1680` to `FCMS1699` the AI assist plugin's (5.5), and `FCMS1700` to `FCMS1719` the search plugin's (5.7). **SDK versions.** The running version is the informational version of `FluentCMS.Sdk` without its build metadata, and the range is parsed in core. A released SDK is held to the range exactly as NuGet holds a version. A pre-release of the SDK counts as the release it leads to when it is held against a bound that is a release, and is compared in full against a bound that is a pre-release: `1.0.0-preview.3` and `1.0.0-dev` satisfy `[1.0.0,2.0.0)`, `2.0.0-preview.1` does not, and `[1.0.0-preview.2,2.0.0)` refuses `1.0.0-preview.1`. NuGet's own precedence puts `1.0.0-preview` below `1.0.0` and `2.0.0-preview` below `2.0.0`, so a range written against released versions would refuse every dev and preview build of the line it names and take the previews of the next major version, which may break the plugin; the rule turns both around and loosens nothing for a release. The startup report and `/system/diagnostics` list the plugins in this order with name and version; the full manifest is the Management plugin list's.
