**Plugin contributions as built.** `IPluginServiceBuilder` mirrors the core builders, each method doing what its `cms.*` counterpart does: `AddFieldType<T>()`, `AddPermissions<T>()` (an `IPermissionProvider`, whose permissions may name built-in roles that get them by default, 4.8), `AddSettings<T>()`, `AddJob<TJob, TPayload>(type, attemptTimeout)`, `AddJobQueue(name, workers)`, `AddEventHandler<TEvent, THandler>()`, `AddOutboxHandler<TEvent, THandler>(name)`, `AddActivity<TEvent>(name, describe)`, `AddErrorCode(code, status, title)` and, for the MCP server, `AddMcpTools<T>()`, `AddMcpResources<T>()` and `AddMcpPrompts<T>()` (5.4), and `Providers` holds the provider slots (4.13, "Provider slots as built"). `AddJobQueue` adds a job queue whose worker count its factory reads from the host's services when the runner's options are built, so that it can come from the plugin's own options; a count the host sets for that queue, in `cms.Jobs.Options.Queues` or under `FluentCms:Jobs:Queues`, wins, and a name that is empty or longer than 64 characters throws. `FluentCMS.Plugins.Abstractions` adds `AddModel<T>()`, `AddMigrations(assembly)`, `MapGroup(prefix)`, `MapManagement(prefix)`, `MapDelivery(prefix)` and `MapProtectedResource(prefix, options)` (4.7, "Protected resources as built") as extension methods ([ADR 0003](../decisions/0003-plugin-contribution-contracts.md)). **Tables.** A model contributor maps the plugin's entities on an EF `ModelBuilder`. Each table and view is named `cms_{plugin}_...`, with the hyphens of the name as underscores, and is neither `cms_{plugin}__migrations` nor a table of core or of another plugin; `AddFluentCms` checks this by mapping the contributor on a model without conventions and stops with `FCMS1523`. The same mapping, with PostgreSQL's limit of 63 set on it, holds the name of every table, view, column, key, foreign key, index and check constraint to 63 bytes in UTF-8 and stops with `FCMS1529` for a longer one: PostgreSQL keeps the first 63 bytes of a name without a word, and the baseline check (4.3) finds the tables, columns and indexes of the model by name, so it would warn or refuse at every start; on every provider, so that a plugin that runs on SQLite does not fail on PostgreSQL later. That is the name the model gives or else the one EF derives, such as the `IX_...` of an index given none, as EF derives it on PostgreSQL: EF cuts a derived name to 63 characters, which is 63 bytes only in ASCII, so a derived name built from a name with other characters is measured too. Columns are read per table, so a column named for one table alone, with entity splitting, TPT or TPC, is measured on it. What only EF's conventions would find, such as a property that no call of the contributor configures, is not on that model and is not measured ([#3533](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3533)). A content type whose table a plugin maps stops the host with `FCMS1206`. The contributors join `CmsDbContext` after the system tables, excluded from its migrations, and build `PluginDbContext<TPlugin>`, a scoped context with the provider's conventions that the plugin's code queries. **Streams.** `AddMigrations(assembly)` registers the plugin's stream: the migrations of the assembly that name `PluginDbContext<TPlugin>` in `[DbContext]`, with the history table `cms_{plugin}__migrations`, which `IDataProvider.ConfigurePluginMigrations` points at the database. The streams run after the system stream and before the content stream, in plugin order, and a second assembly for one plugin is `FCMS1525`. A model ahead of its migrations stops the migration with EF's pending model changes error. The assembly holds a set per provider, in a namespace ending in `Sqlite`, `PostgreSql` or `SqlServer`, and the host takes the set of its provider (4.3, "Plugin migration sets per provider as built"). **Endpoints.** The groups are relative to the path base and run their endpoints inside the request bridge's `OperationContext`, with no output cache unless an endpoint adds one. `MapManagement(prefix)` is under `/api/management/v1` with what core's Management groups have: the `management` scope (401 without a principal, 403 without the scope), the Management rate limit and CORS policy, and the OpenAPI document. `MapDelivery(prefix)` is under `/api/delivery/v1` with what core's Delivery group has, its output cache included, and an anonymous caller read as its site's Public role (4.10, "Plugin Delivery groups as built"). `MapGroup(prefix)` is under the CMS group with the Delivery rate limit and CORS policy and the plugin's name as its surface, which keeps it out of the OpenAPI document. "CMS auth by default" means that its endpoints need a caller the `FluentCms` scheme signed in, a bearer token or an API key of any class, and answer any other with a 401 problem (`FCMS3102`); an endpoint that is public on purpose, such as the media plugin's asset bytes at `/media/{id}/{filename}`, opts out with ASP.NET Core's `AllowAnonymous()`, on the endpoint or on the group. It is an endpoint filter after the request bridge's, as the `management` scope is, so no host needs `UseAuthorization` for it, and it runs before the per-site gate, so an anonymous caller learns nothing of where the plugin is disabled. Credentials that are sent and refused are a 401 even where anonymous is allowed, as on Delivery. Being signed in is all the group checks: what the caller may do is the plugin's to check with `IPermissionChecker`. The coarse limit before authentication covers these routes as it covers core's (4.10), so a flood of keys sent to them is turned away before any key is looked up. This answers decision 4 of the review of 7 Nov 2026 ([#2847](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/2847), [#2859](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/2859)): a plugin's group is not anonymous-capable by default. The startup report does not list the endpoints that allow anonymous, because each is a choice written in the plugin's code rather than a setting an operator could change, and a warning at every start for a public media route would teach operators to ignore the warnings; the Management plugin list names every endpoint. A prefix under a group of core, one whose first segment is a route parameter, and one that does not start with a slash or ends with one are `FCMS1524`. So is a `MapDelivery` prefix whose first segment is a route parameter or names a content type. **Codes.** A plugin's code is upper case letters and four digits, neither core's nor another plugin's, with a status from 400 to 599 and a title (`FCMS1522`). A `PluginException(code, message)`, a `HookRejectedException` and a validation error with the code get its status and title; a code the plugin did not add is 422. **Field types.** A plugin type's name is an ASCII letter followed by ASCII letters, digits, `.`, `-` or `_` (`FCMS1528`), as `IFieldType.Name` says, because `generate` writes it into code and refuses a schema snapshot whose field type is not such a name (B-106, [#3051](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3051)), and it is neither core's nor another plugin's (`FCMS1521`). The field type registry holds every `IFieldType` of the container to both rules, with an `ArgumentException`, so a type registered as a service without `AddFieldType` is refused as well. A setting of kind `FieldSettingKind.FieldReference` names a sibling field of the same type, component or block: schema validation refuses one that names no other field (`FCMS1520`), and schema introspection leaves the setting out for a caller who cannot read that field, as it does a slug's `source`. Such a setting is optional, as `source` is, so that a narrowed snapshot still loads; a plugin type that makes one required stops the host (`FCMS1526`). Core's `IFieldTypeRegistry` in the container has the plugins' types, and a host that builds its own `SchemaModel` builds it with that registry, as the test host does. The sample plugin of the tests (`tests/FluentCMS.Plugins.Sample`) uses every contribution and stays the fixture of the plugin host's tests; a plugin starts from the `fluentcms-plugin` template (6.2, B-94), which is not generated from it and uses the permissions, settings, hooks, model, migrations and Management endpoints of this section.

**Permission checks of plugins as built.** A plugin's service enforces its own permissions as core's services do, with `IPermissionChecker` (`FluentCMS.Authorization`, in the SDK): `AllowsAsync(permission, site)` and `RequireAsync(permission, site)`, which throws `PermissionDeniedException`, answered with 403, or 401 without a principal. `CanManageAsync()` says whether the principal may manage at all, which is the evaluator's `CanManage` (4.8): one that may not reads content alone, whatever its roles grant ([#3112](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3112)). `HoldsGrantInAsync(site)` says whether some grant of the principal reaches a site, whatever it permits there, as `GET /me/sites` decides which sites to list, for a plugin that serves what belongs to a site rather than what a permission names ([#3070](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3070)). A null site is the instance as a whole, which only a grant with no site limit reaches. A call outside an operation is refused with `InvalidOperationException` (`FCMS1408`, 2.3), so host code that acts as the system wraps it in `SystemOperation.Begin`. Core implements it with the checks of its Management services, so the endpoint carries the identity and the service decides. The webhooks plugin checks `webhooks.manage` with it.

**Problem descriptions for plugin surfaces as built** ([#2977](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/2977)). A plugin surface that does not answer with problem details, such as MCP, asks `IProblemDescriber` (`FluentCMS.Plugins`, in the SDK) what the REST APIs would answer an exception with: `Describe(exception)` gives a `ProblemDescription` with the stable `Code`, the HTTP `Status` the code stands for, its `Title`, a `Detail` for people and the validation `Errors`. Core implements it with the mapping of its own problem details and the codes of the host, those the plugins added with `AddErrorCode` included, and reads from the current operation whether the caller is signed in, which tells a 401 from a 403. An exception that says nothing of its cause is `FCMS3199` with status 500 and a message that holds nothing of the exception, and the surface that asked logs it, since core's own handler never sees it.

**Per-site enablement.** `cms_plugin_site (plugin, site_id, enabled)` read through `IPluginState`. A disabled plugin's endpoints return 404 for that site, its hooks, events, jobs and MCP contributions are skipped, and the Management plugin list reports it disabled so the admin hides its UI.

**Per-site enablement as built.** `cms_plugin_site` is keyed on plugin and site and goes with its site. A plugin with no row for a site is enabled there, and a row of a plugin that is no longer registered says nothing. `IPluginState.IsEnabledAsync(plugin, site)` (SDK) reads it. Each instance reads the rows whole and keeps them for 30 seconds. A write through the Management API drops them at once, so the instance that wrote answers by the new state from the next request, and another instance within 30 seconds. In a site where a plugin is disabled:

- its `MapGroup`, `MapManagement` and `MapDelivery` endpoints answer 404 (`FCMS1550`), after the signed-in caller or the `management` scope is checked; a copy of a `MapDelivery` answer in the output cache goes with the outbox delivery of `PluginDisabledEvent`, which evicts the site;
- its hooks are skipped for an operation of that site, and its in-process event handlers for an event of that site;
- its outbox handlers get no row for an event of that site, as an in-process handler is not called, so what happens there while it is off is never delivered to it. A row recorded before it was disabled is owed to it: the drain puts it back without using an attempt, as it does a row with no handler (`OutboxOptions.MissingHandlerDelay`), and delivers it once the plugin is enabled there again. A row still waiting `OutboxOptions.OrphanAfter` (7 days) after its event happened is moved to the dead-letter list with an error that names the plugin and the site, so it is not lost, and is retried from there once the plugin is enabled (4.11);
- its jobs of that site complete without running, as succeeded. A job of no site is the instance's and runs. When the state cannot be read, the job is released as a stopping host releases it, without using an attempt, and waits one `PollInterval`, as the drain puts a delivery back (item 3 of [#2887](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/2887)).

A hook or handler is the plugin's by the tiebreaker's rule above, and a job type or an outbox handler by the `AddJob` or `AddOutboxHandler` that added it. An activity row described with `AddActivity` is core's log and is always written. An event or job of no site is not gated. Contributions of the whole instance stay: field types, model, migrations, permissions and the settings schema, because every site shares the schema. The MCP plugin reads `IPluginState` and does not ask the MCP providers of a plugin that is disabled for the site of a request (5.4). Enabling or disabling raises `PluginEnabledEvent` or `PluginDisabledEvent` with the site as its source, which the activity log records as `plugin.enabled` or `plugin.disabled` with the target type `plugin` and the plugin's name. A write that changes nothing, such as enabling a plugin with no row, raises nothing. Log event ids 9230 to 9235.

**Provider slots.** Defined in the SDK, defaulted in core, replaceable by plugins or the host: `IFileStorage` (media plugin, local default), `IEmailSender` (core; SMTP through MailKit, plus a log-only sender for Development; Q9), `ISearchIndex` (search plugin), `IDistributedCache` (BCL memory default), `IDistributedLock` (core, `cms_lock` table default), `IImageProcessor` (media plugin), `IChatClient` and `IEmbeddingGenerator` from Microsoft.Extensions.AI (AI assist plugin).

**Provider slots as built.** `IProviderSlots` (`FluentCMS.Providers`, in the SDK) has `Use<TSlot, TImplementation>()`, which fills a slot with a singleton of the implementation, and `cms.Providers` is core's. The slots core fills are `IEmailSender` (SMTP, the log or a refusal, 4.7), `IDistributedLock` (`cms_lock`, 4.15), `IDistributedCache`, whose default is the framework's `MemoryDistributedCache`, and `IOutputCacheStore`, whose default is the framework's in-memory store, the registration `AddOutputCache()` makes, since its type is internal, and `ISearchIndex`, whose default `NoSearchIndex` means no index, so that a query with `q=` is refused until the search plugin fills it (4.4, "Search as built"); `IFileStorage` and `IImageProcessor` come with the media plugin, which declares them with its defaults (B-64, B-65) through `IPluginServiceBuilder.AddProviderSlot<TSlot, TDefault>()`: such a slot is filled and replaced like core's, and the report lists its default with the declaring plugin; a slot declared twice, by two plugins or by a plugin and core, stops the host with `FCMS1544`. The AI assist plugin declares `IAiClientFactory` the same way (B-83, 5.5): a factory that gives the `IChatClient` and the model of a site, where the plan had `IChatClient` itself as the slot, because a single client cannot follow the settings of each site; the interface is in the plugin, since the SDK takes no Microsoft.Extensions.AI dependency. `IEmbeddingGenerator` comes with semantic search (9.16). What fills a slot is settled when `AddFluentCms` ends: the host's `Use`, else a plugin's, else a service registered for the slot directly (by the host before `AddFluentCms` or in its callback, or by a plugin's `ConfigureServices`), else the default. A `Use` takes out every other registration of its slot, so it wins whatever the order of the calls and the registrations, and of two `Use` calls of one caller for a slot the last wins. Two plugins that replace one slot stop the host with `FCMS1540`, which names the plugins and their implementations, unless the host chooses with its own `Use`; a `Use` of a type that is not a slot stops it with `FCMS1541`, one whose implementation is abstract or an interface with `FCMS1542` when it is called, and one made after `AddFluentCms` has settled the slots, which would change nothing, with `FCMS1543`. A registration that the framework makes for a slot on core's behalf (Razor Pages adds the memory cache) is not the host's and gives way to the default. A registration after `AddFluentCms` comes last and wins, as for any service, and the report says so. A plugin calls the same `Use` on `IPluginServiceBuilder.Providers`, which core fills with `ProvidersBuilder.For(name)`, the replacements of that plugin. The startup report and `/system/diagnostics` list the slots in the order core declares them under `providerSlots`, each with `slot`, `implementation` (the type in use; for a factory the type it returns, or null when it throws), `source` (`default`, `host`, `plugin` or `services`) and `plugin`. Codes `FCMS1540` to `FCMS1544`; no event ids.

**Settings.** `[Settings("media")] sealed class MediaSettings` is persisted per site in `cms_setting (site_id, key, json, updated_at, updated_by)` with instance defaults and per-site overrides. `ISettings<T>.GetAsync(site)` reads it; `SettingsChangedEvent` follows writes; the Management API exposes get, put and reset per area with the JSON schema of the type so the admin can render it, and lists the areas.

**Settings as built.** `cms.Settings.Add<T>()` registers a class that has `[Settings("area")]` and a parameterless constructor, and a bad one stops the host (`FCMS2801`). Its permission `settings.{area}.manage` is in the catalogue. `GET /settings` (4.10) lists it with the plugin whose `AddSettings<T>()` added it, unless core marks the class as its own state with the internal `[UnlistedSettings]`, as it marks `seeding`, the record of the seeders; the routes of such an area still answer a caller with its permission. A class whose area is read from the instance defaults only, with `ISettings<T>.GetDefaultAsync`, says so with `[Settings("area", InstanceOnly = true)]`, as `branding` and the MCP plugin's `mcp` do, and `GET /settings` gives it as `instanceOnly`, for a plugin's area as for core's ([#3499](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3499)). It only describes the area in `GET /settings` and changes nothing about how the area is read or written: a site write through `PUT /settings/{area}` is still accepted and stored, and `ISettings<T>.GetAsync(site)` still gives it. Such a write has no effect only because the area's own code reads with `GetDefaultAsync`, and the flag is true only when it does. `cms_setting` also has `id` and `version`, and two unique indexes (one over `key` where `site_id` is null, one over `site_id, key` elsewhere); a site's rows go with the site. A read resolves a whole document: the site's row, else the instance row, else the code default, and a stored row the class can no longer read is skipped with a warning in the log, so one bad row never takes a site down, and a key that a stored document lacks, such as one added since or one marked `[Required]`, takes its code default, except a secret key of a site's row, which the site inherits (below). Reads are cached per scope for `cms.Settings.CacheDuration` (30 seconds): a write on the instance drops the cache of its area at once, on every scope, and another instance sees it when its own reads expire, which is the most it can lag. A write needs `settings.{area}.manage` for the site, or from a grant with no site limit for the instance defaults (`SettingsAccessDeniedException`), goes through a context of its own, so it saves nothing the caller has tracked and drops nothing the caller has tracked, and replaces the whole document with one statement that names the version it read, starting again when another writer got there first, so the last of two writes stands; a caller that names the version it read (0 for no row; the Management endpoint's `If-Match`) gets `FCMS2808` when the row has moved, for a reset too. A document is refused with `FCMS2803` (not a JSON object, too large or not JSON), `FCMS2804` (a key the type does not have, one error per key) or `FCMS2805` (a value of the wrong type or one that breaks a validation attribute, on the class, its nested objects, the items of lists and the values of dictionaries, with the path of the key such as `limits.port` or `many[2].port`), and the error names the key and never the value. An area of core may add rules its class cannot state, which a write and an import run after these: the `preview` area holds its templates to the schema (4.7). After the commit it raises `SettingsChangedEvent`, whose source carries the site (empty for the instance defaults), so the activity row is the site's; the event holds the area and nothing of the values. The row, its event and its activity row are not atomic: the event is raised after the commit, so a failure in between leaves the write without them. A write inside a transaction is refused (`FCMS2807`), and an unknown site is `FCMS2806`. A property of the class itself that is marked `[SettingsSecret]`, or named like a secret, is stored with the rest of the document but appears in no event, activity row, log line, validation message or schema default, and the schema marks it `writeOnly`; `[SettingsSecret]` on a property below the top level stops the host (`FCMS2801`), and so does a nested property named like a secret, because only the top level is kept out of reads, events and schemas (`[SettingsSecret]` is the real control; a name is also taken for a secret as a safety net, by a rule of its own that is narrower than the scrubber of events and logs, which reads free text: the name is read as words, ignoring digits and a version marker at its end, by its C# name as well as its JSON name, and is a secret when its last word is `password`, `passphrase`, `secret`, `token`, `pwd` or `apikey`, or its last two are `api`, `private`, `secret`, `access`, `signing` or `encryption` followed by `key`, `client secret` or `connection string`, plurals included, so `Keywords`, `SortKey` and `AccessKeyId` are not); an error under a secret names the secret's key and nothing below it, since a dictionary key may be as secret as its value. A secret is protected at rest ([#3270](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3270)). A write protects the value of every secret key that holds one with Data Protection, under the purpose `FluentCMS.Settings.Secret` with the area, the key and the scope (the site's id, or `instance`) as sub-purposes, and stores it in the row as the string `fcms-protected:v1:` followed by the payload. So a reader of the database, a replica or a backup of the database alone holds no secret, and a value copied to another key, area or scope, such as an instance `apiKey` into a site's row, is not read. A null stays null, and the other keys stay plain JSON. Every read of a row unprotects its secrets before anything else looks at it, so resolving, inheriting, keeping what a write leaves out, the redacted read, the export and the import see the document as before, and the cache holds it unprotected. An import never writes a secret: the export leaves them out, a secret key that a package names anyway is left out too, and each keeps what the site has, as a write that leaves it out does. The document is checked with the secrets of the site's own row, which the row keeps, never with the instance's or a package's. A secret the class requires, by `[JsonRequired]` or `[Required]`, that the site's own row holds no value for, as for a site without a row of its own, refuses the area with `FCMS2805` on the key, since no value is made up for it and the instance's is not copied; this is checked again on the row the write itself reads, so a reset in between cannot store a site's row without it (#3379). A value the key ring cannot unprotect (its key gone, as after a restore of the database without its key ring or a ring certificate replaced without keeping the old one, or the value changed or moved) reads as its code default, which the key also holds when the class cannot read the document without it, such as a `[JsonRequired]` secret. In a site's row the class can read, it is the site's own and is not inherited from the instance; a row the class cannot read is skipped by the read as before, so its site falls back to the instance. A warning (event id 9804) names the area, the key and the scope, never the value. A write keeps such a value in the row as it is stored unless the document names its key, even as null, or the row is a site's row the class cannot read, which a write replaces with its secrets as before, so the site inherits them again (each that the document leaves out is lost with a warning, event id 9802); an instance row the class cannot read keeps such a value, which changes nothing a read gives, so saving another key of the area never destroys a secret that a restored ring could read again; `ISettings<T>.SetAsync` names every key and so replaces it. A secret value that does not carry the prefix was written before #3270 and is read as it is. The next write of its document protects it, a form of the admin sent back without its secrets included. The startup report lists `settings-secrets-unprotected` for such documents and `settings-secrets-unreadable` for those with a value the ring cannot unprotect, each with the number of documents and their areas; the first names `fluentcms settings reprotect`, which protects them all in one pass. A check that cannot read the table, as under `doctor` before the migrations, logs event id 9805 with the type of the exception and lists nothing. A plaintext value may still be in a backup taken before that write, so the operator replaces it at its provider. A value sent with the prefix is protected like any other, so a caller cannot store a payload of its choosing. The protection is only as strong as the key ring, which is kept in the same database and is unencrypted without a key ring certificate (`data-protection-keys-unencrypted`, `FCMS1106` outside Development). Losing the ring, or replacing its certificate without keeping the old one, loses every secret setting, and both report lines say so. The payload names the ring key that protected it, so the ring's own key roll keeps reading old values. `fluentcms settings reprotect` ([#3364](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3364)), a command of the host that the operator runs on purpose before an old ring key or certificate is retired, protects every stored secret again under the current default key: for each row of a registered area it unprotects each secret key that holds a value, a plaintext one of before #3270 included, protects it again, and writes the row back over the version it read, which goes up as with any write, reading it again when another writer got there first. A value already under the default key, as Data Protection tells (`requiresMigration`), is kept as it is, so a row without a value to protect again is not written and a second run changes nothing; the values do not change, so no event is raised. A row with a value the ring cannot unprotect is left as it is; a value with the prefix under a key the class does not read as a secret (one it no longer has or no longer marks a secret) is not the pass's to protect and is left as it is too, and named when it is not under the default key ([#3436](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3436)). Every row of an area that no class of the host registers is left with every value unchanged and named, since the pass cannot tell which of its values are secrets, nor whether the plugin that owns it is only not loaded in that run; such a row needs an older key, and fails the command, only when a value of it with the prefix is not under the default key. The command names each by area and scope (and the keys), never by value or what is stored, and ends with `FCMS3308` and exit code 1 when a row still needs an older key. It names the id of the default key it protected under, read from the ring before the pass, which is not secret, so that the operator checks it is the key to keep before retiring another. Nothing removes the rows of an unregistered area; a way to is left to the maintainers. A command and not a job, because it is the step of a retirement the operator plans, and a job could run it before the key the operator keeps is the default. The stored shape changes only inside the JSON column, so no model migration was needed. A document written without a secret key keeps what is stored, because the admin never sees a secret and must not wipe it by sending its form back, while a key that is sent, even as null, replaces it; a stored null is kept like a value, so that no code default replaces it. An inherited secret is not copied (10.4, H8, [#3005](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3005)): a secret key that a site's row does not hold (the site never set it, its first write left it out, or the key was added to the class since) is inherited, and every read of the site fills it in from the instance row as that is then, or leaves it to its code default when there is no instance row the class can read. A write of a site that leaves such a key out keeps it inherited; the checks of the document see the inherited value, as a read will, and the row does not keep it. So a secret replaced in the instance defaults reaches every site that inherits it, at once on the instance that wrote it and on another when its cached reads expire. Key by key, a site leaves the key out to keep what it has, sends a value to hold its own, sends null to hold none and inherit nothing, and goes back to inheriting by a reset of the area (`ISettings<T>.ResetAsync`, or `DELETE /settings/{area}` of the Management API, 4.10), which makes the whole document follow the defaults again, after which a write that leaves the key out keeps it inherited. A typed `SetAsync` names every property, so each secret of its value, null included, is the site's own from then on, and a value that `GetAsync` read and that is written back makes what the site inherited its own. `ISettings<T>.ResolveAsync(site)` gives the settings with `ResolvedSettings<T>.InheritedSecrets`, the secret properties the site inherits by their names in C#, from the same read, so that code which sends a secret where a site's settings say can tell the site's own secret from the operator's. The instance defaults inherit nothing: a key their row lacks takes its code default. A row that the class cannot read was skipped by the read, so it gives nothing: a site whose row is skipped inherits every secret its next write leaves out, and an instance row that is skipped gives the sites' inherited secrets their code defaults. A stored secret that the class would no longer accept, written under an older shape, is not kept: it takes its code default and a warning in the log names the key, never the value; one that a site inherits is not the site's to lose, and is neither kept in its row nor reported for it. A row saved before #3005 holds a copy of what it inherited, which counts as the site's own; previews make no promise to upgrade (H5), so it is not migrated, and a reset of the area makes the site inherit again (the baseline of H5 recreates such a database anyway). The stored shape is the same JSON in `cms_setting`, without the inherited keys, so no model migration was needed. The Management endpoint reads through a redacted resolve: the document without its secret keys, its source (site, default or code), the version of the row a write to that site would replace (0 when it has none, even if the document came from the instance default, and the version of the row that is there even if it was skipped because the class cannot read it, so that an unreadable row can be saved over), the secret keys that hold a readable value in a stored document (`secretsSet`, an inherited one included), and the secret keys the site inherits (`secretsInherited`: every one for a site with no row of its own, none for the instance defaults). A secret of a readable row that the key ring cannot unprotect is in neither list, whatever the class requires ([#3379](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3379)): it reads as its code default, which a `[JsonRequired]` secret holds so that the row stays readable, is not inherited, and the startup report lists it (`settings-secrets-unreadable`); a secret a site inherits from an instance row whose value the ring cannot unprotect is in `secretsInherited` and not in `secretsSet`; in a row the class cannot read, the read falls back to the instance as for any such row. A value is never shown, inherited or not. The schema of a type is `System.Text.Json`'s, with the validation attributes of its properties added.

The name rule for secrets applies only to a property whose value can be text or key material, which a secret is ([#3079](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3079)): a Boolean, an enum, a number of up to 64 bits, a date, a time or a duration is never a secret by its name, alone, nullable or as the items of a list, so the AI assist plugin's `maxOutputTokens`, an `int`, is returned by a read, has its default in the schema and is not `writeOnly`. A string, a dictionary of any values (its keys are text), bytes as the items of any list or array (`byte[]`, `List<byte>`, `sbyte[]`), `Memory<byte>`, a `Guid`, an `Int128`, a `UInt128`, a `decimal`, an object and a type that enumerates itself stay under the rule, alone or in a list. The check of a nested property named like a secret follows the same rule, so a nested `int MaxTokens` does not stop the host. `[SettingsSecret]` still marks a property of any type.

**Manifest exposure.** `GET /api/management/v1/plugins` lists name, version, SDK range, dependencies, contributions (field types, permissions, endpoints, jobs, tools) and enablement per site. As built (4.10), `tools` names what the MCP tool providers of each plugin offer the caller (5.4).

**Plugin streams and the baseline as built (B-107).** The stream of each first-party plugin starts with one migration, `Initial`, for each provider (4.3). The runner refuses a plugin stream whose history holds migrations but not the first of its set with `FCMS1307`, before anything is applied, so a plugin that replaces its migrations with a new first one has every database of the old ones refused rather than half-applied.

### 4.14 Transfer and schema snapshot

Rows 10.9, 10.10, 1.19.

**Export package.** A zip, written and read as a stream: `manifest.json` (format version, FluentCMS version, schema hash, site and its locales, created at, options, the list of sections), `schema.json`, `content/{type}.jsonl` (one line per entry with both states), `settings.json` (without secrets), `roles.json` (optional) and `plugins/{plugin}/` for what a plugin contributes, which for the media plugin is the asset and folder metadata and, optionally, the files. Users, members, API keys and the activity log are never in a package. A package is of one site. `POST /transfer/export` answers the zip as a stream, with `transfer.manage`.

**Import.** `POST /transfer/import` takes the zip as its body and answers a stream of progress lines that ends with the report of creates, updates, skips, conflicts and errors. Schema hash check (equal, or compatible when the instance has every type and field the package uses), upsert by id, per-type selection, import into the site of the request, and a dry run with the same report. The import writes through the content service as the system in an import mode that keeps the ids, times and published state of the package, so validation, hooks, references, versions and the activity log behave as for any write. Each entry is its own transaction, and an import that stopped is finished by running it again. Everything an import writes carries `Origin.Import`, and the webhooks plugin delivers nothing for it unless the import asks for webhooks.

**No jobs, no stored package (10.3, P7).** The first design ran export and import as jobs with a download from `/transfer/export/{jobId}`. Core has nowhere to keep a package that every instance can reach, so both are streamed within the request instead.

**Plugin data (10.3, P11).** `ITransferContributor` in the SDK (`FluentCMS.Transfer`) lets a plugin write sections on export and read them on import, with the dry run. The media plugin implements it in M6; the other first-party plugins can follow without a change to the SDK.

**Left out of the first package.** `references.jsonl`, since references are derived from the fields on import, and versions, which the list of sections in the manifest lets a later release add.

**Transfer as built (B-92).** [#3015](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3015). No migration, no job and no stored package: both endpoints run in the request, and a caller that goes away cancels them.

- `POST /api/management/v1/transfer/export` takes an optional JSON body, `{ "types": [..], "globalTypes": true, "publishedOnly": false, "roles": false, "options": { "mediaFiles": "true" } }`, and answers `application/zip` as it is written. It needs `transfer.manage` in the site of the request and reads as the system, hidden fields too, outside the trash. An export that has a global type, as one without `types` has unless `globalTypes` is false, needs it for the instance too, since the entries of a global type are every site's, as an import of them does ([#3069](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3069)). A caller with the grant in one site gets 403, whose message names `"globalTypes": false` and `types`, rather than a package without the global types, so that a request gives one package to whoever may make it; the help of `--types` of `fluentcms export` says so. One export of a site runs at a time across every instance, through `IDistributedLock` (`transfer.export.{site}`), and a second is refused with `FCMS3205` (409). The activity row `transfer.exported` names the site, the types and the count of entries. The entries of every type are read in batches of 100, four queries a batch (document rows, shared rows, locale rows and references, each by `IN (ids)`), from one snapshot, so a save during the export changes nothing in the package and no reference points at an entry it lacks: `REPEATABLE READ` on PostgreSQL, a deferred transaction in WAL mode on SQLite, and `SNAPSHOT` isolation on SQL Server where the database allows it (`ALTER DATABASE CURRENT SET ALLOW_SNAPSHOT_ISOLATION ON`, which no migration runs). Without one, as on a default SQL Server database, the export reads each batch as it is then and logs event 9189. Settings, roles and the contributors' files are read after the snapshot (`IDataProvider.BeginSnapshotAsync`; [#3305](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3305)). The export writes as it reads, so the snapshot is open while the entries go to the client: a slow reader keeps a PostgreSQL `REPEATABLE READ` transaction (vacuum cannot remove the rows it may still see), a SQL Server `SNAPSHOT` transaction (the version store in tempdb grows) or a SQLite reader (the WAL is not checkpointed and grows) open for that time; no writer waits for it. The snapshot stays, since a consistent package is the point of it, and is bounded ([#3485](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3485)): `FluentCms:Transfer:ExportSnapshotTimeout` (1 hour, at most 1 day) is the longest it is held, counted from its first read, not from when a reader stalls, so an export whose entries take longer than that to go out, however steadily it is read, is cut too. Kestrel's `MinResponseDataRate` (240 bytes a second after 5 seconds, unless the host changes it) already ends a response whose reader stopped, but not one read slowly, nor one behind a server that does not apply it; there was no other bound, since the export is no write and no request timeout is set. At the bound the output is cut, so nothing more is written to it, and the response is aborted, whatever write the export waits in (a zip ends each file with a write that heeds no token); a write the abort ended may throw or return as if it went through, and either way the export ends as stopped, never as finished: warning 9190 and then 9182 are logged, the activity row `transfer.exported` is not written, and the snapshot ends. The client, which had the 200 and the start of the body, sees the connection reset and nothing that names the option: `fluentcms export --url` fails with `FCMS3252` (the instance could not be reached or stopped answering), whether the handler reports the reset as an `HttpIOException` or as the `IOException` of the transport, and deletes the file it was writing. `fluentcms export` run in the host fails with the cancellation, which names the option, exits with 1 and leaves no file. Either way the server's log has warning 9190, which names the site and the option. A host whose exports need longer raises the option, as `FluentCms__Transfer__ExportSnapshotTimeout=02:00:00`. Without a snapshot, as on a default SQL Server database, nothing is held and the bound does not apply. A package that stopped, at the bound or for any other reason, is not completed: its output is cut before the archive is let go, so the central directory, which the archive writes last, is never written after the files that went out. So the bytes of a cut export, even where a transport kept every write, end without the central directory the export would have written, and the import refuses them with `FCMS3200`. Format version 1 has no end marker of its own; the export never writes the end of its zip unless the package is whole. The bound is decided by whether the output was cut, whatever a read or a write the bound ended throws, a provider's own error for a cancelled command included.
- The package, format version 1: `manifest.json` (`formatVersion`, `fluentCmsVersion`, `schemaHash`, `site` with id, slug, name and default locale, `locales`, `createdAt`, `options`, `sections`), `schema.json` (the snapshot of 4.2), `content/{type}.jsonl`, `settings.json`, `roles.json` when asked, and `plugins/{contributor}/`. A line of content is `{ "id", "locales": [{ "locale", "status", "draftVersion", "publishedVersion", "createdAt", "createdBy", "updatedAt", "updatedBy", "publishedAt", "firstPublishedAt", "draft": {..}, "published": {..} }] }`, with each state's fields as a version holds them, shared ones included; `draft` is left out by `publishedOnly`, and `published` for a locale that is not published. `settings.json` is the site's own documents, redacted as the Management API shows them; `roles.json` the site's roles that are not built in, with their grants.
- `POST /api/management/v1/transfer/import` takes the zip as its body (`application/zip` or `application/octet-stream`), up to `FluentCms:Transfer:MaxPackageSize` (1 GiB; `FCMS3204`, 413), which the import counts itself as it reads a body that comes chunked, with no length; the endpoint lifts the server's limit of the request, so that no other code answers first. The body is kept in a temporary file deleted when the request ends, because a zip is read from its end, and on Unix only its owner reads the file, since the temporary folder is shared. Query: `dryRun`, `overwrite`, `missingLocales=refuse|skip`, `authors=keep|importer` (`importer` records the caller as the updater of what the import writes and the creator of what it creates, a locale the instance has keeping its own creator; `keep`, the default, keeps the package's authors; [#3305](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3305)), `webhooks`, `roles`, `types` (comma separated) and `options` (`name=value` pairs for the contributors). A line of a type that is not localized with any locale but the empty one is an invalid item (`FCMS3212`). It needs `transfer.manage` in the site of the request and imports into that site, and a package that has the file of a selected global type needs it for the instance, whatever size the file declares, as writing a global type does (4.8). An export has a file for every global type, empty or not, so a site's administrator names the other types with `types`, or `--types` of `fluentcms import`, as the refusal and the help of both say. One import of a site runs at a time across every instance (`transfer.import.{site}`), and a second is refused with `FCMS3207` (409). The lock is taken once the body is in, so that a slow upload holds none, and an import that loses it, as when another holder took it after the lease could not be renewed, stops as a cancelled one does (warning 9186), at its next step, rather than going on beside the next holder. A lease without fencing does not rule out that two imports of a site write for a moment at once, since `Lost` fires only when a renewal fails. Before any write and before the answer starts it refuses a body that is no package, a package over a bound its central directory shows (below), two files of one name, which `ZipArchive.GetEntry` reads one of, a file that holds more than its zip entry declares, a file of a compression method `System.IO.Compression` cannot read, a `manifest.json` without its site or locales, which the serializer does not hold to the record's nullable annotations, or a `settings.json` or `roles.json` it cannot read (`FCMS3200`), a format other than 1 (`FCMS3201`), a schema the instance cannot hold (`FCMS3202`, with an error per type or field), which includes a file of `content/` whose type the instance does not have whatever the hashes say, and a locale of the manifest the site does not allow (`FCMS3203`, unless `missingLocales=skip`). Every file of a package is read through one stream, `PackageEntryStream`, which refuses with `FCMS3200` once a file yields more bytes than its entry declares, so the sizes the import checks are the sizes it reads. `System.IO.Compression` reads a stored file to its compressed size whatever size it declares, and stops a deflated one at its declared size, so each file whose compressed size is larger than its declared one is read through before any write, which costs no more than the package, and every other file is opened, which reads its local header only. `schema.json` is read only when its hash is not the instance's. The schema is taken when its hash is the instance's, or when the instance has each selected type with the same kind, scope and localization, and every field of those types and of the components and blocks they use, with the same field type, multiplicity, localization and target; more types, fields or blocks in the instance are fine.
- Bounds of an import ([#3065](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3065)), so that a package never costs much more than it holds, each an option of `FluentCms:Transfer` checked when the host starts (more than 0, or `FCMS1101`): `MaxEntries` (100,000 files), `MaxUnpackedSize` (the sum of the sizes the files declare unpacked; `MaxPackageSize` when not set), `MaxJsonSize` (64 MiB for each of `manifest.json`, `schema.json`, `settings.json` and `roles.json`), `MaxReadSize` (the compressed bytes the import reads; twice `MaxPackageSize` when not set), `MaxLineLength` (16 MiB for a line of a `.jsonl` file) and `MaxReportItems` (1,000). What the central directory shows is refused with `FCMS3200` before any file is read: the number of files the end of the central directory declares, read before `ZipArchive` holds an entry for each (it stops once it reads more than that number), more files than `MaxEntries`, a sum of declared sizes over `MaxUnpackedSize`, one of the four JSON files over `MaxJsonSize`, and compressed sizes that add up to more than the package, since files whose data overlap would have the import read the same bytes once for each. A JSON file is read through `PackageEntryStream`, so its text is never larger than its bound. Each file the import opens counts its compressed size against `MaxReadSize` and its declared size against `MaxUnpackedSize` again, so a file opened twice, by a contributor or by many lines, costs twice, and `PackageEntryStream` refuses a line of a `.jsonl` file, a contributor's too, once it is longer than `MaxLineLength`, before a reader holds it. A bound that only reading shows, or compressed data that cannot be read, stops the run where it is reached, a dry run alike: what was written stays, as for an import that stopped, the last item of the report is `{ "section": "@package", "id": <the file>, "outcome": "error", "code": "FCMS3200" }` with the option in its message, the activity row is written, and the warning 9188 is logged. The refusal stays: every later open of a file, and every report of a contributor that caught it, throws it again.
- The answer is `application/x-ndjson`: a line `{ "section", "counts" }` when a section ends, `{ "section", "done" }` every 100 entries, and a last line `{ "report": { "dryRun", "total", "sections", "items", "omitted" } }`, where each count is `created`, `updated`, `skipped`, `conflicts` and `errors`, and each item is `{ "section", "id", "outcome", "code", "message" }`. The counts hold every item, and `items` lists the conflicts and errors only, in the order the import took them and at most `MaxReportItems` of them (the item that stopped an import is listed whatever their number), with `omitted` the number it leaves out, which the text output of `fluentcms import` says too. So the answer does not grow with the entries that were imported, as `ITransferImport.Report` says, nor with what a line holds: the message of an entry that the instance refuses names its first 20 validation errors and the number of the others, and an item's id and message are cut at 4,096 characters. The sections are the content types by name, `@settings`, `@roles`, `@plugins` (a package folder that no contributor enabled in the site reads, `FCMS3214`), `@package` (the bound that stopped an import) and those the contributors name. The order is settings, roles, contributors, then content, so that an entry can refer to what a contributor brought.
- Import mode: each entry runs in a scope and a transaction of its own, through `ContentService` as `Principal.System` with `Origin.Import`, which the service lets the system principal use only while the import's scoped mode is set; `SystemOperation.Allows` is unchanged. The caller is the operation's `Actor`, so versions and activity name who imported. An entry the instance lacks is created under the package's id; one it has is skipped when every locale has the package's `updatedAt` (to the microsecond, which every provider keeps) and status, is a conflict when a locale is newer in the instance unless `overwrite=true`, and is otherwise updated. An id that belongs to another type or site is `FCMS3210`, and one in the trash `FCMS3211`. A line without an id, which reads as the empty GUID, or with the empty GUID, and a line with a draft or published version number outside 1 to 1,000,000,000, so that no write after the import overflows it, are `FCMS3212` ([#3069](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3069)). A locale of a line of a localized type that the site does not allow, which the manifest need not list, makes the entry `FCMS3203` unless `missingLocales=skip`, which leaves the locale out. The published state of each locale is written as the draft and published, a locale the package does not publish is unpublished, and a draft that differs is then written over it, each through the steps of any write: validation, hooks, references, versions, events and cache eviction. A publish in import mode takes its references' targets in whatever state they are, since the package brings them too. Once written, the rows of `cms_document` take the package's `createdAt`, `createdBy`, `updatedAt`, `updatedBy`, `publishedAt`, `firstPublishedAt` and an `Unpublished` status, and an entry the import created takes the package's draft and published version numbers, its own versions renumbered to them; an updated entry keeps its own numbering. A dry run runs the same steps and rolls each entry's transaction back, which takes its events and outbox rows with it, and counts the assets that the contributors' dry run reports as there, so its report is the import's. The steps include the hooks ([#3069](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3069)): a hook refuses in a dry run what it refuses in the import, and a hook whose effect leaves the transaction, such as a call to another service, acts in a dry run too, as the description of `dryRun` and the help of `--dry-run` say. A dry run without the hooks was declined: core cannot tell a hook that acts outside the database from one that does not, and a dry run that skipped them would not report what a hook refuses, after hooks included, whose failure rolls the write back. An activity row `transfer.imported` carries the counts; a dry run writes none.
- Plugin data: `ITransferContributor` (SDK, `FluentCMS.Transfer`) with `Name`, `ExportAsync(ITransferExport)` and `ImportAsync(ITransferImport)`, added with `IPluginServiceBuilder.AddTransferContributor<T>()`. A contributor runs only in a site where its plugin is enabled, writes and reads only `plugins/{Name}/`, gets the options, the dry-run and overwrite flags and both sites, and reports each item with a `TransferOutcome`. A name that is not lower kebab-case or that two contributors share stops every transfer (`FCMS3230`).
- Spans `transfer export` and `transfer import`, codes `FCMS3200` to `FCMS3230` (the media plugin adds `FCMS3220` and `FCMS3221`), log event ids 9180 to 9188.

**Decisions as built (B-92).** Settled for [#3015](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3015):

- Global types are in a package unless `globalTypes` is false, and their entries are compared by id across sites like any other. Exporting them needs `transfer.manage` for the instance, as importing them does ([#3069](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3069)).
- Users, members, API keys, the activity log, schedules, versions and the trash are in no package. A `createdBy` or `updatedBy` the instance does not know is kept as the id it is.
- Roles go into a package and come out of it only when asked, and a grant limited to the site of the package is limited to the site of the import. The caller needs `roles.manage` in the site and every grant the role had and gets, as an edit of its own would, so an import is no way to raise one's own grants; the write is then the import's.
- The settings of an area the instance does not have are an item error (`FCMS3213`), a document equal to the site's is skipped, and the caller needs `settings.{area}.manage` in the site.
- The export request may write synchronously to the response body, because a zip written to a body that cannot seek ends each file that way.

**The commands as built (B-101).** [#3024](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3024). `fluentcms export --output <file> [--site <slug>] [--types <list>] [--published-only] [--roles] [--media-files]` and `fluentcms import <file> [--dry-run] [--site <slug>] [--types <list>] [--overwrite] [--webhooks] [--missing-locales refuse|skip] [--roles] [--json]` run in project mode, where the host calls `TransferExporter` and `TransferImporter` itself as the system with `Origin.Cli`, or with `--url`, where the tool calls the two endpoints with the key in `Authorization: ApiKey` and the site, by slug or id, in `X-Cms-Site`. Both modes build the same request (`--media-files` is the option `mediaFiles=true`, and the options are an empty object without it), so they write the same package but for the manifest's `createdAt`. A package is written to a temporary file beside the target and moved over it once complete, so a failed export leaves the target as it was. With `--url` the tool takes an answer as the package only when it is `application/zip`, starts with a zip's local file header and, once complete, opens as a zip; otherwise, such as for the sign-in page of a proxy, it writes nothing, so the file at `--output` stays as it was, and exits 1 with `FCMS3253` ([#3067](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3067)). Its client follows no redirect, which would drop the key and read what the other address answers: a 3xx to `export` or `import` is `FCMS3253` too, naming the status and the scheme, host and port it points to, never its path or query. An import prints the progress lines as text on standard error and the report as text on standard output, or with `--json` the endpoint's last line `{"report":{..}}` unchanged, in both modes alike (`ImportOutput` in Core, which the tool uses too). It exits 1 with `FCMS3250` when the report has a conflict or an error, on a dry run too, so a pipeline can stop on it. A refusal keeps its code in both modes: the endpoint's problem code first (`FCMS3200` to `FCMS3207`, `FCMS2301` for a site that does not exist), and inside the host the same codes from the services' `ValidationException`. The tool adds `FCMS3248` (the package cannot be read), `FCMS3251` (any other refusal of the instance; a 401 and a 403 say what the key needs), `FCMS3252` (the instance cannot be reached) and `FCMS3253` (a redirect, an export's answer that is not a package, and an import's answer that is not JSON lines or ends before its report). The key is never written out, and of the address only its scheme, host and port, in `FCMS3252`. The HTTP client has no timeout, since a package takes as long as it takes; Ctrl+C stops it. `import --roles` is added to the issue's options, so that a package exported with `--roles` can bring them.

**Schema tools.** `fluentcms schema snapshot` from code or from an instance; `fluentcms schema validate`; `fluentcms schema diff` between the code, a running instance's introspection, a schema file or the applied history, with a non-zero exit on drift so CI can gate deploys. The diff marks destructive changes and reports a field removed beside a field added of the same kind as a possible rename, with the `[FormerNames]` line to add; `migrations add` prints the same report and refuses a destructive change without `--allow-destructive` (10.3, P5). `fluentcms schema apply` brings the content tables of a host whose types are data up to its schema (4.3, 10.3, P4).

**Schema tools as built (B-97, [#3020](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3020)).** `schema snapshot [--output <file>]` writes the canonical snapshot of 4.2, inside the host from its schema and, with `--url`, from `GET /api/management/v1/schema/snapshot` byte for byte, so both give the same bytes for a key that reads every type. `schema validate` builds the host's schema and prints every `FCMS12xx` error on standard output, then `FCMS3320` on standard error, and exits 1. `--schema <file>` checks a file: in the tool's own process with no host, unless `--project` or `--assembly` names one, and then inside the host with its field types. Without a host a field type that core does not register, such as the media plugin's, is accepted unchecked and named in a note. `schema diff` reads from the schema that is deployed to the new one, so `added` is what a deploy adds and a destructive change is what it would lose: from the instance of `--url` (read with `schema.read`), the newest row of `cms_schema_history` (`--applied`) or a schema file (`--schema`) to the host's schema, and from `--url` or `--applied` to a schema file when `--schema` is given beside them. `--url` with a schema file needs no host; `--url` alone asks the host for its snapshot with `schema snapshot --output` into a file of the tool's. It prints the report of 4.2, one line of JSON with `--json` (`{"changes":[{"kind","family","type","field","formerField","properties","destructive","text"}],"possibleRenames":[{"family","type","from","to","attribute"}]}`), and exits 0 when nothing differs and 1 with `FCMS3322` on standard error when something does. An empty history is `FCMS3323` and a history that cannot be read or holds another shape `FCMS3324`. `migrations add` prints the report from the schema on the model snapshot to the host's, from nothing for the first migration, and refuses with `FCMS3327`, writing nothing, when a change is destructive or a field is renamed, unless `--allow-destructive` is given; a model snapshot written before it carried the schema leaves the change unknown, which is refused the same way with `FCMS3328`.

### 4.15 Infrastructure and operations

Rows 10.3, 10.5 to 10.8, 10.11 to 10.15.

**Configuration.** Everything under the `FluentCms` section (`FluentCms:Database:Provider`, `FluentCms:Database:MigrateOnStartup`, `FluentCms:Security:*`, `FluentCms:Delivery:Cache:*`, `FluentCms:Jobs:*`), bound to options with data annotations and `ValidateOnStart`. Environment variables map through the standard `__` separator, which is how the reference host is configured. Secret providers (user secrets, Key Vault, AWS Secrets Manager) are the host's configuration builders; core takes no dependency on them.

**Health.** `/health/live` (process) and `/health/ready` (database, storage provider, job runner heartbeat, plugins) through `Microsoft.Extensions.Diagnostics.HealthChecks`; detailed JSON at `/api/management/v1/system/health` behind a permission.

**Observability.** One `ActivitySource` (`FluentCMS`) with spans for content operations, hooks, jobs, webhooks and MCP calls; one `Meter` with request counts by surface, query duration, job latency, cache hit ratio, webhook outcomes; structured logs with event ids and `FCMS` codes. Exporters are the host's choice. Nothing is sent to the project.

**Health and observability, as built.** `FluentCmsHealthCheckTags` (`FluentCMS.Diagnostics`, in the SDK, so plugins tag their checks) has two tags, `live` and `ready`, and `/health/live` and `/health/ready` run the checks tagged with each through `Microsoft.Extensions.Diagnostics.HealthChecks`, with no authentication and no rate limit policy of the endpoints. They stay reachable without credentials, because the image's `HEALTHCHECK` and an orchestrator's probes call them, so they are kept cheap and bounded instead: the FluentCMS authentication step skips the `health` surface, so a key sent there is never looked up, and the check before authentication counts them per client address in a partition of their own, apart from the coarse limit, with `FluentCms:RateLimits:Health:PermitLimit` (1200 a minute, twenty a second from one address, far above what an orchestrator sends one instance). A path under `/health` that no endpoint answers counts there too. A refusal is the same 429 problem, and the probes carry no `RateLimit-*` headers. Where the network allows, probes should still be restricted to the orchestrator and the load balancer. This answers decision 16 of the review of 7 Nov 2026 ([#2847](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/2847), [#2855](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/2855)). They answer 200 for Healthy and Degraded and 503 for Unhealthy, with the status as the only body, never a description or an exception. Core registers `process` (live), `database` (ready, one row of a system table, so it also fails when the system migrations did not run) and `job-runner` (ready); `services.AddHealthChecks().AddFluentCmsReadyCheck<T>("name")` adds a ready check with a five second timeout (it is in Core, because the SDK has no reference to the health check abstractions; a plugin that references only the SDK calls `AddCheck` with the tag). The job runner beats in memory at every turn of its maintenance loop (`JobRunnerOptions.MaintenanceInterval`, 15 seconds), and ready is Unhealthy when the last beat is older than `FluentCms:Health:JobRunnerHeartbeatTimeout` (default 2 minutes; `Timeout.InfiniteTimeSpan` leaves it out for an instance that runs no jobs) or when the runner has not started, and Degraded when the oldest pending job of a queue has waited longer than `FluentCms:Health:PendingJobAgeLimit` (4.12). `GET /api/management/v1/system/health` needs `system.read`, an instance-level permission: it is in the catalogue and checked with `InstanceAccess`, so a role of one site or a grant limited to sites does not reach it, nor does a key bound to sites or a Delivery or Preview key. It runs every check and answers 200 with `{ status, totalDurationMs, checks: [{ name, status, durationMs, tags, description, error }] }`, whatever the status; descriptions and exception text pass through `SecretScrubber`. The one `ActivitySource` and the one `Meter` are both named `FluentCMS` (`FluentCmsTelemetry.Name`), and nothing is exported: a host subscribes with `AddSource` and `AddMeter` from its own OpenTelemetry setup, and Core takes no dependency on it. Spans are named `content {operation}` (create, update, patch, delete, restore, purge, publish, unpublish, discard-draft, schedule, cancel-schedule, versions, version, restore-version, diff, get, query, query-trash, count, bulk), `hook {kind}` (one per run of the hooks of a kind, with `fluentcms.hooks` their number) and `job {type}` (with `fluentcms.queue`). The MCP plugin adds `mcp {tool}` for a tool call (5.4). They carry `fluentcms.operation`, `fluentcms.type` (the name the schema resolves, and not on jobs) and `fluentcms.site`, and never a value of content; a job's span has the Error status when the attempt throws. The instruments are `fluentcms.requests` (counter, by `fluentcms.surface` of delivery, management, connect (with `/.well-known`), account, health or docs (the OpenAPI document and Scalar), and `fluentcms.status_class`, counted with the status the client got, including a refusal an endpoint throws), `fluentcms.query.duration` (histogram, seconds, by the `fluentcms.type` the schema resolved, and not recorded for a query refused as invalid), `fluentcms.job.latency` (histogram, seconds, the length of an attempt by `fluentcms.operation` the job type and `fluentcms.outcome`), `fluentcms.job.oldest_pending_age` (observable gauge, seconds, by `fluentcms.queue`, 4.12), `fluentcms.job.abandoned` (counter, attempts left running past their timeout, by `fluentcms.operation` the job type, 4.12), `fluentcms.job.abandoned_running` (observable gauge, the abandoned invocations still running in the process, 4.12), `fluentcms.event.handler.duration` (histogram, seconds, the length of a run of an in-process event handler by `fluentcms.operation` the event type, `fluentcms.handler` and `fluentcms.outcome`, 4.11), and `fluentcms.cache.requests` (counter by `fluentcms.cache` and `fluentcms.result`) with `fluentcms.cache.hit_ratio` (observable gauge by `fluentcms.cache`); the Delivery output cache reports its lookups as `delivery`, `fluentcms.rate_limit.refused` (counter, a request one of the seven rate limits refused with a 429, by `fluentcms.policy` of `pre-auth` (the coarse limit before authentication), `auth`, `health`, `static-files` (the static files a surface marks, before authentication), `delivery`, `management` or `caller` (the policy of a plugin endpoint that serves both kinds of caller); recorded where the refusal is written, whether the pre-auth middleware or the endpoint's limiter refused, so an operator sees which limit refuses, and that one partition takes the whole site, as behind an untrusted proxy, without reading the log; the registrations-per-hour limit of the client registration endpoint answers 429 too and is not counted). Every `[LoggerMessage]` in Core has an event id, in a range per area that follows the `FCMS` families: 90xx identity (the authorization server 9001 to 9003, 9005, 9006, 9008, 9009 and 9013, the hosted pages 9004 and 9012, admin users 9007, admin SSO 9040 to 9047, the member pool 9070 to 9076, member providers 9090 to 9094), 91xx host (health 9101 to 9104, the problem handler 9110, email 9120, the startup report 9130 to 9133, the distributed lock 9160 to 9168 of the range 9160 to 9169, the command runner 9170 to 9172 of the range 9170 to 9179), 92xx plugins (per-site enablement 9230 to 9234), 93xx storage (migrations 9301 to 9306, schema history 9311), 94xx content (9401, scheduled jobs 9411, trash purge 9421), 95xx jobs, 96xx events, 97xx activity, 98xx settings and 99xx seeding. The event name is the method name, and a message carries the `FCMS` code where one applies.

**Static files of a surface, as built** ([#3452](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3452)). An endpoint that carries `FluentCmsStaticFileMetadata`, which a surface puts on the endpoints that serve its files as they are, counts in a partition of its own per client address, `FluentCms:RateLimits:StaticFiles:PermitLimit` (15000 a minute, so that forty cold loads of the admin from one address, some 230 files each, stay below it), before authentication and apart from the coarse limit, with no `RateLimit-*` headers and the same 429 problem, and the FluentCMS authentication step skips such an endpoint, as it skips `/health`, so a key sent with a file is never looked up and the partition lets through no key lookup that the coarse limit would have held back; nothing behind a file reads the caller. Only the endpoint routing chose tells a file, never the path or extension the caller sent, so a request that reaches an endpoint without the mark, such as a page, a configuration file, the admin's HTML fallback, a fallback that answers a path matching no file, or any API or sign-in endpoint, still counts under the coarse limit; a marked endpoint under a group of core (the APIs, `/connect`, `/account`, `/.well-known`) counts there too. `FluentCMS.Admin.Hosting` does not mark its files yet, so until it does they count under the coarse limit.

**Multi-instance.** Stateless API; Data Protection and signing keys persisted to the database by default; jobs lease through the database; `IDistributedLock` defaults to a `cms_lock` table and gets a Redis provider in `FluentCMS.Caching.Redis`; output cache in memory by default or Redis; media on shared storage.

**Multi-instance as built.** `IDistributedLock` (`FluentCMS.Providers`, in the SDK) has one method, `TryAcquireAsync(name, timeout)`, which returns a handle that releases the lock when disposed, or null when the timeout passed first (`TimeSpan.Zero` tries once, `Timeout.InfiniteTimeSpan` waits until the lock is free); the handle's `Lost` token is canceled when the lock is lost while held. Core's default, added with `TryAdd` and with no options, keeps one row per name in `cms_lock (name, owner, acquired_at, lease_until)`. A holder takes a 30 second lease and renews it every 10 seconds while it holds the lock, so a holder that stopped without releasing it blocks the others until its lease runs out, and the next holder takes it over with a warning (event id 9160). Taking the lock is an update conditional on the row being free or its lease over, so of two that try at once one wins, and a waiter only reads the row while the lock is held, polling from every 50 milliseconds up to once a second. A free lock keeps its row with no owner, so only the first use of a name inserts, and lock names are a fixed set, one per task rather than one per item. A renewal that finds another holder, or that fails until the lease has run out, cancels `Lost` (9161); a renewal or a release that fails is logged (9162, 9163), and a release changes nothing once another holder has the lock. SQLite has one writer, so while another connection keeps a write transaction open, such as the schema apply or schema sync that holds the lock, a renewal waits for it and gives up after the busy timeout and the retries of Microsoft.Data.Sqlite, about 35 seconds, and so does a take; in the rollback journal mode a read of the row can wait too. Such a command, which `IDataProvider.IsBusy` recognises (SQLite's busy and locked errors by their primary code, never on PostgreSQL or SQL Server), changed nothing: a waiter counts the lock as held and ends with the lock or its timeout, which it can overshoot by one such wait, and the holder reads the row, which in WAL mode does not wait, and keeps the lock while the row still names it, renewing again at once and logging once that it waits (9165). A take and a renewal each run in a transaction of their own and read the clock once it has begun, so on SQLite, where beginning it waits for the writer, a lease starts when the database let the write in, not when it was asked for; on PostgreSQL and SQL Server the clock is still read before a statement that can wait on the row, as before. A take that loses the insert of a name's first row rolls its transaction back before it reads the row, since a read on another connection of the in-memory database waits for it ([#3200](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3200)). Before, a holder whose transaction lasted longer than about 45 seconds lost the lock, which canceled the rest of its work and rolled the transaction back, and a waiter failed with `SqliteException` ([#3176](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3176)). A take reads the row again inside its transaction, and its update is conditional on that row, on the owner it read with a lease over or on no owner, so event 9160 names a holder only when the take took over that holder's lease; a holder that released the lock while the take waited for the writer leaves a free lock, which is taken with no warning. On a database with one writer (`IDataProvider.HasOneWriter`, true on SQLite only), a lease also runs out while a write transaction keeps a live holder's renewals waiting, and when the transaction ends a waiter that found the lease over can get the writer before the renewal: with a waiter present, it took the lock in 4 of 16 measured rounds, and the holder's `Lost` fired 64 to 119 milliseconds after the commit ([#3186](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3186)). So there a take-over is confirmed, which narrows the race and does not close it. A take never takes over a lease the first time it sees it over: it answers that the lock is held, and its next try comes a second later and takes the lease only if it finds the same owner and `lease_until` in a transaction whose BEGIN, timed after the connection is open, took at most 50 milliseconds; a try whose BEGIN waited longer, or that gave up as busy, starts the confirmation again. A long transaction alone no longer lets a waiter in, since a take that waits for it starts again: with no other writer, the waiter took the live holder's lock in none of 44 and 20 measured rounds. Other writers still can. A renewal that has waited for the writer longer than about 228 milliseconds polls only every 100 milliseconds, while every fresh writer, the confirming take included, polls after 1, 2, 5, 10 and 15 milliseconds, so the window gives the waiter several tries against the renewal's one or none; a backlog of requests that queued behind the long transaction is that load. Measured on two hosts on one file with a holder lease of 1.5 seconds, the lease over inside a long write transaction and other writers running 5 millisecond transactions with 10 millisecond pauses, the waiter took the live holder's lock in 0 of 24 rounds with 2 or 4 writers, 3 of 55 with 8 and 2 of 23 with 16, and in a second probe of the same shape in 0 of 20 with 8 and 1 of 20 with 16; a second review counted 0 of 50 with no other writer, 3 of 50 with 8 and 7 of 50 with 16, where `main` before this change gave 22, 17 and 16 of 30. The 64 to 119 milliseconds after which `Lost` fired hold with no other writer; under write load the renewal that would notice the loss is starved by the same writers, and the second review saw `Lost` fire 63 milliseconds to 1.73 seconds after the take-over in 11 of 14 take-overs and not yet after 1.6 to 2.1 seconds in the other 3. Requiring three such tries spread over the second did not help (4 of 60 rounds with 16 writers, 3 of 40 with 32), nor would a longer confirmation under sustained load. The holder's own transactions back to back, such as a table rebuild, where a waiter could see the lease over in one gap and confirm it in another, let it in 0 of 30 rounds in each of two runs of the second review, against 12 and 19 of 30 on `main` before this change. What closes the race for the holder's own work is a renewal inside it: on such a database the host renews a lock it holds inside each of its own write transactions as it commits, once half the lease has passed, which the renewals every 10 seconds leave only while they wait for the writer (`LeaseRenewalInterceptor`, on the host's system, content and plugin contexts, synchronous and asynchronous commits alike). No other writer can come between that renewal and the commit, so a waiter that gets the writer first when the holder's schema apply, schema sync or import commits finds the lease renewed; a renewal that fails is logged (9162) and the commit goes on. What such a renewal found counts once its transaction has committed, and is dropped when the commit failed: a failed commit rolls the renewal back with the rest, so the holder does not take the lease for renewed, and its next commit renews it. A renewal inside a commit that finds the row another holder's cancels `Lost` (9161) as soon as that commit is through, where the loop's next renewal can be 10 seconds away, and the commit itself goes on. `Lost` is canceled once, whichever of the two finds the loss first, and its callbacks run on another thread that the commit does not wait for, so a callback that throws or waits neither fails nor delays a commit. A callback that throws is logged once as a warning with what it threw (9167), never as a failed renewal, the other callbacks run and the lock is lost all the same; that, and the telling once, hold on every provider, since the loop tells a loss the same way. A handle that is being disposed tells no loss, since a commit that still had it finds the row its own release left free ([#3522](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3522)). Its disposal waits for a telling under way at most 5 seconds, the bound of the job runner's disposal: the renewal loop no longer waits for the callbacks either, so a callback that does not return keeps neither waiting longer; the disposal then logs 9168 (Warning) and returns after the release, and the source of `Lost` is disposed once the callbacks return, so one still running can use the token, and what it throws is logged as 9167 ([#3544](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3544)). The lock's callers do not change, and a holder whose process stopped commits nothing, so it is still taken over as below. The confirmation stays for the write transactions of other connections, inside which no renewal of the holder can run ([#3186](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3186)). A holder that stopped is taken over within about 2 seconds of its lease running out on a database whose writer is free (up to a second of polling to see it and the second of the confirmation), a second more for each try whose BEGIN waited; while other connections keep the writer it is not taken over, since a holder at work could not be told from it. A take-over being confirmed goes on for up to 5 seconds past the caller's timeout, so a caller with `TimeSpan.Zero`, such as an export or an import, still takes over a holder that stopped, a second late. Of two waiters that confirm at once, the conditional update lets one take the lock, and the other then finds it held. A free lock, a released one and the first use of a name are taken at once, and on PostgreSQL and SQL Server, where a renewal waits only for the row, a take-over is not confirmed, as before.
