A wait that reaches its timeout while its last try gave up as busy logs 9166, since the row may have been free behind another connection's transaction, and the error of the `fluentcms:schema-sync` timeout says that this can also be the cause. A `schema apply` that loses its lock, whatever canceled `Lost` (another holder took it, its lease ran out or the host stopped), says that the lock was lost, with the type of a provider's own exception when the canceled command ended with one, and ends with `FCMS3339` when that happened while it planned, which applied nothing, or while its operations ran, which rolls them back, and with `FCMS3340` when it happened after they committed and before the schema history was written; each says to run it again. Leases are read against each instance's clock, so instances keep their clocks in step, as the job leases already need. The Data Protection ring is in `cms_data_protection_key` (4.7), so instances on one database read each other's cookies and protected payloads, and the keys survive a restart. Data Protection makes a key when its ring has none it can use, and instances that started together on an empty ring each made one; one that had read only its own kept it for up to a day, refusing the others' cookies once the two minutes in which Data Protection reloads the ring for an unknown key were over. The key manager now makes a key under the lock `fluentcms:data-protection-key`, after reading the ring again, and returns a key that another instance made in the last five minutes (the clock skew Data Protection allows) instead of making a second; when the lock is not free within 10 seconds it makes the key without it (9164). The wait blocks the thread Data Protection asks on, since `IKeyManager` is synchronous, so it covers a live holder, which makes its key within a second, and not one that stopped without releasing the lock, whose 30 second lease it used to wait out. A synchronous `TryAcquire` on `IDistributedLock` is declined: the default lock is a table read through EF Core's asynchronous calls, so it would only move the blocking, and every provider of the slot would have to implement it (item 6 of [#2887](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/2887)). The changes that can take Super Admin from its last holder run under `fluentcms:super-admins` (4.7), and fail rather than go on without it; so do the saves that give an admin user a name or an address, under `fluentcms:user-names` (4.7). The Delivery output cache is the `IOutputCacheStore` slot, in memory by default, which evicts only the copies of its own instance; a host that registers a shared store, such as `Microsoft.AspNetCore.OutputCaching.StackExchangeRedis`, evicts for every instance, and the key's schema hash keeps instances on different schemas apart (4.10). The member mail limit (4.7) is kept in the `IDistributedCache` slot, the framework's `MemoryDistributedCache` by default, one per instance, so N instances behind a load balancer send an address up to N mails a minute of each slot; a host with several instances registers a shared cache, such as `RedisCache`, and the startup report lists `member-mail-limit-per-instance` outside Development while an email sender is set up and the cache is the in-memory one ([#3073](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3073)). Each instance keeps its own copy of the sites and their locales, of plugin enablement and of settings, and sees a change another instance made within 30 seconds (4.6, 4.13); role changes are seen on the next request everywhere, since every request reads the role stamps (4.8). The Redis provider in `FluentCMS.Caching.Redis` is still to come.

**Reverse proxy.** Forwarded headers, `PathBase` option for sub-path hosting, HTTPS redirection and HSTS options; documented for Nginx, Caddy, YARP, Azure Front Door and Cloudflare.

**Reverse proxy as built.** The options are `FluentCms:ReverseProxy` (`ReverseProxyOptions`, `cms.ReverseProxy` in code) and `UseFluentCms()` applies them first, in this order: forwarded headers, the path base, HSTS, the HTTPS redirect, then the pipeline of 4.10, so the pre-auth limiter and everything else that reads the client address or scheme sees what the proxy forwarded. A host no longer calls `UsePathBase` or `UseForwardedHeaders`. Forwarded headers are off by default; when `ForwardedHeaders:Enabled` is set, `X-Forwarded-For` and `-Proto` are read and `-Host` only with `ForwardHost` and for a host of `AllowedHosts`, from the `KnownProxies` and `KnownNetworks` listed (loopback only when both are empty), up to `ForwardLimit` hops (1). `PathBase` is a path such as `/cms`; every FluentCMS endpoint group (the ones `MapFluentCms` maps, so also one added later) is served only under it, and the same path without it is a 404 problem, written after the security headers and CORS. The gate holds a request to the path base when routing found a FluentCMS endpoint or its path is under a prefix of a group, which covers the authorization server's own paths. Routes the host maps itself (its own endpoints, `/openapi/v1.json`, `/scalar`, `/media`) are its own concern and are not held to it. The HTTPS redirect is off by default, because behind a proxy that ends TLS it loops unless the scheme is forwarded, and never applies to a path under `/health`, which the image's `HEALTHCHECK` and an orchestrator call on the container's own http port, and takes `HttpsRedirection:Port` (left out, the framework finds the port from `HTTPS_PORT` or the server's address, and redirects nowhere if it finds none); proxy addresses and networks must be written in full, so `1` is refused rather than read as `0.0.0.1`; HSTS is on outside Development and off in Development, with `Hsts:MaxAge` (30 days), `IncludeSubDomains` and `Preload`. A bad value stops the host with `FCMS1101` and its key. A request that carries `X-Forwarded-For` or `X-Forwarded-Proto` from an address the host does not trust, which the framework ignores with a Debug line, is logged at Warning with event id 9133, once per address for the first four distinct addresses of the process, and `forwarded-headers-untrusted` in the startup report names those addresses; `StartupReportHolder` keeps the item, so a report set after it, or while it was found, carries it too. With forwarded headers off any such request counts, and with them on one from an address outside `KnownProxies` and `KnownNetworks` (loopback when both are empty) for a header the options read; the address is looked at before the forwarded headers middleware changes it ([#3286](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3286)). Behind a proxy that is not listed every caller has the proxy's address, so the pre-auth, auth, health and anonymous Delivery limits counted the whole site as one client with nothing said. A direct caller that sends the headers itself is reported the same way, since the host cannot tell it from a proxy, which is why several addresses are named, so that a forged header cannot hide a real proxy that comes later, and why the message says to list an address only once it is confirmed as the instance's proxy and otherwise to close the route around the proxy. Setup for each proxy is in `docs/reverse-proxy.md`.

**Public origin as built.** `FluentCms:PublicOrigin` (`cms.PublicOrigin` in code; the configuration wins) is the address users reach the instance at, such as `https://cms.example.com`. It is an absolute URL with no user, query or fragment, `https` outside Development, whose path is empty or `FluentCms:ReverseProxy:PathBase`, which is added when it is left out; anything else, a path without a path base included, stops the host with `FCMS1101` and the key. When it is set, the password reset and invitation links, the issuer, the endpoint URLs of the discovery document, and the OpenAPI document's server and with it its `oauth2` URLs are built from it and the path base (without it the document's server is the path base alone, relative, 4.10), so neither `Host` nor `X-Forwarded-Host` reaches them, and tokens carry that issuer, so setting or changing the origin refuses the tokens issued before; the endpoints still answer under any host that host filtering lets through. Without it they follow the request's scheme, host and path base, so every environment but Development refuses to start (`FCMS1105`, `PublicOriginCheck`) unless `FluentCms:Security:AllowRequestOrigin` is true, which is for an instance whose `AllowedHosts` or proxy admits only its own names; the startup report then lists `public-origin-unset`, and Development starts and lists nothing for it. Until [#3298](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3298) the three startup checks refused in Production only, so a Staging or custom-named environment started with a forgeable reset link, a generated signing key and an unencrypted ring. Host filtering runs before the forwarded headers and sees the proxy's `Host`, so with `ForwardHost` on, `X-Forwarded-Host` is applied only for a host of `AllowedHosts` (copied into `ForwardedHeadersOptions.AllowedHosts`), and for any host when `AllowedHosts` is `*` or unset. Code `FCMS1105`.

**Startup diagnostics.** A `StartupReport` (providers, plugins and versions, pending migrations, schema drift, insecure defaults, environment) logged once at start and served at `/api/management/v1/system/diagnostics`.

**Startup diagnostics as built.** The report is made once per start, after the migrations, by a hosted service that logs it at Information with event id 9130 (the report as JSON in the message, and its counts as properties) and one warning with event id 9131 per insecure default; a report that cannot be made is logged (9132, the exception type and its scrubbed message) and replaced by a reduced one that carries the type of the failure, so the endpoint still answers; before any report exists it is a 503 (`FCMS3106`). The report types are public records in the SDK (`FluentCMS.Diagnostics`). It holds the environment, the FluentCMS version, the data provider with its version and the resolved `MigrateOnStartup` and `SchemaSync`, the FluentCMS assemblies in the process, the plugins (an empty list until the plugin host exists), the pending migrations of every stream, the schema hash in code against the newest row of the history (applied hash, time and version, and what differs), and the insecure defaults. Those come from the production startup checks, which are asked in every environment so the report and the refusal cannot disagree (the public origin and key ring checks are not asked to refuse in Development, so that it keeps its own lines), and from twelve more: a generated signing key that `AllowGeneratedSigningKey` accepted, the key ring unencrypted where it is not refused (`data-protection-keys-unencrypted`: in Development, and elsewhere with `AllowUnencryptedKeyRing`), the Development email sender, the HTTPS redirect off outside Development, `AllowedHosts` of `*` or unset outside Development (`allowed-hosts-any`, read from the host filtering options, so any `Host` is answered), `FluentCms:PublicOrigin` unset outside Development and accepted by `AllowRequestOrigin` (`public-origin-unset`, so links in emails and the issuer follow the request's host), schema sync outside Development (`schema-sync-outside-development`, 4.3), forwarded headers trusted from every network, forwarded headers sent from an address the host does not trust (`forwarded-headers-untrusted`, reverse proxy as built in 4.15), the direct login of members turned on outside Development (`member-direct-login`, 4.7), a log category of the Apple member provider below Information outside Development (`member-apple-logging`, member providers as built in 4.7), and the member mail limit in the in-memory `IDistributedCache` outside Development while an email sender is set up (`member-mail-limit-per-instance`, 4.7; anyone may ask for a reset, so registration is not asked, and an instance cannot tell that it has peers, so the line is worded for several instances). A plugin adds its own with an `IInsecureDefaultSource` (`FluentCMS.Diagnostics`, in the SDK), which the report asks in every environment, so it decides itself where a setting is insecure; core uses one too for secret settings that are not protected at rest or that the key ring cannot unprotect (`settings-secrets-unprotected` and `settings-secrets-unreadable`, event ids 9804 and 9805, settings as built in 4.13); the webhooks plugin reports `webhooks-private-networks`, the MCP plugin `mcp-open-client-registration` (5.4), and the media plugin, beside its entries outside Development, `media-shared-library-hides-sites` in every environment when the shared library hides the assets of other sites (5.1). Free text goes through `SecretScrubber`, and the report holds no connection string, key or password. `GET /api/management/v1/system/diagnostics` serves that same report, so a change after the start does not show until the next one. It needs `system.read`, an instance-level permission: a role of one site, a grant limited to sites and a key bound to sites are refused with 403, and so is a key without the management scope. There is no switch to turn CORS or the rate limits off, so neither can be reported as off.

**Doctor as built (B-97, [#3020](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3020)).** `fluentcms doctor` makes the same report inside the host before any hosted service starts, so nothing listens, migrates or writes; it reads the database only for the applied and pending migrations, the schema history and what the insecure defaults of plugins read, such as the media plugin's check of the shared library (5.1). It prints the report as text, or with `--json` as one line in the shape of `/system/diagnostics`, and on standard error why the host would not start (`FCMS3325`: a schema with errors, a startup check that refuses outside Development, a migration set of another provider among the streams a start checks, a database of the migrations from before the baseline (`FCMS1307`, B-107), or a report that could not be made), the pending migrations (`FCMS3303`), and a warning when the FluentCMS assemblies have different versions (`FCMS3326`). It exits 1 for a refusal or a pending migration, which makes it the pre-upgrade check of row 11.12; obsolete usage stays the compiler's warning until the first member is obsolete. A report that cannot be made is logged with event id 9172. With `--url` it reads `GET /system/diagnostics` with `system.read`, prints it as text or, with `--json`, as the instance sent it, and judges the pending migrations and the versions the same way.

**Backups.** Documented per database and storage provider in `docs/backups.md`. For a host on SQLite, `fluentcms backup` writes an online snapshot of the database and the files of the local media storage into one archive, and `fluentcms restore` puts them back. On PostgreSQL and SQL Server both commands refuse and name the guide, since the database's own tools do it better. A backup is not built on export packages, which leave out users, keys and the activity log (10.3, P6).

**Backups as built (B-101).** [#3024](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3024). No migration and no package. `fluentcms backup --output <file>` and `fluentcms restore <file> [--force]` are project commands that run inside the host. The snapshot is the SQLite provider's: Core declares the internal slot `IDatabaseFile` (`FilePath`, `Snapshot`, `IsInUse`, `Check`, `Restore`) and sees it through `InternalsVisibleTo` to `FluentCMS.Data.Sqlite`, whose `SqliteDataProvider` fills it; PostgreSQL and SQL Server do not, so both commands refuse there with `FCMS3240`, which names `docs/backups.md`. `Snapshot` runs SQLite's online backup (`SqliteConnection.BackupDatabase`) with a busy timeout and up to ten tries, since a reader of a database in WAL mode is turned away for a moment while the running host checkpoints, and leaves the copy, written beside the archive rather than in the temporary folder, in the rollback journal, one file. The archive is a zip with `backup.json` (format 1, the FluentCMS version, the provider, the schema hash in code, the applied migrations of every stream, the time, the storage of new uploads and the number of media files), `database/fluentcms.db` and `media/`, with the files under the local storage's root as they lie there, taken after the database, so every asset of the snapshot has its file: a deleted asset keeps its file for the retention of 5.1. Files ending in `.tmp`, an upload the local storage is still writing, are left out. The media plugin registers the `DirectoryInfo` of that root under the service key `FluentCMS.Media.LocalRoot`, which Core reads, since neither references the other; a host without the plugin backs up its database alone. When the slot's storage is not the local one, the files still on the local disk are in the archive and the command says that the others are not. `restore` asks everything before it writes: an archive that is no backup of the format 1 of a host of the same provider (`FCMS3242`), a newer FluentCMS than the host's by SemVer precedence (`FCMS3243`), media files with no local storage to take them (`FCMS3247`) or a name that leaves the root (`FCMS3242`), a database file that exists without `--force` (`FCMS3244`), one that another connection has open (`FCMS3245`: with the exclusive locking mode, the first read is refused while any connection of a host in WAL mode is open; a host that keeps the rollback journal holds no lock while idle and is not seen, which the guide says), and media files over files the root has without `--force` (`FCMS3246`). It unpacks the database beside its file and checks it (`Check`, below), moves the database it replaces aside with its `-wal`, `-shm` and `-journal` files (and back when one of them or the snapshot cannot be moved), moves the snapshot into place in the journal mode of the backup, deletes the files it moved aside, which the provider's first connection that may write, the listing of pending migrations below or a start, puts in WAL mode when the provider uses it (4.3, [#3116](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3116)), then writes the media files over the root's, leaving the others (a file that cannot be written ends it with `FCMS3307` and the count written, to run again with `--force`), and lists the migrations then pending of every stream, to apply with `fluentcms migrate` or a start. A file that cannot be read is `FCMS3248`, and an output that cannot be written `FCMS3307`. Codes `FCMS3240` to `FCMS3253` of the range `FCMS3240` to `FCMS3259`; no log event id.

**Backup and restore follow-ups as built ([#3068](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3068)).** `restore` compares `backup.json` with the host before it writes: a schema hash other than that of the host's schema, a stream the host does not have, or a migration of a stream that the host's set lacks (`MigrationRunner.Streams` and `MigrationsOf`, read without the database) is `FCMS3249`, which names what differs, unless `--allow-schema-mismatch` is given, which restores it and writes the same as a warning once the database is in place; `--force` still only replaces an existing database and media files, so the usual restore over a database is checked too. A migration the host has and the backup had not is pending, as before. `FCMS3244` says to stop every host first, since one that keeps the rollback journal holds no lock while idle and is not seen by `FCMS3245`; no other way to find such a host was added. `IDatabaseFile.Check` runs `PRAGMA quick_check` on the unpacked file, on a connection of its own that closes it, and refuses an empty file, so an entry that is not a whole SQLite database is `FCMS3242` before the database is touched. `Restore` moves the database and its `-wal`, `-shm` and `-journal` aside together, moves them back when one of them or the snapshot cannot be moved, and deletes them only once the snapshot is in place; when one cannot be moved back, the error (`FCMS3307`) names the `<database>.<guid>.replaced` files that hold it. `backup` creates the archive and the snapshot beside it with `UnixCreateMode` 600 where files have a Unix mode (SQLite writes the snapshot into the empty file, which keeps its mode, and the move over the target keeps it); on Windows they take the folder's permissions, and `docs/backups.md` says how to keep the archive.

**Supported platforms.** Linux and Windows containers, Azure App Service, AWS ECS, Kubernetes, bare `dotnet run`. .NET 10 LTS baseline.

### 4.16 Reference host

Rows 10.2, 11.5 (sample data); decision 3.

`src/FluentCMS.Host`: an ASP.NET Core app referencing `FluentCMS.Core`, all three data providers (chosen by configuration), every first-party plugin and, later, the admin at `/admin`. Content types come from a schema file loaded through the fluent builder (`FLUENTCMS_SCHEMA=/config/schema.json`) or from the built-in sample pack (`FLUENTCMS_SAMPLE=blog`) for evaluation; see Q2. SQLite with zero configuration by default. Multi-stage Dockerfile on the chiseled `aspnet:10.0` image, non-root, multi-arch; a compose file with PostgreSQL and MinIO; health probes; migrations on start for SQLite and an init container pattern (`fluentcms migrate`, with the CLI in M6) documented for the others.

**Reference host as built.** `src/FluentCMS.Host` references Core, the three data providers, the Blog sample and the plugins that have code, and adds each with `cms.AddPlugin<T>()`: the media plugin (5.1) with the S3 storage package, the webhooks plugin (5.2), the MCP plugin (5.4), the multi-site plugin (5.6), the GraphQL plugin (5.3) and the AI assist plugin (5.5), which is off until a site names a provider and opts fields in. `ReferenceHost.Build(WebApplicationBuilder)` sets it up from configuration alone, so environment variables are enough, and maps `UseFluentCms`, which includes `UseAuthorization`, `MapFluentCms` and `MapFluentCmsOpenApi`, whose document and Scalar UI stay off outside Development unless `FluentCms:OpenApi:Enabled` is true. `FluentCms:Database:Provider` is `Sqlite` (the default), `PostgreSql` or `SqlServer` (`FCMS3150` otherwise), and `ConnectionStrings:FluentCms` is the connection string: `Data Source={content root}/fluentcms.db` by default on SQLite, and required on the others (`FCMS3151`). `FLUENTCMS_SAMPLE=blog` adds the Blog types and `cms.AddBlogSample()` (`FCMS3152` for another value), and `FLUENTCMS_SCHEMA` loads a schema file through `SchemaBuilder.LoadSchemaFile` (`FCMS3153` when the path does not exist; a file with errors stops the host with the `FCMS12xx` codes of the builder); both may be set. On SQLite either one calls `cms.AllowSchemaSyncOutsideDevelopment()` (4.3), so their tables are created and extended at start in every environment. On PostgreSQL and SQL Server neither calls it, so nothing is applied at start and `FCMS3154` no longer stops the host; `fluentcms schema apply` brings the content tables up to the schema (4.3, B-98), and until it has, startup validation stops the host with `FCMS1301`, which names that command. Any key of `FluentCms:Media:S3` calls `media.UseS3` with that section, so new uploads go to that bucket; a section without a valid bucket stops the host at start with the options' message, and without the section media stays on the local disk. The Dockerfile, built from the repository root, compiles on the build platform for `TARGETARCH`, so `buildx` builds amd64 and arm64 without emulation, and runs on `aspnet:10.0-noble-chiseled-extra` (the extra variant for the ICU and time zone data that localized content and schedules in a time zone need) as the non-root `app` user. It sets `ConnectionStrings__FluentCms=Data Source=/data/fluentcms.db` on a `/data` volume and lifts no Production refusal, so `docker run` with nothing set stops before it touches the database with `FCMS1102`, `FCMS1105` and `FCMS1106`, each naming its setting and its opt-in; `docs/docker.md` shows an evaluation run that sets the public origin and the two other opt-ins, and the production setup. `FluentCms:DataProtection:Certificate:Path` and `Password` name a PKCS#12 file with an RSA key, which the host passes to `ProtectKeysWithCertificate` before `AddFluentCms`, since the image cannot call it itself (`FCMS3155` when the file cannot be read or holds no RSA private key). Each entry of `FluentCms:DataProtection:PreviousCertificates` (`Path` and `Password`) is loaded the same way and passed to `UnprotectKeysWithAnyCertificate` alone, so it reads the keys an older certificate protected while the certificate is replaced and never protects a new key, the only encryptor being the current certificate's ([#3435](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3435)); an entry whose members are all empty is absent, as an empty current `Path` is; an entry that cannot be loaded or names no `Path`, and previous certificates without a current one, stop the host with `FCMS3155` in every environment, as the current certificate does, and outside Development the startup report lists `data-protection-previous-certificates` with their thumbprints until they are removed; Azure Key Vault reaches the image as a certificate the platform mounts, and `ProtectKeysWithAzureKeyVault` needs a host of its own. Its `HEALTHCHECK` runs the host with `--probe`, which asks `/health/ready` on the container's own port and path base, since the image has no shell or `curl`; it reads both from the configuration the host builds (`urls`, `http_ports` and `FluentCms:ReverseProxy:PathBase`, from `appsettings.json`, its environment's file or a variable) and follows no redirect. Before [#3275](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3275) it read variables alone and followed the HTTPS redirect to an https port where nothing listened, so the container never became healthy with the redirect on or a path base in JSON. A new instance gets its first administrator from `fluentcms user create` (6.1), which the image runs too, or, for an instance that is set up from its environment alone, from a confidential client with Super Admin registered from the environment, which invites one. A deployment sets `FluentCms__PublicOrigin`, which links in emails and the issuer are built from (`FCMS1105` otherwise), and `AllowedHosts` (`allowed-hosts-any` otherwise), and email goes through SMTP when `FluentCms:Email:Smtp:Host` is set (4.7). `docs/docker.md` is the guide. `src/FluentCMS.Host/compose.yaml` (B-99) runs the image on PostgreSQL 15 with its media in MinIO and `FLUENTCMS_SAMPLE=blog`, as six services: `postgres` and `minio`, each with a volume and a health check; `minio-init`, which makes the bucket `fluentcms-media` and a MinIO user whose policy allows that bucket only, and which the host signs with; `migrate` (`fluentcms migrate`) and then `schema-apply` (`fluentcms schema apply`) with the host's image and database settings, two services because the image runs one command per container and has no shell, both idempotent and so run on every `up`; and `cms`, which starts when both steps and `minio-init` have ended with 0 and `postgres` and `minio` are healthy, on `127.0.0.1:8080`. The public origin is `https://localhost` and `AllowedHosts` `localhost`, both overridable, and the passwords and keys come from `.env`, which is git-ignored: `.env.example` leaves them empty and names the two opt-ins of an evaluation commented out, so a copy of it lifts no refusal ([#3298](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3298)), and each value is `${VAR:?message}`, so compose refuses to start without it. MinIO publishes no images any more, so the file uses `pgsty/minio`, pinned by the digest the media tests use, and its own `mc` for the init step. The `Compose` workflow, which runs on pushes to `feature/*` branches and which CI calls on pushes to `main` and manual runs but not on pull requests, builds the image, checks the refusal without `.env`, starts the file with throwaway values and an override that stands in for a TLS proxy and registers a public client, waits for `/health/ready` with a bound and dumps the logs on failure, checks that both steps ended before the host started, makes an administrator and a management key with the CLI in the container, signs the administrator in through the hosted pages with PKCE, publishes an article through the Management API and reads it through the Delivery API, uploads an image that it finds in the bucket and reads back, checks that the wait fails with MinIO stopped, and checks that a second `up` on the same volumes applies nothing and serves the article. The Dockerfile copies every project the host references before the restore. The admin at `/admin` waits for its milestone. Codes `FCMS3150` to `FCMS3155`.

**The published image (B-108).** The `Publish` workflow (3.2) pushes the image of each release to `ghcr.io/fluentcms/fluentcms` for `linux/amd64` and `linux/arm64`, tagged with the version and with `preview` or, for a release that is not a preview, `latest`. The Dockerfile takes the version as the build argument `VERSION` (`1.0.0-dev` without it) and labels the image with its title, description, source and licence; the workflow adds the version and the revision. `src/FluentCMS.Host/compose.yaml` runs the published image with `FLUENTCMS_IMAGE` and `docker compose up --no-build`, and a manual run of the `Compose` workflow with an image runs its smoke test on it instead of a build.

**Commands in the reference host (B-90).** `Program.cs` calls `app.RunFluentCmsCommandAsync(args)` after `ReferenceHost.Build`, so `docker run <image> fluentcms migrate` applies the migrations of every stream and ends the container with the command's exit code, which is what an init container runs; `--probe` is unchanged. A command serves nothing, so it needs none of the settings that a start outside Development refuses without (`FCMS1102`, `FCMS1105`, `FCMS1106`), only the database. With `FluentCms:Admin:Enabled` true, a command also runs the admin's checks, since it is built with the host's settings: a wrong admin setting or address stops it with `FCMS1101`, and a missing app, manifest or `index.html` with `FCMS1103`. A configuration error of `ReferenceHost.Build` still ends the process with its code and exit code 1. `docs/docker.md` shows the init container, and since B-98 the second one that runs `fluentcms schema apply` after `migrate` for a schema file or the sample. The data tests run the image's host on each provider with a schema file and with the sample: `migrate`, a start that stops with `FCMS1301`, `schema apply`, a second `schema apply` that finds nothing, and a start whose report shows no drift.

**GraphQL in the reference host (B-75).** The host references `FluentCMS.Plugins.GraphQL` and adds `GraphQLPlugin` after the other plugins, so `/graphql` serves the types of `FLUENTCMS_SAMPLE` or `FLUENTCMS_SCHEMA`, and a host with neither answers a schema with no content field. Its options are `FluentCms:GraphQL` (5.3), and in Production introspection needs `schema.read`.

**Seeding.** `ISeeder`, `SeedContext` and `ISeedRunner` are in the SDK (`FluentCMS.Seeding`). A seeder writes through the public services, so permissions, versions, events, activity, trash and scheduling behave as in real use. It runs as the system principal with the `Seed` origin and the full service scope, so trust comes from registering it: only seeders added with `cms.Seeding.Add<T>()` run, at boot and through `ISeedRunner` alike, and a seeder registered on the container in any other way is ignored. `cms.Seeding.RequireLocale("fr")` makes the default site allow a locale before the first seeder runs (sites cannot be managed through the API before M2) and writes a `site.locale-added` activity row when it adds one; without a seeder added the host refuses to start (`FCMS2901`). `cms.Seeding.RunOnStart` (default true) and `ClaimTimeout` (default 15 minutes, `FCMS2902` if not positive) are the other options. With no seeder added nothing is registered and boot does no extra work.

**Seeding runs once per version.** The seeders run in `StartAsync` after the migrations, on the default site and in its default locale, in registration order. What was applied is kept in the instance-wide settings area `seeding` (no system table): per seeder `Name` (default the type name) the highest `Version` that ran to the end. A seeder applied at its current version is skipped at boot; raising `Version` runs it again. This keeps purged content, renamed authors or tags and changed settings from coming back or being overwritten.

**One claim per run.** A run that has seeders to apply takes a single claim for all of them (a `ClaimId` and a time in the same document), with a write that names the version of the settings row it read, and renews the time every `ClaimTimeout / 3` while it runs. Only a claim older than `ClaimTimeout` (default 15 minutes) is taken over, whatever the version of the seeders, so a rolling deploy never runs two versions at once. An instance that loses the race (`FCMS2808`) or finds a fresh claim boots without seeding and logs it at Information; the seed is retried at a later boot or with `ISeedRunner`. A run finishes or releases the claim only while the stored `ClaimId` is still its own: a run whose claim was taken over stops, logs a warning and leaves the row alone. Any exception after the claim is taken (a locale that is refused, a failed mark, a seeder that throws) releases the claim if it is still the run's own, and is rethrown. A seeder is marked applied only after it succeeds. A seeder that throws releases the claim, is not marked, and fails host start (as does any exception in a seeder at boot).

**Seeders converge.** A seeder finds each entry by a natural key that a person does not change (a slug, never a name) and brings it to the state it wants step by step: create if missing, publish if still a draft, add the translation if missing, trash if not trashed, schedule if not scheduled. It does not return early because the entry exists, so a run that stopped halfway is finished by the next. A trashed entry counts as found, and a unique conflict (`FCMS2111`) or a version conflict while creating means another run got there first, so the entry is read again. `SeedContext.AppliedVersion` is the version the seeder last ran to the end at, 0 if never. State changes (publish, trash, schedule) are made only to entries the run created, or when `AppliedVersion` is 0, a first run that may have stopped halfway. After a version bump existing entries stay as a person left them (a restored article is not trashed again, a schedule is not moved) and only missing ones are created. If a version-bump run crashes after creating an entry and before applying its state, the rerun leaves that entry as it is, because it can no longer tell the entry from one a person changed. A state step that finds its entry gone or in the trash (an overlapping run or a person got there first) counts as done.

**Blog sample.** `samples/Blog` (references the SDK, Core and the media plugin, which a host that references the sample discovers) holds `Article`, `Author`, `Tag`, `Seo`, `HeroBlock`, `QuoteBlock`, `GalleryBlock`, the typed settings class `BlogSettings` (area `blog`; it replaces the `SiteSettings` singleton of 4.2, per 8.7), `BlogSeeder` and `cms.AddBlogSample()`. The seed set is two authors, three tags, five articles (one published in `en` and `fr`, one published, one draft, one trashed and one scheduled for 30 days after the host clock) and the site's `blog` settings. Authors, tags and articles have a slug, which is the key. The media fields are back with the media field type (B-67): `Article.Hero`, `Author.Avatar`, `HeroBlock.Image` and `GalleryBlock.Images`. `Logo` is `BlogSettings.Logo`, the id of the logo's asset, because the `SiteSettings` singleton it was on became a settings area, and settings have no field types; it is neither populated nor counted in `cms_reference`. The seed uploads six small PNGs through `IMediaService`, tagged `blog-sample` and found again by file name, with titles and alternative text (the welcome banner's in `fr` too), and the articles, authors, blocks and settings refer to them. Acceptance on PostgreSQL and SQL Server follows #2711, because SQLite is the only provider run for real until then.

## 5. First-party plugins, server halves

### 5.1 Media plugin

Rows 5.1 to 5.12; 5.13 to 5.18 as Should; decisions 34 to 37.

**Tables.** `cms_media_asset (id, site_id, folder_id, file_name, storage_provider, storage_key, owns_file, mime_type, size, checksum, width, height, duration, title, alt json per locale, caption json per locale, credit, tags json, focal_x, focal_y, is_private, created_*, updated_*, deleted_at, version)`, `cms_media_folder (id, site_id, parent_id, name, path)`, `cms_media_variant (asset_id, params_hash, storage_key, size, created_at)`, `cms_media_preset (site_id, name, params json)`.

**Storage.** `IFileStorage` (stream open, write, delete, exists, metadata) with per-site provider selection; providers: local disk (allowed in production with a documented warning), S3-compatible, Azure Blob.

**Upload.** `POST /api/management/v1/media` multipart, multi-file; size limits; MIME allow-list with magic-byte sniffing; executables rejected; SVG sanitised or rejected per setting. Upload from URL is a Should.

**Transforms.** `GET /media/{id}/{filename}?w=&h=&fit=cover|contain|fill&q=&f=auto|webp|avif|jpeg|png&fx=&fy=&preset=hero&sig=` where the filename is ignored on lookup (decision 36). `f=auto` negotiates by `Accept` with `Vary: Accept`. Results are cached as variants in the same storage. Limits on dimensions and operation count. Signatures are HMAC over the canonical parameter string with a key kept protected by Data Protection (as built below, B-65); `Media:RequireSignedUrls=false` allows unsigned URLs for trusted setups. `IImageProcessor` has a SkiaSharp implementation by default, and the slot stays replaceable (Q8; as built, B-65).

**Media Delivery API.** `GET /api/delivery/v1/media/{id}` returns metadata and URL templates with the same key classes and cache rules as content.

**Usage and integrity.** `cms_reference` rows with `target_type = media` answer `GET /media/{id}/usage`; delete is refused while referenced unless `force=true`, which nulls the references; replace keeps the id and invalidates variants.

**Private assets.** `is_private` assets are never served from `/media/{id}`; `POST /media/{id}/download-url` returns a short-lived signed URL after a folder permission check (the folder reach of `media.read`, ADR 0004; as built, B-66).

**Field type.** Registers `media` with `MediaRef` and `MediaRefList`, OpenAPI schema, filters by id, and an `IMediaResolver` used by population so `populate=hero` returns metadata and URLs. Rich text and block media references use the same resolver.

**Media as built (B-64).** `MediaPlugin` (`media`) in `FluentCMS.Plugins.Media` references the SDK and `FluentCMS.Plugins.Abstractions`, and the reference host adds it. Its stream on `PluginDbContext<MediaPlugin>` has the SQLite set (`InitialMedia`): `cms_media_asset` with the columns above (`duration` stays null until audio and video metadata, 5.14) and `cms_media_folder (id, site_id, parent_id, name, path)`, where `path` is the ids from the root down to the folder, `/{id}/{id}/`. The variant, preset and signing key tables come with transforms (B-65, below). The PostgreSQL and SQL Server sets are [#2711](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/2711)'s, as for webhooks.

- **Contracts.** The SDK has `IFileStorage` (`OpenReadAsync`, `WriteAsync`, `DeleteAsync`, `ExistsAsync`, `GetMetadataAsync`, and the `Name` an asset records) and `IMediaService`, which the endpoints call and the field type, the MCP plugin and other plugins will. The service checks every permission with `IPermissionChecker`.
- **Storage.** `IFileStorage` is a provider slot the plugin declares, whose default `LocalFileStorage` (`local`) keeps files under `FluentCms:Media:Local:Root` (`App_Data/media` under the content root). The root is the instance's, in configuration, not a site setting: the storage is one for every site, and a root a site administrator could change would write anywhere the process can. A key is segments of letters, digits, dots, hyphens and underscores, checked before it becomes a path, and a write goes to a temporary file that is moved over the target. An asset's key is `{site}/{asset}/original{.ext}`, so variants can sit beside it. The local disk is allowed outside Development, and the startup report lists `media-local-storage` there with the warning that every instance needs the directory on one shared volume and that backups must include it. Each asset records `storage_provider` and `storage_key`, so a storage plugin ([#2836](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/2836), as built in B-68 below) that replaces the slot for new uploads leaves older assets where they are, to be read through the storage they name.
- **Upload.** `POST /api/management/v1/media` reads a multipart body of up to `FluentCms:Media:MaxRequestBodySize` (256 MB): every file part is a file, and the field `folderId` names the folder. The rules are the site's, in the settings area `media` (`settings.media.manage`): `MaxFileSize` (50 MB), `MaxRequestSize` (200 MB, cut to the instance limit), `MaxFilesPerRequest` (20), `AllowedTypes` and `Svg`. A file is read once to a temporary file, which only its owner reads on Unix, counted and hashed with SHA-256 on the way, so one over a limit is refused (`FCMS1582`, 413) without reading it to the end. Its type comes from its first bytes, from a built-in table: JPEG, PNG, GIF, WebP, AVIF, PDF, MP4, WebM, MP3 and WAV, which is the default allow-list; a type off the site's list or no known type is `FCMS1581` (415). Executables are always refused, whatever the name or `Content-Type` say (`FCMS1583`, 415): by signature (PE `MZ`, ELF, Mach-O in both byte orders and universal binaries, and a shebang) and by an executable or script extension. Width and height are read from the headers of JPEG, PNG, GIF and WebP with built-in code. A request stores all its files or none: they are written to the storage only once every file has passed, and a file written for a request that then fails is deleted again (event 9260 when that fails).
- **SVG.** `Svg` is `Refuse` (the default), which refuses every SVG (`FCMS1584`, 415), or `Sanitize`. An SVG is XML read with DTD processing prohibited, no resolver, no processing instructions and no comments, whose root is `<svg>`; anything else is no SVG. Sanitising is a conservative pass with `System.Xml`: it removes `script`, `foreignObject`, `iframe`, `embed`, `object`, `handler` and `listener`, elements of other vocabularies such as XHTML, attributes of other namespaces, `on*` attributes, animations whose `attributeName` is a link or a handler, every attribute whose value holds a `javascript:` or `vbscript:` URL or a `data:` URL other than a PNG, JPEG, GIF or WebP image, and CSS with `@import`, `expression(` or such a URL. As built ([#3309](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3309)), the pass keeps what its lists name and nothing else: SVG elements of drawing, paint servers, filters, text and animation, and their attributes, compared in their exact case, so that no name turns into an HTML element or attribute when a page inlines the SVG and reads names in lower case; `<image>`, `<feImage>`, `<foreignObject>`, `<script>`, fonts and every HTML element go. An `<a>` becomes a `<g>`, and `<title>` and `<desc>` keep their text only, since a page that inlines the SVG parses their content as HTML. A link, `href` or `xlink:href`, stays only when its value, without the ASCII white space a URL parser takes off, is a `#` and name characters, read as written, with no CSS escape or comment undone; every other attribute that can carry a URL (`src`, `srcset`, `xml:base` and the like) is on no list. A value read as CSS, a `style` attribute and every other attribute kept, goes when, after CSS escapes are undone and white space is taken out, the value with its comments kept or with them taken out holds `url(` to anything but a `#fragment`, `@import`, `expression(`, `image(`, `image-set(` or `src(`, or when the value holds a backslash and a `url(`, since an escaped quote ends no string in an unquoted `url(`; the two forms cover a comment that a string hides and a string that a comment hides, and a value either form flags goes whole, even when a browser would read it as harmless: one `url(data:…)` removes the whole `style` attribute. A `<style>` element goes whatever it holds, and every id and reference to one takes a prefix of the file ([#3484](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3484), "SVG and backfill follow-ups as built" below). An animation whose `attributeName` is a link or an event handler goes. A `data:` image is no longer kept. SVGs stored before are not sanitised again. Size and checksum are those of the sanitised file. Serving SVGs with a restrictive `Content-Security-Policy` is the Delivery route's ([#2834](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/2834)).
- **Metadata.** `GET` and `PATCH /media/{id}` read and change `title`, `alt` and `caption` in the locale of the request (`?locale=` or `X-Cms-Locale`, as for content), `credit`, `tags` (at most 50, each up to 64 characters without quotes, backslashes or control characters), `focalPoint` (`x` and `y` from 0 to 1) and `isPrivate`; a member left out stays, and an empty text clears it. `POST /media/{id}/move` moves an asset to a folder or, with a null `folderId`, the root. `DELETE /media/{id}` sets `deleted_at`, after which the asset is not found; its bytes stay until purge and the usage checks of [#2834](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/2834). `GET /media` lists the assets newest first, by `folder` (an id, or `root`), `subfolders`, `type` (a MIME type or a family such as `image/*`), `tag` and `search` (a text in the title, the file name or the tags, whatever the case; B-72), with `page` and `pageSize` (up to 100).
- **Version and If-Match** ([#3333](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3333)). An asset has an integer `version`, 1 on upload, that every write of its row through the service or an import moves on by one: a change of its metadata, a move, a replace, a delete and an update by an import. The soft delete of every asset of a deleted site (`DeletedSiteMedia`) leaves it as it is, since nothing writes those assets again. It is one for all locales, since an asset is one row and its alt text and caption per locale are columns of it. `GET /media/{id}` gives it as a strong `ETag`, `"7"`, and `PATCH /media/{id}`, `POST /media/{id}/move` and `POST /media/{id}/replace` give the new one. Those three and `DELETE /media/{id}` take an optional `If-Match`, read as an entry's is (`EntityVersions` in `FluentCMS.Plugins.Abstractions`, which core's entries and settings share): a weak tag is read by its value, `*` or no header applies the write to whatever version is stored, as before, and more than one tag or anything but a whole number is `FCMS3131` (400). A version that is not the stored one is refused with `412` and `FCMS3105`, as an entry's is, with a detail that names the asset and no `current` member: the client reads the asset again. The version is the row's concurrency token, so the compare and the write are one: the update names the version read, and a write that came in after the read makes it change nothing. The write is then applied again to the row as it is now, read afresh, so that it never puts back what it read before the other write (an alt text saved in one locale keeps another's saved at the same time, and the replace that saves last takes the file and the variants of the row the other saved). With `If-Match` a version that moved on is the 412; a write that loses five times is refused with the same code. A stale replace is refused before the file is written, and a replace refused later deletes its file again. A delete is one update that marks the row deleted at the next version, conditional on the version with `If-Match`. A forced delete with `If-Match` first moves the version on in one conditional update, and only then takes the asset out of the entries and marks it deleted, in an update that no other write makes miss, so that a delete refused for its version leaves the references and one that removed them leaves no asset. The column is in the media baseline `Initial`, so a database of an earlier build is recreated (H5). The list and the Delivery read give no version.
- **Folders.** `/media/folders` lists, creates (`name`, `parentId`), renames (`PATCH`), moves (`POST /{id}/move` with `parentId`) and deletes folders. Names are unique within their parent (`FCMS1587`, 409), folders nest up to 20 deep, a folder cannot move into itself or a subfolder (`FCMS1589`, 409), and only an empty folder is deleted (`FCMS1588`, 409). A folder moves with its subfolders, whose paths are rewritten, and its assets, which keep their folder.
- **Permissions.** `media.read`, `media.upload`, `media.update` (metadata and moves, in both folders), `media.delete` and `media.folders.manage`. The built-in Editor and Author get `media.read` and `media.upload` by default, and Editor `media.update`, once per instance (4.8, [#3103](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3103)). A grant may be limited to folders with the `resources` constraint (4.8, [ADR 0004](../decisions/0004-folder-limited-media-grants.md)): it reaches those folders and every folder below them, wherever they move, and only a grant with no folder limit reaches the root. An asset or folder outside the reach is not found (`FCMS1585`, `FCMS1586`), and a caller without the permission gets 403. This replaces `media.folder.{id}.read` above, which a catalogue fixed at startup cannot hold.
- **Events.** `MediaAssetUploadedEvent`, `MediaAssetUpdatedEvent`, `MediaAssetMovedEvent` and `MediaAssetDeletedEvent` (SDK, `FluentCMS.Events`; `MediaAssetReplacedEvent` came with B-66) carry the asset's id, folder and file name, and write the activity rows `media.uploaded` (with `mimeType` and `size`), `media.updated`, `media.moved` (with `from` and `to`) and `media.deleted`, with the target type `media`. Webhooks deliver them (5.2).
- **Codes.** `FCMS1580` an invalid field, `FCMS1581` a type that is not allowed, `FCMS1582` an upload over a limit, `FCMS1583` an executable, `FCMS1584` an SVG the settings refuse, `FCMS1585` no such asset, `FCMS1586` no such folder, `FCMS1587` a folder name that is taken, `FCMS1588` a folder that is not empty and `FCMS1589` a folder moved into itself. Event ids 9260 to 9279.

**Decisions as built (B-64).** Settled for [#2832](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/2832):

- **Migrations.** The media stream ships a set per provider since [#2711](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/2711) (4.3, "Plugin migration sets per provider as built"), so a host on PostgreSQL or SQL Server passes the foreign-set check of [#2907](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/2907).
- **Folder-limited grants.** A `resources` constraint on the grant, naming folder ids, which `IPermissionChecker.ResourcesAsync` answers and the media service extends to subfolders, keeps the catalogue fixed at startup ([ADR 0004](../decisions/0004-folder-limited-media-grants.md)).
- **File types.** A small built-in table of magic bytes, with executables refused by signature as well as by name. No package.
- **Image size.** Read from the headers with built-in code; the image processor comes with transforms ([#2833](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/2833)). As built ([#3309](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3309)), an upload, a replace and an import take the size from `IImageProcessor.IdentifyAsync`, which is the size the image is shown at after its EXIF orientation, so a portrait photo stored turned reports its portrait size; a type the processor does not read, or bytes it does not take, keeps the size of the header. The job `media.dimensions.backfill` gives the assets stored before the same size, once per database: it reads the JPEG, WebP and AVIF images whose type the processor reads, 100 a run, in the order of their ids, and enqueues the next run until a run finds none. Where it stands is the row with id 2 of `cms_media_signing_key`, beside the signing key's row 1: the id of the last image read, or `done`, and the time a start claimed it or a run last moved it on. The media startup enqueues a first run only when that row is missing, or is not `done` and was last claimed more than an hour before; it claims the row first, so instances that start together start one chain, and tries the enqueue again while it holds that claim; a run moves the row on from where it read it, so two chains, which a stale claim can leave, take turns on it. A JPEG is identified from the first MiB of its file and from the whole file only when that is not enough; WebP and AVIF can put their orientation after the image, so their whole file is read, copied to a temporary file from S3 and Azure Blob. It writes only `width` and `height`, not the version or the time and author of the update, and raises no event of the asset, so no webhook hears of it; it evicts the Delivery copies of the assets it corrects with one `CacheInvalidatedEvent` a run. An image whose file is missing, or whose bytes the processor does not take, keeps its size (event 9276); a failure of the storage, or of the disk under the processor, fails the run, which the runner tries again from the same place, and on its last attempt the image is logged (9276) and skipped, so that a file that can never be read, such as an archived object, does not stop the check; a retry evicts the Delivery copies of the whole batch. A run has an attempt timeout of 30 minutes and is not resumable, since it moves the row only at its end ([#3529](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3529)). A timeout is not an image that cannot be read, so the last attempt does not skip it: a batch that cannot finish within the 30 minutes on any attempt is dead-lettered with `FCMS2508`, and the chain ends until a start of a host claims the check again, once an hour has passed since the row was last claimed or moved on. A run logs what it read and corrected (9275), and a start that cannot start the check tries again (9277). Deleting the row with id 2 runs the check again at the next start. An image uploaded by an instance of an earlier version during a rolling upgrade can keep the size of its header after `done`. The row stays in `cms_media_signing_key` until plugins have a store of their own for the state of a job ([#3484](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3484), below).
- **SVG.** `Refuse` by default, or `Sanitize` with the `System.Xml` pass above, DTD processing off.
- **Local storage.** Its root is in the plugin's configuration (`FluentCms:Media:Local:Root`), since the provider is one per instance; the rules of uploads are the site's media settings. Allowed in Production with the startup report's warning.

**Transforms as built (B-65).** The stream's second SQLite migration, `MediaTransforms`, adds `cms_media_variant (asset_id, params_hash, storage_key, size, created_at)` keyed by asset and hash, `cms_media_preset (site_id, name, params)` keyed by site and name, with `params` a JSON object of the query's names and values, and `cms_media_signing_key (id, key, created_at)`.

- **Contracts.** The SDK has `IImageProcessor` in `FluentCMS.Media`, beside `IFileStorage`: `CanDecode`, `CanEncode`, `IdentifyAsync`, which reads the width, height and frame count from the header without decoding, and `ProcessAsync`, which applies the EXIF orientation, resizes, leaves out EXIF, IPTC and XMP, and encodes an `ImageTransform` (`Width`, `Height`, `Fit`, `Quality`, `MimeType`, `FocalPoint`). It is a provider slot the plugin declares, whose default `SkiaImageProcessor` reads the header with `SKCodec`, reads JPEG, PNG, GIF (its first frame) and WebP into sRGB, and writes JPEG, PNG and WebP; cover, contain and fill are its own. It encodes neither AVIF nor GIF, so `f=auto` never picks AVIF, and a GIF without `f` becomes a PNG. An image that does not decode whole is `FCMS1592`. `IMediaService` adds `GetImageUrlAsync(asset, MediaImageOptions)`, which returns `/media/{id}/{fileName}?…&sig=` relative to the CMS base and checks no permission, and `ListPresetsAsync`, `SetPresetAsync` and `DeletePresetAsync`. `OpenImageAsync(id, MediaImageOptions)` (B-83) opens a copy of an image for code in the host that needs its pixels, such as the AI assist plugin's `altText` (5.5): it needs `media.read` in the asset's folder, reads a private asset too, takes no preset, is held to the same limits of the site and stores its variant as a URL's transform does. Under the shared library it reads the library's row and asks the permission, and takes the limits, in the site of the request, as the other calls do ("Shared library as built"). `RequireUpdateAsync(id)` ([#3418](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3418)) makes the check of `UpdateAsync` and writes nothing, for code that suggests a change the caller saves itself, such as `altText` for an asset alone: `media.update` in the site (403 otherwise) reaching the asset's folder, and an asset of the site outside the bin, so that a missing, binned or unreachable asset is `FCMS1585` as for `PATCH /media/{id}`. The media plugin shares the check with `UpdateAsync`; the interface's default refuses with `NotSupportedException`, so another implementation that does not make the check allows no one.
- **Route.** `GET /media/{id}/{fileName}` is a group of the plugin's own (`MapGroup`), outside `/api`, open to anyone with `AllowAnonymous`, under the Delivery rate limit and CORS; the site is the request's, by host or `site`. The file name is ignored. The parameters are `w` and `h` in pixels (with one, the other follows the aspect ratio), `fit` (`contain` by default, `cover` or `fill`), `q` (1 to 100, 80 by default), `f` (`auto`, `webp`, `avif`, `jpeg` or `png`; the asset's type when left out), `fx` and `fy` (0 to 1, together), `preset` and `v`, a part of the asset's checksum that the signature covers and nothing compares ([#3278](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3278)). A name given twice, or one that is none of these, `sig` or `site`, is `FCMS1580`. `cover` crops around `fx` and `fy`, else the asset's focal point, else the centre. `f=auto` takes AVIF, then WebP, where `Accept` lists the type with a quality above zero and the processor encodes it, and the asset's type otherwise, and answers with `Vary: Accept`. An answer has the `Cache-Control` of `FluentCms:Media:CacheControl` (`public, max-age=86400`) and an `ETag` of the variant, and answers `If-None-Match` with 304. A URL without parameters re-encodes the image in its own type, or as a PNG where the processor does not encode that type.
- **Order of checks.** The signature first, before the asset is looked up (`FCMS1590`, 403); the parameters; the asset, which must be public and not deleted, whatever the signature (`FCMS1585`); the preset (`FCMS1593`); the site's limits on width, height and operations (`FCMS1591`, 422); the type, which the processor must read (`FCMS1592`, 415); and the size recorded at upload against `MaxSourcePixels`. Only then is a stored variant looked up. A new one is identified from its header first: its width times height times frames must be within `MaxSourcePixels`, and a side that follows the aspect ratio within the width and height limits, before it is decoded, against decompression bombs and outsized results. The original is read from its storage once: the local disk's file as it is, any other into a temporary file deleted afterwards, where the header and then the pixels are read; the size recorded at upload does not replace the header, since it is before the orientation and counts no frames. Requests for one variant that come while it is made wait for that one decode, keyed by the site, the asset, the hash and whether the site's limits apply; at most `FluentCms:Media:MaxConcurrentDecodes` (4) decode at once on an instance, `IMediaService.OpenImageAsync` included. A caller that cancels stops waiting; a decode still queued for a slot when its last caller has left is dropped, and one that has started goes on and stores the variant. A run checks for the variant again before it decodes, for a request that missed it just before an earlier run stored it. A decode that fails fails every waiting caller and is not kept, so the next request tries again ([#3281](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3281)). SkiaSharp decodes an image at least twice the size of the result where its codec scales, as JPEG and WebP do.
- **Limits.** The site's media settings have `MaxTransformWidth` and `MaxTransformHeight` (4096 each), `MaxTransformOperations` (6; `w`, `h`, `fit`, `q` and `f` count one each, and so does the focal point, a preset's included) and `MaxSourcePixels` (50,000,000).
- **Variants.** The hash of a variant is the SHA-256 of the transform as resolved: the asset's checksum, the size, the fit, the quality, the type chosen and, for a cover, the focal point. The same request under any file name, a preset that resolves alike, and `f=auto` with the type it chose share one variant, and a new focal point or a new file makes a new one. It is written to the asset's storage beside the original, `{site}/{asset}/{hash}{.ext}`, then recorded; two requests that make it at once write the same key and meet at the primary key, and a row whose file is gone is made again. The recurring job `media.variants.cleanup` (daily at 04:45) removes the variants of assets that are deleted or gone, the file first, in batches of 500; a file it cannot delete keeps its row for the next run (event 9264). It has an attempt timeout of an hour and is resumable: each file and row goes on its own, so a run stopped at its limit has succeeded and the next day's run goes on with what is left ([#3529](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3529)).
- **Presets.** `GET /api/management/v1/media/presets` lists them (`media.read`); `PUT /presets/{name}` creates or replaces one and `DELETE` removes it (`media.presets.manage`, which a grant limited to folders does not give). A name is up to 64 lower-case letters, digits and hyphens. The body is `MediaImageOptions` in JSON (`width`, `height`, `fit`, `quality`, `format`, `focalPoint`), checked as a URL's parameters are and against the site's limits; a preset names no other preset. A URL's parameters win over its preset's.
- **Signing.** `sig` is the base64url HMAC-SHA256 of `{id}?{name=value&…}`: the transform parameters as the URL gives them, sorted by name and escaped, without `sig`, `site` and the file name. The key is `FluentCms:Media:SigningKey` (base64, at least 32 bytes) when it is set. Otherwise it is 32 random bytes generated once, at the first start under the lock `fluentcms:media-signing-key`, and stored in `cms_media_signing_key` protected by Data Protection, so that instances that start together share it. An instance reads it once. `FluentCms:Media:RequireSignedUrls` false serves unsigned URLs, and the startup report lists `media-unsigned-urls` while it is off outside Development.
- **Codes.** `FCMS1590` a missing or wrong signature (403), `FCMS1591` a transform over a limit (422), `FCMS1592` an asset the processor cannot read (415) and `FCMS1593` no such preset (404). Events 9261 to 9267.

**Decisions as built (B-65).** Settled for [#2833](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/2833):

- **Dependency.** `SkiaSharp` 3.119.4 and `SkiaSharp.NativeAssets.Linux.NoDependencies` 3.119.4 (MIT), pinned in `Directory.Packages.props`, instead of the ImageSharp the issue named: ImageSharp 4.x and 3.2 fail a Release build without a Six Labors license key, and 3.1.12, the last version without that check, has six open advisories. The Linux assets without dependencies run on the CI runners and in containers without fontconfig; Windows and macOS assets come with `SkiaSharp`. A host that wants ImageSharp, with its own Six Labors license, puts it in the slot.
- **Replaceable processor.** `IImageProcessor` is an SDK provider slot with the SkiaSharp default, and nothing outside `SkiaImageProcessor` knows Skia, so a host or a plugin can use another library, ImageSharp included, or a CDN.
- **Route.** `MapGroup("/media")`, outside `/api`; the file name is ignored on lookup. AVIF only where the processor encodes it, which Skia cannot; WebP from `Accept`, else the asset's type, with `Vary: Accept`.
- **Fail closed.** The route never serves a private or soft-deleted asset, with any signature. Signed downloads of private files are [#2834](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/2834)'s, and so are originals and files that are not images.
- **Limits.** Width, height and operations are the site's media settings and are checked before the image is read; the source's pixels, and a side that follows the aspect ratio, are checked from its header (`SKCodec`) before it is decoded.
- **Variants.** In the asset's own storage, recorded in `cms_media_variant`, removed for deleted assets by a recurring job.
- **Presets.** `cms_media_preset` per site, managed under `/api/management/v1/media/presets`, with the permission checks in the service and a new permission, `media.presets.manage`.
- **Signing key.** A key generated once and protected by Data Protection, not a key derived from the ring, so that signed URLs saved in content and CDNs outlive its rotation (90 days by default). The configured key wins. Replacing the key, by configuration or by deleting the row and restarting every instance, invalidates every URL signed before.
- **Query.** An unknown or repeated parameter is refused rather than ignored, so that a typing error does not serve the original size, and `site` is allowed for the host's site resolution.
- **Migrations.** Only the SQLite set of the media stream at first, as for B-64; [#2711](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/2711) added the PostgreSQL and SQL Server sets.

**Delivery, usage and private assets as built (B-66).** No migration: references are core's `cms_reference`, download URLs are signed and stored nowhere, and purging needs no column.

- **Contracts.** The SDK has `IContentReferences` in `FluentCMS.Content`, which core implements over `cms_reference` for targets that are not content types: `FindAsync` lists the references to a target from entries outside the trash, in both states, without the entries, locales and fields the principal may not read (the evaluator asks once per type, site, state and locale; a non-localized value is asked in the locale of the operation); `CountAsync` counts the entries outside the trash, whatever the principal reads; `RemoveAsync` deletes every reference to the target, trash included, without a version, and evicts the `entry:{id}` tags of those entries with a `CacheInvalidatedEvent`, as a publish does, while the output cache or the cache tag header is on; with the header on, the event also names the tags of their types, which a copy whose header passed its budget carries in their place (4.10, [#3113](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3113)). `IMediaService` adds `DeleteAsync(id, force)`, `ReplaceAsync`, `GetUsageAsync`, `GetDownloadUrlAsync` (`MediaDownloadUrl`) and `DeliverAsync` (`MediaDelivery`, `MediaUrls`), and `FluentCMS.Events` has `MediaAssetReplacedEvent` with the new file's type and size.
- **Delivery.** `GET /api/delivery/v1/media/{id}`, mapped with `MapDelivery("/media")`, answers `id`, `fileName`, `mimeType`, `size`, `width`, `height`, `title`, `locale`, `alt` and `caption` in the request's locale, `credit`, `tags`, `focalPoint`, `isPrivate`, `createdAt`, `updatedAt` and `urls`; no site, folder, checksum or ids of people. A public asset is read by any caller. `urls` has `original`, the signed URL of the original file; `presets`, a signed transform URL per preset of the site for an image the processor reads; and `template`, only while `RequireSignedUrls` is false, a transform URL with `{w}`, `{h}`, `{fit}`, `{q}` and `{f}` placeholders. Each carries `v`, the first 16 characters of the checksum, as the Management API's URLs do, so that a replace gives new URLs and no browser or CDN serves the old file under them for the day of `FluentCms:Media:CacheControl` ([#3278](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3278)); `GetImageUrlAsync` adds it to every transform URL. The answer has the cache rules of content: an `ETag` that hashes the body, no `Last-Modified`, `FluentCms:Delivery:Cache:CacheControl` as written to an anonymous read of a public asset and in its private form otherwise, the `Vary` of content, and a copy in the output cache tagged `media:{id}` and `media-presets:{library}` beside core's `site:{id}`. Outbox handlers evict `media:{id}` on the updated, moved, replaced and deleted events, and a preset's change evicts `media-presets:{library}` at once; each eviction raises `CacheInvalidatedEvent` with its tags, and none happens while `FluentCms:Delivery:Cache:OutputCache` is off and `CacheTagHeader` unset, as for core. With `CacheTagHeader` set the answer carries `site:{id}`, `media:{id}` and `media-presets:{library}` in that header.
- **Private assets.** A private asset is answered, with `urls` null, only to a caller whose `media.read` reaches its folder (ADR 0004), and is otherwise the same `FCMS1585` as an unknown id. A Delivery principal holds no permission but reading content (4.8), so the anonymous caller and Delivery keys never read one; a bearer token or a management key does. `POST /api/management/v1/media/{id}/download-url` needs `media.read` in the asset's folder and answers `{ "url", "expiresAt" }`, `/media/{id}/original/{fileName}?exp=&sig=`, where `exp` is in Unix seconds and the lifetime is the site's media setting `DownloadUrlSeconds` (300, from 10 to 86,400).
- **Original files.** `GET /media/{id}/original/{fileName}`, in the plugin's `/media` group beside transforms, open to anyone. With `exp` it is a download URL: the signature is always checked, whatever `RequireSignedUrls` says, a private asset is served, the answer is an attachment with `Cache-Control: private, no-store`, and from `exp` on it is `FCMS1595` (403). Without `exp` it serves a public asset only, signed unless `RequireSignedUrls` is false, inline with `FluentCms:Media:CacheControl`. The `ETag` is the checksum, `X-Content-Type-Options: nosniff` is always sent, and an SVG gets `Content-Security-Policy: default-src 'none'; img-src data:; style-src 'unsafe-inline'; sandbox`. The stored type is compared with the types the plugin finds from bytes without regard to case and sent as the plugin names it, so `IMAGE/SVG+XML` gets the policy too; any other stored type, such as one an import took from its line before it checked the files ([#3063](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3063)), is sent as `application/octet-stream`, as an attachment. A transform never sends the stored bytes or the stored type: the processor must read the stored type (`FCMS1592`), and what it makes is sent as a type it encodes. It answers `HEAD` too, and a `Range` of one range with `206` and its `Content-Range`, `416` for a range past the end, and the whole file when `If-Range` names another `ETag` or a date, which never matches since the answer has no `Last-Modified`; several ranges or a malformed `Range` get the whole file, so that a player seeks in a video and a download resumes ([#3279](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3279)). The local disk, S3 and Azure Blob open a file as a stream that seeks: S3 and Azure Blob read the length and version with one HEAD and fetch the body at the first read, from where the stream stands, with a ranged request of that version (`StorageReadStream`), so a read in order or a range fetches one body and a `HEAD` of the route none; a storage whose stream cannot seek sends the whole file. A soft-deleted asset is never served. The canonical string is `{id}/original` or `{id}/original?exp={exp}`, which a transform's `{id}?…` never equals. Only `exp`, `sig` and `site` are taken (`FCMS1580`).
- **Usage.** `GET /api/management/v1/media/{id}/usage` needs `media.read` on the asset and answers `{ "items": [{ "type", "entryId", "status", "locale", "field" }] }` from `IContentReferences.FindAsync`, where `status` is `draft` or `published`, `locale` is null for a non-localized value and `field` is the path as queries name it.
- **Delete.** `DELETE /media/{id}` is refused while entries outside the trash refer to the asset (`FCMS1594`, 409), counted across sites since an id is unique; the message gives no count, which would take in entries the caller may not read. `?force=true` removes the references first and then deletes the asset, so that a delete that fails between the two is tried again rather than leaving references to an asset that is gone. A reference from the trash does not hold a delete back; a restored entry then refers to an asset that is not found. Rich text keeps its own copy of a reference, which reads as an asset not found.
- **Purge.** The daily `media.variants.cleanup` also purges, after the variants, every asset deleted longer ago than `FluentCms:Media:DeletedRetention` (30 days) whose variants are gone: the original file, then the row, in batches of 500. A file it cannot delete keeps its row for the next run (event 9269; 9270 counts them). Files go as after a replace ([#3264](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3264)): for an asset whose key a line of an import named, only its rows go, variants included, and an asset that owns its file keeps it, and the row of each variant or original that another asset of the library names, until no other names it, so that the last of them to go deletes the file. The variant rows of an asset whose key a line named go, when it is deleted, to the asset that owns that key with the same checksum, so that their files go with its own ([#3472](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3472)). A variant whose asset is gone owns its file only when the key is in that asset's folder, and then its file goes only when no asset names it; a variant beside another asset's key was made for a key a line named, so only its row goes. The assets whose key a line named are purged first, so that the owner of their key can go in the same run.
- **Replace.** `POST /media/{id}/replace` takes a `multipart/form-data` body of exactly one file and needs `media.update` in the asset's folder. The file passes the checks of an upload against the site's media settings. It is written under a key of its own, `{site}/{asset}/original-{v7}{.ext}`, so the old file serves until the row names the new one; then the name, storage, key, type, size, checksum and image size change in one save with the removal of the variant rows, and the old original and the variant files are deleted (event 9268 when one cannot be) when the instance wrote the old file (`owns_file`, below in 5.1); for a key a line of an import named, only the variant rows go. They all stay when another asset of the library names one of their keys, as a line of an import can, and go to a holder in the same save: a deleted row of their own that owns the old original and takes the variant rows, which the purge then deletes as an owner deleted first ([#3472](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3472)). A request that read the old row just before the save may find the old file gone and answer `FCMS1585`. The id, the metadata and the references stay, and `MediaAssetReplacedEvent` is raised, with the activity row `media.replaced` (`mimeType`, `size`) and the webhook event of the same name.
- **Codes.** `FCMS1594` an asset in use (409) and `FCMS1595` a download URL that has expired (403). Events 9268 to 9270.

**Decisions as built (B-66).** Settled for [#2834](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/2834):

- **Delivery.** Metadata in the request locale with signed URL templates: the original, one per preset, and a placeholder template only where unsigned URLs are served, since a client cannot sign one. The content `ETag`, `Cache-Control` and output cache, with tags that media events and preset changes evict.
- **Usage.** `cms_reference` rows with `target_type = media`, read through `IContentReferences`, which core owns since plugins do not reference it; entries the caller cannot read are left out.
- **Delete.** Refused with `FCMS1594` while referenced; `force=true` deletes and removes the references, as above. Soft-deleted assets are purged after a retention, by the daily cleanup.
- **Replace.** Keeps the id and metadata, runs the upload checks, drops the variants and raises the replaced event, which webhooks deliver as `media.replaced`.
- **Private downloads.** A short-lived signed URL after the folder check, with its lifetime in the media settings, served until it expires and refused after.
- **SVG.** Served from the original-file route with a restrictive `Content-Security-Policy` and `nosniff`.
- **Migrations.** None.

**Field type as built (B-67).** The plugin adds the `media` field type (4.2) and two scoped services. `IMediaResolver` (SDK, `FluentCMS.Media`) resolves asset ids in the site of the operation to the `MediaDelivery` of B-66: the `MediaAsset` with its alternative text and caption in the locale asked for (that of the operation by default) and the `MediaUrls` the Delivery API gives (`original`, a URL per preset of the site for an image the processor reads, and `template` while unsigned URLs are served), with the preset names read once per call. Outside the Delivery API, for a principal that may manage, it needs `media.read` and keeps to the folders a grant limited to folders reaches (ADR 0004), so an asset the caller cannot read, one of another site and one that is deleted are left out, and a caller with no grant of it gets none. A private asset has no URLs. On the Delivery API it gives the public assets, whoever reads, and so it does elsewhere for a principal that may not manage ("Media references on the Delivery API as built" and "Media references for callers that read as on Delivery as built", below). `MediaReferenceResolver` is the plugin's `IReferenceResolver` of `media` (4.4), built on it: a populated asset is `{ "id", "type": "media", "fileName", "mimeType", "size", "width", "height", "title", "alt", "caption", "credit", "focalPoint", "urls" }`, without its folder, tags or who uploaded it, and rich text gets the original's URL and `alt`. `FindMissingAsync` names the ids that are no asset of the site or are deleted, whatever the caller's folders, so a write refuses them with `FCMS2113`. Core stores every media reference, in a field, a list, a block, a repeated component or a rich text node, as a `cms_reference` row with `target_type = media`, `target_id` the asset id, `field` the top-level path of the field that holds it and `locale` `''` unless the field is localized, which is what `IContentReferences` reads for usage and for a delete (B-66). No migration: `cms_reference` exists. `MediaFocalPoint` reads and writes its numbers strictly, so OpenAPI describes `x` and `y` as numbers only.

**Accepted types as built ([#3331](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3331)).** A media field takes the setting `accept` (`FieldSettings.Accept`), a list of MIME types such as `image/png` and families such as `image/*`, from `[Media(Accept = ["image/*"])]` or `"accept": ["image/*"]` in a schema file; no setting, or an empty list, takes every type. The setting is part of the field's settings in the schema snapshot, so no table changes.
