A type and a subtype are each 1 to 127 ASCII letters, digits, `.`, `+`, `-` and `_`, starting with a letter or digit, and the subtype may be `*`; RFC 6838 allows a little more punctuation, which no registered type uses and which generated code and the `type` filter of `GET /media` would have to escape. `*/*` and parameters are refused. A plugin's field type checks the values of its settings with `FieldSettingDescriptor.Validate`, which schema validation calls for a value of the right kind and reports with the descriptor's `ErrorCode` (`FCMS1211` when it has none); the media type's refuses a malformed pattern with `FCMS1596` (422), whose message names the setting, the field and the value, and the analyzers report it as `FCMS0017`. Matching ignores case and the parameters after `;`, and a family matches its prefix with the slash, so `image/*` takes `image/PNG` and not `imagex/png`, as the admin matches. `MediaFieldValidator`, an `IFieldValidator<object>` of the plugin, reads the types of the assets in the site's library once a validation run, at the first field with `accept`, for every field with `accept` in `ValidationContext.Fields`, the fields the run validates at any depth ([#3349](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3349)), and refuses each asset of another type with `FCMS1597` (422) on the path of the field, at any depth, with a message that names the field and its types and not the asset's own type, which a caller may not be allowed to read. The verdict still shows something: for an asset in a folder the caller cannot reach, or a private one, a refusal tells that its type is outside the field's types, as the reference check already tells that it exists (`FCMS2113`). This is accepted, and the message says no more. An asset that is not in the library is left to the reference check. The `type` filter of `GET /media` lower-cases what it is given, since stored types are lower case, so a picker whose `accept` is written in capitals lists the same assets. Like a tightened length, the rule holds on every save and publish: an entry saved before `accept` was added or narrowed keeps its asset, and its next save is refused until the asset is replaced, with the field named. The source generator and the attribute reader of the analyzers read `[Media(Accept)]` without a change to the builders, and `fluentcms generate csharp` and `typescript` write the types in the comment of the field (`media, accepts image/*`) when every one passes the same check, which the analyzers and the CLI link from the plugin. An import's dry run counts the assets the package brings as present for the reference check, but they are not in the library yet, so it does not report `FCMS1597`; the import itself may. The Blog sample's `Article.Hero` takes `image/*`.

**Decisions as built (B-67).** Settled for [#2835](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/2835):

- **Field type.** `media` is the plugin's, through `AddFieldType`, with `MediaRef` and `MediaRefList`, stored in `cms_reference` with `target_type = media`, an OpenAPI fragment and `eq` and `in` on the asset id. Without the plugin `[Media]` stops the host with `FCMS1203`.
- **Writes.** A write refuses an asset it adds that does not exist in the site or is deleted, with the core code `FCMS2113`, since the check is core's for any target with a resolver; an asset the entry already had is not checked again.
- **`IMediaResolver`.** In the SDK: asset ids to `MediaDelivery`, metadata and the signed URLs of the Delivery API in the request locale, checked against `media.read` and the caller's folders. An asset the caller cannot read resolves to null, and a private one populates without a public URL.
- **Population.** Core populates through `IReferenceResolver`, an SDK hook for targets that are no content type, so it references no plugin and knows no media. `populate=hero` returns the asset; without populate or beyond the depth the value is `{ "id", "type": "media" }`. A populate path naming a block list or repeated component resolves the media inside it.
- **Rich text and blocks.** Media nodes and media fields in blocks and components use the same resolver and count in `cms_reference`. The HTML and Markdown renderers write the URL and the alternative text, the node's own or else the asset's, escaped.
- **Blog sample.** `Hero`, `Avatar` and the images of the hero and gallery blocks are media fields again, and `Logo` is a setting of the `blog` area holding the asset's id, since settings have no field types (4.16).
- **Migrations.** None.

**Media references on the Delivery API as built ([#3109](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3109)).** A Delivery principal never holds `media.read` (4.8), so `IMediaResolver` refused every Delivery caller: a media field with `populate` answered null and an image node of `richText=html` had no `src`, for the Public role, a Delivery key whose role holds `media.read` and a preview token alike, while `GET /api/delivery/v1/media/{id}` served the same public asset to anyone. On the Delivery API (`OperationContext.IsDelivery`, 2.2) the resolver asks no permission and gives the assets of the library of the operation's site (shared or per site) that are public and not deleted, whoever reads, with the URLs of the media route: `original`, a URL per preset and the template where unsigned URLs are served. A private asset, a deleted one and one of another site's library are left out there, so a field is null, a list leaves them out and an image node has no `src`, also for a managing principal that calls the Delivery API. In a site where the plugin is disabled (4.13), whose media routes answer 404, the resolver gives nothing on the Delivery API. The Management API, and every other way in, resolves by the caller's folder grants as before, for a principal that may manage ([#3112](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3112), below). A populated asset carries the tags of the media route's copy, `media:{id}` and `media-presets:{library}`, as `ResolvedReference.CacheTags`, so a Delivery read that shows it, from a field, a block or rich text, is tagged with them in the output cache and the cache tag header (4.10), and the outbox handlers that evict the route's copy on an update (`isPrivate` included), move, replace or delete, and a preset's change, evict it too. No migration, and no new diagnostic code or log event.

**Decisions as built.** Settled for [#3109](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3109):

- **Surface.** The request bridge tells the surface from the endpoint's metadata and sets `IsDelivery` on the operation; it states which API was called and decides nothing. The media plugin's resolver holds the rule, and the plugin reads the SDK only. The evaluator is unchanged, so a Delivery principal still holds no `media.read`.
- **Whoever reads.** On the Delivery API the rule is the media route's for anyone, not the principal's grants: a private asset never resolves there, whoever calls, and a public one resolves for every caller. The media route still answers a private asset, without URLs, to a caller whose `media.read` reaches it, as B-66 built.
- **Preview tokens.** No rule of their own: a preview token reads the Delivery API, so its draft resolves media as any Delivery read does.
- **Disabled plugin.** The rule holds only where the plugin is enabled for the site of the operation (`IPluginState`), so population shows no asset that the media routes of that site would not serve. The copies a site's Delivery API held go with the eviction of the site that disabling the plugin raises.
- **Caching.** A resolver of another kind of target names the tags of what it resolved, and core adds them to the Delivery copy beside its own, keeping only tags of visible ASCII without spaces or commas, which the header can carry. An asset that turns public shows in a copy that held it out when the copy expires, as a newly published relation target does (4.10).

**Media references for callers that read as on Delivery as built ([#3112](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3112)).** `/graphql` sets no `IsDelivery`, since it also serves managing principals, mutations and drafts, so a principal without the `management` scope, which holds no `media.read` (4.8), got a null media field and an image without `src` there, where REST Delivery answered the asset: the anonymous caller as the Public role, a Delivery or Preview key, a preview token and a member's token. The resolver now takes the Delivery rule on the Delivery API and, outside it, for a principal that may not manage, which `IPermissionChecker.CanManageAsync` (4.13) answers from the evaluator's `CanManage`: the `management` scope, the `mcp` scope on the MCP server, or the system. Such a caller gets on `/graphql` what REST Delivery gives it: a public, not deleted asset of the library of the operation's site, with the URLs of the media route, never a private one, a deleted one or one of another site's library, and none in a site where the plugin is disabled. A managing principal on `/graphql` resolves by its folder grants, as on the Management API. In a site where the plugin is disabled no asset resolves, whoever reads and through whichever API.

**Decisions as built.** Settled for [#3112](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3112):

- **Principal, not surface.** `/graphql` does not set `IsDelivery`. Outside the Delivery API the rule keys on whether the principal may manage, which core's evaluator decides and `IPermissionChecker.CanManageAsync` reports through the SDK; the request bridge and the GraphQL plugin are unchanged, and the rule stays in the media plugin's resolver. On the Delivery API the surface still decides, for a managing caller too.
- **Every surface.** The rule follows the principal wherever it reads outside the Delivery API, so a Delivery key on `/mcp`, which reads content alone, gets public assets there too; such a principal never held `media.read` (4.8), so it got none before. Jobs, the command line tool and seeding run as the system, which manages, and resolve as before.
- **Preview tokens.** A preview token carries the scope `preview` alone, so on `/graphql` its draft resolves media as on the Delivery API, and its relation targets are read as [#3095](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3095) built.
- **Disabled plugin.** Where the media plugin is disabled for the operation's site, the resolver gives nothing to a managing caller too, the Management API included, as it gives nothing to any other: a disabled plugin shows nothing in that site (4.13), and its media routes there answer 404.
- **Caching.** `/graphql` is a plugin's group, which core maps with `NoCache()`, so none of its answers is output cached and the `CacheTags` its reads resolve go unused.
- **SDK addition.** `IPermissionChecker.CanManageAsync`, in `PublicAPI.Unshipped.txt`. No new code, log event id or migration.

**Asset URLs on the Management API as built ([#3100](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3100)).** The media library and the picker of the admin show assets as thumbnails, and the Management API read an asset without a URL: the Delivery route gave URLs one asset per call and none for a private asset, a download URL served the original as an attachment, and a site without presets had no URL at thumbnail size, which the admin cannot sign while `RequireSignedUrls` is on. `GET /api/management/v1/media` and `GET /api/management/v1/media/{id}` now answer every member of the asset and `urls`: `original`, the signed URL of the original file; `thumbnail`, for an image the processor reads (`CanDecode` of its type), the signed URL `/media/{id}/thumbnail/{fileName}`, and null for any other file; and `expiresAt`. The URLs are relative to the base of the CMS, as the Delivery ones are. An asset a read gives is one the caller's `media.read` reaches (ADR 0004), so a caller whose grant does not reach the folder gets no URL: the asset is `FCMS1585` and the list leaves it out. A public asset's URLs do not expire, as the Delivery API's original does not, and `expiresAt` is null. A private asset's are short-lived, about as long as a download URL (`DownloadUrlSeconds` of the site of the request, below), and `expiresAt` says when they stop: the original is `/media/{id}/original/{fileName}?disposition=inline&exp=&v=&sig=`, which serves the file inline where a download URL sends an attachment, and the thumbnail carries `exp` too. Every URL of these reads carries `v`, the first 16 characters of the asset's checksum, which the signature covers and the routes do not compare, so that a replace gives the asset new URLs and no browser or CDN serves the old file under them. With `FluentCms:Media:Library` set to `PerSite`, each URL also ends with `site={id}`, the site of the request, outside the signature as the routes read it: an image the browser asks for sends no `X-Cms-Site`, and without it the routes would look in the library of the host's site. A list signs two URLs per asset, an HMAC each, and reads the settings once, only for a page with a private asset; it reads no file and makes no variant. The OpenAPI document describes the bodies as `MediaAssetResponse` and `MediaAssetPage`; the other routes that answer an asset keep the shape of `MediaAsset`. The MCP plugin's `media_get`, `media_list` and `media_search` answer without `urls` (5.4).

- **Expiry.** A private asset's `exp` is the time of the read plus `DownloadUrlSeconds`, rounded down to a multiple of half of `DownloadUrlSeconds` in Unix seconds. The reads within one such half give the same URLs, so a library that reads its page again does not fetch every private thumbnail again, and each URL still works for more than half of `DownloadUrlSeconds` and never longer. A download URL keeps its exact lifetime.

- **Thumbnail.** `GET /media/{id}/thumbnail/{fileName}`, in the plugin's `/media` group beside the original, open to anyone. It takes `exp`, `v`, `sig` and `site` (`FCMS1580` for any other parameter, a size included) and always checks the signature, whatever `RequireSignedUrls` says. The canonical string is `{id}/thumbnail` with the `exp` and `v` it carries, sorted by name (`{id}/thumbnail?exp={exp}&v={v}`), which neither a transform's `{id}?…` nor an original's `{id}/original…` equals, so a thumbnail's signature serves no other size, asset or file, and theirs no thumbnail. The image is fitted inside 320 by 320 pixels (`contain`, so a smaller one is enlarged), at the default quality, as WebP where the processor encodes it and otherwise as a transform without `f` is. Its size is the plugin's and no URL changes it, so it is not held to the site's `MaxTransformWidth`, `MaxTransformHeight` and `MaxTransformOperations`; `MaxSourcePixels` still holds, against the recorded size and the header (`FCMS1591`), and a type the processor does not read is `FCMS1592`. It is stored as a variant, shared with a transform that resolves alike, and goes with the variants on a replace and in the cleanup. Without `exp` it serves a public asset only, with `FluentCms:Media:CacheControl`; with `exp` a private asset too, until it expires (`FCMS1595`). It sends `X-Content-Type-Options: nosniff`.
- **Inline originals.** The original-file route also takes `disposition=inline`, only with `exp` (`FCMS1580` without it or with another value), and `v`. The canonical string is then `{id}/original?disposition=inline&exp={exp}&v={v}`, so a download URL is not turned into an inline one or back; a public original of these reads signs `{id}/original?v={v}`. Such a URL serves the file as a public original is served: an SVG with its policy, and a type the plugin does not find from bytes as an attachment.
- **Caching.** A URL with `exp` that serves inline, the original or the thumbnail, is answered with `Cache-Control: private, max-age=` the seconds it has left, so no shared cache keeps a private file and a browser keeps it no longer than the URL works; a download URL keeps `private, no-store`. The Management answers are `no-store`, as every Management answer is, and no URL of a private asset reaches the Delivery API, its output cache or its tags: the Delivery route and the resolver still give a private asset no URL.

**Decisions as built.** Settled for [#3100](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3100):

- **A route of its own.** The thumbnail is a route beside the original, not a transform URL with the plugin's parameters, so that it is always signed, takes no size, is the only image outside the site's transform limits, and serves a private asset with an expiry, which the transform route never does (B-65).
- **Size and format.** 320 by 320 with `contain`, as WebP at quality 80: a grid tile at twice its size whatever the aspect ratio, with no `Vary: Accept`. The default processor encodes WebP and the browsers the admin runs in read it.
- **Private URLs.** Short-lived, about as long as download URLs, served inline, and given only with a read that found the asset within the caller's folders; `expiresAt` tells a client when to read again. The expiry is rounded down to half the lifetime so that reads close together give the same URLs.
- **Version.** `v` from the checksum, signed and not compared, since a public thumbnail is cached for a day and keeps its URL otherwise; an older URL still serves the asset as it is now.
- **Shape.** `urls` on the two reads, as the issue asks; upload, update, move and replace answer `MediaAsset` as before. `IMediaService` is unchanged: the reads with URLs are the plugin's own, for its endpoints. The MCP tools keep the JSON of the routes without `urls`, which an agent cannot use and which would put the bearer URLs of private assets into its context.
- **Site.** With `PerSite`, `site` names the site of the request, as a Delivery URL is read with it; with `Shared` every site reads the same library, so the URLs carry none.
- No new code, log event id, migration or `PublicAPI` entry.

**Storage providers as built (B-68).** `FluentCMS.Plugins.Media.S3` and `FluentCMS.Plugins.Media.AzureBlob` reference the media plugin (2.4) and fill its `IFileStorage` slot for new uploads, through `MediaPlugin.UseStorage`, the seam their extension methods call: `cms.AddPlugin<MediaPlugin>(media => media.UseS3(section))` or `media.UseAzureBlob(section)`. The last call wins, and the host's own `Providers.Use` wins over it. The options are bound from the section and checked at start, with a message that names the keys and quotes no value. No migration.

- **S3.** `S3FileStorage` (`s3`) on `AWSSDK.S3`: `Bucket`, `Endpoint` for an S3-compatible service (with it, the request and response checksums the SDK adds by default are sent only where required, since many such services do not know them), `Region`, `KeyPrefix`, `ForcePathStyle`, and `AccessKeyId`, `SecretAccessKey` and `SessionToken`, or none of them for the AWS SDK's default chain. A write is one `PUT` (a stream that cannot seek is copied to a temporary file first, as the SDK needs the length), and a delete looks the object up first, since S3 answers a `DELETE` alike either way. The credentials need `s3:GetObject`, `s3:PutObject`, `s3:DeleteObject` and `s3:ListBucket`.
- **Azure Blob.** `AzureBlobFileStorage` (`azure-blob`) on `Azure.Storage.Blobs`: `ConnectionString` with `Container`, or `ContainerUrl` with a SAS in its query (read, write, delete and list), and `KeyPrefix`. A 404 of a missing container is an error, not a missing file.
- **Keys.** `FileStorageKeys` (public, in the media plugin) checks a key for every storage as the local disk did: segments of letters, digits, dots, hyphens and underscores. A cloud storage puts its prefix before it.
- **Private objects.** Objects are written without an ACL or public access, and bytes leave through `/media` and the signed URLs only. Variants are written beside their asset, in its bucket or container.
- **Older assets.** `MediaStorage` resolves an asset's storage by the name it recorded: the one in the slot, else a keyed `IFileStorage` of that name. The plugin always registers the local disk as the keyed `local`, so an asset written there is still transformed, served from the original-file route and download URLs, and replaced, and its variants and file are still written, cleaned up and purged there, after the slot moves to S3 or Azure Blob. Transforms, the original-file route, replace (which writes the new file to the slot and deletes the old one and its variants from the asset's storage), and the cleanup and purge read through it; a storage it cannot find leaves the file and its row for the next run.
- **Health.** The plugin adds the ready check `media-storage` (five seconds), which the storage in the slot answers as an `IHealthCheck`: the local disk creates and deletes a file in its root, S3 sends a `HEAD` of the bucket, and Azure Blob lists one blob under the prefix, which a container SAS allows. A failure is Unhealthy with a description that names the bucket or container and the status and error code, or the type of the exception, and carries no exception, since the health service logs one whole. A storage that is no `IHealthCheck` is reported Healthy as unchecked.
- **Tests.** One contract (write, open, exists, metadata, replace, delete, a missing key, keys refused, a stream that cannot seek, ready) runs on the local disk, S3 on MinIO, and Azure Blob on Azurite with a connection string and with a SAS, through Testcontainers on the Linux CI jobs. Hosts on each cloud storage upload, transform through a signed URL and find the variant in the bucket or container, serve an asset of the local disk after the move, report `/health/ready` Unhealthy once the bucket or container is gone, and log, report and answer no credential.

**Decisions as built (B-68).** Settled for [#2836](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/2836):

- **Dependencies.** `AWSSDK.S3` 4.0.104.11 (Apache-2.0), the latest 4.0 patch, and `Azure.Storage.Blobs` 12.30.1 (MIT), each referenced by its provider only; `Testcontainers.Minio` 4.15.0 and `Testcontainers.Azurite` 4.16.0 (MIT) for the tests. All are pinned in `Directory.Packages.props`.
- **Configuration.** `media.UseS3(section)` and `media.UseAzureBlob(section)`. Managed identity would need `Azure.Identity` and is left out.
- **Private objects.** Bytes leave through `/media` and signed URLs, never through public bucket URLs.
- **Health.** One ready check, `media-storage`, answered by the storage in the slot, local disk included; descriptions hold no credential and results no exception.
- **Older assets.** Read by the recorded provider name, the local disk always among them; the change to transforms, the original-file route, replace, and the cleanup and purge job is the resolver alone.
- **Tests without Docker.** The tests that need MinIO or Azurite are skipped with the reason where Docker is missing, and on Windows, whose runners run Windows containers only; elsewhere an image that does not start fails them. `CONTRIBUTING.md` says so, and no GitHub Action was added. MinIO no longer publishes images (`minio/minio` is gone from Docker Hub and `quay.io/minio/minio` asks for a login), so the tests use the Pigsty project's continuation of the MinIO community build, `pgsty/minio`, pinned by the digest of its `RELEASE.2026-08-04T00-00-00Z` index (`sha256:b6bfe723…`), since a community tag can be moved; it keeps MinIO's entrypoint and ports, and Azurite `3.35.0` with `--skipApiVersionCheck`.
- **Migrations.** None.

**Transfer as built (B-92).** The plugin's `ITransferContributor`, `media`, writes `plugins/media/folders.jsonl` (parents first) and `plugins/media/assets.jsonl` with every column of an asset that is not deleted but its storage provider and key, which are kept as a hint, and with `mediaFiles=true` each original under `plugins/media/files/{id}/original{ext}`. An import keeps the ids, times and authors, and writes the rows of the library of the site of the import directly, without the media permissions, which `transfer.manage` stands for. A folder moved in the package takes its subtree with it. The rules of an upload are not waived ([#3063](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3063)): every file an asset takes passes the checks of an upload in the site of the import, through the same code (`MediaService.PrepareAsync`), which are the executable check of the bytes and of the name, the type found from the bytes, the site's `AllowedTypes`, the `Svg` setting (an SVG is sanitised or refused) and `MaxFileSize` on the bytes read, with no limit of a request. A file they refuse is an item error with the upload's code (`FCMS1581` to `FCMS1584`), and its asset is not imported; a dry run runs the same checks. An asset takes its type, size, checksum and image size from what the checks found, never from its line, and no duration, which the checks do not find, as after a replace; its file name is held to an upload's (`FCMS1580`). A line whose checksum is that of the asset in the instance keeps the asset's file with its name, type, size, image size and duration, and its file in the package is not read, so that rules the site tightened after the asset was stored do not refuse the line ([#3069](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3069)). Any other asset whose file is in the package has it checked against its checksum, then by those checks, and written to the storage in the slot under a key of that write's own, `{library}/{asset}/original-{id}{ext}` with the extension of the type found, as a replace through the service writes; a file written for a row the database then does not save is deleted again, as for an upload. A key of its own keeps that deletion from taking a file another import wrote for its row: with `Shared` the imports into two sites write one library, and each holds the lock of its own site only. A line without a file is taken only when the storage the package names has the key it names, that key is of the library of the site of the import, as a storage shared between instances has (an asset deleted there then deletes the shared file), and the file at the key passes the same checks and is the line's file as they would store it, so the asset takes the type the instance's file has and no line gives a stored file another type. When the key is in the folder of an asset of the library, that asset, read before the file is checked, must not be deleted and must still name the key, as its file or a variant's, and the save of the line is conditional on its version as read, so that a delete and a purge between the check and the save refuse the line rather than leave it naming a file that is gone ([#3472](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3472)). Otherwise it is reported with `FCMS3220` and not imported, so no entry refers to an asset that cannot be served. A file of the package and a key of a storage are taken by one line of an import ([#3065](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3065)): a later line that names one is `FCMS3220` and its asset is not imported, without the file being read, whatever became of the first line, so an import writes no more to the storage than its package holds and reads a key once. Keys are compared without regard to case, and a key with a segment that ends with a dot is `FCMS3220` (a space is no character of a key), since Windows and macOS by default read `original.png`, `ORIGINAL.png` and `original.png.` as one file. The lines of both files are imported as they are read and none is kept, and core holds each to `FluentCms:Transfer:MaxLineLength` (4.14). The media settings are read when the media section starts, after the import wrote the settings of the package, while a dry run, which writes no settings, checks against those the site has. An id of another library or of a deleted asset is `FCMS3221`. The fields of a line are held to what the service allows, before anything is read or written and in a dry run alike, so that the database refuses none of them and a dry run reports what the import does ([#3069](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3069)): an id, a file name, a checksum, a title and a credit of at most 256 characters, an alternative text and a caption that are a text of at most 1,000 and 2,000 characters for each locale, no locale twice, at most 50 tags the service takes, stored as it stores them, trimmed and once each, and a focal point whose x and y are both given from 0 to 1 (`FCMS1580`). A folder line is held to what a create, a rename and a move allow, checked against the lines before it in a dry run too: an id, a name the service takes (`FCMS1580`), no parent that is the folder or one below it (`FCMS1589`), and no folder deeper than the 20 levels folders nest to, its subtree included (`FCMS1580`). A read or a save of the database that fails all the same, as when another writer took the id at that moment, is `FCMS3221`, and a read or a write of the storage that fails `FCMS3220`, each with a message that says no more than that, since the exception can name the server's paths; the exception goes to the log (warning 9274). An asset is skipped when its `updatedAt` and checksum are the instance's, a conflict when the instance's is newer unless `overwrite=true`, and its events are the upload, replace or update events, with the import's origin. An import removes two kinds of file ([#3133](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3133)). A file it wrote for a row it did not save is deleted again. A file that an update replaces is removed with its variants, rows and files, as a replace through the service removes them, after the row that names the new file is saved, and only when the instance wrote it: `owns_file` is set on an upload, a replace and a file an import writes, and cleared on a key a line named, so a key a line of a package named, in this import or an earlier one, is never deleted, by an import or by a replace through the service. The column is in the media baseline `Initial`, so a database of an earlier build is recreated (H5). An update whose key a line named again counts as named, and the key the saved row names is never among the files removed, as when a line names a variant of the asset as its file. Only an asset whose key a line named can share a key with another, since every key the instance writes is in its asset's folder under a name of its own, so the check of the other assets reads those rows of the library and their variant rows only (index `ix_cms_media_asset_site_owns_file`), and, for keys outside the folder of the asset checked, as a holder's are, the asset of that folder by its id. When the old key is named, only the variant rows go, so that no later replace deletes the variant files beside that key. A file that another asset of the library names, as its file or a variant's, stays with the variants: an asset that names another's key makes its variants in that folder, at the other's variant keys when the bytes are the same, and a dry run, a row whose save fails and an import stopped before the save delete nothing. A delete that fails is event 9268 and the item is still updated. An update that keeps the asset's file writes none of its file columns, so an import into another site of a `Shared` library that replaced the file meanwhile keeps its key. A line for another asset that names the old key of an asset while a replace through the service or an import into another site replaces that asset's file no longer loses it: the line's save holds the asset's version, and the replace checks for other assets again after its save and before it deletes. A line for the asset itself that names a key of its own folder which it no longer names, as the old key is once the replace has saved, is refused as a line whose file is not there (`FCMS3220`), so the asset never takes a file that a holder, or nothing, owns ([#3472](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3472), below). Assets are reported under the section `media`, the reference target, so that a dry run counts them as there for the entries that refer to them, and folders under `media/folders`.

**Shared library as built (B-80).** `FluentCms:Media:Library` is `Shared` (the default) or `PerSite` (row 7.10, and the first half of 7.15). With `Shared`, every site reads and writes the library of the default site: its assets, folders and presets, whose `site_id` is the default site's, and whose files are under that site's storage keys. Only the rows follow the library. The permission is checked in the site of the request, with the folder limits of ADR 0004, so each site's roles decide who uploads and who reads, and the media settings (upload rules, transform limits) are those of the site of the request. Content of any site refers to the shared assets: population, the check of the assets a write names, the transform and original routes and the Delivery route all read the library. The usage of an asset lists the entries of every site, and a delete is refused while any refers to it. A Delivery copy is tagged with the site of its request, the asset and `media-presets:{library}`, so a preset change evicts the copies of every site. With `PerSite`, each site keeps its own library, as built in M3, and an asset of one site is unknown in another. An instance with one site behaves the same in both. An instance that already has several sites with assets of their own sets `PerSite`, since `Shared` reads the default site's library only. The option adds no migration and moves no row.

**Hidden libraries in the startup report as built ([#3011](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3011)).** A host that turns `PerSite` back to `Shared` hides what its sites uploaded while each kept its own library, and nothing said so. With `Shared`, the plugin's `IInsecureDefaultSource` `SharedLibraryReport` looks, each time the report is made (at start and by `fluentcms doctor`), for assets that are not deleted in a site other than the library's, and when it finds some the report lists `media-shared-library-hides-sites`, logged as a warning with event id 9131, which names `FluentCms:Media:Library=PerSite` and up to five of those sites by id and name, ordered by name, with "and more" when the query found a sixth. A name loses its control characters and line separators, since a site's own administrator chooses it and the line reaches the log and the terminal of `doctor`. A site deleted while its rows wait for the outbox is left out. With `PerSite`, with one site, or with no asset elsewhere, it lists nothing. The check is one query of the plugin's table: the union of the two ranges of `site_id` on either side of the library, at most six ids, which each provider reads from the index on `site_id`, so no row of the library is read and none is counted (`<>` is a scan on SQLite and PostgreSQL, and so are the two ranges joined by `OR` on SQLite); then the names of at most five sites through `ISiteAdministration.FindAsync`, as the system with `Origin.Job`, since that read needs `sites.manage`. A check that throws, as on a table not yet migrated under `doctor`, is logged with the type of the exception and no message, which can name the connection (event id 9273), and lists nothing, so the report is still made. It is an entry of the insecure defaults because that list is the one a plugin adds to; it adds no section to the report. Per H9 (10.4) there is no upgrade note for an instance built on M3: with one migration baseline and nothing published before it, that upgrade cannot occur. Event 9273.

**Deleted sites as built ([#3001](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3001)).** The plugin hears `SiteDeletedEvent` through the outbox (`media.cleanup.site.deleted`, `DeletedSiteMedia`) and removes the library of the site. It marks every asset of the site deleted, removes the site's presets and folders, and then removes each asset, 500 at a time: its variants, each file from the storage the asset recorded and then the row, and then the original file and the asset's row. Files go as in the purge ([#3264](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3264)): the assets whose key a line named go first, with their rows only, their variant rows going to the asset that owns the key as in the purge, so that the asset that owns the key then finds no other that names it and deletes them with its own. A file that cannot be deleted, whether the storage fails, times out on its own or is no longer one the instance has, is logged (event 9271) and leaves its asset as a deleted row, which the daily cleanup purges with its file after `DeletedRetention`; the site stays deleted. A delivery cut short by the outbox's `DeliveryTimeout`, or delivered again, goes on with what is left, and event 9272 counts what was removed. A library too large for the outbox's attempts leaves the event failed, and since every asset was marked deleted first, the cleanup purges the rest. With `Shared` the library is the default site's, which cannot be deleted, so only the rows a site kept while the library was `PerSite` are found. No `MediaAssetDeletedEvent` is raised for the removed assets: the Delivery copies go with the eviction of the site (4.6), and the site's webhooks go with the site (5.2).

**Files shared between assets as built ([#3472](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3472)).** Follow-ups of [#3264](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3264), settled by one rule: no file that an asset of this database names, live or kept for another, is deleted or disowned, and every file kept for another asset has a row that owns it, so that it goes once the last asset that names it is gone. No migration, new error code or log event.

- **A replaced file that another asset names.** A replace, through the service or by an import, of an owner whose old original or variant another asset names keeps the files, as before, and gives them to a holder in the same save: a new row of the library with an id of its own, `owns_file` set, the old file's name, key, type, size and checksum, no folder, metadata or references, `deleted_at` the time of the replace, and the old variant rows. The cleanup treats it as an owner deleted first: its variant files that no other asset names go at the next run, and its original once `DeletedRetention` has passed and no other names it. Handing `owns_file` to the asset that names the key was not taken. The check of other assets reads only the rows that do not own their file, which holds because an owner's keys are in its own folder; an asset made owner of a key in another's folder would be missed, and a later replace or purge of that other would delete the variant files it names. A holder's keys are in the folder of the asset it was split from, so for keys outside the folder of the asset checked, the check also reads the asset of that folder by its id: when that asset holds the same bytes again, its variants take the holder's keys and stay with it. A line of an import can name the owner's key after the replace's check and before its save: the line's save holds the owner's version without moving it, so both saves stand. The replace therefore checks again after its save, just before it deletes the old files, and when an asset names one of them by then it gives them to a holder instead, in a save of their own; a holder that cannot be saved leaves the files with no row, rather than delete them, and is logged as warning 9278, which says that an asset still names them and that they must not be removed by hand while it does.
- **Variant files of an asset on another's key** (found in the second review of [#3467](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/pull/3467)). When such an asset is deleted, replaced, or its site is deleted, its variant rows go to the asset that owns its key with the same storage and checksum, which makes the same variants at the same keys, for each parameter hash that asset has no row of; their files then go with that asset's. Each row is handed over in a save of its own: one that the owner made at the same moment beats it and counts as held, and one whose save fails otherwise stays with the asset, which the deletion of a site then leaves as a deleted row, for the cleanup to try again (after a replace the row is gone, and its file stays with no row). Without such an asset only the rows go, since the files may be another instance's. A variant whose asset row is gone owns its file only when the key is in that asset's own folder; one beside another asset's key was made for a key a line named, so only its row goes.
- **Instances that share a storage and not a database.** Each instance checks other assets in its own database, so an owner on one instance that is purged, replaced or removed with its site deletes a file that an asset imported on another instance by a line without a file still names. One database cannot see the other, so this is a limit the plugin states (README): such a storage is safe only with a shared database, and an import between such instances brings the files (`mediaFiles=true`). An import line without a file could take a copy under a key of its own, since it reads the whole file for its checks already; that changes what an import stores, so it is left to a follow-up.
- **A deleted owner kept for a shared file keeps its id.** An import of its id is `FCMS3221` for as long as another asset names its file. Giving the id back would mean moving the row to a holder at the purge, but no column tells a holder from an asset that was deleted, so the purge would move each holder again on every run; and a row cannot change its id in place. The id stays taken. An operator who must import the asset under its id deletes the assets that name its file, the rows of the library with `owns_file` false and the same `storage_key`; once `DeletedRetention` has passed, the cleanup purges them and then the owner, and the id is free.
- **An import that names a file the cleanup deletes.** A line without a file whose key is in the folder of an asset of the library is refused with `FCMS3220`, in a dry run too, when that asset is deleted, since the cleanup deletes the file once nothing else names it, and when the asset no longer names the key as its file or a variant's, since a holder or nothing owns it then. Otherwise the save of the line is conditional on that asset's version as read before the file was checked, and writes nothing to it, as a replace is conditional on its own (the version moves on with a delete), so a delete and a purge between the check and the save refuse the line with `FCMS3220` rather than leave it naming a file that is gone. A key in the folder of no asset of this database is another instance's and is taken as before. A line for the asset itself whose key is in the asset's own folder is taken only while the asset names that key; otherwise it is refused as a line whose file is not there (`FCMS3220`). Left: a holder whose first asset is purged too is found by no folder, so a line that names its file while the last asset that names it is purged at that moment can still lose the file; the variant rows of a holder whose row is gone are those of a gone asset; and the tests of these checks run on SQLite only, so the conditional save and the combined queries of the check of other assets have not run on PostgreSQL or SQL Server.

**SVG and backfill follow-ups as built ([#3484](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3484)).** Follow-ups of [#3309](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3309). No migration, new error code or log event.

- **Style and ids of an inlined SVG.** The sanitiser stores less for new files, uploads, replaces and the package files of an import with `Svg` set to `Sanitize`, and touches no stored file. A `<style>` element goes whatever it holds, since its rules apply to the whole page that inlines the SVG; scoping them would mean rewriting selectors, `@keyframes` names and `@media` blocks, which is easy to get wrong, while a `style` attribute applies to its element only and stays, but not on the root. The root also loses `overflow`, `transform` and `filter`: with them, or a `style` such as `position: fixed`, or a filter region or drop shadow larger than its box, the drawing paints over the page around it, and its own drawing needs none of them there. An animation of the root, one that is a child of it without a link or one whose `href` or `xlink:href` is the root's id (either, since browsers differ on which wins), goes when it sets one of those four or the root's `width`, `height`, `x`, `y`, `viewBox` or `preserveAspectRatio`, which would grow the drawing past the size the page's CSS gives it, and so does an `animateTransform` or `animateMotion` of the root. These are the ways out of its box that are known; the sanitiser does not promise there is no other. What contains an inlined drawing is the page's box for it, with `overflow: hidden` and `contain: paint` (README). A value read as CSS that holds `element(` or `-moz-element(`, which paint an element of the page, goes, and so does an animation of `class` or `id`, as one of a link does. Then every `id`, and every name that a link (`href`, `xlink:href`), a `url(#…)` in any value, `aria-labelledby`, `aria-describedby`, or the `begin` and `end` of an animation (`id.event`, `id.begin`, `id.end`, `id.repeat(n)`) refers to, takes the prefix `fcms_`, sixteen hex digits of the SHA-256 of the file as `Save` writes it before the prefix, and an underscore (`SvgDocument.Scope`); with a hyphen, Chromium reads `begin="fcms-…-a.end"` as a name and an offset, and the animation never starts. So an inlined SVG puts no name a page uses on `window`, clashes with no id of the page or of another SVG, and reaches no element of the page: a reference to no id of the file takes the prefix too and still finds nothing. Its own references still find their elements. A `begin` or `end` list goes first when one of its values is not read whole by the timing grammar (an optional id and a dot before an event, `begin`, `end` or `repeat(n)`, with its offset; `indefinite`; a clock value), since a browser may read what the grammar does not, such as a backslash's escape, a no-break space or a parenthesis, as a name the prefix does not cover. `accessKey(…)` and `wallclock(…)` are not read either: Chrome reads an id and an event inside `wallclock()`, and `accessKey()` listens to keys on the whole page, of no use to a drawing. An animation whose `begin` went starts when the drawing loads. So does a value whose `url()` the pass cannot read. The uploader can write ids and references with a prefix of the form already, such as another SVG's read from its public file, or give the ids the file's own prefix and leave the references plain, so a prefix the file carries is kept only when taking it off every name and scoping the file again gives the same bytes, which holds for a file the pass wrote, so that sanitising it again gives the same bytes and a line of an import can still take its key; otherwise every name takes the file's own on top. A file nested more than `SvgDocument.MaxDepth` (128) levels of elements deep, the root included, or holding more than `SvgDocument.MaxNodes` (100,000) nodes (elements, text, CDATA sections, comments and processing instructions, which the tree leaves out but its building still reads), or with an element of more than `SvgDocument.MaxAttributes` (1,000) attributes, namespace declarations included, is no SVG (`FCMS1581`): the reader finds it before the tree is built, and the passes over the tree, `Save`'s included, recurse as deep as the file nests. These limits bound what the passes cost. `Sanitize` reads each element's children and attributes once and sets the kept ones again once, cleaning an element's attributes before it decides whether an animation is one of the root, rather than removing nodes one at a time, which walks the siblings each time and took tens of seconds for a file of about 1 MB of removed siblings; an empty CDATA section goes, so that a second pass writes the same empty element. A `class` name on an inlined drawing picks up whatever the page's CSS gives that class, position included with utility CSS; the page's box contains it. Sixteen hex digits make finding a file that derives a chosen prefix too costly. Refusing ids would refuse nearly every drawing, which refers to its gradients, clip paths, masks, markers, filters and symbols by id, and a warning would leave the page open. A drawing that took its colours from a `<style>` loses them, which the release note says. A page puts an inlined SVG in a box with `overflow: hidden` and paint containment (README).
- **What an HTML parser reads.** A page that inlines a file parses it as HTML, where a CDATA section and a comment end at the first `>` and an element with a prefix, such as `<s:svg>`, is no SVG, so the text an XML parser read in a CDATA section of `<s:text>` became markup of the page, script included; the hole was older than this change. `SvgDocument.Save` now writes the file itself: SVG as the default namespace on the root and no prefix on any element (a prefixed root is written so rather than refused, since an element's namespace is all a prefix says and the allow-list has checked it), `xlink:` and `xml:` only on attributes, where HTML adjusts them, no XML declaration, CDATA section (its text is written as text), comment, processing instruction or DOCTYPE, `&`, `<` and `>` escaped in text and values and `"` in values, and the characters XML would normalise, a carriage return and, in values, tabs and new lines, as references. New lines are `\n` on every platform, where `XmlWriter` wrote `Environment.NewLine`, so a file sanitises to the same bytes on Linux and Windows.
- **SVGs stored before 1.0.0-preview.2** are not sanitised again, and no command does it in this release: sanitising rewrites stored bytes and changes checksums, and that is not undone. Proposed: a command, `fluentcms media sanitize-svg`, with a dry run that lists the SVGs whose bytes would change, that replaces each such SVG not deleted as `POST /media/{id}/replace` does, under a key of its own, with a new size, checksum and version and `MediaAssetReplacedEvent`, so the Delivery copies are evicted and a webhook hears of it. SVGs have no variants, since the processor reads none. URLs keep working, as `v` is signed and not compared, but a CDN serves the old bytes under an old URL until its copy expires (`FluentCms:Media:CacheControl`). The old file goes as after a replace: deleted when no other asset names it, else kept by a holder. A package exported before, whose line names the old key without a file, is then refused with `FCMS3220`, as such a line already is today since the current pass changes those bytes; its file in a package is sanitised again and taken. Until then, operators serve SVGs only through the CMS, whose original route sends them with its `Content-Security-Policy` sandbox, and do not inline SVGs stored before 1.0.0-preview.2, nor those stored by builds before this change, which keep `<style>`, ids without a prefix and CDATA sections, or serve them through a CDN that drops the header.
- **The backfill's marker** stays in row 2 of `cms_media_signing_key` until plugins have a store for the state of a job. It is safe there: the signing key is row 1 (`MediaSigning.KeyId`), which is the only row the signing code reads or writes, the marker's writes are conditional updates or an insert of row 2 that raise no event, and deleting row 2 only runs the check again. A store of their own for plugins, a small key-value table with compare-and-set, needs a table in core (the repository's one model slot) and new public SDK members, which is a decision of its own, so it is proposed as a follow-up.

### 5.2 Webhooks plugin

Webhooks appear in rows 2.2, 2.20, 6.10, 8.16 and 10.8, and decision 47 names them a first-party plugin.

**Tables.** `cms_webhooks_subscription (id, site_id, name, url, secret (Data Protection encrypted), previous_secret (encrypted the same way), previous_secret_expires_at, events json, types json, headers (json, Data Protection encrypted), enabled, created_*, updated_*)`, `cms_webhooks_delivery (id, delivery_id, webhook_id, event_id, event, attempt, state, status_code, duration_ms, request_body, response_excerpt, error, next_retry_at, manual, created_at)`. A plugin's tables start with `cms_{plugin}_` (4.13).

**Delivery.** Subscribes through the outbox; each delivery is a job. Payload `{ version, id, event, occurredAt, site, origin, actor, data: { type, documentId, locale, status, version }, links }`. Headers `X-FluentCMS-Event`, `X-FluentCMS-Delivery`, `X-FluentCMS-Signature: t=<unix>,v1=<hex HMAC-SHA256(secret, t + "." + body)>`, with a `v1` for each secret that signs. Retries at 1m, 5m, 30m, 2h, 24h by default, then dead; manual redeliver; test ping; log retention 30 days.

**Endpoints.** `/api/management/v1/webhooks` CRUD, `/{id}/deliveries`, `/{id}/deliveries/{d}/redeliver`, `/{id}/ping`.

**Webhooks as built.** `WebhooksPlugin` (`webhooks`) in `FluentCMS.Plugins.Webhooks` references the SDK and `FluentCMS.Plugins.Abstractions`, and the reference host adds it. Its stream on `PluginDbContext<WebhooksPlugin>` has the SQLite set (`InitialWebhooks`, `AddDeliveryClaim` with the claim's `manual` column and in-flight index, and `AddPreviousSecret` with the `previous_secret` columns); the PostgreSQL and SQL Server sets are [#2711](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/2711)'s ([ADR 0003](../decisions/0003-plugin-contribution-contracts.md)), and [plugin migrations](../plugin-migrations.md) has the steps.

**Subscriptions.** A webhook belongs to the site of the operation that made it. Every endpoint needs `webhooks.manage` in that site, which `WebhookService` checks with `IPermissionChecker`, and a webhook of another site is not found (`FCMS1564`). A webhook names:

- one or more events, by the names of their activity rows: `content.draft_saved`, `content.published`, `content.unpublished`, `content.draft_discarded`, `content.version_restored`, `content.trashed`, `content.restored`, `content.purged`, `content.scheduled`, `content.schedule_cancelled`, `cache.invalidated`, `media.uploaded`, `media.updated`, `media.replaced`, `media.moved` and `media.deleted` (another is `FCMS1563`);
- content types of the schema, where none is every type; a cache or media event has no type and matches by name alone;
- up to 20 headers, none of them one that a delivery sets, whose values are secrets (below).

Names are unique per site (`FCMS1566`). The secret is the caller's, of 16 to 256 characters, or generated (`whsec_` and 64 hex digits) and returned by that create call only. It is stored with Data Protection and is in no other response, event, activity row or log line. `PUT /{id}` replaces the webhook, and leaving the secret out keeps it. A new secret keeps the one it replaces signing beside it until `FluentCms:Webhooks:SecretOverlap` (24 hours; `00:00:00` for none) has passed, below. `webhook.created`, `webhook.updated` (with `secretChanged`) and `webhook.deleted` are activity rows of the plugin's events, which carry the id and the name only. A delete removes the webhook and its delivery log in one transaction on the plugin's context, answers `FCMS1564` when a delete at the same time removed it first, and publishes `WebhookDeletedEvent` after the commit, since a publish inside the plugin's transaction is refused (`FCMS2602`, 4.11); the activity row comes from that event through the outbox. The media events are the SDK's (`FluentCMS.Events`), so a webhook subscribes to them whether or not the media plugin is in the host.

**Headers are secrets.** A receiver's credentials, such as `Authorization` or `X-Api-Key`, are what the headers are for, so their values are treated as the secret is ([#2881](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/2881)):

- The `headers` column holds the JSON map protected with Data Protection, under the purpose `FluentCMS.Plugins.Webhooks.Headers` beside the secret's `FluentCMS.Plugins.Webhooks.Secret`, so a protected value of one cannot be read as the other.
- No answer holds a value. List, get, create and update give each header's name with the value `***`. The create answer does not echo them either, because the headers are the caller's, like a secret the caller gives, which that answer leaves out too; only a generated secret is shown once.
- A `PUT` that sends `***` for a header keeps the value stored under that name, compared without case, so a webhook read and sent back keeps its headers. `***` for a header that has no stored value is `FCMS1560`. So is `***` in a `PUT` that changes the scheme, host or port of the URL, naming the header: otherwise a caller with `webhooks.manage` who does not know the values could point the webhook at a host of their own and receive them. A change of the path or query, or of the case of the scheme or host, keeps them. Only the origin is checked: on a host that routes its tenants by path, a new path keeps the values, which this does not cover ([#3299](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3299)).
- Deliveries send the real values, and the delivery log, the job payload, the events, the activity rows and the log lines hold none.
- A row written before the headers were protected holds plain JSON, which a protected value, in base64url, never starts with. It is read as it is, so it is still delivered, and protected by the next write of the webhook. Once the host has started, after the migrations, the plugin also protects every such row; the update names the plain value it replaces, so a write in between, or another instance doing the same, wins. Event 9248 counts the rows it protected, and 9249 reports a failure, after which it tries again every 30 seconds.
- When the key ring can no longer read the headers, an answer lists none and an attempt fails with "The headers of the webhook could not be read; set them again."

**Secret rotation.** A `PUT` with a new secret moves the one it replaces to `previous_secret`, still protected, with `previous_secret_expires_at` set `SecretOverlap` ahead ([#2886](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/2886)). Until then a delivery is signed with both, `t=<unix>,v1=<new>,v1=<previous>`, as Stripe and Svix do, so a receiver that still has the previous secret keeps verifying while it is changed over. Then only the new secret signs. Nothing has to clear the previous one: it is ignored once it has expired, and the next write of the webhook drops it. A second rotation within the overlap replaces the previous secret with the one that rotation replaces, so at most two secrets sign. While the previous secret signs, the answers carry `previousSecretExpiresAt`.

**Target URLs.** A URL is absolute `https`, or `http` in Development, with no user information (`FCMS1561`). Delivery refuses addresses that are not public: loopback, link-local (with `169.254.169.254`), private (RFC 1918, `fc00::/7`), carrier-grade NAT, multicast, unspecified and reserved, and the IPv4-mapped, IPv4-compatible and NAT64 forms of these. The check runs on the resolved addresses at connect time in `SocketsHttpHandler.ConnectCallback`, so DNS rebinding cannot get past a check made only at save. Saving checks the host too, for an early error (`FCMS1562`); a host that does not resolve yet is accepted. Redirects are not followed, and a 3xx is a failed attempt. Deliveries do not go through a system proxy. `FluentCms:Webhooks:AllowPrivateNetworks` (default `false`) turns the address check off, and the startup report lists `webhooks-private-networks` while it is on outside Development.

**Delivery.** An outbox handler per event (`webhooks.{event}`) matches the event against the enabled webhooks of its site and enqueues the job `webhooks.deliver` for each. The delivery id is derived from the outbox delivery and the webhook, so a handler that runs again changes nothing. The body is `{ version, id, event, occurredAt, site, origin, actor: { kind, id, name }, data, links }`:

- `version` is `1`. It changes only with a change of the body that a receiver of the current version could misread; a new field is not one. A redelivery sends the body as it was first sent;
- `id` is the event's, the same for a redelivery;
- `data` is `{ type, documentId, locale, status, version }` for content, where `status` is what the event leaves the version as (`draft`, `published`, `unpublished`, `trashed`, `purged`, `scheduled` or `unscheduled`), `{ tags }` for `cache.invalidated`, and `{ assetId, folderId, fileName }` for a media event;
- `links` holds the `management` and `delivery` paths of the entry, or the `management` path of a media asset, relative to the root of the instance behind its path base.

The headers are `X-FluentCMS-Event`, `X-FluentCMS-Delivery` (the same for every attempt of a delivery) and `X-FluentCMS-Signature: t=<unix>,v1=<hex HMAC-SHA256(secret, t + "." + body)>`, with a `v1` for each secret that signs, the current one first. A receiver recomputes the HMAC over `t + "." + body`, compares it with each `v1` in constant time and accepts the body when any matches, refuses a `t` more than five minutes from its own clock, and ignores a delivery id it has already handled. A non-2xx answer, no answer within `FluentCms:Webhooks:Timeout` (10 seconds) or a network error fails the attempt. The job records each attempt and schedules the next one itself, 1m, 5m, 30m, 2h and 24h after a failure on the host's `TimeProvider`, so a delivery has six attempts and the last failure is `dead`. The job itself fails only when the database does. A disabled or deleted webhook gets no further attempts.

**Dead deliveries.** When the last attempt of a delivery fails, or is taken over as abandoned, the job raises `WebhookDeadEvent` with the webhook's id and name, the delivery id, the event and the number of attempts; its activity row is `webhook.delivery_dead` with `deliveryId`, `event` and `attempts`. A ping or a redelivery, tried once, raises none, and neither does a delivery cut short because its webhook was disabled. The webhook stays enabled by default, because turning a receiver off that nobody asked to turn off would surprise an operator. `FluentCms:Webhooks:DisableAfter` (unset by default, and at most `Retention`) opts in: when a delivery dies and the first attempt that failed after both the webhook's last success and its last change was claimed at least that long ago, so that a webhook turned back on or pointed elsewhere starts afresh, the job disables the webhook, logs event 9250 and raises `WebhookDisabledEvent`, the activity row `webhook.disabled` with `failingSince`. The delivery log is the record of the attempts, so no column is added, and the check runs only when a delivery dies. GitHub disables a hook after 30 days of failures and Stripe after 3 days with an email. This answers item 7 of [#2887](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/2887).

**Queue and claims.** Every attempt runs on the job queue `webhooks`: the first attempt of a delivery, its retries, a redelivery and a ping. Its job has an attempt timeout of 10 minutes, past the longest `Timeout` (five minutes) and the minute after which its claim is abandoned, so that no send is cut off ([#3529](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3529)). The plugin adds the queue with `AddJobQueue`, with `FluentCms:Webhooks:Workers` workers on each instance (default 2, and 0 for none), so a receiver that hangs holds the workers of that queue for its timeout and no others: scheduled publishing, trash purge and the retention jobs keep the `default` queue's. `FluentCms:Jobs:Queues:webhooks`, or the host's own `Queues` in code, wins over `Workers`. A job queues its attempt by inserting the attempt's row and claims it as `pending` before it sends it. `ux_cms_webhooks_delivery_attempt` makes that insert the attempt's own, and `ux_cms_webhooks_delivery_in_flight`, unique over `webhook_id` where the state is `pending`, lets a webhook have one attempt in flight at a time across every instance. An attempt first joins its webhook's queue as a `queued` row, whose `created_at` is the time of its event (`OccurredAt`, carried in the job's payload; the time it was queued for a ping, a redelivery or a job without it), and is claimed by turning it `pending`, with `created_at` set to the claim, when it heads the queue and no attempt of the webhook is in flight. Then the job sends, schedules the next attempt when one follows, writes the result to the row, and enqueues the job of the attempt that now heads the queue, which the enqueue wakes at once. The next attempt is scheduled first because a second job for it finds it claimed and sends nothing, while a result written without its next attempt would end the delivery. A job that finds:

- its attempt recorded stops, so a repeat of the job, or a second job for one delivery when the outbox runs a handler again, sends nothing and enqueues nothing;
- its attempt pending schedules itself again for the moment the claim would be abandoned;
- another attempt of its webhook in flight leaves its own queued and ends without holding a worker. The attempts behind it do not run until they are woken;
- no attempt in flight claims the head of the queue when that is its own. A job that ends an attempt, by recording it or by taking over an abandoned claim, enqueues the job of the head; a job that queued its attempt behind another wakes nobody, since that head's own job, or the job that ended the last attempt, saw it, unless the queue has stalled as `webhooks.queue` judges it below: the head's event and the webhook's last claim are both at least 10 minutes old. Only the job of an attempt sends it, so that the runner's retry of that job after a crash finds the claim. That test has a window when a backlog of events at least 10 minutes old, as from the outbox, reaches a webhook with no claim in the last 10 minutes and several workers run its jobs at once: a job that reads the queue after the head's job queued it and before that job's claim commits finds the queue stalled and enqueues the head's job too. That is at most one extra job per worker until the claim commits, and the extra job finds the attempt claimed and waits for it, or recorded and stops, so nothing is sent twice. Closing it would need the time a row joined the queue, which only its version-7 id holds, on the wall clock of the instance that queued it rather than the host's `TimeProvider` ([#3380](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3380)).

So a burst to one webhook is sent one attempt after another, in the order of its events, each as soon as the one before ends: N events to a receiver that answers in 200 ms take about N times 200 ms, and no waiting job runs meanwhile ([#3268](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3268)). The outbox hands the webhooks plugin its events one after another in the order they were recorded on an instance (4.11), so their attempts are queued in that order. The order holds among the attempts that wait together: an attempt whose job runs while nothing is queued or in flight is sent at once, so with several workers an event can still pass one whose job has not run yet. A retry is ordered by the time of its event too, ahead of the newer events that wait. The attempt that follows a taken-over claim is ordered by that claim's time instead, which the abandoned row keeps in place of its event's, and which is still ahead of the events that came after it. `ix_cms_webhooks_delivery_queued`, over `(webhook_id, created_at, id)` where the state is `queued`, finds the head without reading the webhook's log; it holds the whole order of the query, so SQLite picks it without statistics and sorts nothing. The recurring job `webhooks.queue` (every minute, with an attempt timeout of 5 minutes, stored once the host has started) recovers a queue that has stalled, because the job that should have woken it was dead-lettered or the claim's job is gone: when no attempt of the webhook is in flight, or only an abandoned one, and both its head and its last claim are at least 10 minutes old, it enqueues the head's job and logs warning 9252. The 10 minutes leave alone a queue whose head's job is still on its way through a busy `webhooks` queue. A queued attempt of a disabled webhook is dropped when it reaches the head, and the attempt before it becomes `dead`; a ping or a redelivery is still sent. Retention keeps queued attempts, except those whose webhook is gone. In a site where the plugin is disabled the queued attempts wait: `webhooks.queue` skips that site. Once the plugin is enabled again, a stalled queue there is woken by its next delivery, or by `webhooks.queue` within a minute once its head's event and its last claim are both at least 10 minutes old, so up to about 11 minutes after the later of the two.

A pending row claimed at least `Timeout` plus one minute ago is abandoned: its worker stopped, or could not write the result, before recording it. The minute covers the writes around the send and the clocks of the other instances. The next job to find it, of the same attempt or of another delivery to the webhook, takes it over: the row becomes `failed` (`dead` for the last attempt, a ping or a redelivery) with the error "The worker stopped before it recorded the result of this attempt.", and the next attempt is scheduled on the usual schedule. The attempt is not sent again, so a failed write after a send never sends that attempt twice. Its result is not known, so the delivery goes on, and the receiver may get it once more as the next attempt, which `X-FluentCMS-Delivery` lets it ignore. A row's `created_at` is when it was claimed, and `manual` marks a ping or a redelivery, so that a job that takes over its claim knows it is tried once. This answers decision 17 of the review of 7 Nov 2026 ([#2847](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/2847)): a `webhooks` queue, with one attempt in flight per webhook ([#2880](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/2880), [#2882](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/2882)).

**Body and header contract.** This answers decision 12 of the review of 7 Nov 2026 ([#2847](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/2847)). Header values are secrets, as above. Before 1.0 the body keeps its own shape with `version` 1, and the headers keep `X-FluentCMS-*` with the `t=<unix>,v1=<hex>` signature. Neither CloudEvents 1.0 (`specversion`, `type`, `source`, `id`, `time`, `data`) nor the Standard Webhooks headers (`webhook-id`, `webhook-timestamp`, `webhook-signature`) are adopted now. Either can come later as a format a webhook opts into, with the current shape as its default, so version 1 does not break.

**Thin payloads.** The body names the entry and what the event left it as, and carries none of its fields. That stays the default: it holds nothing a receiver's own key could not read, it cannot go stale between attempts, and it stays small. A receiver that needs the entry fetches it on receipt: it takes `data.type`, `data.documentId` and `data.locale` and reads the entry at `links.delivery` with a Delivery key and `locale`, or at `links.management` with a Management key for a draft, so it gets the entry as its key sees it and as it is now. A retry that arrives hours later then still leads to the current entry, and `data.version` tells the receiver whether what it read is older or newer than the event. An opt-in that puts the entry in the body, as the subscriber's key would see it, is left for later; it would be a new field of version 1. This answers item 11 of [#2887](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/2887).

**Log.** `cms_webhooks_delivery` holds a row per attempt: the state (`queued` while it waits in its webhook's queue, `pending` while it is sent, `succeeded`, `failed` with `next_retry_at`, or `dead`), the status code, the duration, the body sent, the first 1024 characters of the answer and the error. The answer is kept without control characters other than tab and line breaks, and never cut between the halves of a surrogate pair, since PostgreSQL refuses a NUL in text and a strict UTF-8 encoder a lone surrogate: a write that failed after a 2xx would have sent the attempt again until the delivery died ([#3299](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3299)). `GET /{id}/deliveries` pages them newest first (`page`, and `pageSize` up to 100). `POST /{id}/deliveries/{deliveryId}/redeliver` sends the body of a delivery again, and `POST /{id}/ping` sends a `ping` event. Each is a new delivery that is tried once, even when the webhook is disabled, and answers 202 with its `deliveryId`. The recurring job `webhooks.retention` (daily at 04:15 UTC, stored once the host has started) removes attempts older than `FluentCms:Webhooks:Retention` (30 days) in batches of 500, with a 10 minute attempt timeout, so that a large prune does not hold a worker for good; the batches before it are committed, so the attempt the runner retries goes on where it stopped.

**Per-site enablement.** Core gates the plugin by site (4.13) and the plugin checks nothing itself. In a site where it is disabled, an event records no `webhooks.{event}` row, its endpoints answer 404 and a `webhooks.deliver` job of that site completes without sending. `webhooks.retention` has no site and runs. A retry that was already scheduled when the plugin was disabled completes without sending, so the attempt before it keeps the state `failed` with its `next_retry_at`.

**Imports.** An event of an import (`Origin.Import`) is delivered only when the import was started with `webhooks=true`, which its correlation id tells (4.11, B-92). The body's `origin` is then `import`.

**Codes.** `FCMS1560` an invalid field, `FCMS1561` an invalid URL, `FCMS1562` a URL that is not public, `FCMS1563` an unknown event, `FCMS1564` no such webhook, `FCMS1565` no such delivery and `FCMS1566` a name that is taken. Event ids 9240 to 9252.

**Deleted sites as built ([#3001](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3001)).** The plugin hears `SiteDeletedEvent` through the outbox (`webhooks.cleanup.site.deleted`, `DeletedSiteWebhooks`) and removes the webhooks of the site with every attempt in their delivery log, in one transaction, and logs how many (event 9251). No `WebhookDeletedEvent` is raised for them, since no one could read the activity of the site; the `site.deleted` row of the instance stands for them. A delivery job of the site that is still scheduled finds its webhook gone and completes without sending (event 9243). A failure is tried again by the outbox, and a second delivery finds nothing.

### 5.3 GraphQL plugin

Row 2.16; decision 24.

HotChocolate schema built from `SchemaModel` at startup: one object type per content type and block, input types for create and update, filter inputs mirroring the REST operators, sort inputs, offset and cursor pagination, population through selection. Mutations per type: create, update, delete, publish, unpublish. One endpoint `/graphql` authenticated like REST; a delivery key sees queries over published content only, a management principal sees mutations its roles allow. Depth and complexity limits, introspection off in Production for unauthenticated callers, optional persisted queries. `IGraphQLSchemaContributor` lets plugins add types.

**As built (B-75).** `FluentCMS.Plugins.GraphQL` holds `GraphQLPlugin`, named `graphql`, on HotChocolate 16 (`HotChocolate.AspNetCore` 16.7.5, Q9). It maps HotChocolate's HTTP endpoint at `/graphql` with `MapGroup("/graphql")`: `POST`, and `GET` for queries, with no subscriptions, uploads, batches, schema download or IDE. The group's sign-in requirement is lifted with `AllowAnonymous()`, so an anonymous caller is the Public role of its site, as on Delivery. The request bridge sets the operation, with `Origin.Api`, or `Origin.Admin` for the admin client; the per-site gate answers 404 (`FCMS1550`) where the plugin is disabled; and the group is not in the output cache. The plugin's [README](../../src/plugins/FluentCMS.Plugins.GraphQL/README.md) is the guide, and [ADR 0006](../decisions/0006-one-graphql-schema-per-instance.md) records the choice of one schema per instance.

- **Schema.** A hosted service builds the schema once, when the host starts, from `SchemaModel` and the field type registry, so options that are not valid (`FCMS1640`) or two names of the content schema that give one GraphQL name (`FCMS1641`) stop the host, and so does a sortable field whose name gives no value of the sort enum, such as `null`, with `FCMS1641` and not an exception of HotChocolate ([#3070](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3070)); two fields whose names would give one value, such as `a1b` and `a1B`, differ in case alone, which the schema refuses (`FCMS1202`). The schema is named `FluentCMS`, so a host with a HotChocolate schema of its own keeps the default one. A content type `site-settings` is the object type `SiteSettings` with the system fields of the response shape (4.4) beside its own, a component `seo` is `SeoComponent`, a block `hero` is `HeroBlock`, a blocks field is a list of a union (`ArticleSectionsBlock`), and a select is an enum whose values are its options in upper case (`ArticleCategory`), or a `String` when an option cannot be a name. A relation is the target's type. Rich text, `json`, and a field type a plugin stores as a reference, such as media, are the scalar `JSON`; any other field type gets `String`, `Long`, `Decimal`, `Boolean`, `DateTime`, `Date` or `ID` from the JSON schema it gives the OpenAPI document, so a plugin's field type needs no GraphQL code. A hidden field is in no type and a read-only one in no input. A relation inside a block or a repeated component is an `EntryReference` (`id`, `type`), since those are stored whole and never populated. Reverse relations have no field (4.4).
- **Queries.** A collection has `articleList(filter, sort, search, page, offset, pageSize, cursor, locale, status, fallback, richText)`, which answers `{ items, total, page, pageSize, nextCursor }`, and `article(id, locale, status, fallback, richText)` with one more argument per unique field, such as `slug`, as the `by/` route; a singleton has one field. The arguments become the `ContentQuery` REST builds: `status` is published unless `DRAFT` is asked, a missing `locale` is that of the request, `locale` is checked by the locale resolver and `all` is refused (`FCMS2201`), and `total` is counted only when it is selected. An entry that is not there is null.
- **Filters and sort.** `ArticleFilter` has `and`, `or`, `not`, the system fields and every field whose field type has filter operators, each with an input of those operators (`eq ne in nin lt lte gt gte between contains startsWith null notNull`, and `hasBlock`). A single component nests its fields and a relation takes its target's id or, under `where`, its target's fields, which become dotted paths. The reader builds the AST of the bracket parser, operand for operand: a value is the text REST would send, the conditions of a level are joined by `And`, and `null: false` is `notNull`. A test holds the two readers to the same tree for the same conditions, and another the same entries. Sort is a list of `{ field, direction }` over an enum of the type's sort keys.
- **Population.** The selection is the read: every selected field the principal may read goes to `Select`, a relation whose fields are selected goes to `Populate` with those fields as dotted paths, level by level, and a media field, a block list and a repeated component are populated for the references inside them. The content service batches each level.
