A selection that populates deeper than `IContentSurface.MaxPopulateDepth` is refused with `FCMS2207` on `populate` before the content service is called, by a query and a mutation alike, so the depth cap is REST's ([#3066](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3066)). A relation selected for its `id` alone is not populated, so it does not count as a level.
- **Mutations.** `createArticle(input, locale)`, `updateArticle(id, input, expectedVersion, locale)`, `deleteArticle(id)`, `publishArticle(id, expectedVersion, locale)`, which answers `{ entry, warnings }`, and `unpublishArticle(id, locale)` call `CreateAsync`, `PatchAsync`, `DeleteAsync`, `PublishAsync` and `UnpublishAsync`. `update` is a merge patch, as `PATCH`: an input shows which fields it names, so one it leaves out keeps its value, which is the safer reading of an update. The input becomes the JSON document the Management API reads from a body; a relation and a media field are ids, and a block list is JSON, each block with its `type`, since an input cannot be a union. A write returns what the content service returned, read again as a draft only when the selection goes into a relation or a media field. The read is planned before the write, with the readable fields and the locale it needs, so a selection it would refuse for its depth is refused with nothing written. A read that still fails after the write, such as one over `MaxPopulatedEntries` or an unexpected failure, cannot undo it: the root field answers the entry the content service returned, and each field the read was to populate is null with the read's error at its own path. An unexpected failure is logged once, at the root field, however many fields report it. A field or a relation the principal may not read is left out of the read, as in a query, so it never fails it. Scheduling, versions, bulk, the trash and discarding a draft stay with the Management API.
- **Enforcement.** The plugin references the SDK and `FluentCMS.Plugins.Abstractions` alone, reaches content through `IContentService`, and decides nothing: the content service refuses a draft to a Delivery key (`FCMS2209`) and every write to a principal without the `management` scope (`FCMS3103`, or `FCMS3102` when not signed in). Before a read, the plugin asks `IContentSurface.ReadableFieldsAsync` (4.4) for each content type the selection reaches. A type the principal may not read is null with `FCMS3104`, the code of REST's 404, at its root field or its relation; a field it may not read is left out of the read and is null with `FCMS2202`, the code REST gives a query that names it. Each is reported once in a response, and the rest is answered.
- **Media.** A media field and an image of rich text resolve by the media plugin's rule (5.1): for a principal without the `management` scope, such as the anonymous caller, a Delivery or Preview key and a preview token, to a public asset of the site's library with the URLs of the Delivery API, as REST Delivery gives it, and never to a private, deleted or other library's asset; for one with the scope, by its folder grants of `media.read`, as the Management API; and to none where the media plugin is disabled for the site ([#3112](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3112)). `/graphql` sets no `IsDelivery`, since it also serves managing principals, mutations and drafts; the resolver asks `IPermissionChecker.CanManageAsync`. The answers are not output cached, so the cache tags of the assets go unused.
- **Errors.** A root field catches the exceptions of the content service and reports each as a GraphQL error at its path (the read after a committed write reports at the fields it was to populate, as Mutations says), described by `IProblemDescriber` (4.13): the REST detail as `message`, and `code`, `status` and the validation `errors` with their field paths in `extensions`, with the `current` entry of a version conflict (`FCMS3105`). Exception details are never included, in any environment; an unexpected failure is `FCMS3199` and is logged. Below a root field, where no resolver catches, such as a stored value that does not read as the scalar of its field, an error filter answers an exception HotChocolate reports with no code as `IProblemDescriber` describes it, `FCMS3199` with status 500, at its own path, and logs it (event 9321) with the name of the field; the fields beside it are answered ([#3070](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3070)).
- **Limits.** `FluentCms:GraphQL` has `MaxDepth` (12), `MaxFieldCost` and `MaxTypeCost` (1000 each, HotChocolate's cost analysis, where a root field costs 10, a populated relation or media field 1 per entry of its page, by the `pageSize` sent, a variable's value included, and every other field nothing, `total` among them), `MaxRequestSize` (256 KiB, refused with 413 and `FCMS3111` as REST refuses a body over its cap; a body sent in chunks, which names no length, is read up to the limit before HotChocolate parses it, so it is refused the same way and not as a request its parser could not read, [#3070](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3070)) and `ExecutionTimeout` (30 seconds). A query over the depth or the cost limit is refused before a field runs (`FCMS1644`, `FCMS1645`). The page size limits are REST's, enforced by the content service (`FCMS2207`), and the list field tells the cost analysis the default page size. Fields run one at a time, since the content service and its context belong to the request. The endpoint is limited by `RequireCallerRateLimit()` (4.10).
- **Introspection.** Off for the schema and allowed per request: always in Development, and elsewhere for a signed-in caller for whom `IPermissionChecker` allows `schema.read` in the site of the request; `FluentCms:GraphQL:OpenIntrospection` overrides the environment. Any other caller gets `FCMS1643`, over `GET` and from a persisted query as over `POST`. `MaxDepth` does not count the fields of introspection, so a caller that may introspect is held by HotChocolate's introspection depth rule, which bounds how deep the recursive fields of `__Type` nest, and its refusal is `FCMS1644`, as a query too deep. The cycle rule of HotChocolate's default security, which `disableDefaultSecurity` drops, is not added back: it would hold the relations of the content schema too, which `MaxDepth` and `MaxPopulateDepth` hold already ([#3070](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3070)).
- **Persisted queries.** `PersistedQueries` names a folder whose `{id}.graphql` files are read once at start, and a request names one by `id`. `OnlyPersisted` refuses a request that carries a query of its own (`FCMS1646`); an unknown id is `FCMS1647`.
- **Contributors.** `IGraphQLSchemaContributor.Configure(ISchemaBuilder)`, in the plugin's own package, is called once for every contributor registered as a singleton, after the content types. The architecture tests let a plugin reference the package for it (3.4).
- **Left for later.** Relay connections, a typed `Media` object (the media plugin can contribute one once it takes the dependency), typed block inputs, the `fallbacks` map of a read, `locale: all` and the pending schedules of an entry (4.4). A plugin group takes the Delivery CORS policy, which allows `GET` and `HEAD`, so a browser on another origin cannot `POST` to `/graphql` until a group can name its own methods.

**Codes.** `FCMS1640` options that are not valid, `FCMS1641` a name conflict, `FCMS1642` a single entry named by no argument or by several, `FCMS1643` introspection not allowed, `FCMS1644` a query too deep, `FCMS1645` a query too costly, `FCMS1646` only persisted queries, `FCMS1647` no such persisted query and `FCMS1648` an execution that timed out; `FCMS1649` to `FCMS1659` are free. Event ids 9320 to 9322, of 9320 to 9329.

### 5.4 MCP plugin

Rows 9.1 to 9.13; 9.16 to 9.21 as Should; decisions 50 to 52.

**Server.** Streamable HTTP at `/mcp` through `ModelContextProtocol.AspNetCore`, enabled per site, in-process. Authentication: bearer tokens from the authorization server or API keys. RFC 9728 protected resource metadata at `/.well-known/oauth-protected-resource`; the authorization server metadata is OpenIddict's discovery document; dynamic client registration (RFC 7591) at `/connect/register` is implemented over OpenIddict's application manager. A registration creates a pending client that an administrator approves and assigns a role before tokens are issued; an open-with-rate-limit mode is a setting for trusted setups (Q11).

**Principal.** Every call runs as the authenticated user or key, with the same `IAuthorizationEvaluator`, site and locale scoping. The tool list is filtered to what the principal may do.

**Tools.** For each exposed type (`Mcp:ExposedTypes`, default all readable types, capped): `{type}_list`, `{type}_get`, `{type}_search`, `{type}_create_draft`, `{type}_update_draft`, `{type}_discard_draft`, `{type}_delete`. Shared: `publish`, `unpublish`, `schedule` (require the publish permission), `query` (type plus filter JSON for the long tail), `media_list`, `media_search`, `media_get`, `media_upload` (URL or base64), `media_update`. Input schemas are generated from `SchemaModel` filtered by field permissions.

**Resources and prompts.** `fluentcms://schema`, `fluentcms://schema/{type}`, `fluentcms://sites/{site}/guidelines`, `fluentcms://types/{type}/instructions`. Prompts from `IMcpPromptProvider` implementations and from editors, stored in `cms_mcp_prompt`.

**Safety.** `dryRun` defaults to true on delete, unpublish, a scheduled unpublish and bulk tools and returns the plan with a `confirmationToken` (HMAC of the plan, five-minute validity); execution requires the token; bulk operations need an explicit cap; per-client call limits, payload caps, result truncation with paging.

**Origin tagging.** `Origin.Mcp`, client id and principal on every write.

**Instructions and clients.** `cms_mcp_instruction (site_id, type nullable, text, updated_*)` feeds resources and tool descriptions. `/api/management/v1/mcp/clients` lists OpenIddict applications flagged as MCP clients with last activity and revoke; `/mcp/prompts` and `/mcp/instructions` manage the rest.

**Server as built** ([#2977](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/2977)). `FluentCMS.Plugins.Mcp` is the plugin `mcp`. It references the SDK, `FluentCMS.Plugins.Abstractions` and `ModelContextProtocol.AspNetCore` 2.4.0, has no table, and holds no tool, resource or prompt of its own until B-72 and B-73: it serves what plugins add through `FluentCMS.Agents`.

- **Endpoint.** `POST /mcp`, mapped with `MapProtectedResource("/mcp", ...)` (4.7, "Protected resources as built") and the package's `MapMcp()`. The transport is stateless: every POST is a server of its own with the services of its request, so any instance answers any request (4.15), nothing is kept between requests, and there is no `GET` stream, no session, no `DELETE` and no legacy SSE endpoint. A client of a revision up to `2025-11-25` connects with `initialize` and gets the server's name, title and version and the `tools`, `resources` and `prompts` capabilities, and `ping` answers; a client of `2026-07-28` or later, whose revision has neither, discovers the same and calls without them. The server offers no subscriptions and no list-changed notifications.
- **Callers.** The `FluentCms` scheme: a bearer token with the `mcp` scope, or an API key. A Management key works as it does on the Management API, and a Delivery or Preview key reads what its class reads and writes nothing, since the evaluator lets a principal manage through `Origin.Mcp` with the `mcp` or the `management` scope (4.8). The 401 and 403 answers, the metadata documents and the `resource` parameter are core's (4.7).
- **Principal.** The request bridge opens the operation of a request with `Origin.Mcp`, the client id of the token (the key id for a key) and the authenticated principal, in the site and locale the resolvers of core give (`X-Cms-Site` or `?site=`, `X-Cms-Locale` or `?locale=`, the host, or the binding of a key). Every provider call runs inside it, so a version and an activity row written through a tool carry the origin, the client and the principal. The plugin reads no database context and asks no permission, which an architecture test holds it to (3.4).
- **Providers.** `IMcpToolProvider`, `IMcpResourceProvider` and `IMcpPromptProvider` (`FluentCMS.Agents`, in the SDK, with no reference to the protocol package), added with `AddMcpTools<T>()`, `AddMcpResources<T>()` and `AddMcpPrompts<T>()` on `IPluginServiceBuilder` as scoped services. `IMcpProviders`, which core implements, lists them with the plugin that added each, in plugin order, and the server does not ask the providers of a plugin that `IPluginState` says is disabled for the site of the request. A list request (`McpListRequest`) and a call (`McpToolCall`, `McpResourceRead`, `McpPromptGet`) carry the `OperationContext`, and a provider lists what the principal may use. A tool is an `McpToolDescriptor`: a name of 1 to 128 letters, digits, underscores, hyphens and dots, a description, the JSON Schema of its arguments, an optional title and output schema, and `McpToolAnnotations` (`ReadOnly`, `Destructive`, `Idempotent`, `OpenWorld`), whose default is a tool that changes data and may destroy it; the server writes all four hints out, since a client reads a missing one as the protocol's default. A descriptor is a record, so that the server can copy it with a longer description (B-73). A resource is an `McpResourceDescriptor` with a URI, or a URI template (RFC 6570) when the URI has an expression, which is listed under `resources/templates/list`; a prompt is an `McpPromptDescriptor` with its `McpPromptArgument`s. A call answers `null` when the name or the URI is not the provider's, and the server asks the next provider; when two providers list one name or URI, the one of the earlier plugin is served and the other is logged. A provider that throws while it lists is left out of that answer and logged, so one broken plugin does not empty the list of the others.
- **Results and errors.** A tool answers an `McpToolResult`: text, structured content, or `Error(code, message)`. A failure is a result with `isError`, whose text names the code first (`FCMS3103: ...`) and whose `_meta` carries it as `fluentcms/code`, so a model and a program both read it. An exception a provider lets through is described by `IProblemDescriber` (4.13) and answered the same way, with the code the REST APIs give it and each validation error on a line of its own; for a resource read or a prompt it is a JSON-RPC error with the code in its message, invalid params for a status below 500 and an internal error otherwise. A name or URI no provider answers for is a JSON-RPC error too, as the protocol asks: `FCMS1600` for a tool and `FCMS1602` for a prompt with invalid params, and `FCMS1601` for a resource with the resource-not-found code, or invalid params from revision `2026-07-28` on.
- **Per site.** Where the plugin is disabled for a site, `/mcp` answers 404 (`FCMS1550`) after the caller is checked, and keeps serving the other sites.
- **Options and permission.** `FluentCms:Mcp` has `Name` (`fluentcms`; 1 to 64 letters, digits, hyphens, underscores and dots) and `Title` (`FluentCMS`), which the handshake and the resource metadata carry; a bad value stops the host. The permission `mcp.manage` is in the catalogue for the endpoints of B-71 and B-73.
- **Telemetry.** A tool call is a span of the `FluentCMS` activity source named `mcp {tool}` once a provider answers for the name, and `mcp tool` for a name no provider has, so a name a client made up never names a span. It carries `fluentcms.operation`, `fluentcms.site` and, for a failure, the Error status and `fluentcms.code`, and never an argument or a result. Requests count in `fluentcms.requests` under the surface `mcp`. Log event ids 9280 to 9283: 9280 a tool call with its tool, principal, client, site, outcome and duration, 9281 an exception that says nothing of its cause, 9282 a provider that failed to list, and 9283 a tool, resource or prompt left out of a list.
- **Codes.** `FCMS1600` no such tool, `FCMS1601` no such resource and `FCMS1602` no such prompt; `FCMS1600` to `FCMS1639` are the MCP plugin's.
- **Decisions.** The audience of a token is not narrowed to `/mcp` and the `mcp` scope is the restriction, since the authorization server and the resource are one instance and a client may ask for a token without naming a resource. The server is one per host: the protocol package keeps one set of server options in the container, so a host that maps an MCP endpoint of its own with the same package shares them. `/mcp` takes the Management CORS policy, whose allowed headers do not include the protocol's, so a client that runs in a browser is not served yet.

**Clients as built** ([#2978](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/2978)). An MCP client registers itself at core's `/connect/register` (4.7, "Dynamic client registration as built"), and the plugin brings the policy, the Management endpoints and the cleanup; core keeps the clients, since the plugin references neither core nor the OpenIddict stores (3.4).

- **Policy.** The settings area `mcp` (`McpSettings`, permission `settings.mcp.manage`): `clientRegistration` (`approval`, the default, `open` or `off`), `defaultClientRole` (the role of a client under `open`), `maxPendingClients` (100), `registrationsPerHour` (10), `maxClients` (1000), `unusedClientDays` (30), `neverUsedClientHours` (24) and `maxConsentsPerUser` (20). The plugin's `IMcpClientRegistrationPolicy` answers core with the instance defaults of the area on every registration and discovery request, and on a consent to a client the open policy approved, and the cleanup reads its two periods from the same defaults, since the instance has one authorization server; a site's own row of the area changes nothing of it. A change reaches another instance within the 30 seconds a settings read is kept (4.13).
- **Management API.** `GET /api/management/v1/mcp/clients` answers `{ items }`, each with `id`, `clientId`, `name`, `status`, `roleId`, `roleName`, `redirectUris`, `scopes`, `registeredAt`, `approvedBy`, `approvedAt`, `lastActivityAt`, and `usedAt` and `usedBy`, the time and the user of the first consent ([#3441](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3441)), the newest registration first. `POST /mcp/clients/{id}/approve` with `{ "role" }`, a role id or the name of an instance-wide role, approves a pending client, `POST /mcp/clients/{id}/reject` rejects one, and both answer the client; `DELETE /mcp/clients/{id}` removes a client of any status with its sessions and tokens and answers 204. `POST /mcp/clients/remove` with `{ "registeredFrom", "registeredBefore" }` ([#3397](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3397)) removes in one call the clients the open policy approved that registered from the first time on and before the second, used or not, each as `DELETE` removes one and with its row `mcp.client_revoked`, and answers `{ "removed", "more" }` (`IMcpClients.RemoveOpenApprovedAsync`). Each delete is a transaction of its own, so a call removes 100 clients at most, and `more` says that it stopped there and is to be sent again. It needs the permission of the single delete and one of the two times at least, since a call that names none would remove every such client. A time is an ISO 8601 date and time with an offset, read as the schedule route reads one, since a time without an offset would be taken in the zone of the server; a call with no time, with a time that is not one, or with a span that is empty or ends before it starts is refused with `FCMS1610`. It never removes a client an administrator approved, registered or gave a role, which carries `approvedBy`, nor a pending or rejected one; each client is read again just before it is deleted, so one an administrator acted on meanwhile stays, and one the database refuses to delete, because a consent arrived meanwhile, is left and logged with its id (event 9009). Only a delete that crossed a grant is taken so: the refusal of a foreign key (`IDataProvider.IsForeignKeyViolation`: SQLite's extended code 787, PostgreSQL's `23503`, and SQL Server's 547 whose message quotes a constraint named `FK_`, as EF names every foreign key of the model, or names a `REFERENCE` or `FOREIGN KEY` constraint, since 547 is a `CHECK` too; a refusal worded otherwise by a server is thrown and the client tried again at the next call or run), or what the provider calls a deadlock (`IDataProvider.IsDeadlock`), as SQL Server may answer the same race; a serialization failure of PostgreSQL (`40001`) has no member of the providers and is thrown. Any other failure of the save, the write of the outbox included, ends the call with the error, and the clients removed before stay removed, each with its row. The candidates are found before any transaction, the status and `approvedBy` in SQL and the span in the state the same query reads, since `registeredAt` is in the stored JSON, which the system model has no function of the providers for, so a call reads again, in a transaction of its own, only a client it would remove ([#3448](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3448)). `POST /mcp/clients` with `{ "name", "redirectUris", "role" }` registers a client for an agent that does not register itself, under the rules of `/connect/register` and approved at once with the role (4.7, "Registered by an administrator"), and answers 201 with the client, which has no secret; `PUT /mcp/clients/{id}/role` with `{ "role" }` changes the role of an approved client, ends its tokens and the users' consents to it, and answers the client ([#3102](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3102)); with the role the client has it ends nothing, and on a client the open policy approved it records the caller as `approvedBy`, as a change does, so an administrator keeps such a client by naming its role ([#3399](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3399)). Both declare `McpClientInfo` as their answer for the OpenAPI document. `{id}` is the id of the client in the store, not its OAuth client id. `McpClientService` checks `mcp.manage` with `IPermissionChecker` for the instance, so only a grant with no site limit reaches the clients, which belong to no site; it is a named exception to the architecture rule that the plugin evaluates no permission (3.4), which still holds for the server. The role given to a client need not be one the caller holds, since it only limits (4.8).
- **Cleanup.** The recurring job `mcp.client-cleanup` (hourly at minute 45, with an attempt timeout of 10 minutes) removes the clients that were not approved within 7 days of their registration, the rejected ones included, and logs how many (event 9284). It also removes the clients the open policy approved, which no administrator looked at, that have no token or authorization left: one no user ever consented to `NeverUsedClientHours` (24) after it registered, so that clients registered to fill `MaxClients` do not hold it, and any other `UnusedClientDays` (30) after it registered (`IMcpClients.RemoveUnusedAsync`, event 9286). A user's first consent records the client's first use in its state (`usedAt`). A run first writes `usedAt` and `usedBy`, the time and the user of its oldest authorization, on a client the open policy approved that holds a token or an authorization and has none: one that registered before `usedAt` was recorded, or one whose first consent crossed another write of the client, which is not tried again. Such a client is then kept for `UnusedClientDays`; one whose grants were gone before a run saw them cannot be told from a client never used, and goes after `NeverUsedClientHours` ([#3399](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3399)). Each candidate is read again just before it is deleted, and one that changed meanwhile is left for the next run, as is one the database refuses to delete because a consent arrived meanwhile, which no longer ends the run and is logged with the client's id (event 9009). The run takes its clients in the order of their ids, and a delete that fails otherwise is logged with the client's id (event 9013) and passed over, so that one client that cannot be deleted does not keep the run from the others; the run then fails with it, and the job runner tries again ([#3448](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3448)). The run writes `usedBy` under the lock of the consents (below), so a user whose consent ends between its read of the oldest authorization and its write is not named again ([#3441](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3441)). A client an administrator approved, registered or gave a role, the role it has included, stays, since each of those sets `approvedBy` (#3301, [#3399](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3399)). A hosted service stores its schedule once the host has started and tries again while the database is out of reach (9285).
- **Consents and fillers** ([#3397](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3397), [#3399](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3399)). A consent is a permanent authorization, so it keeps a client the open policy approved from the cleanup for as long as it stands, and a user of any role could register clients under `open` and consent to each, to hold places of `MaxClients` for good. Of the three ways to bound it, a cap of the clients a user keeps by consenting was taken, as the one that leaves every other user as they were: `ClientConsents.GrantAsync` refuses a consent that would give a user a place for more than `MaxConsentsPerUser` (20) clients the open policy approved, with log event 9008, and the consent page then ends on the hosted error page with `consent_limit_reached`, whose sentence says to end a consent the user no longer uses or to ask an administrator ([#3441](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3441)). The refused client has no consent, so it stays a client never used and goes after `NeverUsedClientHours`. A user has a place for a client while the client is there and the user holds a permanent authorization for it, whatever its status, or is the user whose consent first used it, which the state keeps as `usedBy` beside `usedAt`. Ending a consent by its session or a change of password therefore frees no place: the client it kept stays until the cleanup removes it, `UnusedClientDays` after it registered at the earliest, at first with the revoked authorization and, once the prune of the authorization server has taken that after 14 days, by its state alone for the user it names; a user who was not the first to consent holds the place until that prune. Counting valid consents only would let a user consent to 20 clients, end the consents by ending the sessions or changing the password, and consent to the next 20, each kept for those 30 days, which reaches `MaxClients` from one address in about four days. A consent to a client the user has a place for takes none, so a user whose consents a change of password ended allows the same clients again. A consent to a client an administrator approved, registered or gave a role, which the cleanup keeps whoever consents, is neither counted nor refused. A place is free again once its client is removed, by an administrator or by the cleanup, or once the user ends the consent as such ([#3441](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3441)). `GET /api/management/v1/mcp/consents` lists the caller's places, `{ items }` with `id`, `clientId`, `name`, `redirectUris`, `registeredAt`, `consentedAt` (the newest permanent authorization of the caller for the client, null once only `usedBy` holds the place) and `active` (a valid one), so a user at the cap sees a consent whose tokens have expired, which `/me/sessions` does not list; `DELETE /mcp/consents/{id}` (`IMcpClients.ListConsentsAsync` and `EndConsentAsync`) deletes the caller's authorizations of the client with every token issued under them, so its access tokens stop from the next request on, takes the caller out of `usedBy`, and, when the client then holds no grant of anyone, out of `usedAt` too, so that the cleanup takes it for a client never used and removes it `NeverUsedClientHours` after it registered. The authorizations are deleted rather than revoked, since a revoked one keeps the client from the cleanup until the prune, and `usedAt` goes, since otherwise a user could consent, end the consent and consent to the next client, each kept for `UnusedClientDays` without counting for anyone; a freed client is therefore bounded as one never used is, by `RegistrationsPerHour` and `NeverUsedClientHours`. The routes need no permission, take the caller's id from the operation, never another's, and answer `FCMS1603` for a client that does not count for the caller, found before the lock is taken so that a call with any id keeps no consent waiting, and an empty list to a caller that is no user; an end that crossed another change, a token issued under the consent while it is deleted included, is `FCMS1609` and ends nothing, and the same call again ends it; the other users' consents to the client stay. The end of a consent that was valid raises `UserSessionsEndedEvent` with the reason `revoked` and the authorization's id. A consent that takes a place and the end of one run one at a time under the database lock `fluentcms:mcp-consents` (`IDistributedLock`, taken before any transaction, as for `SuperAdminGuard`), and the count is read under it, so consents at the same moment, on any provider or instance, never pass the cap; one lock for the instance, since such consents are few and bounded by the `auth` rate limit, and a lock per user would leave a row of `cms_lock` for each. The count reads the user's permanent authorizations by client and those clients in two queries without tracking, whatever sign-ins the user holds. When the lock is not free within 45 seconds, the consent page ends on the hosted error page with `temporarily_unavailable` and records nothing, and `DELETE /mcp/consents/{id}` answers 503 with `FCMS3106`. A minimum role to consent was not taken, since it would take MCP from the users of an `open` instance until their roles are changed, nor the activity of a client's tokens in place of its authorizations, since a refresh every two weeks keeps a client as a consent does and the client of a user who was away would be removed. What stays: the clients kept by consent are `MaxConsentsPerUser` for each user who can sign in, so with the defaults it takes 50 accounts to hold `MaxClients` that way; a client used before `usedBy` was recorded whose authorizations are gone counts for no one until the cleanup removes it; and `RegistrationsPerHour` is counted per address and instance, so one address keeps some 240 clients never used alive under the 24 hours of `NeverUsedClientHours`, and four or five addresses hold `MaxClients` for as long as they go on registering. No ceiling of live clients per address was added, since a client does not record the address it registered from. Such registrations are gone a day after they stop, `POST /mcp/clients/remove` removes them at once, and `open` stays a policy for an instance whose every client is trusted.
- **Startup report.** `mcp-open-client-registration` is listed while the policy is `open` outside Development. The report is made once per start, so a change of the setting shows at the next one.
- **Codes.** `FCMS1603` no such MCP client, or none that counts for the caller (404), `FCMS1604` the client is not pending (409), `FCMS1605` an approval, a registration or a role change without a role (422), `FCMS1606` no such role (422), `FCMS1607` a role change of a client that is not approved (409), `FCMS1608` a registration whose name or redirect URIs are refused (422) and `FCMS1609` a role change, or the end of a consent, that crossed another change of the client (409) and `FCMS1610` a bulk removal whose span of time is missing, is written without an offset, or is empty or reversed (422); `FCMS1611` is kept for this area. The refusals of `/connect/register` are core's and carry no code of the plugin.

**Tools as built** ([#2979](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/2979)). The plugin adds two tool providers of its own, `McpContentTools` and `McpMediaTools`, with `AddMcpTools`, as any plugin would.

- **What the caller may do.** The plugin asks no permission, so the list is filtered through a seam of the SDK that core answers: `IEffectiveAccess` (`FluentCMS.Authorization`). `ContentAsync` gives the schema as the principal of the operation may read it in its site and locale, narrowed as the schema group narrows it (4.10) but without `schema.read`, and for each type whether it may read drafts and whether some grant lets it create, update, delete, publish and unpublish, with the readable fields some grant lets it set on a create and on an update, a grant with a row rule included, since the entry is not known. `Search` says whether the `ISearchIndex` slot is filled. `HeldAsync` says which of the permissions named a grant gives the principal in the site, a grant limited to resources included (4.8). It describes and enforces nothing: the content and media services check every call. `IPermissionChecker` stays out of the plugin, as the architecture test asks.
- **REST forms.** The tools answer and read through `IContentSurface` (4.4, "Other surfaces as built"), which this issue gave the JSON of a list, a publish result and a schedule, `ParseQuery` and `ParseSchedule`. A read tool turns its JSON arguments into those parameters, so `{ "title": { "eq": "A" } }` is `filter[title][eq]=A`, the items of `or` and `and` take their index, and the values of `in`, `nin` and `between` are joined by commas with the grammar's escapes; core's parser then builds the query, which is why a tool and a route read the same entries, and a query the grammar refuses carries the code of the REST APIs and names the parameter.
- **Types.** `FluentCms:Mcp:ExposedTypes` names the types the tools reach, every type when empty, and a name the schema does not have stops the host. Of the exposed types the caller may read, the first `MaxExposedTypes` (10) in the order of the schema, which is by name, get tools of their own; the others are reached through `query`, their own tools are unknown when called by name as when listed ([#3070](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3070)), and a warning (event 9288) names each once per process. A collection has `{type}_list`, `_get`, `_search` (only while `IEffectiveAccess` says the search slot is filled), `_create_draft`, `_update_draft`, `_discard_draft` and `_delete`, each listed with its permission; a singleton has `_get`, `_update_draft` and `_discard_draft`, without an id, and its update creates the entry when it has none and `expectedVersion` is 0 (`FCMS1613` otherwise). `publish`, `unpublish`, `schedule` and `query` take a `type` and are listed while the caller holds their permission for some exposed type, with those types as the `enum` of `type`. The descriptions of `{type}_list` and `{type}_get` add a type's display name after its name (`story ("News story")`), and that of `{type}_list` its title field, when the schema gives them (4.2, [#3150](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3150)). A descriptor of a type's tool carries `ContentType`, and `McpRequests.ListToolsAsync` adds the instructions the site saved for that type (B-73, `McpInstructions.ByTypeAsync`) to the description of each such tool that a provider listed for the caller, after the tool's own text; a shared tool and a tool of another type get none, so the instructions of a type reach only a caller its tools are listed for.
- **Calls.** `_update_draft` is `PatchAsync`, a merge patch, because a tool that replaced the draft would clear every field an agent left out. Create and update run in the locale of the connection, and the reads, `publish`, `unpublish`, `schedule` and `_discard_draft` take a `locale`. A read answers the draft by default where the caller may read drafts, as the Management API does, and what is published otherwise, so a Delivery key reads through the same tools. A caller that manages also gets the pending schedules of each entry, as the Management API gives them, and a Delivery key does not (4.4). `_delete` calls `DeleteAsync` at once; the dry run of B-74 goes in front of it. A tool of a type the caller may not read, of a type that is not exposed or over the cap, or of no type is the same unknown tool (`FCMS1600`), and a shared tool answers such a type with `FCMS1612`, so a name tells nothing of the schema. An argument the tool does not take, or of the wrong kind, is a `ValidationException` on its name (`FCMS2108`, `FCMS2101`), answered like a body of the Management API.
- **Schemas.** The input schema of a write tool is built from the narrowed schema: `fields` has the fields the caller may write, without the hidden and read-only ones, each from `IFieldType.OpenApiSchema`, the fragment the OpenAPI document uses, with its references to components and blocks moved under `$defs` and the OpenAPI-only `discriminator` taken out. `additionalProperties` is false. A required field says so in its description and is not in `required`, since a draft may leave it out; a relation names its target type, and on an update a single value may be `null`. The read tools name the readable fields in their descriptions.
- **Media.** `media_list`, `media_search`, `media_get`, `media_upload` and `media_update` call `IMediaService`, which the plugin resolves by its contract and may not find. They are listed and answered only where it is registered and `IPluginState` says the plugin `media` is enabled for the site, to a caller that `HeldAsync` says holds `media.read`, `media.upload` or `media.update`; elsewhere they are unknown tools. `MediaQuery.Search` (new in the SDK, and `search` on `GET /media`) finds a text of 1 to 256 characters in the title, the file name or the tags, whatever the case. The answers are the JSON of the media routes, less the `urls` that `GET /media` and `GET /media/{id}` give since [#3100](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3100) (5.1): `media_get`, `media_list` and `media_search` carry no URL, since signed URLs are for a browser and those of a private asset would put bearer URLs into the model's context. A content read that populates a media field resolves it by the media plugin's rule (5.1, [#3112](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3112)): a caller that may manage, with the `mcp` scope or a management key, gets the assets its `media.read` reaches, and a Delivery or Preview key, which reads content alone, gets the public assets of the site's library, as the Delivery API gives them.
- **Upload by URL.** `McpDownloads` takes `https` on its own port, 443, only ([#3070](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3070)), no user name or password, and a literal address only when it is public; a host name is resolved on the connection, every address is checked with `PublicAddresses.IsPublic` (`FluentCMS.Plugins.Abstractions`, the check the webhooks plugin had, now shared), and the socket connects to the addresses checked. The prefixes of a translator of the network's own, `64:ff9b:1::/48` (RFC 8215) and the IPv4-translated `::ffff:0:0:0/96` (SIIT), are not public whatever address they carry, for the webhooks plugin too ([#3070](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3070)). Redirects are followed by hand, at most three, each held to the same rules, with no proxy, cookies, credentials or decompression. The stream is opened on its first read, so the media service checks the permission, the folder and the settings before anything is fetched, and it stops reading at the upload limits of the site (`FCMS1582`). `UploadTimeout` (30 seconds) covers the request and the body. A refused URL is `FCMS1614`, a fetch that fails `FCMS1615`, whose text does not name what the host answered, so that the server is no probe of what public hosts run ([#3070](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3070)), and the log names the host only (event 9289).
- **Hints.** The read tools are read-only and idempotent; `_create_draft` and `media_upload` are not destructive, and `media_upload` is open world; the update, discard, delete, publish, unpublish, schedule and `media_update` tools keep the destructive default, since they overwrite or remove what is there.
- **Plugin list.** `GET /plugins` (4.10) names the tools of each plugin under `contributions.tools`: core asks the tool providers of `IMcpProviders` for the caller of the request, as a tool list does, skipping a plugin disabled for the site and a provider that fails (event 9236).
- **Codes.** `FCMS1612` a type that is not available, `FCMS1613` a singleton without an entry, `FCMS1614` an upload URL refused and `FCMS1615` one not fetched.

**Resources, instructions and prompts as built** ([#2980](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/2980)). The plugin now has two tables and serves resources and prompts of its own, through an `IMcpResourceProvider` and an `IMcpPromptProvider` it adds like any plugin.

- **Tables.** `cms_mcp_instruction (id, site_id, type nullable, text, updated_at, updated_by)`, with one row per site where `type` is null, the guidelines, and one per site and type, each held by a unique index, and `cms_mcp_prompt (id, site_id, name, title, description, arguments json, template, updated_at, updated_by)`, unique by site and name. They are the stream `cms_mcp__migrations`, one migration, `InitialMcp`, in a set per provider (4.3, "Plugin migration sets per provider as built"); the plugin references the two EF provider packages and `System.Security.Cryptography.Pkcs` with `PrivateAssets="all"`, as the webhooks plugin does. The sets are checked offline against the model and against each other, and the SQLite set runs in the plugin's tests; the PostgreSQL and SQL Server sets have not been applied to a server.
- **Schema resources.** `fluentcms://schema` and `fluentcms://schema/{type}` are what `ISchemaIntrospection` gives the caller (4.10, "Schema group as built"), as the JSON the Management API serves at `/schema` and `/schema/types/{type}`: a type the caller may not read is left out and answers as an unknown resource (`FCMS1601`), like a type that does not exist, and a field it may not read is left out. A caller without `schema.read` is refused with `FCMS3103`, which the plugin learns from `IProblemDescriber` when it lists, so that such a caller is not listed the schema either.
- **Guidelines and instructions.** `fluentcms://sites/{site}/guidelines` names the site by its id and is served for the site of the request only, to an agent of it; another site's are read with a request to that site. An agent of a site, since [#3070](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3070), is a caller that may manage (`IPermissionChecker.CanManageAsync`: the `mcp` scope or a Management key) and holds some grant there (`IPermissionChecker.HoldsGrantInAsync`, new in the SDK: through a role of the site or of the instance, within the grant's site limit, a key's binding and a client's role). A principal whose grants are all in other sites may name the site, since the request bridge lets a principal that no binding holds name any, and a Delivery or Preview key reads published content alone; neither gets the guidelines, the instructions of a type, the `instructions` of `initialize` or the stored prompts of the site, which the Management API keeps behind `mcp.manage`. `McpInstructions` and `McpStoredPrompts` answer such a caller as a site with none, so every consumer holds to it. `fluentcms://types/{type}/instructions` is served to a caller that reads the type, by the same question to `ISchemaIntrospection`, so that no name of another type shows. What is saved is listed under `resources/list`, and the two forms with `{type}` under `resources/templates/list`. The guidelines are also the `instructions` of the answer to `initialize`: `HttpServerTransportOptions.ConfigureSessionOptions` sets `McpServerOptions.ServerInstructions` from the site of the request, on options that are the request's own since the server is stateless, at the cost of one read of the site's instruction rows per request, which the same request's lists reuse. Instructions of a type that is no longer in the schema are left out of the resources and of what the plugin hands to tool descriptions, and stay in the Management list, marked `orphaned`, to be deleted. `McpInstructions` (scoped, internal) is where the rest of the plugin reads them: `GuidelinesAsync(site)` and `ByTypeAsync(site)`.
- **Stored prompts.** `prompts/list` and `prompts/get` serve the stored prompts of the site of the request beside the providers' prompts, to the agents of the site as the guidelines are. A name is 1 to 64 lower case letters, digits, underscores, hyphens and dots, so that two names never differ by case alone, which the providers' collations would not agree on. A template names an argument as `{{name}}` and has no expressions: an argument it names must be declared (`FCMS1627`), and a `{{` that opens no argument is refused, so a template reads one way only. `prompts/get` puts each value in as it is, in one pass, as one user message; a required argument that is missing or empty is refused with `FCMS1628`, an optional one is left empty and an undeclared one is ignored. A stored prompt is refused at write time when a provider of a plugin that is enabled in the site lists a prompt of its name to the caller (`FCMS1626`, compared without case); a provider that fails to list is passed over, and where a provider's prompt appears later under a stored name the earlier plugin's is served, as for any two providers.
- **Management API.** `MapManagement("/mcp/instructions")`: `GET` lists the rows of the site, `PUT` and `DELETE` on the group are the guidelines and on `/{type}` the instructions of a type, from `{ "text" }`. `MapManagement("/mcp/prompts")`: `GET` and `POST` on the group, and `GET`, `PUT` and `DELETE` on `/{id}`. Two groups, so that the prefix `/mcp/clients` of B-71 is its own. The services check `mcp.manage` in the site of the request with `IPermissionChecker` (4.13), and a prompt of another site is not found. The architecture test of 3.4 names the model, the two readers and the two services as the plugin's own data, and holds the rest of the plugin, the server included, to reading no database context and asking no permission.
- **Limits.** A text of guidelines or instructions has at most 4000 characters, a template 16000, a prompt 20 arguments and a site 100 stored prompts; a text takes no control character other than a line break or a tab. The texts are data shown to an agent and are never run.
- **Events.** `McpInstructionSavedEvent`, `McpInstructionDeletedEvent`, `McpPromptCreatedEvent`, `McpPromptUpdatedEvent` and `McpPromptDeletedEvent`, raised after the commit as the webhooks plugin raises its own, with the activity rows `mcp.instruction_saved`, `mcp.instruction_deleted`, `mcp.prompt_created`, `mcp.prompt_updated` and `mcp.prompt_deleted`. The target is `mcp_guidelines`, `mcp_instruction` with the type as its id, or `mcp_prompt` with the id and the name. No event or row holds a text.
- **Codes and log ids.** `FCMS1620` a text that cannot be saved, `FCMS1621` a type that is not in the schema, `FCMS1622` nothing to delete, `FCMS1623` a prompt that cannot be saved, `FCMS1624` no such stored prompt, `FCMS1625` a name another stored prompt of the site has, `FCMS1626` a name a provider's prompt has, `FCMS1627` a template that names an undeclared argument and `FCMS1628` a required argument that is missing. Log event ids 9291 a change of instructions, 9292 a change of a stored prompt, 9293 a stored prompt left out because its arguments cannot be read and 9294 the rows of a deleted site removed.
- **Deleted sites** ([#3001](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3001)). The plugin hears `SiteDeletedEvent` through the outbox (`mcp.cleanup.site.deleted`, `DeletedSiteMcpData`) and removes the site's guidelines, type instructions and stored prompts, through `RemoveAllAsync` of the two services, which check no permission and raise no event, since no one could read the activity of the site. A failure is tried again by the outbox, and a second delivery finds nothing.

**Safety as built** ([#2981](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/2981)). No table, no migration and no package: the plugin still references the SDK and `FluentCMS.Plugins.Abstractions` only, and reads `IDataProtectionProvider` from the shared framework, as the media and webhooks plugins do. The one seam is `IContentSurface.ToJson(BulkResult)` (4.4), so that the `bulk` tool answers as the bulk route does.

- **Dry runs.** `{type}_delete`, `unpublish`, `schedule` for an unpublish and `bulk` take `dryRun`, true unless the call says false, and `confirmationToken` (`McpDryRuns`, scoped). A dry run changes nothing and answers `{ "dryRun", "type", "action", "count", "entries", "references", "confirmationToken", "expiresAt" }`. `entries` holds `id`, `title`, `status` and `version` of each entry, read through `IContentService.QueryAsync` as the caller, the draft where it may read drafts, in the order of the ids. The schema names no title, so `title` is the text field called `title` or `name`, or else the first text field. `references` are the entries whose relation would break, `{ "type", "id", "title", "field" }`: one query by relation field of each exposed type the caller may read (`filter[field][in]`, 4.4, "Reverse relations as built"), against the drafts for a delete and against what is published for an unpublish, none for a publish, 50 at most, with `moreReferences` when there may be more. Relations inside components and blocks are not looked for, and the query runs in the `locale` of the call, as the entries are read, or in the locale of the connection when the call names none, as for a delete. A plan without an entry carries no token. A plan names every entry, since the token is of them all, so its titles keep it to `MaxResultBytes` ([#3070](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3070)): one over `MaxListTextLength` is shortened as a list shortens a text, with the tool that reads it whole (`{type}_get`, or `query` for a type over the cap), and where the plan is still over the limit every title is null and the plan says `"titlesOmitted": true`. A plan over the limit even then is answered whole. `unpublish` ([#3300](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3300)) plans its one entry as a bulk unpublish does, the entry in the call's `locale` with no fallback, and its run calls `UnpublishAsync` with that locale; an entry the caller does not read in that locale is `FCMS3104` before any plan. `publish` stays direct, since it takes nothing from a reader. `schedule` with `action: unpublish` ([#3361](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3361)) is planned as `unpublish` is, since an agent could set it a second ahead and take an entry down with no plan. Its plan is that of the unpublish with `at`, and `timeZone` when the call has one, as the call wrote them; its token is of the tool and its arguments, so the plan of one time confirms no other, and a token of `unpublish` none of `schedule`; and its run calls `ScheduleAsync`. `ParseSchedule` reads the time before the plan, and whether it is in the future is the service's to say at the run. A scheduled publish stays direct and refuses `dryRun` and `confirmationToken` (`FCMS2108`), so that a dry run asked of it is not taken for a run.
- **Token.** `McpConfirmations` (singleton). The token is `v1.{expires}.{entries}.{key}.{mac}`: the Unix second it ends, the SHA-256 of the ids, versions and statuses of the entries in the order of their ids, the key wrapped by Data Protection, and an HMAC-SHA256 with that key over one JSON object of the tool, the arguments (without `dryRun` and `confirmationToken` and without null values, the keys of every object in ordinal order), the principal (kind and id), the client id, the site, the locale of the operation, the end and the hash of the entries. The key is 256 random bits an instance makes on its first token and wraps once under the purpose `FluentCMS.Plugins.Mcp.ConfirmationKey`; an instance that reads a token with another wrapped key unwraps it with the key ring, so every instance that shares the ring (4.7) accepts the token of another, and one with another ring refuses it. Only the ring makes a wrapped key of that purpose, so a caller cannot bring a key of its own. The time is `TimeProvider`'s and a token lasts five minutes; the MAC is compared in constant time and checked before the end and the entries are read.
- **Run.** With `dryRun` false the server reads the plan again and checks the token against it: missing is `FCMS1630`, a token of another tool, arguments, principal, client, site or locale, or one this ring did not make, `FCMS1631`, an expired one `FCMS1632`, and a plan that has changed, which is an entry with a new version or status or another set of entries, `FCMS1633`. Nothing runs for any of them, and each is logged (event 9296). The token is not spent, since no instance keeps state: it confirms again for its five minutes while the plan stands, which a run that changed the entries ends. The check and the write are not one transaction, so a change between them is not seen; a delete can be undone from the trash, and a bulk publish passes the draft version of the plan as `ExpectedVersion`, so a draft changed since is a conflict. The plugin asks no permission: a dry run reads as the caller, and the service refuses the run of a caller without the permission.
- **Bulk.** The shared tool `bulk` takes `type`, `action` (`publish`, `unpublish`, `delete`), `ids` or `filter`, a required `limit` and `locale`. It is listed while `IEffectiveAccess` says a grant gives the caller one of the three operations for some exposed type, with those actions and types as the `enum`s. `limit` is at most `FluentCms:Mcp:MaxBulkItems` (100, up to `BulkRequest.MaxItems`); a limit over it, more ids than the limit, and a filter that matches more than the limit are `FCMS1635`, with nothing run. The filter is the list's, read by `ParseQuery`, and an empty one is refused. The plugin reads the filter itself, for the plan and again for the run, at one entry more than the limit, and then calls `BulkAsync` with the entries of the plan as `Items`, never with the filter, so the run touches what was confirmed and no other entry; an entry the caller may read and not change fails on its own (`FCMS3103`), where `BulkAsync` by filter would have left it out. The answer is `IContentSurface.ToJson(BulkResult)`, with `notFound` for the ids that name no entry the caller reads, which are not run.
- **Call limits.** `McpRequests.CallToolAsync` counts each call before a provider is asked (`McpCallLimits`, singleton): per client, which is the client id of the token or the key id of an API key, and the principal where there is neither. `Limits:CallsPerMinute` (120) holds every call and `Limits:WritesPerMinute` (30) the calls of tools that are not read-only. Whether a tool is read-only is what its provider lists for the caller: the server asks the enabled providers in order until one lists the name, remembers how every provider it asked listed each of its tools, the earlier provider's for a name two list, for a minute for that principal and site, so the next calls of the window list nothing ([#3070](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3070)), and counts a name no provider lists as a write, which it does not remember. A call is taken from the bucket of calls first, so a client over that limit costs no list. A write of a tool a provider listed that its own limit refuses gives its call back, so a client that retries a refused write keeps its reads, while a name no provider lists keeps the call it took, since each costs a list, so made-up names run out of calls ([#3070](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3070)). A bucket is the generic cell rate algorithm of core's limits (4.10) on `TimeProvider`, per instance. A call over a limit is answered as a tool error, `FCMS1634`, with the seconds to retry in its text and under `_meta` as `fluentcms/retryAfter`, not as a 429; its span keeps the name `mcp tool`, since no tool answered, and it is logged once a minute per client (event 9295) and not as a tool call. Core's Management rate limit still counts the principal across its clients.
- **Sizes.** An endpoint filter on `/mcp` refuses a body over `MaxRequestBytes` (1 MiB) by its `Content-Length` before the protocol reads it, and lowers the server's body limit for a body sent in chunks; the answer is the 413 and `FCMS3111` of core's cap (4.10), which runs first, so the lower of the two holds. `{type}_list`, `{type}_search` and `query` page as the engine pages, with its default and largest page size in the input schema. A list over `MaxResultBytes` (256 KiB) as it is sent, its JSON as structured content and again as the text beside it, a JSON string, each measured with the encoder of the protocol package ([#3070](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3070)), is read again at the number of entries that fit, by its offset or its cursor, so that the engine makes the cursor of the entry it ends at, and answered with `truncated`, that number as `pageSize` and the `nextCursor`; an entry is never cut, and one that is over the limit alone is answered alone. A text of more than `MaxListTextLength` characters (500) is shortened in those lists with a marker that names its length and `{type}_get`, which answers it whole; `query` leaves the texts of a type over `MaxExposedTypes` whole, since that type has no `_get`, even by name. The media lists are pages of at most 100 assets and are not cut.
- **Hints.** `{type}_delete` and `bulk` are destructive, and their descriptions say that a confirmation is needed and how to give it.
- **Codes and log ids.** `FCMS1630` a run without a token, `FCMS1631` a token that is not of the call, `FCMS1632` an expired token, `FCMS1633` a plan that has changed, `FCMS1634` a client over its call limit and `FCMS1635` a bulk call over its limit. Log event ids 9295 a client over a limit and 9296 a confirmation refused.
- **Decisions.** The limits are per client as the issue asks, so a client id that many users share is one bucket, and a principal with several clients has one bucket for each, under core's limit per principal. A dry run counts as a call of its tool, a write. The token carries the status of each entry beside its version, so that an unpublish ends its own token; a scheduled unpublish changes neither, so its token schedules the same again for its five minutes, which replaces the pending schedule with itself. `tools/list` is not paged, and results of tools other plugins add are not cut.

**The stdio proxy (B-91).** `fluentcms mcp --url <instance>` (6.1) carries an MCP client that speaks stdio only to `/mcp`, with a management API key; the plugin is unchanged.

### 5.5 AI assist plugin

Rows 9.14, 9.15; 9.16 to 9.20 as Should; decision 53.

Microsoft.Extensions.AI `IChatClient` with providers for OpenAI, Azure OpenAI and OpenAI-compatible endpoints (the plan also named Anthropic and Ollama, until 10.4, H2), configured per site with keys from configuration or Data Protection encrypted settings. Actions: rewrite, shorten, expand, fixGrammar, title, summary, seo, translate, altText. Endpoint `POST /api/management/v1/ai/actions/{action}` with `{ type, field, locale, text or documentId, options }` returning `{ suggestion, usage }`, optionally streamed over SSE. Results are suggestions; nothing is written to content by the plugin. `cms_ai_usage (site_id, user_id, action, provider, model, prompt_tokens, completion_tokens, duration_ms, succeeded, created_at)`. Permission `ai.use`; per-type and per-field opt-in in settings.

**As built (B-83, [#2994](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/2994)).** `FluentCMS.Plugins.AiAssist` holds `AiAssistPlugin`, named `ai`, on Microsoft.Extensions.AI (`Microsoft.Extensions.AI` 10.11.0 and `Microsoft.Extensions.AI.OpenAI` 10.11.0, which brings `OpenAI` 2.14.0, Q9). It references the SDK and `FluentCMS.Plugins.Abstractions`, and the reference host adds it. The plugin's [README](../../src/plugins/FluentCMS.Plugins.AiAssist/README.md) is the guide.

- **Settings.** The settings area `ai` (`AiSettings`, permission `settings.ai.manage`), per site with instance defaults: `provider` (`openAi`, `azureOpenAi`, `openAiCompatible`, or `none`, the default, which leaves AI assist off), `model`, `endpoint`, `apiKey` (a secret of the settings, 4.13, so it is written and never returned), `maxOutputTokens` (1024), `legacyMaxTokens` (false), `requestTimeout` (60, in seconds), `callsPerMinute` (20), `dailyTokenBudget` (0, none), `assetAltText` (false: whether `altText` may send images of the media library, for a field or an asset alone; "Actions") and `fields`, the opt-in: a map of content type to the fields that take actions. A field that takes actions is a text, long text or rich text field of a content type that is neither hidden nor read-only. A key that the settings do not hold comes from `FluentCms:Ai:{Provider}:ApiKey`, and is sent to `FluentCms:Ai:{Provider}:Endpoint` only ("Endpoints"). `FluentCms:Ai` (`AiOptions`) also has `AllowedEndpoints`, `MaxInputLength` (20,000), `UsageRetention` (90 days) and `DailyTokenBudget` (0, none; "Limits"), and a value that is not valid stops the host, as does a section `FluentCms:Ai:Anthropic` or `FluentCms:Ai:Ollama`. Settings that name `anthropic` or `ollama`, which the plugin had until B-103, are refused when they are saved (`FCMS2805` on `provider`, as is a number that names no provider) and, where a site still holds them, at every call (`FCMS1692`, 409, and event 9368), each time with what to use instead. `AiAssistService` checks this before it asks the slot, so a host's own factory never gets such a call. A converter of the plugin reads those two names so that such settings are refused and not skipped as unreadable, which would leave the site on the instance defaults without a word. System.Text.Json describes no custom converter, so core's settings schema gives an enum property that has one the values of the enum, and the schema of `provider` lists `none`, `openAi`, `azureOpenAi` and `openAiCompatible` with its default.
- **Endpoints.** An endpoint is an absolute `http` or `https` URL with no user, query or fragment. One that a site names is used only when it is one of `FluentCms:Ai:AllowedEndpoints`, by scheme, host, port and path, so that a site administrator cannot point the server at an address inside the network (`FCMS1684`, and event 9366 names the host). An endpoint is checked against the list at every call and not only at a save, so one that was saved while it was on the list is refused once the host runs with a list that no longer holds it; the list itself is read when the host starts, so a change to it applies after a restart. The clients follow no redirect, so an allowed endpoint cannot lead the server elsewhere, and they keep no cookies. OpenAI uses its own endpoint unless one is set, and the other providers need one (`FCMS1685`, as for a missing model or key). The configuration's key of a provider goes to the endpoint it is configured for only ([#3300](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3300)): `FluentCms:Ai:{Provider}:Endpoint`, which is OpenAI's own for `OpenAi` when unset and which `AzureOpenAi` and `OpenAiCompatible` need beside a key, or the host does not start, as for an endpoint that is no URL of that shape. A site that names another endpoint, even an allowed one, holds its own key: `openAi` and `azureOpenAi` are refused without one (`FCMS1685`), and `openAiCompatible` is called with no key, as a keyless Ollama site is. The endpoints are compared as the allow-list compares them. A key of the instance defaults is held to the same idea. A site that sets no key, or saves its settings without one, inherits the key of the defaults and reads it at every call (4.13, "Settings as built"), so a site administrator could otherwise name another allowed endpoint and have the operator's key sent there. When a site's settings hold a key, the default factory therefore asks `ISettings<AiSettings>.ResolveAsync` whether the site inherits `ApiKey`: an inherited key is sent only to the provider and the endpoint of the defaults, and a site that names another is refused (`FCMS1685`) until it sets a key of its own. A key is the site's own only when that read says so and holds the same key, since the settings the factory is given were read before, and a key inherited then must not pass because the site set another since. Whether a key is inherited does not depend on its value, so this holds after the key is replaced in the defaults, and the replaced key reaches every site that inherits it. A site that sends `"apiKey": null` holds no key and inherits none, which an endpoint that takes no key, such as Ollama's, needs when the defaults hold one.
- **Providers.** OpenAI, Azure OpenAI and any OpenAI-compatible endpoint share the client of `Microsoft.Extensions.AI.OpenAI`; Azure through its OpenAI-compatible endpoint, with the deployment as the model and an API key, so `Azure.AI.OpenAI` is not added. `openAi` and `azureOpenAi` need a key (`FCMS1685`). `openAiCompatible` works without one, for a local endpoint such as Ollama's `/v1`, and is then called with no `Authorization` header. Ollama is reached that way, and a model that has no OpenAI-compatible endpoint through a gateway that gives it one, or through the slot. A call is one request: the retries of the package are off, so that a call is paid for and logged once.
- **Slot.** The plugin declares the provider slot `IAiClientFactory` (4.13): `CreateAsync(site, settings)` gives an `AiClient`, the `IChatClient` with the names of its provider and model, or null for a site with no provider. The plugin does not ask it for a site whose provider is `none` or a removed one. It is a factory per site, where the plan had one `IChatClient`, because a single client cannot follow the settings of each site; it takes the settings because a slot is a singleton and settings are read in a scope. The default builds the client from the settings, and a host that fills the slot brings any other `IChatClient`, such as one that signs in to Azure with an identity, and decides itself which endpoints it calls. The factory owns its clients, which the plugin does not dispose. `AiClient.SiteCredential` (an `init` property, false by default) says that the client calls with a credential of the site's own, which the operator's budget ceiling then leaves alone ("Limits"). The interface is in the plugin, since the SDK takes no Microsoft.Extensions.AI dependency.
- **Actions.** `POST /api/management/v1/ai/actions/{action}`, mapped with `MapManagement("/ai")`, for `rewrite`, `shorten`, `expand`, `fixGrammar`, `title`, `summary`, `seo`, `translate` and `altText`; another action is 404 (`FCMS1681`). The body is `{ "type", "field", "locale", "text" or "documentId", "options" }`, where `type` and `field` name the field the suggestion is for. With `documentId` the plugin reads that field of the draft through `IContentService` as the caller, a rich text field as Markdown. `locale` is an enabled locale of the site and the locale of the request when left out. `translate` takes `options.targetLocale`, an enabled locale of the site, and `altText` takes `options.mediaId` and needs no text: it sends a copy of the image that fits 1024 by 1024 pixels and carries no metadata, read through `IMediaService` as the caller (`GetAsync`, then `OpenImageAsync`, 5.1), where the media plugin is in the host and enabled for the site (`FCMS1691` otherwise, and for an asset that is no image). `altText` with neither `type` nor `field` is for the asset alone, whose alt text the media field and the library edit per locale with `PATCH /media/{id}` ([#3418](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3418)): it takes `options.mediaId`, `locale` and an optional `text` beside the image, and no `documentId` (`FCMS1680`), its instructions name the media library in place of a field, and it counts against the same call limit, budget and usage rows as every other call. An action other than `altText` still names both. Every `altText` sends an image of the media library, for a field or for an asset alone, and an opted-in text field opts in its text and not the images the caller may read, so a site lets images go to its provider with `assetAltText`, which is off by default and in the instance defaults ([#3449](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3449)): off, every `altText` is refused as a field that is not opted in, before the image or anything of the asset is read, and the text actions are not affected. No other action takes an image. Settings are a site's whole, so a site whose stored settings were saved before the setting existed reads it off, whatever the instance defaults say. The answer is `{ "suggestion", "usage" }`, where `usage` is `{ "provider", "model", "promptTokens", "completionTokens", "durationMs" }` and `suggestion` is text, or for `seo` an object with `metaTitle` and `metaDescription`, read from the JSON object the model is asked for, with or without a code fence around it. An answer with no text, or for `seo` without the two, is `FCMS1690` (502). Every answer is `no-store`.
- **Checks.** `AiAssistService` checks, in this order: `ai.use` in the site with `IPermissionChecker`, the type and field opted in under `fields` (`FCMS1683`, 403), and the caller's right to update that field of the type, which `IEffectiveAccess` describes (`FCMS3103`), since a suggestion is for a field the caller edits. The built-in Editor and Author get `ai.use` by default, once per instance (4.8, [#3103](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3103)); `ai.usage.read` goes to no role. The right is asked in the locale of the suggestion, the body's `locale` or else the request's, which is the locale the draft is read in: `IEffectiveAccess` answers for the operation, so the service asks it inside the caller's own operation with that locale, and a grant limited to locales gives suggestions for those locales only. Then the body (`FCMS1680`, 422), the call limit, which takes the call, the provider of the site (`FCMS1682`, 409, for none, and for a factory that gives no client), the daily budget, which follows whose credential the client calls with, and the read of the draft or the image. A caller over the limit or the budget is refused before any work is done for it, and a call refused after the limit took it is given back ([#3300](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3300)). A site whose provider is `none` is refused before the slot is asked, as a removed provider is, so that a host's own factory that hands out a client whatever the settings name cannot turn on a site that is off. For every `altText` the site's `assetAltText` is checked after the opt-in of the field, or in its place for an asset alone, which names no field (`FCMS1683`, 403, before the rest of the body is checked, so the answer is the same whatever the id). For `altText` on an asset alone the right to update a field gives way, after the body and before the call limit, to the check of `PATCH /media/{id}`, made by its own code: `IMediaService.RequireUpdateAsync`, which the media plugin shares with `UpdateAsync` and which writes nothing ([#3418](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3418)). Its default in the interface refuses with `NotSupportedException`, so a media service of another implementation that does not make the check lets no caller through. So no `media.update` in the site is 403 (`FCMS3103`) whatever the id, and an asset outside the folders of a limited grant is `FCMS1585` (404), as one that does not exist, so that the answer does not tell whether the asset exists ([ADR 0004](../decisions/0004-folder-limited-media-grants.md)); the image is then read as for a field, with `media.read`. The plugin never writes the asset: the suggestion is for the caller to save. The endpoints carry the request and decide nothing.
- **Fields** ([#3101](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3101)). `GET /api/management/v1/ai/fields` tells an editor where the actions work, since the settings area `ai` needs `settings.ai.manage`. It needs `ai.use` in the site (403 otherwise) and answers `{ "enabled", "locale", "fields", "assets" }`, declared as `AiFields` in the OpenAPI document. `assets` ([#3418](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3418)) is whether the caller may ask for the alt text of an asset alone: the site is on and so is its `assetAltText` ([#3449](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3449)), the media plugin is in the host and enabled for the site, and the caller holds `media.read` and `media.update` there, each with no folder limit or for some folders; whether a given asset is in those folders is learnt at the call. `fields` does not follow `assetAltText`, since the text actions do not, so where it is off `altText` for a listed field is refused although the field is listed. `enabled` is whether the site names a provider, one that is not `none` nor a removed one, which are the two the actions refuse before the slot is asked; `locale` is the locale of the request; `fields` maps each content type to the fields, in the order of the schema, that the checks above let the caller act on in that locale: opted in, taking actions, and ones the caller may update. A type the caller may not read is left out, as is a type with no such field, and so is a field the caller may set on a new entry only, which an action refuses too (`FCMS3103`). A site that is off lists no field. The answer holds nothing of the provider's configuration, not the provider, the model, the endpoint or the key, nor whether a key is set, so a provider that cannot answer (`FCMS1684`, `FCMS1685`), or a host's factory that gives no client (`FCMS1682`), is learnt at the call.
- **Limits.** The text has at most `MaxInputLength` characters (`FCMS1686`, 413) and the answer at most `maxOutputTokens`, sent as `max_completion_tokens`, or as `max_tokens` alone when the site turns `legacyMaxTokens` on, since Ollama's `/v1` reads only that one ([#3008](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3008)). It is off by default: the reasoning models of OpenAI refuse `max_tokens`, also behind a gateway or a compatible endpoint, and some endpoints refuse a request that holds both, so the two are never sent together. A call ends at `requestTimeout` (`FCMS1688`, 504) or when the caller goes away. The plugin's timeout alone ends it: the client of the package has no network timeout of its own, since one of the same length raced it and answered a call past `requestTimeout` as `FCMS1689` until [#3008](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3008). A principal makes at most `callsPerMinute` calls in a site (`FCMS1687`, 429, logged once a minute as event 9364): a bucket per site and principal in memory on each instance, by the generic cell rate algorithm of core's limits (4.10) on `TimeProvider`, beside the Management rate limit. The call is taken from the bucket after the checks of the caller and the body, before any work is done for it, so that calls sent together pass only as often as the limit allows, and given back when it is refused before it is sent, whatever refuses it: a request that is refused takes nothing from the bucket, and every counted call writes a usage row. A site has a daily token budget (`FCMS1693`, 429, whose detail names the budget and the time it resets, logged as event 9369 once a day for the site on each instance): the lower of the site's `dailyTokenBudget` and, for a call the slot does not say goes with a credential of the site's own (`AiClient.SiteCredential`), `FluentCms:Ai:DailyTokenBudget`, where 0 is none. That ceiling is the operator's: the default factory says `SiteCredential` only for a key the site set itself at an endpoint that bills the holder of the key: OpenAI's own, or one of `FluentCms:Ai:SiteBilledEndpoints` (empty by default), which the operator fills with the public providers on `AllowedEndpoints`. At an endpoint the operator runs, such as a local Ollama or a gateway that reads no key, a key the site types is not its own credential, since the endpoint serves it on the operator's compute; nor are the key of the configuration, the key of the instance defaults or no key. A factory of the host's own that says nothing counts as the operator's, whatever key the settings hold. A site may lower the ceiling and cannot raise it or turn it off. Settings are a site's whole, its secrets apart, so the `dailyTokenBudget` of the instance defaults holds a site that follows the defaults and not one that saved settings of its own. A budgeted call reserves an estimate on the instance before any work is done for it, after the client is known: a token for each UTF-8 byte of its instructions and its text, which no tokenizer that works on bytes passes in any script, 25,600 for an image (the copy fits 1024 by 1024 pixels and is sent with no detail, so the provider's default applies; at high detail gpt-4o-mini counts it as 25,501 tokens, the most of the models whose formula is published, gpt-4o as 765 and Anthropic as about 1,400), and `maxOutputTokens`; a call that reads a draft reckons its text at `MaxInputLength` characters of three bytes until the draft is read, and one that reads none reckons the text it was given, if any, so `altText` with no text reserves its instructions, the image and `maxOutputTokens` alone ([#3449](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3449)). Once what it sends is known, the reservation is changed to that estimate, the one a row writes for a usage the provider did not report. It is refused when the tokens of the site's rows since midnight UTC and the reservations of its other calls in flight reach the budget, and it holds its reservation until its row is written. A call with the operator's credential that ends before its provider reports its usage (cancelled, timed out, failed after it was sent), or whose provider reports none, writes its estimate as the row's usage for what is missing, the prompt at the estimate and the completion at `maxOutputTokens`, and so does one whose provider reports 0 for the prompt, which is never empty, or 0 for an answer that has text; the answer still gives what the provider reported, and a row does not say that it holds an estimate, since the table has no column for it and this change adds none. A call with the site's own credential writes what its provider reported, 0 where it reported nothing. So a site passes its budget by at most one call on each instance, of at most that estimate, as long as the provider counts no more than a token for each byte of text, 25,600 for the image and `maxOutputTokens` for the answer. These are estimates for the models named, not a bound for any model. In particular, 25,600 for an image is an estimate, not a bound: on a provider that counts an image as more, image calls in flight together can each pass the budget by the excess, which the reservation of `MaxInputLength` characters that `altText` with no text held until [#3449](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3449) used to absorb. Instances do not share their reservations.
