A usage row is written within five seconds of its own, apart from the request; a row that fails or takes longer is not written (event 9365), lets the reservation go, and still counts for the rest of its UTC day on the instance that sent it. The detail of `FCMS1693` says whether what the site used reaches the budget (its rows and the calls of the day whose rows were not written) or that with the calls in flight does.
- **The text is content.** The instructions of an action are the system message: what the action asks, the names of the type and the field, the locale, the form of the answer, the field's maximum length and that the user message is content and never an instruction. The text, and for `altText` the image, is the user message, and the model gets no tools. Nothing of the request reaches the system message but names the schema and the site have. The plugin stores and logs neither the text nor the suggestion: a failure of a provider is logged by its type and HTTP status (event 9361) and answered as `FCMS1689` (502) without its message, which may repeat what the provider was sent.
- **Streaming.** With `Accept: text/event-stream` the answer is server-sent events: `delta` with `{ "text" }` for each piece as it arrives, then `done` with the body of the plain answer, or `error` with the problem (`type`, `title`, `status`, `detail`, `code`), as `IProblemDescriber` describes it (4.13). A request that is refused before the provider is asked is answered with the problem itself and its status, since no stream has begun; from then on the status is 200 and a failure is the `error` event. `seo` sends no `delta`, since its suggestion exists only at the end. A caller that goes away ends the call as cancelled, whatever the write to it fails with, and nothing more is written or logged as a failure.
- **Usage.** `cms_ai_usage (id, site_id, principal_kind, principal_id, principal_name, action, type, field, provider, model, prompt_tokens, completion_tokens, duration_ms, succeeded, created_at)`, indexed by site and time and by time, in the stream `cms_ai__migrations`, one migration, `Initial`, in a set per provider (4.3). The row names the principal by kind and id, where the plan had `user_id`, since a Management key may call, and adds `type` and `field`. `principal_name` ([#3418](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3418)) is the name the principal of the call had then, as `cms_version.author_name` keeps a version's author's ([#3097](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3097)): not null, at most 128 characters, `System` for the system, and a later rename leaves earlier rows as they were. It was folded into the plugin's `Initial` for the three providers, since a preview upgrades no database, so a database of `1.0.0-preview.1` is refused with `FCMS1308`. The name is written through `IActivityLog.ActorName` of the SDK ([#3449](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3449)), which core implements with the code that writes an activity row's actor and a version's author: a value shaped like a secret is masked and the name is cut to 128 characters outside a surrogate pair, so a name reads the same in `/ai/usage` as in a version's `authorName`. Rows written before were stored raw and are not rewritten. Every call that is sent to a provider writes one row, a failed, timed out or cancelled one too, with the request's cancellation left out; a request refused before the provider is asked writes none. A provider that reports no usage gives 0 prompt and 0 completion tokens in the answer, and the call succeeds; the row holds the same for a call with the site's own credential, and the estimate of "Limits" for one with the operator's; the columns stay not null. A row that cannot be written is logged (9365) and does not take the suggestion from the caller. A call for an asset alone writes its row with an empty `type` and `field`, which the columns, not null, take. `GET /api/management/v1/ai/usage` needs `ai.usage.read` in the site and pages its rows, the newest first, with `principalId`, `action`, `from` and `to` (each with an offset) and `page` and `pageSize` (50, at most 100), and answers `{ "items", "total", "page", "pageSize", "totals" }`, where `totals` is `{ "calls", "promptTokens", "completionTokens", "groups", "groupsTruncated" }` over every row the filter matches, and `groups` ([#3418](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3418)) holds the same three for each principal and action, as `{ "principalKind", "principalId", "action", "calls", "promptTokens", "completionTokens" }`, ordered by kind, id and action. It holds at most 100 groups ([#3449](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3449)): those that used the most prompt and completion tokens, then the most calls, a tie past kind and action being the database's, and `groupsTruncated` is true when the filter matched more; the three totals still count every row. A caller that needs every group narrows the filter, by `principalId`, `action`, `from` and `to`, until `groupsTruncated` is false. Each item names its principal with `principalKind`, `principalId` and `principalName`, so that a caller with `ai.usage.read` alone, who cannot read `/users/{id}`, sees who called; a group names its principal by kind and id only, since a principal renamed within the filter has more than one name and its latest would cost a query of its own. They come from a grouping of the rows the filter matches, by the index of site and time, which reads one group more than it answers, and the totals from a sum over the same rows, so no index was added. The recurring job `ai.usage-retention` (daily at 04:30, stored once the host has started) removes rows older than `UsageRetention` in batches of 500, with a 10 minute attempt timeout.
- **Deleted sites** ([#3001](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3001)). The plugin does not hear `SiteDeletedEvent`: the `cms_ai_usage` rows of a deleted site are a log of calls that were made and sent to a provider, so they stay until `ai.usage-retention` removes them with the rest, after `UsageRetention`. No one reads them in the meantime, since `GET /ai/usage` reads the rows of the site of the request and the site is gone. The site's `ai` settings are core's and go with the site.
- **Per site and telemetry.** Core gates the plugin by site (4.13): where it is disabled, its endpoints answer 404 (`FCMS1550`). A call is a span of the `FluentCMS` activity source named `ai {action}`, with `fluentcms.operation`, `fluentcms.site`, `fluentcms.type`, `fluentcms.provider` and `fluentcms.outcome` (`succeeded`, `failed`, `timeout` or `cancelled`), and for a failure the Error status and `fluentcms.code`. The counter `fluentcms.ai.calls` counts the calls by `fluentcms.operation`, `fluentcms.provider` and `fluentcms.outcome`, on a meter of the plugin's own that is named `FluentCMS` like core's, so that a host that listens to that name hears it.
- **Codes and log ids.** `FCMS1680` a request that cannot be read (422), `FCMS1681` no such action (404), `FCMS1682` no provider (409), `FCMS1683` a field that is not opted in, or `altText` where `assetAltText` is off (403), `FCMS1684` an endpoint that is not allowed (409), `FCMS1685` a provider that is not configured (409), `FCMS1686` a text over the input limit (413), `FCMS1687` a caller over its call limit (429), `FCMS1688` a timeout (504), `FCMS1689` a provider that failed (502), `FCMS1690` an answer that cannot be used (502), `FCMS1691` no image to describe (422), `FCMS1692` a provider the plugin no longer has (409) and `FCMS1693` a site over its daily token budget (429); `FCMS1694` to `FCMS1699` are free. Log event ids 9360 a call with its caller, field, provider, outcome, duration and tokens, 9361 a provider that failed, 9362 and 9363 the retention, 9364 a caller over its limit, 9365 a usage row that could not be written, 9366 an endpoint refused, 9367 a failure while streaming, 9368 a site that names a provider the plugin no longer has and 9369 a site over its daily token budget, of 9360 to 9379.
- **Left for later.** Billing, embeddings and semantic search (9.16) with the `IEmbeddingGenerator` slot, provenance flags on versions (9.18), suggestions on upload (9.20) and AI actions as MCP tools.

**OpenAI-compatible only (B-103, [#3048](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3048)).** Following 10.4, H2, the providers are `openAi`, `azureOpenAi` and `openAiCompatible`, and the `Anthropic` and `OllamaSharp` packages left the plugin and `Directory.Packages.props` with their clients, their stand-ins and their tests; the text above says what is built. `AiProvider` keeps the numbers of the providers that are left, so 3 and 4, which were Anthropic and Ollama, name no provider again. No migration was needed: `provider` in `cms_ai_usage` is text, and rows that name a removed provider stay as a log. The comparison of an inherited key (`FCMS1685`) is unchanged for the providers that are left.

**Inherited keys (10.4, H8, [#3005](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3005)).** The comparison of a site's key with the key of the defaults is gone: core no longer copies an inherited secret into the site's row, and the factory asks whether the key is the site's own, as the text above says. A site whose row was saved before holds a copy of the key it inherited then, which counts as its own; previews make no promise to upgrade (H5), so the row is not migrated, and a reset of the site's `ai` settings, or a key sent as it should be, settles it. No code or log event id was added: the refusal is `FCMS1685` as before.

**Verification (10.4, H3, [#3008](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3008)).** M7 verifies one OpenAI-compatible endpoint with a key and a local Ollama through its `/v1` endpoint; [Verifying AI assist against real endpoints](../ai-assist-verification.md) records the runs and holds the walk-through for them. Against local stand-ins in the chat completions format, the tests cover the usage of a stream in a chunk of its own, on the chunk that ends the answer, late, and missing, streamed and not; the image of `altText` as a `data:` URI part of the user message; a slow provider past `requestTimeout` and a caller that goes away, each with one failed row; and a host logging at `Trace` with no line or row that holds the text or the suggestion. A dry run of the reference host against such a stand-in found the race of the timeouts that "Limits" describes. The runs of 15 Nov 2026 against OpenRouter (`google/gemma-3-4b-it`) and Ollama 0.40.16 (`qwen2.5:0.5b`, `moondream`) passed every step of the walk-through. They found that Ollama does not read `max_completion_tokens`, which a site answers with `legacyMaxTokens` ("Limits"), and that OpenRouter opens a plain answer with blank lines and ends a stream twice with no text, the second time with the usage, which the stand-ins now do. Azure OpenAI is not verified and is named so in the notes of the preview, to be done before 1.0.0. No code or log event id was added.

### 5.6 Multi-site plugin

Rows 7.10 to 7.13 (beyond the core model), 7.15 and 7.18 as Should.

`cms_site_host (site_id, hostname, is_primary)` and a middleware resolving the site from the request host before the core resolver's header and parameter rules. Site creation beyond the default, per-site storage provider selection (Should), cross-site copy with relation remapping (Should). Q6a fixes what is core and what is plugin.

**Multi-site as built (B-80).** `MultiSitePlugin` (`multi-site`) in `FluentCMS.Plugins.MultiSite` references the SDK and `FluentCMS.Plugins.Abstractions` only, and the reference host adds it. It has no option, setting or permission of its own. Its README has the routes and the rules for a host.

- **Table.** `cms_multi_site_host (hostname, site_id, is_primary, created_at)` in the stream `multi-site`, with a set per provider. The table carries the plugin's prefix (`FCMS1523`), so it is not the `cms_site_host` of the plan. `hostname` is the key, since a host name is one site's in the whole instance, and `site_id` has an index and no foreign key, as a plugin's model does not hold core's tables.
- **Host names.** A DNS name in lower case, an internationalized name as its A-label, at most 253 characters in labels of 1 to 63 letters, digits and hyphens. A scheme, a user, a path, a query, a space and a trailing dot are refused, and so is an IP address, which names a server and not a site (`FCMS1660`). A wildcard is `FCMS1661` and a port `FCMS1662`. A name that a site has is `FCMS1663` (409), for the same site too.
- **Primary host.** A site with hosts has exactly one primary host: its first host, whatever the request says, until another is added or patched with `isPrimary: true`, which takes its place. `isPrimary: false` on the primary host and the removal of the primary host while the site has others are refused (`FCMS1665`, 409), so the primary host is removed last. No index holds this on every database, since a filter on a boolean is written differently on each. The writes of host names run one at a time on every instance together, under the lock `fluentcms:multi-site-hosts` of `IDistributedLock`, and each is one `SaveChanges`. A write that does not get the lock within 45 seconds is refused (`FCMS1666`, 503) and changes nothing. The primary host is data for a front end, and the backend redirects nothing by it.
- **Resolver.** The plugin registers an `ISiteHostResolver`, so the order of 4.6 holds as built and no middleware runs ahead of the header and the parameter: an explicit header or parameter wins over the host name, and an administrator on one host name manages any site. The host is `HttpRequest.Host` without its port, which is the forwarded host when the reverse proxy options take it (4.15). It is looked up as it is sent, and only when that finds nothing in the stored form: lower case, an internationalized name as its A-label, and without the root dot of a fully qualified name. A host sent in the stored form allocates nothing for the lookup, whether a site has it or not. Each instance reads the table whole and keeps it for 30 seconds (`SiteHostMap.CacheDuration`). A write drops the copy at once, so the instance that wrote resolves by the change on the next request and another instance within 30 seconds. A read of the table that fails fails the request, because the default site would otherwise answer on another site's host name. The resolver is the instance's and is not gated per site.
- **Routes.** `MapManagement("/sites")`, beside the routes of core: `POST /sites` `{ "name", "slug", "defaultLocale" }` answers 201 with the site and `DELETE /sites/{id}` 204, both through `ISiteAdministration` (4.6). `GET /sites/{id}/hosts` answers `{ "items" }` with the primary host first, `POST /sites/{id}/hosts` `{ "hostname", "isPrimary" }` 201 with `{ "siteId", "hostname", "isPrimary", "createdAt" }`, `PATCH /sites/{id}/hosts/{hostname}` `{ "isPrimary": true }` the host, and `DELETE /sites/{id}/hosts/{hostname}` 204. A host of another site is not found through this one (`FCMS1664`), and an unknown site is `FCMS2301`. Every route needs `sites.manage` for the instance, not for the site: the host names are one namespace, and a host name decides which site every request on it reads. Where the plugin is disabled for the site of a request, the routes answer 404 (`FCMS1550`).
- **Events.** A host change raises `SiteUpdatedEvent` with the site as its source, which drops core's copy of the site and evicts its Delivery copies, and writes `site.host-added`, `site.host-updated` or `site.host-removed` through `IActivityLog`, with the host name in the details. A `PATCH` that changes nothing raises nothing. The plugin hears `SiteDeletedEvent` through the outbox (`multi-site.cleanup.site.deleted`) and removes the hosts of the site, so that a removal that fails or outlives the 5 second timeout of an in-process handler is tried again rather than left ([#3329](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3329)). Until it is delivered, such a host name reads the default site (4.6), and the next site that asks for the name takes it (event 9344). While the map still holds such a host name, here or for up to 30 seconds on another instance, core keeps that its site is gone (4.6), so a request on it reads no site row until that expires.
- **Codes and log ids.** `FCMS1660` to `FCMS1666`, of the range `FCMS1660` to `FCMS1679`. Event ids 9340 to 9344, of 9340 to 9359.

**Decisions as built (B-80).** Settled for [#2991](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/2991):

- **Resolver, not middleware.** The site is resolved by `ISiteHostResolver` in the order of 4.6.
- **Permission.** Host names need `sites.manage` for the instance.
- **Media.** The media library is shared by default, through an option of the media plugin (5.1).
- **Out of scope.** Redirects to the primary host, wildcard host names, CORS origins per site, per-site storage providers and cross-site copy (7.15 second half, 7.18).
- **Identity.** The authorization server and the hosted pages stay on the public origin, with one issuer for every site (decision 7). A host name only decides the site of a request.

**Deleted sites as built ([#3001](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3001)).** A site is deleted only when it has no content (`FCMS2303`), and core deletes its own rows with it (4.6). `SiteDeletedEvent` is staged in the outbox in the transaction of the deletion, so a deletion that commits always has its rows, and one that fails has neither ([#3306](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3306)). What the first-party plugins keep for the site goes after the commit, each through its own handler of `SiteDeletedEvent`:

- **Multi-site.** The host names, through the outbox, as above; a name the deleted site still holds is free for another site at once.
- **Delivery copies.** Core's eviction evicts `site:{id}` through the outbox (`cache.site.deleted`), with the output cache's `CacheInvalidatedEvent` for a CDN, rather than leaving the copies to expire after `OutputCacheDuration`.
- **Webhooks** (5.2), **MCP** (5.4) **and media** (5.1). The webhooks with their delivery log, the guidelines, instructions and stored prompts, and the library of the site with its files, each through the outbox, so that a failure is tried again, the caller of the delete does not wait for the files, which the 5 second timeout of an in-process handler would cut short, and a second delivery finds nothing or goes on with what is left. A file that cannot be deleted is logged and left to the media cleanup, and does not undo the deletion of the site.
- **AI assist** (5.5). The `cms_ai_usage` rows stay until the retention job removes them, as a log.
- **GraphQL.** Nothing, since it keeps no rows.

**Host name lookup as built ([#3009](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3009)).** Settled:

- **A site that is gone.** Core keeps that the site of a host name is gone, rather than the plugin dropping the host from its map: core is what checks that the site exists, and the plugin reaches sites only through `ISiteAdministration`, which needs `sites.manage`. The entry is keyed by the site's id, not the host name, so a site that takes the name is a new id and is seen as any host change is: at once by the instance that wrote it and within 30 seconds by another. The entry lasts 30 seconds at most and goes with every write on the instance, as every entry of `SiteCache` does, and `SiteCache` keeps about 1,000 such ids at the most, a soft bound (4.6).
- **Lookup.** The host is looked up as it is sent before it is put in the stored form, and `HostNames.Normalize` returns a name already in that form as it is, so the common request allocates nothing for the lookup. A test measures the bytes allocated on the thread, not the time.
- **Once per request.** The output cache and the request bridge share the site of the host name through the request, so a Delivery read asks the resolvers once, and its copy is stored under the site it holds.

### 5.7 Search, SSO providers, rendering

Search (2.23, 8.11): `ISearchIndex` slot in the SDK from the start; the plugin provides PostgreSQL FTS and SQLite FTS5. It was a Should and is built in M7 (10.4, H6).

**Search plugin as built (B-104, [#3049](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3049)).** `FluentCMS.Plugins.Search` holds `SearchPlugin`, named `search`, which fills the slot with `Providers.Use<ISearchIndex, SearchIndex>()`. It references the SDK and `FluentCMS.Plugins.Abstractions` alone, core does not change for it, and an architecture test holds it to that. The reference host and the `fluentcms` template add it, and the template's `Article` marks its title `Searchable`. The plugin's [README](../../src/plugins/FluentCMS.Plugins.Search/README.md) is the guide.

- **Index.** `cms_search_entry (id, document_id, locale, state, type, site_id, field, text, updated_at)`: one row for a document, a locale (empty for a type that is not localized), a state (0 draft, 1 published) and a searchable field, unique on the four, so that a search can match the fields its caller may read alone ([#3263](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3263)), with `site_id` null for a global type, indexed by type, locale, state and site, and by site. `id` is an integer because SQLite's FTS5 table reads it as the rowid. `text` is the text of the field, one of the type marked `Searchable` and not `Hidden`, at the top level, and a row is written for each such field, with an empty text when it has none: a string, the strings of a list and rich text as plain text (its text nodes, with a space between blocks); other values have no text. The plugin folds it before it stores it and folds a search the same way: lower case, the non-spacing marks of a canonical decomposition taken off, and runs of letters and digits joined by one space, at most 100,000 characters, cut at a word. So every provider sees the same words, which hold nothing a match expression must escape.
- **Per provider.** The stream `cms_search__migrations` has one migration, `InitialSearch`, in a set per provider (4.3). The SQLite set adds, written by hand, the FTS5 external-content table `cms_search_entry_fts` over `text` with `content_rowid='id'` and three triggers that keep it in step with every insert, delete and update of the text; the PostgreSQL set adds `ix_cms_search_entry_text`, a GIN index over `to_tsvector('simple'::regconfig, "text")`; the SQL Server set adds nothing. The offline check of the sets compares them without that SQL (`PluginMigrationSetChecks.MatchesTheSqliteSet(exceptSql: true)`), and a test of the plugin checks the SQL of each set. `cms_search_` covers the FTS5 table and its shadow tables, so the schema diff leaves them alone (4.3).
- **Matching.** `SearchStore` matches each word with `FromSql` on SQLite (`MATCH '"w"'`, the last `'"w"*'`) and PostgreSQL (`@@ to_tsquery('simple', '''w''')`, the last `'''w'':*'`), the word a parameter's text, and on SQL Server with `LIKE '%w%'`, a match on a part of the text that reads every row of the type, locale and state. Every word must match a row of the document, not always the same field, and on SQLite and PostgreSQL the last as the start of a word. The rows are narrowed to the request's type, locale and state, to its site when it names one, and always to the fields searchable now, those of the request among them when it names them, so a field the caller may not read, or the rows of a field no longer searchable or now hidden, which stay until the entry is written again or rebuilt, neither match nor order; on SQL Server each word is a `LIKE` scan over those rows, a row per entry and field; the most recently indexed `SearchRequest.Limit` ids are returned (by the latest row of those fields); the order of the list is the query's sort. The filter and the row rule of the query apply after that cap, so with more matches than the cap a reader can miss entries it may read, which the README says and the list's `searchTruncated` tells ([#3302](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3302), 4.4). A search of no word, such as `q=?!`, finds nothing.
- **Refusals.** Where the plugin is disabled in the site of the request (the request's site, else the operation's for a global type), a search is refused as without an index, `FCMS2211` (400) on `q`. A type with no `Searchable` field that is not hidden is refused with `FCMS1700` (400), whose message names the attribute. Both are a `ValidationException` on `q`, so every surface of the engine answers them alike.
- **Keeping current.** Outbox handlers of `EntryDraftSavedEvent`, `EntryPublishedEvent`, `EntryUnpublishedEvent`, `EntryDraftDiscardedEvent`, `EntryVersionRestoredEvent`, `EntryTrashedEvent`, `EntryRestoredEvent` and `EntryPurgedEvent` (`search.content.{event}`) read the entry again through `IContentService`, as the system principal of the delivery and without fallback, in the locale of the event and in every other locale the index holds for it, since a field that is not localizable is shared, and write the draft and the published row of each: a read that finds nothing removes the row, and a row whose text is the same is left alone. Reading the entry rather than the event makes a repeated or reordered delivery harmless. The handlers of `EntryTrashedEvent` and `EntryPurgedEvent` remove every row of the entry, in every locale, whatever the site allows, since both act on the whole entry, unless a read in the event's locale shows that a restore delivered first brought it back; `q=` on the trash list therefore finds nothing. A restore writes the rows again in the locales the restoring site allows, and the other locales of a global entry come back when a site that allows them rebuilds or the entry changes there. A locale the site of the delivery does not allow is not read (`ILocaleResolver` refuses it): the rows of a site's entry in it go, and those of a global entry stay for the sites that allow it. A read refused with another `ValidationException` is treated the same way and logged (9383). An import and a seed raise the same events. A delete or a purge while the plugin is disabled in the site reaches no handler, and the plugin learns nothing when it is disabled, since core gives a plugin none of the events of a site where it is disabled, `PluginDisabledEvent` included; so the rows stay, never served, until enabling the plugin there again rebuilds the site or the site is deleted. `SiteDeletedEvent` (`search.cleanup.site.deleted`) removes the rows of the site and logs how many (9381). The Delivery output cache is evicted by core's own handlers, which start after the plugin's, so a search cached between the two is served until the next change of the type or `OutputCacheDuration`.
- **Rebuild.** `POST /api/management/v1/search/rebuild`, mapped with `MapManagement("/search")`, with an optional body `{ "type" }`, needs `search.manage` in the site of the request (`IPermissionChecker`), refuses a type that is not a content type with `FCMS1701` (422) and one without a searchable field with `FCMS1700`, and answers 202 with `{ "jobId" }` of the job `search.rebuild` it queues for the site, with a 30 minute attempt timeout; `fluentcms jobs list` shows it. The job lists the drafts and the published versions of each searchable type, or of the one named, in the site and of the global types, a page at a time (at most 100, and at most `MaxPageSize`, with a cursor), writes what differs, then removes the rows of the type that it did not see and that nothing wrote since it started, and, for every type, the rows in the site and the global rows of a type that is no longer searchable; it logs what it wrote and removed (9380). A second run writes nothing. A list reads one locale and the SDK lists no locales of a site, so a localized type is read in each locale the site allows (asked of `ILocaleResolver`) among the site's default, the locales that the entries in it hold (a list with `locale=all`) and the locales the index holds. The rows of the site in a locale it no longer allows are removed; for a global type only the rows in the locales the site allows are swept, so a rebuild of one site never removes what another site reads. Enabling the plugin in a site (`PluginEnabledEvent`, `search.rebuild.plugin.enabled`) queues a rebuild of the site (9382), since its handlers heard nothing of it while it was disabled.
- **Decisions.** A deleted entry's rows go, as the issue has it: the cap comes before the trash filter, so trashed drafts kept for the trash list would crowd out entries that can be read. Accents and case are folded by the plugin for every provider, so PostgreSQL's `simple` configuration and SQL Server's collation do not decide them. More matches than the limit give the most recently indexed, as the seam has no ranking. A hidden field is not indexed, since no response holds it. A search matches only the fields the caller may read, as a filter does, rather than being refused to a role whose grant is limited to some fields; that costs a row per field instead of one per document. A delivery and a rebuild read only the locales their site allows, and leave the rows of a global type in other locales to the sites that allow them.
- **Codes and log ids.** `FCMS1700` a type that cannot be searched (400) and `FCMS1701` a rebuild that names no content type (422); `FCMS1702` to `FCMS1719` are free. Log event ids 9380 to 9383, of 9380 to 9399.
- **Left for later.** Ranking by relevance, stemming by language, a full-text catalogue on SQL Server, the fields of components and blocks, search of the trash, an external index, search over media and semantic search (9.16).

**Search seam as built.** Core defines the seam and the plugin fills it: `ContentQuery.Search`, the `q=` parameter of the Delivery and Management lists, and the `ISearchIndex` slot, whose core default refuses `q=` with `FCMS2211` (4.4, "Search as built"). The index finds ids and the engine narrows the query to them, so the row rule, site, locale and status still decide what a caller reads. `Searchable` (4.2) is the plugin's to read, and an index matches only the fields marked `Searchable` that are not `Hidden`, whatever rows it holds, also when `SearchRequest.Fields` is null; how it keeps its index current, such as from the entry events, is its own. Storage hooks in core (`tsvector` with GIN on PostgreSQL, an FTS5 external-content table on SQLite, a full-text catalogue on SQL Server, through `IStorageConventions`) are out of scope for now and stay additive, as does ranking by relevance; the search plugin builds its own full-text objects in its migrations instead. The Entra ID, Okta and Keycloak presets of generic OIDC are in core (4.7); other presets and SAML (4.18) are Should. The rendering plugin (Blazor components and Razor tag helpers) is Should and mostly not backend; its server pieces (rich text to HTML, draft preview middleware, sitemap) reuse `RichText/` renderers from core.

## 6. Tooling that targets the backend

Rows 11.1 to 11.6, 11.12, 11.13.

### 6.1 CLI

`fluentcms` dotnet tool; standalone binaries follow after 1.0 (decision 59; 10.3, P12). Works against the host project (`--project`) or a running instance (`--url`, with a management API key in `--token` or `FLUENTCMS_TOKEN`; 10.3, P3). The command line is parsed with System.CommandLine (10.3, P2).

**Project commands run inside the host (10.3, P1).** The command logic is in Core. A host calls `app.RunFluentCmsCommandAsync(args)` between `Build()` and `Run()`; the tool builds the project and starts its assembly with `fluentcms <command>`, so a command runs with the host's own FluentCMS.Core, configuration and plugins, as the system with `Origin.Cli`, and before any hosted service starts. The reference host makes the same call, so `docker run <image> fluentcms migrate` is what an init container runs. `migrations add` and `migrations remove` stay in the tool's own process, because they need EF Core's design-time package, which a host does not get.

| Command | Does | Mode |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `migrations add <name>`, `migrations remove` | content migration stream for the host's provider; `add` reports possible renames, renames what a field with `[FormerNames]` had (4.2), and refuses a destructive change without `--allow-destructive` | project, in the tool |
| `migrations list`, `migrations script` | applied and pending migrations per stream; the SQL of a stream | project |
| `migrate [--check]` | applies all streams in order; `--check` lists pending and exits non-zero | project |
| `schema snapshot`, `schema validate`, `schema diff` | snapshot, validation errors, drift between code, an instance, a schema file or the applied history, with rename detection (4.14) | project, `--url` |
| `schema apply [--script [--output]] [--dry-run] [--allow-destructive]` | brings the content tables of a host whose types are data up to its schema, on every provider (4.3) | project |
| `generate csharp`, `generate typescript`, `generate openapi` | from the schema snapshot of a running instance, of the host or of a schema file; `--check` for CI | project, `--url` |
| `export`, `import [--dry-run]` | transfer packages (4.14) | project, `--url` |
| `apikey create`, `apikey list`, `apikey revoke` | a key bound to a role, printed once; the keys; revoking one | project, `--url` |
| `user create` | an active administrator, with the password from a prompt or standard input, which the user keeps: no forced change | project |
| `user invite`, `user add-role` | an invitation by email; a role for a user | project, `--url` |
| `user reset-password` | the reset email, or with `--password-stdin` a new password | project |
| `jobs list`, `jobs retry <id>` | the newest jobs; a dead-lettered job queued again | project, `--url` |
| `jobs run <name> [--inline]` | a run of a recurring job, queued or in the command's process | project |
| `seed [--name]` | runs the registered seeders (4.16) | project |
| `mcp` | stdio to Streamable HTTP proxy for IDE clients | `--url` |
| `backup`, `restore` | SQLite snapshot with the local media files (4.15) | project |
| `doctor` | the startup report without starting the host; with `--url`, an instance's diagnostics | project, `--url` |

`new host` and `new plugin`, which only wrapped `dotnet new`, are dropped: the templates are used through `dotnet new` (6.2).

**The framework as built (B-90, [#3013](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3013)).** The tool (`FluentCMS.Cli`) parses with System.CommandLine 2.0.15, which only it references. Its tree is a command named `fluentcms`, not a `RootCommand`, which would take its name from the assembly, with help and `--version`; `migrate` and `migrations` with `add`, `remove`, `list` and `script` are its commands. Exit codes are 0, 1 for a failure and 2 for bad usage, which prints the parser's errors and the help of the command to standard error. Every project command takes `--project <path>` (the current directory by default), `--assembly <path>` (a built host; nothing is built, and `--project` is only its directory), `--no-build` and `--environment <name>` (`ProjectOptions`). `InstanceOptions` holds `--url` and `--token` for the commands of B-91, B-93, B-97 and B-101: the address is http or https with no user, query or fragment, the key comes from `--token` or `FLUENTCMS_TOKEN`, neither is ever written out, and a missing or invalid one is `FCMS3316` with exit code 2. The HTTP client the tool makes for an instance follows no redirect, to the same origin or another ([#3067](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3067)): HttpClient drops the key on one and would read what the other address answers, such as the sign-in page of a proxy, as the instance's. Each command refuses a 3xx with the code it gives an answer that is not the instance's (`FCMS3253` for `export` and `import`, `FCMS3361` for the others), naming the status and the scheme, host and port of its `Location`, never its path or query, or, for a redirect on the origin of `--url`, saying so and asking to check the path of `--url` or a proxy in front of the instance (`InstanceAnswer`).

A command that runs inside the host is checked by the tool and handed on as arguments: the tool builds the project unless told not to (its notice goes to standard error), reads `TargetPath`, `RootNamespace` and `MSBuildProjectDirectory`, and runs `dotnet exec <TargetPath> fluentcms <command> <options>` (the muxer of `DOTNET_HOST_PATH` when set, `dotnet` from `PATH` otherwise) in the project directory with the caller's environment, `DOTNET_ENVIRONMENT` and `ASPNETCORE_ENVIRONMENT` set from `--environment`, and `Logging__Console__LogToStandardErrorThreshold=Trace` unless the caller set it, so that standard output is the command's own (`ProcessHostLauncher`). The tool's own options are not handed on, and a path such as `--output` is made absolute first. On the console the host writes to it directly; the tool waits for it, and Ctrl+C reaches both. The exit code is the host's. A build that fails is `FCMS3310`, an assembly that is not there `FCMS3311`, and a host that cannot be started `FCMS3312`. The tool never loads the host's assembly for these commands.

Inside the host, `FluentCMS.Core.Commands` has the one public type `FluentCmsCommandExtensions` with `RunFluentCmsCommandAsync(this IHost, string[], CancellationToken)`, which answers null when the arguments do not start with `fluentcms`, and the exit code otherwise. Core reads the words after `fluentcms` with its own small parser (`CommandParser`), which knows the commands of the internal `CommandRegistry`, their options as `--name value` or `--name=value`, their positional arguments and `--help` (`-h`, `-?`), which is help where an option or an argument stands and a value where it is the value of an option, as the tool hands `--name=-h` on as `--name -h` ([#3068](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3068)); bad usage is `FCMS3300` with the usage of the command and exit code 2. Each command is a `HostCommand` (name, description, arguments, options, `RunAsync(CommandContext, CancellationToken)`); the context gives the scope's services, the parsed arguments and the two writers. A command runs in a service scope under `SystemOperation.Begin` with `Origin.Cli` in the default site, and before any hosted service has started, so nothing migrates, validates, seeds, runs jobs or listens. A `CommandException` carries its code and exit code; a `FluentCmsConfigurationException` exits 1 with its message; anything else, a cancellation included, is `FCMS3302` with its type and a message that passed through `SecretScrubber`, and the log (9172) keeps the exception with its stack, scrubbed the same way. A test checks that every command of the registry is in the tool with the same options. `AddFluentCms` registers `CommandEntryPointCheck` first among its hosted services: a host started with arguments that begin with `fluentcms` and that never called the entry point stops in the Starting phase, before the production checks and the migrations, with `FCMS3301`, which names the line to add to `Program.cs`; the migrations at start ask the same, for a host that starts its services concurrently and so does not stop at the first that fails. Codes `FCMS3300` to `FCMS3308` (host; `FCMS3308` is `settings reprotect`'s since [#3364](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3364)) and `FCMS3310` to `FCMS3317` (tool; `FCMS3317` since [#3068](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3068)), and log event ids 9170 to 9172, of the ranges `FCMS3300` to `FCMS3319` and 9170 to 9179.

`migrations add` and `migrations remove` stay in the tool's process (`HostProject`). `remove` asks the host with `migrations list --json` whether its database has the newest content migration, and refuses with `FCMS3314` when it has, or with `FCMS3315` when the host cannot answer; `--force` skips the question and leaves the database alone; a host with no content migration is `FCMS3313`. `migrations list --json` writes one line, `{"streams":[{"name","applied","pending"}]}`, which is the form the tool reads.

**The schema tools and doctor as built (B-97, [#3020](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3020)).** `schema snapshot`, `schema validate`, `schema diff` and `doctor` are commands of the host (4.14, 4.15) and of the tool, which adds `--url` and `--token` to `snapshot`, `diff` and `doctor` and keeps them; it never hands them on. With `--url` the tool reads the instance itself through `InstanceClient`, which sends the key as `Authorization: ApiKey <key>`, and diffs and prints with the host's code, which it reaches as a friend assembly of Core. A refused key is `FCMS3330`, which names the permission (`schema.read`, `system.read`), and an instance that cannot be reached or answers another error `FCMS3329`; a host that does not write its snapshot for a diff is `FCMS3331`. An answer that is not the instance's is `FCMS3361`, as for the commands that administer an instance ([#3067](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3067)): a redirect, a success that is not `application/json`, a snapshot that is not an object of the groups the snapshot writer writes (`collections`, `singletons`, `components` and `blocks`, each left out when empty, so `{}` is the snapshot of an empty schema) or whose types and fields do not have the shape `schema diff` reads, and a diagnostics answer that is not a startup report or names no version of core. `schema snapshot --url` checks the snapshot before it writes it, so the file at `--output` stays as it was. `migrations add` takes `--allow-destructive` (4.14). Since [#3059](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3059) a field renamed with `[FormerNames]` is generated as a rename (4.2) and needs no `--allow-destructive`; `FCMS3327` names, beside the destructive changes, a rename whose values are kept in JSON (`field 'block hero.heading' renamed to 'title', whose values are kept in JSON under 'heading', which the migration does not rename`), and a field removed beside one added without the attribute is still a possible rename, refused as a removed field. Codes `FCMS3320` to `FCMS3326` (host) and `FCMS3327` to `FCMS3331` (tool); `FCMS3332` to `FCMS3334` are free. No log event id was added.

**The commands that administer an instance as built (B-91, [#3014](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3014)).** In project mode they are `HostCommand`s that call the services the Management endpoints call (`ApiKeyService`, `UserAdministration`, `InvitationService`, `PasswordResetService`, `JobAdministration`, the seed runner), as the system with `Origin.Cli`, so their events and activity rows carry that origin and reach the log through the outbox, which a running instance delivers. With `--url` the tool calls the Management API with `Authorization: ApiKey <key>` from `--token` or `FLUENTCMS_TOKEN`, as the key's principal, so the API decides what the key may do; a refusal prints the API's problem with its code first (the errors of a validation problem one per line) and exits 1, an instance that cannot be reached is `FCMS3329`, as for the schema tools, and an answer that is not FluentCMS's `FCMS3361`. The project options and `--url` exclude each other. A role is named by `--role` and `--site` (the site's role, or an instance-wide one without `--site`), and a site by its slug; with `--url` the roles come from `GET /roles` and the sites from `GET /me/sites`, the sites the key holds a grant in. List commands print a table, and with `--json` one line in the shape of the Management API, which is the same in both modes.

- `apikey create --name --class --role [--site] [--types <list>] [--expires <date>]` writes the key alone on standard output, once, and what describes it to standard error; with `--json` the line is the API's answer, which holds the key as `secret`. A key is bound to one role (4.7), so `--role` is required, and the issue's `--scopes` is `--types`, the content types the key is bound to, since a key's only scope is its class. With `--site` the role may be the site's or an instance-wide one, which the binding narrows. `--expires` is UTC unless it names an offset. `apikey list` never shows a key again; `apikey revoke <id>` keeps the row.
- `user create --email --role [--site] [--password-stdin]` makes an active user whose address is its user name and counts as confirmed (a command applies no migration, so a new database is migrated first, which also seeds the built-in roles), with a password checked by Identity's policy (`UserAdministration.CreateAsync`, which no endpoint calls). The password is read at the terminal without an echo and asked twice, or from the first line of standard input with `--password-stdin`, never from an argument; with standard input redirected and no `--password-stdin` the command refuses (`FCMS3352`, exit 2). The prompt goes to standard error. `user reset-password --email` sends the reset email of the hosted pages, and with `--password-stdin` sets the password as a reset does, which ends the user's sessions. `user invite` and `user reset-password` build the link from `FluentCms:PublicOrigin`, since a command has no request (`FCMS3354` when it is unset). `user create` and `user reset-password` exist in the host only: over HTTP a user is invited, so that a key alone never sets a password, and the API sends no reset email.
- `jobs list [--queue] [--status]` shows the newest 100 jobs. `jobs run <name>` takes the name of a recurring job, whose row holds the payload and queue of its runs (an instance adds its recurring jobs when it first starts), and adds a job due now with `Origin.Cli`, which an instance that works the queue runs. With `--inline` the job is written as already claimed by the command's process, with one attempt, and run there through the job executor with its heartbeat; a failure is dead-lettered (`FCMS3356`, exit 1) rather than left for an instance that may never start, and `jobs retry <id>` queues it again. `jobs run` exists in the host only, since the API has no endpoint that enqueues a job.
- `seed [--name <seeder>]` runs the seeders added with `cms.Seeding.Add` through the runner a start uses, with its claim and versions (4.16), whatever `RunOnStart` says, so a second run does nothing; `--name` limits the run to one seeder. A claim held by another run is `FCMS3358` with exit 1, after what was applied is listed.
- `mcp --url` is a proxy from stdio to Streamable HTTP (decision 51; 5.4): each line of standard input is posted to `/mcp` with the key, and the answer, JSON or server-sent events, is written to standard output one message per line, as UTF-8 whatever the console's encoding is. It keeps the `Mcp-Session-Id` an instance gives and ends that session when standard input ends, and sends the `MCP-Protocol-Version` of the answer to initialize. It reads no content and adds no tool. Messages are forwarded as they come, so that requests a client sends together are not held behind each other. A 401 or 403 ends it with `FCMS3362` and the problem on standard error and exit 1. Any other answer that is not a JSON-RPC 2.0 message (a problem such as a rate limit, an expired session's 404) and a request that does not reach the instance are written to standard error, and the request gets a JSON-RPC error, so that the client does not wait, and the proxy goes on; its HTTP client has no timeout, since a tool call takes as long as it takes. Only protocol messages go to standard output. It opens no GET stream, since the MCP plugin serves Streamable HTTP statelessly and sends its messages on the answer to each POST.

Codes `FCMS3350` to `FCMS3359` are the host's and the tool's (an unknown role, site, recurring job or seeder, and a value that is not valid, are bad usage with exit 2), and `FCMS3361` to `FCMS3363` the tool's, the last a warning on standard error when a key goes over plain http to a machine other than this one; `FCMS3360` and `FCMS3364` to `FCMS3369` are free. A service's refusal inside the host prints each error with its code, as the API does, and exits 1. No migration, package or SDK type was added.

**Schema apply as built (B-98, [#3021](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3021)).** `schema apply` is a command of the host and of the tool, which hands on `--script`, `--output` (made absolute), `--dry-run` and `--allow-destructive`, and refuses `--output` without `--script` and `--script` with `--dry-run`, as the host does (`FCMS3300`). It prints the changes from the newest row of the schema history to the host's schema in the words of `schema diff`, when the history has a row, then one line per operation, a destructive one marked as `schema diff` marks it, then under `Left alone:` each table of a plugin that the host does not register and each whose name has a control character (4.3), and ends with what it applied and the schema hash that the history now records. The operation and `Left alone:` lines write a control or bidi character of a name read from the database as its escape (`\u000A`, `PrintableText`), so in the script, where they are comments, a line break cannot end one. What it does is in 4.3. A host with content migrations in its set or in its history is `FCMS3335`; a pending system or plugin migration `FCMS3336`, which asks to run `fluentcms migrate` first; a change that loses data `FCMS3337`, refused unless `--allow-destructive` is given and then repeated as a warning; a required column with no empty value for the rows a table has `FCMS3338`; an operation the database refuses `FCMS3339`, after the transaction is rolled back, and so does a lock lost while the operations are planned or run, which also says to run the command again; and a history row that cannot be written after the operations were applied, or whose lock was lost first, `FCMS3340`, which says to run it again to record the history. `--dry-run` exits with the code the run would have, so 1 when it would be refused. Codes `FCMS3335` to `FCMS3340`; `FCMS3341` to `FCMS3349` are free. No log event id was added: the operations are the command's output, and the history row logs 9311 as before.

**Transfer and backup commands as built (B-101, [#3024](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3024)).** `export` and `import` take `InstanceOptions` and `ProjectOptions`: with `--url` the tool calls the transfer endpoints itself (`TransferClient`, on the client the tool makes for an instance), and without it hands the command to the host with its paths made absolute (4.14). `backup` and `restore` are project commands only (4.15). The test that every command of the host is in the tool with the same options leaves `--url` and `--token` out of the comparison, as they are the tool's own. Tests give `CommandLine.RunAsync` a client of a test server for the instance. With `--url`, `export` writes only an answer that is `application/zip`, starts with a zip's signature and opens as one, and refuses anything else and a redirect with `FCMS3253`, leaving `--output` as it was (4.14, [#3067](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3067)). The guide is `docs/backups.md`.

**Generate as built (B-93, [#3016](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3016)).** `generate typescript --output <dir>`, `generate csharp --output <dir> --namespace <name>` and `generate openapi --output <file>` run in the tool. The first two read the schema snapshot of 4.2: with `--schema <file>` from a file, with `--url` from `GET /api/management/v1/schema/snapshot` through `InstanceClient`, as `schema snapshot --url` reads it, and in project mode from the host's `schema snapshot --output`, as `schema diff` reads it, without the database. The text is read back with `SchemaBuilder` over `UncheckedFieldTypes`, which stands in for a field type of a plugin, and the files depend on that text alone, so the three sources give the same bytes, which a test holds for the Blog sample. `--schema` and `--url` together are `FCMS3377` (exit code 2), and a text that is not a snapshot is `FCMS3370`, before any file is written. A field type that core does not know is text of the snapshot that no code checked, and the emitters write it into comments, so one that is not a name (an ASCII letter, then ASCII letters, digits, `.`, `-` and `_`, the rule of `IFieldType.Name` in 4.1, which the tool checks with core's `FieldTypeNames.IsValid`) is also `FCMS3370`, naming the field and not the type ([#3064](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3064)); the field types of core and of the first-party plugins are names. An instance that refuses the key or cannot be reached is `FCMS3330` or `FCMS3329`, and a redirect or an answer that is not `application/json` `FCMS3361`, as for the schema tools ([#3067](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3067)). `generate openapi` saves `/openapi/v1.json` of an instance, or in project mode the document `IOpenApiDocumentProvider` builds in the host without listening, which the hidden host command `openapi document --output <file>` writes (`FCMS3379` when the host has none). With `--url` it saves only an answer that is `application/json` and an object with an `openapi` string; otherwise, and on a redirect, it writes nothing and exits 1 with `FCMS3361`, as the schema tools do ([#3067](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3067)). A hidden command is left out of the host's usage and has no command of its name in the tool.

- TypeScript: one module, `fluentcms.ts`, which imports `@fluentcms/client` as the namespace `fluentcms`, so no name of the schema clashes with an export: an interface per content type (extending `Entry`, with the system fields), component (`SeoComponent`) and block type (`HeroBlock`, with `type` as its literal and an optional `id`); a blocks field is the union of its block types, a relation `Relation<Author, "author">`, the reference or the populated entry, a media field `MediaReference`, rich text `RichText`, a select the union of its options, and a localized type has `fallbacks` with its localizable fields. The builders are those the C# emitter gives, by name, with their members in camel case (`ArticleFields.sections.hero.heading.eq("x")`), and `createClient(options)` binds each content type to a collection or singleton client. A schema where a content type has a display name or a title field (4.2) also gets `contentTypeLabels`, a constant keyed by type name with `displayName` and `titleField`, `null` where the type has none ([#3150](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3150)).
- C#: a class per content type (`[ContentType("article")]`, with `DisplayName` and `TitleField` when the schema gives them, [#3150](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3150); deriving from `Entry`), component (`SeoComponent`) and block type (`HeroBlock`, deriving from the generated `Block`, which names its derived types for `type` and reads one it does not know as itself), with `JsonPropertyName` on every property; a relation is `Relation<Author>`, a media field `MediaReference`, rich text and JSON `JsonElement`, and a localized type has `{Name}Fallbacks`. The builders are those of the emitter of B-95 over the field types of `FluentCMS.Client`, so that `ArticleFields.Title.Contains("x")` reads the same in a host and in a client. Every file carries the schema hash in its header and is written only when it changed; a file with the header of an earlier run that is no longer generated, such as the class of a removed type, is removed.
- `--check` writes nothing, and exits 1 with `FCMS3372`, naming each file that differs, is missing or is no longer generated, when the files on disk are not what the schema gives. A field type without a generated type is JSON or `unknown` with `FCMS3375`, and a name that gives no identifier or is taken, in any case, gets nothing, with `FCMS3376`; both are warnings and the command exits 0. A class name is a file name, so `foo-bar` and `foobar`, `FooBar` and `Foobar`, would be one file where a file system ignores case: the second gets no type, in C# and in TypeScript alike, and no builder (`FCMS0100`, 6.4, [#3071](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3071)), and a set of files whose names differ in case alone is refused before anything is written or checked (`FCMS3380`), whatever gave them ([#3070](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3070)). A C# file of the command's that names another namespace is another run's: it is neither removed as no longer generated nor counted so by `--check`, and a run that would write over one is refused with nothing written (`FCMS3381`), so two namespaces need two folders, as the tool's README says. Other codes: `FCMS3371` for a request for the OpenAPI document of an instance that fails (with the status and the problem's code, never the key), `FCMS3373` for files that cannot be written, `FCMS3374` for a namespace that is not one and `FCMS3378` for a host that wrote no snapshot or document. Codes `FCMS3370` to `FCMS3381` of the range `FCMS3370` to `FCMS3389`.
- The clients: `FluentCmsClient` over the caller's `HttpClient`, which carries the key or the bearer token, and `FluentCmsClient` of `@fluentcms/client` over `fetch` with the headers given; an answer that is not a success throws `FluentCmsProblemException` or `FluentCmsProblem` with the status, `code`, `title`, `detail`, `errors` and `traceId` of the problem. A success whose body is not JSON, such as a page of a proxy, throws the same type with the status, no code and the detail `The answer is not JSON.`, and not the exception of a JSON reader ([#3070](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3070)). Every call but a delete returns a result, so a success with an empty body, whether it says so by its length or not, a 204 or a literal `null` throws it too, with the detail `The answer is empty.` or `The answer is null.`, rather than handing the caller a null its type says it cannot be; a delete returns nothing, and an empty answer to it is no problem. Both clients refuse an empty segment of the path and the segments `.` and `..`, which resolving the address would remove and so reach another route with the caller's key, and escape every other value, `%` too, so `%2e` is sent as the text it is. The problem has the server's `Retry-After`, as `RetryAfter` (a `TimeSpan`) and `retryAfter` (seconds): its seconds, or a date read against the `Date` of the same answer, and nothing for a date with no `Date` or a value that is neither a whole number of seconds nor a date, such as `-5` or `1.5`. In a browser a date is read only where `Date` is readable: it is not among the headers that CORS exposes, so cross-origin `retryAfter` is the seconds the API sends, which is what it sends. A TypeScript call takes a `signal`, in its last options argument, and the client a `timeoutMs`, above 0 and at most 2147483647; either aborts the request and the read of its answer, which rejects with the reason of the signal or a `TimeoutError` (`DOMException`), not a problem ([#3273](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3273), [#3310](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3310)). A write leaves out the system fields and sends a relation or a media field as its id, and an update sends `If-Match` with the version read. Both write a query as the bracket grammar of 4.4 in the order and with the escaping of the server's writer, and are held to one list of filters and the query strings they give, `clients/filter-cases.json`, whose cases on the Blog sample's fields a test sends to its Delivery API, checking below it that the content service is given the query each case builds, with its operands as the case names them ([#3070](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3070)), so the query strings both clients are held to read on the server as they were built. A moment is written in UTC with `Z` and no trailing zeros in its fraction, which a JavaScript `Date` and a `DateTimeOffset` both give.

**CLI, backup and restore follow-ups as built ([#3068](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3068)).** No migration, package or SDK type was added.

- **The key and the address.** `InstanceOptions.Resolve` refuses a key with a character outside `!` to `~`, such as a line break, with `FCMS3316` and exit code 2 for every command with `--url`, before any request; the check was in `ManagementClient` only, and `InstanceClient`, `TransferClient` and `generate` ended with the stack trace of a `FormatException`. `export` and `import` refuse a `--site` with such a character as bad usage, since it goes in `X-Cms-Site`. Every command with `--url` warns once with `FCMS3363` when the key goes over plain http to a machine other than this one: `export`, `import`, `schema snapshot`, `schema diff`, `doctor` and `generate` now too. A usage error is written by the parser from the arguments with the value after `--token` (`--token <key>`, `--token=<key>`, `--token:<key>`) replaced by `***`, so a key given to a command that has no `--url`, such as `user create`, is not repeated, and the tool reads no response file (`@file`), which the parser would otherwise expand before the key could be replaced. The MCP client's configuration with the key in its `env` is in `docs/docker.md`.
- **Ctrl+C.** A command with `--url` runs in the tool alone, except `schema diff --url` without `--schema`, which runs the host for its schema; the parser cancels such a command on Ctrl+C or SIGTERM and gives it 10 seconds to clean up (`ProcessTerminationTimeout`). It exits 1, or, when it has not finished by then, 130 after Ctrl+C and 143 after SIGTERM. An export deletes the file it was writing beside its target, and `mcp` sends the `DELETE` of its session. A project command, and `schema diff --url` without `--schema`, keep no timeout, since the tool waits for the host it started, which gets Ctrl+C itself; cancelling them would kill it.
- **`user add-role --url`** reads the pages of `GET /users?search=<address>` until it finds the address or reaches the total, since the search matches a part of the name or address, oldest first.
- **`mcp`.** `initialize` is sent without the session and the protocol version, and a 404 to a request that named the session clears both, so a client that starts again after its session expired gets a new one; the answer to `initialize` sets the protocol version. A request that the answer does not cover gets a JSON-RPC error (`FCMS3361`): one whose event stream or JSON answer leaves it out, one whose stream breaks before its answer, and one that the server accepts with 202 or 204; a request answered before the stream broke gets no second answer. Ids are compared by value, exactly, so `1` answers `1.0` and `1e-30` is not `0`. The requests of a batch that fails get a batch of errors. Standard input and an event stream are read with `BoundedLineReader`, which decodes UTF-8 itself and takes what each read of the stream gives, since `StreamReader.ReadAsync` goes on reading while each read fills its buffer and so waits on a pipe whose writer waits for the answer; a line ends at a line feed, a carriage return or both. A line of standard input, an answer and an event of a stream are each bounded to 16 MiB of UTF-8 (`FCMS3317`): a longer line is dropped with a note on standard error, and a longer answer or event is not held and its requests get the error. Once standard input ends, the calls in flight have 30 seconds to answer before they are cancelled, with a note. The proxy still opens no GET stream, so a message that a server with sessions starts on one is not relayed.
- **Text from an instance, a package or a schema** is printed with each control character and bidi control (U+061C, U+200E, U+200F, U+202A to U+202E, U+2066 to U+2069, U+2028, U+2029) written as its escape, such as `\u001B` (`PrintableText` in Core): the parts of a problem (code, detail, errors) and the reason phrase, the cells and lines of the commands that administer an instance, a job's `lastError`, the sections and items of an import report, the text of `doctor` (whose refusals, the host's own, keep their line breaks) and `schema diff`, the warnings of `generate` (`FCMS3375`, `FCMS3376`), the field types that `schema validate` notes it did not check, the messages of the schema reader and validator (`SchemaBuilder`, `SchemaValidator`), which `FCMS3370` and the schema commands repeat, and, keeping their own line breaks, every message the tool and the MCP proxy write to standard error. JSON output and the snapshot's bytes are left as they are. `generate typescript` escapes the same characters in a string literal, so a select option with U+202E cannot make its union read otherwise in an editor. `FCMS3370` says that a field type starts with an ASCII letter and holds only ASCII letters, digits, `.`, `-` and `_`.
- **Codes.** `FCMS3249` is restore's (4.15), of the range `FCMS3240` to `FCMS3259`, and `FCMS3317` the MCP proxy's, of the tool's `FCMS3310` to `FCMS3319`; no log event id was added.

### 6.2 Templates

`fluentcms`: an API host with `Program.cs` (with the command entry point of 6.1), `appsettings.json`, a sample `Article` type, the design-time factory and an empty `Migrations/` folder, a tool manifest that pins `fluentcms`, references to the generator and the analyzers, a Dockerfile and a README; options for provider and sample content. It ships no generated migration, since a migration set belongs to one provider. `fluentcms-plugin`: a server project with manifest, settings, a hook, an endpoint, a migration and a test project on `FluentCMS.Testing`; the admin half is added when the admin exists.

**Templates as built (B-94, [#3017](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3017)).** `templates/FluentCMS.Templates.csproj` packs `templates/fluentcms` and `templates/fluentcms-plugin` as the template package `FluentCMS.Templates`, and compiles nothing. At pack time it copies the template files to `obj/` and stamps them: `{{version:FluentCMS}}` becomes the version being packed, so every FluentCMS package and the `fluentcms` tool of a created project have the version of the templates package, and `{{version:<package>}}` the version of that package in `Directory.Packages.props`, so the third-party packages of a created project follow the repository's. An unknown package fails the pack. No package is published; CI installs the templates from a folder feed.

`dotnet new fluentcms -n <name> [--provider sqlite|postgresql|sqlserver] [--sample none|blog]` (defaults `sqlite` and `none`) writes a web project: `Program.cs` registers the `SchemaModel` of the project's types, built with the container's `IFieldTypeRegistry` so that the media field type is known, calls `AddFluentCms`, the command entry point between `Build()` and `UseFluentCms`, `MapFluentCms` and `MapFluentCmsOpenApi`; `CmsSetup` holds what the host and the design-time factory share: `SchemaBuilder.AddContentTypesFromAssembly` over the project, the provider of the option with `ConnectionStrings:FluentCms`, and the media and webhooks plugins with `AddPlugin`, plus `AddBlogSample()` with the sample. `CmsContextFactory` is the `IDesignTimeDbContextFactory<CmsDbContext>` of `migrations add`: it builds the field types from the same services and passes a connection string that is never opened. `--sample none` gives one `Article` (title, slug, rich text body, an indexed date); `--sample blog` gives a copy of the Blog sample's types, settings and seeder in the project (`Blog/`, without the OpenAPI examples), since the sample is not a package; an architecture test holds the copy to `samples/Blog` with the namespace replaced, but for `BlogSample.cs`, which registers the types from the assembly and no examples. `Migrations/Content` is empty: a migration set belongs to one provider (`FCMS1306`), so the first one is `fluentcms migrations add Initial` for the provider chosen. The tool manifest pins `fluentcms.cli` to the templates' version with `rollForward` off. The generator and the analyzers are package references with `PrivateAssets="all"`. `appsettings.json` has `Data Source=fluentcms.db` on SQLite and no connection string on the servers; `appsettings.Development.json` names a local server without a password, which comes from user secrets (the project has a `UserSecretsId`) or `ConnectionStrings__FluentCms`. The first launch profile runs in Development on `https://localhost:5443` and `http://localhost:5080`, since the authorization server answers on HTTPS only, and there SQLite needs no further step: schema sync creates the tables at start. The Dockerfile is the reference host's, built from the project folder: SQLite sets `ConnectionStrings__FluentCms` to `/data/fluentcms.db`, the media files go to `/data/media`, it has no health check, since the host has no probe, and no compose file comes with it (B-99). A `.gitignore`, a `.dockerignore`, which leaves secrets and local settings (`.env`, `*.pfx`, `*.p12`, `*.key`, `*.local.json`, `secrets.json`) out of the build context that the build stage copies, and a README of the first ten minutes complete it. The source names of both templates have a hyphen (`FluentCms-App`, `FluentCms-Plugin1`), so that the template engine tells a path from a namespace: a name that is not a namespace, such as `my-site`, keeps it in file names, project paths and the Dockerfile, and gets `my_site` in code.

`dotnet new fluentcms-plugin -n <name>` writes a solution with `src/<name>` and `tests/<name>.Tests`. The last segment of the name, letters and digits, gives the class prefix (`Acme.Reviews` gives `ReviewsPlugin`), its kebab case the plugin's name, settings area and routes (`reviews`), and that name with underscores its tables (`cms_reviews_note`). The plugin has the manifest with `SdkRange = "[1.0.0,2.0.0)"` and the assembly's version, a permission `{name}.notes.manage`, a settings class with `MaxNotes`, an `IAfterPublishHook<ContentDocument>`, a note table through `PluginDbContext<TPlugin>` with its stream of three sets (`Sqlite`, `PostgreSql`, `SqlServer`, each `Initial{Prefix}`), and `GET` and `POST /api/management/v1/{name}/notes`, which check the permission with `IPermissionChecker` and the settings of the site; a post counts the notes and adds one under the plugin's `IDistributedLock` (`{name}.notes`), so that posts sent together cannot pass `MaxNotes`, and answers 503 when the lock is not free within ten seconds. It references `FluentCMS.Sdk` and `FluentCMS.Plugins.Abstractions`, the EF Core providers of the two server sets, the generator and the analyzers with `PrivateAssets="all"`; an architecture test holds the template's project file to that, as it does a first-party plugin. The test project, on `FluentCMS.Testing` with xUnit v3, boots the discovered plugin in the test host and checks its table, the hook's registration, the permission on the endpoints and the settings limit through the Management API, posts sent together among them, and that each migration set matches the model on its provider; it holds the design-time factory of `dotnet ef`. The sample plugin of the tests stays their fixture and the template is not generated from it.

The `Templates` job of CI packs every package of the build with the version `1.0.0-ci.{run id}.{attempt}` into a folder feed, installs `FluentCMS.Templates` from it with a NuGet configuration that takes the FluentCMS packages from that feed only, creates a host for each provider with and without the Blog sample and a plugin, builds each with warnings as errors, runs the plugin's tests, checks that every FluentCMS package and the tool are at the version of the package, that each Dockerfile has the lines of its provider and that each `.dockerignore` leaves out the secrets, creates a host and a plugin whose names are not namespaces, starts the two SQLite hosts in Development and reads their sample type through the Delivery API, and on the job's PostgreSQL service runs `fluentcms migrations add Initial` and `fluentcms migrate` and starts the host on that database (`templates/test-templates.sh`, which runs locally too). No FCMS code, event id, migration or SDK member was added.

### 6.3 Test host

`FluentCmsTestHost` on `TestServer` (`WebApplicationFactory` needs the entry point of the application under test, and the CMS library has none): `UseSqlite(inMemory or file)`, `WithContentTypes<...>()`, `WithPlugin<T>()`, fixtures `CreateUserAsync(role)`, `CreateApiKeyAsync(class)`, `AsUser(user)` and `AsKey(key)` HTTP clients, `Content<T>()` for in-process calls, `Seed(...)`. `Seed` steps and the registered seeders (4.16) run before the host reports started. The project's own integration tests use it from milestone M1, which is why it ships early; the Blog sample tests are the first to use a seeder. `RunCommandAsync(args)` runs `fluentcms <args>` as a host's command entry point does and returns the exit code and the two outputs (`TestCommandResult`): before the test host has started, on a host of its own over the same database that is built and never started, and after, against the started host (B-90).

### 6.4 Source generator and analyzers

`FluentCMS.Generators` emits, per content type in the host: a typed filter and sort builder (`ArticleFields.Title.Contains("x")`) and block union helpers. The field types the builders return are in the SDK. `FluentCMS.Analyzers` reports `FCMS0xxx` diagnostics: invalid attribute combinations, reserved names, slug source missing, relation to a non-content type, `Localizable` on a non-localized type, a filtered field without `Indexed` (info). Each ships as a package of its own that a host references.

**Source generator as built (B-95).** `FluentCMS.Generators` is an incremental generator that a host references as an analyzer, as the Blog sample and the reference host do. It finds the classes marked `[Collection]`, `[Singleton]`, `[Component]` or `[Block]` and reads them as the attribute reader does (4.2): the same names (`JsonNamingPolicy.CamelCase` for a field, `KebabCaseLower` for a type, ported because the generator runs on netstandard2.0), the inherited properties first, and only the properties with one field attribute and a public getter and setter. A class the reader refuses gets no builder and no word from the generator, since the host reports it at start and the analyzers in the editor. Nor does it report a field the reader refuses, such as `[Blocks(null)]`, which the host refuses with `FCMS1211` and the analyzers with `FCMS0011`, as for `[Blocks]` with no types, or `[Select(null)]`, which the host refuses with `FCMS1216` and the analyzers with `FCMS0007` on an enum and `FCMS0008` elsewhere, as for a list on an enum and no options elsewhere. A blocks field it cannot read gets no member, and the other members and classes keep their builders ([#3071](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3071)). It emits, in the namespace of the class:

- `{Type}Fields` for a collection or singleton, a static class with a member per field, so that `ArticleFields.Title.Contains("x")` gives the same `FieldFilter` as `new FieldFilter("title", FilterOperator.Contains, "x")` and `ArticleFields.PublishedOn.Descending()` a `SortField`.
- `{Name}ComponentFields` for a component, whose members carry the path of the field that holds it: `ArticleFields.Seo.MetaTitle` is `seo.metaTitle`, at any depth of single components. A repeated component is JSON a filter cannot reach and gets no member.
- `{Name}BlockFields` for a block type, with a member for each scalar field one level inside the list (Q7), and for each blocks field a nested class over `BlocksField` with the block types of the field and their presence: `ArticleFields.Sections.Hero.Heading.Eq("x")` is `sections.hero.heading`, `ArticleFields.Sections.HasHero()` is `hasBlock` with `hero`.

The class names come from the schema names, not the CLR names, so that a client emits the same code from a snapshot; the suffixes keep a component or a block from taking the name of a collection, and a name that still clashes, such as a collection `x-block` beside a block `x`, gives the builder to the first and reports the other with `FCMS0100`. Class names are compared ignoring case, as Roslyn compares the names of sources, each named after its class, and as `generate` compares its files: `x-y` and `xy` would give `XYFields` and `XyFields`, and only `x-y` gets a builder ([#3071](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3071)). A block type whose name has a hyphen gets no builder (`FCMS0104`), because a segment of a filter path holds only letters, digits and underscores; its block lists still have `Has{Name}()`. It still takes its class name, as it takes its class in `generate`, so that the class and the builder of a name belong to one type: beside the block type `a-b`, `ab` gets no builder (`FCMS0100`), as it gets no class (`FCMS3376`) ([#3071](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3071)). The members are named from the field names. The builders are public, so a library that declares content types shares its builders with the host.

The field types are in the SDK, in `FluentCMS.Content`, each a `QueryField` with its path and only the operators the engine accepts for its kind: `TextField` (`text`, `slug`, `email`, `url`), `LongTextField`, `RichTextField`, `NumberField<T>` (`long` for `integer`, `decimal`), `DateField<T>` (`DateOnly` for `date`, `DateTimeOffset` for `dateTime`), `BooleanField`, `SelectField` and `SelectListField` for a single and a multiple select, `RelationField` and `MediaField` by id, `JsonField` with `Key(name)` giving a `JsonKeyField`, `BlocksField`, and inside a block `BlockTextField`, `BlockValueField<T>` and `BlockContainsField`. A field that the engine can sort has `Ascending()` and `Descending()`. A select takes the option as the API shows it (`video`), not the enum member. System fields such as `createdAt` have no builder yet.

The emitter is shared source in `src/FluentCMS.Generators/Emit`: `FieldsEmitter.Emit` takes descriptions of the types (names, kinds, fields with their field type, `Multiple`, component and block types) and the namespace of the field types, and returns the sources and diagnostics. The generator fills the descriptions from symbols; `Snapshot/SchemaDescriptions.cs`, which needs the SDK and is linked rather than compiled into the generator, fills them from a `SchemaModel`, so `generate csharp` (B-93) emits the same text from a snapshot, as a test holds for the Blog sample. Its diagnostics are `FCMS0100` (a type gets no builder: its name gives no class name or the name is taken), `FCMS0101` (info: a field type with no builder, such as a plugin type other than `media`), `FCMS0102` (a member name taken in the builder) and `FCMS0103` (info: a component or block type declared in another project, which gets no builder here) and `FCMS0104` (info: a block type with a hyphen in its name). Each has a section of `docs/analyzers.md`, which its help link names, as the analyzers' rules do, and a test holds the sections to the descriptors ([#3310](https://github.com/fluentcms/FluentCMS/issues/3310)). `FCMS0105` to `FCMS0119` are free. Tests in `tests/FluentCMS.Generators.Tests`, which the analyzers share, check the output for given sources, that an unrelated edit regenerates nothing, that each builder offers exactly the filters and sorts the content service accepts, and that the fields of the Blog builders are the fields of its schema.

**Analyzers as built (B-96).** `FluentCMS.Analyzers` is a package of its own with the analyzers under `analyzers/dotnet/cs` and no library, built on netstandard2.0 against Roslyn 5.0.0 like the generator, so that every .NET 10 SDK from 10.0.100 loads it.
